## Detail

### 0xAA760 -- Accelerator-pedal threshold (vs vehicle speed) for a latched high-pedal flag, with hysteresis

1D x16 uint16, data 0xC02D4, range 30 .. 75. Triage hint: throttle / pedal.

Looks up a pedal-angle threshold from vehicle speed: 30 below about 40 km/h, 75 above. The pedal angle is compared against it with hysteresis to set a high-pedal flag, and a second flag is latched on that flag's rising edge while speed is at or above a constant. What that second flag drives is not in the listing.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: vehicle speed, km/h (fr4 loaded from vehicle_speed_kmh 0xFFFF65FC, breakpoints 0..120)
- **Output units:** same units as accel_pedal_angle (0xFFFF64D8), which is compared directly; likely percent or degrees
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of the latched flag at 0xFFFF5BE4 (and 0xFFFF5C02) is not shown. The table's role as a pedal threshold is observed, but which feature consumes the flag is unknown. Candidates include a tip-in, kickdown or 'driver demanding power' detector feeding some throttle or torque logic. The units of accel_pedal_angle (% vs degrees) are also unconfirmed.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF5BE4 (gbr+0 latched flag) and 0xFFFF5C02 to see what feature the flag enables. Also read the constants at 0xC0240 (hysteresis) and 0xC0244 (minimum speed) to complete the definition.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x0121A8-0x0121B6: fr15 is loaded from 0xFFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh) and moved to fr4 in the delay slot of jsr table_lookup_1D. The axis is speed; 0x011EAE-0x011EB0 does the same.
  - 0x0121BC: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF5C04-24 = 0xFFFF5BEC. The first function stores to the same address (0xFFFF5BF4-8).
  - 0x0121C8-0x0121D0: fr8 = table value; fcmp/gt fr14(pedal),fr8; if pedal >= threshold, the flag at gbr+30 (0xFFFF5C02) is set to 1. Comparing directly against accel_pedal_angle fixes the output as a pedal threshold in pedal units.
  - 0x0121D2-0x0121E8: threshold minus the constant at 0xC0240 is floored at 0 by float_max (0xBE960). If pedal is below that, the flag is cleared. This is a hysteresis band.
  - 0x0121BE-0x0121C0: the previous flag value is copied to gbr+31 before the update, enabling edge detection.
  - 0x0121EA-0x012222: if speed < const@0xC0244, gbr+0 (0xFFFF5BE4) = 0. On a falling edge it is also 0. On a rising edge (prev 0, now 1) it is set to 1; otherwise it is held.
  - 0x011EC4-0x011ECC: the first function, apparently an init or reset path, does the same threshold compare and sets flag 0xFFFF5C02, then initialises neighbouring RAM to -1.

### 0xAAA20 -- Max engine load (g/rev) vs RPM: load ceiling for a low-speed/low-RPM/low-load enable window (purpose of window unknown)

1D x10 uint8, data 0xC15EC, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Gives an engine-load ceiling by RPM. Current load must be below it, together with speed below a constant and RPM below a speed-indexed ceiling. While all three hold, a delay counter at 0xFFFF5D24 counts down; when it reaches zero a flag at 0xFFFF5D8A is set, and that flag also feeds a second flag at 0xFFFF5D84.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, RPM (lookup input fr15 = rpm_current 0xFFFF6624)
- **Output units:** g/rev (engine load threshold)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Which feature the window enables is unknown. The flags 0xFFFF5D8A and 0xFFFF5D84 are consumed elsewhere. The window is also bypassed when check_cl_active returns 1, and only evaluated when 0xFFFF5EDE!=0 or 0xFFFF5ED9==3, and those meanings are unverified.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF5D8A and 0xFFFF5D84 (GBR 0xFFFF5D24 + 102/96) to identify the enabled feature. Also read the speed constant at 0xC1590 and the timer reload byte at 0xC1496, and check what writes 0xFFFF5EDE and 0xFFFF5ED9.
- **Cited:**
  - 015B60-015B66: descriptor 0xAAA20 is passed to table_lookup_1D with fr4 = fr15. fr15 was loaded at 015AF2 from 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]).
  - 015B68: fmov.s fr0,@r10 stores the result to 0xFFFF5E18 (r10 loaded at 015B4C).
  - 015B7A-015B7E: fmov.s @r10,fr8 then fcmp/gt fr12,fr8. fr12 is engine_load_current (015AF6), so the table value is a load threshold that load must stay below; failing it falls through to 015B80 mov #1,r14.
  - 015B6A-015B78: the sibling conditions are const@0xC1590 > vehicle speed (fr14), and the speed-indexed table 0xAAA0C result (stored at 0xFFFF5E14) > rpm (fr15). Any failure sets r14=1, an 'out of window' flag.
  - 015B98-015BB6: if r14==1 the counter at gbr+0 (0xFFFF5D24) is reloaded from the byte at 0xC1496. Otherwise it is decremented while above zero. At zero, r4=1 and 015BBE stores it to gbr+102 (0xFFFF5D8A). check_cl_active==1 (015B92) forces r4=1 directly.
  - 015BC0-015BE0: 0xFFFF5D84 (gbr+96) is set when engine running, engine_state_byte!=0, byte 0xFFFF5FCA!=1 and 0xFFFF5D8A==1.
  - The values (0.20..1.0) fit g/rev loads, and the threshold drops to 0.2 at 4400 rpm, consistent with a low-load-only window.

### 0xAC82C -- Coolant-temp per-step decay multiplier for the 0xFFFF728C decaying value (variant: 0xFFFF90C1≠0, 0xFFFF3158≠0, 0xFFFF72D1==1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD1C6, range 0.89999 .. 0.96001. Triage hint: transient fuel.

Gives a per-execution multiplier of 0.90 to 0.96, indexed by coolant temperature and slower when cold. Each pass of the routine multiplies the running value at 0xFFFF728C by it, clamps the result at 0, then holds four outputs (0xFFFF7290..729C) at the larger of the decayed value and their own floors (0xFFFF7328..7334). It sets the decay rate of a value seeded from 0xFFFF72DC that relaxes toward four per-channel floors.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, °C (axis shared with a defined table; ECT input loaded from 0xFFFF6354 into fr4 at 02F03C/02F03E)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier per routine execution (fraction retained per step)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The mechanism is fixed by the code, but the physical quantity being decayed is not: it comes from 0xFFFF72DC and its consumers (readers of 0xFFFF7290..729C) are not in this listing. The meaning of the three selector flags (0xFFFF90C1, 0xFFFF3158, 0xFFFF7306/72D1) is also unverified. 'transient fuel' and the coolant_decay_* labels are hints only.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7290, 0xFFFF7294, 0xFFFF7298 and 0xFFFF729C to learn what the decayed outputs feed. Search for writers of 0xFFFF72DC (seed), 0xFFFF7328..7334 (floors) and 0xFFFF726C (re-arm trigger). Find the setters of 0xFFFF90C1 and 0xFFFF7306 to tell when this variant, rather than its seven siblings, is selected.
- **Cited:**
  - 02F03C-02F03E: fr4 = float at 0xFFFF6354 (ECT) just before the lookup, which matches the coolant axis from the definition.
  - 02F118-02F148: this descriptor (0xAC82C) is chosen only when r5 = byte@0xFFFF90C1 ≠ 0, r6 = byte@0xFFFF3158 ≠ 0 and byte@(101,gbr) = 0xFFFF72D1 == 1. The other seven descriptors (AC840/890/854/8A4/818/868/87C) cover the remaining combinations, and all eight feed the same code at 02F162.
  - 02EFE0-02EFE8: 0xFFFF72D1 is latched to 1 when byte@0xFFFF7306 == 1.
  - 02F162-02F164: the lookup result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF72D0-92 = 0xFFFF7274.
  - 02F166-02F178: fr4 = @(0xFFFF728C) * @(0xFFFF7274), passed to float_max with fr5 = 0, and the result is written back to 0xFFFF728C. This is a multiplicative decay clamped at 0.
  - 02F17A-02F1E4: for each channel i, the output 0xFFFF7290/94/98/9C becomes floor_i (0xFFFF7328/2C/30/34) if the floor exceeds the decayed value, and the channel's gbr flag (3..6) is cleared; otherwise the output takes the decayed value.
  - 02F1E6-02F21E: when gbr byte 0 (0xFFFF726C) ≠ 0, 0xFFFF728C and all four outputs are seeded from 0xFFFF72DC and flags 3..6 are set to 1. The decay runs only while some flag 3..6 == 1 (02EFF4-02F016).
  - Data 0.96 at -40..-30, 0.94 at -20..-10, 0.90 from 0 °C up: the multiplier is below 1, so the value always decays, and faster when warm.

### 0xAC854 -- Coolant-temperature decay coefficient (multiplicative, per-pass) for the 0xFFFF728C decaying value — variant: 0xFFFF90C1==0, afl_diagnostic_flag!=0, latch 0xFFFF72D1==1

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD206, range 0.85001 .. 0.94. Triage hint: transient fuel.

Gives a dimensionless 0.85–0.94 factor from coolant temperature. Each pass multiplies a decaying float at 0xFFFF728C by it, floors the result at 0, then copies it to four outputs (0xFFFF7290/94/98/9C), each held no lower than its own floor value at 0xFFFF7328..7334. This is one of eight sibling tables chosen by three flags.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (from definition); the input is presumably ect_gated_c 0xFFFF6354, loaded into fr4 at 0x02F03E just before the branch tree
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier per execution of the routine (fraction retained each pass; a lower value means faster decay)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity being decayed (the charge at 0xFFFF72DC, the floors at 0xFFFF7328..7334 and the four outputs) is not identified by this listing. The decay time constant also depends on how often 0x02EFD4 is called, and that call rate is not shown. Use of ect_gated_c in fr4 as the lookup input is assumed from the calling convention and was not seen inside table_lookup_1D.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7290, 0xFFFF7294, 0xFFFF7298 and 0xFFFF729C (the decayed outputs), and for writers of 0xFFFF72DC (initial charge) and 0xFFFF7328..0xFFFF7334 (floors), to identify what quantity decays. Separately, find the scheduler/caller of 0x02EFD4 to get its execution period, which converts this factor into a time constant.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x02F07E-0x02F082: descriptor 0xAC854 is passed in r4 to table_lookup_1D 0xBE830. This path is reached only when byte 0xFFFF90C1==0 (0x02F048 tst r5 / bf), afl_diagnostic_flag 0xFFFF3158!=0 (0x02F04C tst r6 / bf/s 0x02F076) and gbr+101 (0xFFFF72D1)==1 (0x02F07A cmp/eq #1 / bf). The other seven combinations use the sibling descriptors AC840, AC890, AC8A4, AC818, AC868, AC82C and AC87C.
  - 0x02EFE0-0x02EFE8: if 0xFFFF7306==1, gbr+101 is latched to 1, which selects this table over AC8A4.
  - 0x02F162-0x02F164: the result fr0 is stored to r14-92 = 0xFFFF7274.
  - 0x02F166-0x02F16E: fr4 = @(0xFFFF728C), then fmul by the table value, i.e. state *= factor.
  - 0x02F170-0x02F178: float_max(fr4, fr5=0) with the result written back to 0xFFFF728C. This is a geometric decay toward 0 with a 0 floor.
  - 0x02F17A-0x02F1E4: for each of 0xFFFF7328/732C/7330/7334, the output 0xFFFF7290/7294/7298/729C = max(floor, decayed value). When the floor exceeds the decayed value, the matching flag gbr+3/4/5/6 is cleared.
  - 0x02F1E6-0x02F21E: when flag gbr+0 (0xFFFF726C) is nonzero, the state is reloaded from 0xFFFF72DC (coolant_decay_charge_value), copied to all four outputs, and flags gbr+3..6 are set to 1. This is the start of a decay event.
  - 0x02EFF4-0x02F03A: if all of flags gbr+3..6 are already 0, the outputs are simply set to their floors and no lookup is done.

### 0xAC868 -- Coolant-temp-dependent per-cycle decay multiplier (variant: fn_2F03C_gate set, afl_diagnostic_flag clear, flag 0xFFFF72D1 != 1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD226, range 0.89999 .. 0.96001. Triage hint: transient fuel.

Gives a per-execution geometric decay factor (0.96 cold, 0.90 from 0 °C up) by coolant temperature. Each pass multiplies a decaying value at 0xFFFF728C by this factor. The result is then floored against four per-channel limits at 0xFFFF7328..7334, and each channel's active flag clears once its floor is reached.

- **Weakest link:** The decay mechanism is observed, but the physical quantity being decayed is not. It is whatever is loaded from 0xFFFF72DC and consumed from 0xFFFF7290..729C, possibly a transient or after-start enrichment. The time constant also depends on an unknown task rate.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7290, 0xFFFF7294, 0xFFFF7298 and 0xFFFF729C to see where the decayed values enter fueling or timing. Search for the writers of 0xFFFF72DC (initial charge value) and 0xFFFF7328..0xFFFF7334 (floors). Find which task calls 0x02EFD4 to get the execution rate.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x02F03C-0x02F03E: fr4 = @0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c) before the lookup, consistent with the Coolant Temperature axis from definition
  - 0x02F044-0x02F04A and 0x02F118-0x02F124: this descriptor is chosen when gate byte 0xFFFF90C1 != 0, afl_diagnostic_flag 0xFFFF3158 == 0 and GBR+101 (0xFFFF72D1) != 1; it is one of six sibling coolant tables
  - 0x02F162-0x02F164: the lookup result fr0 is stored to base-92 = 0xFFFF7274
  - 0x02F166-0x02F178: fr4 = @0xFFFF728C * @0xFFFF7274, then float_max(fr4, 0.0) (fldi0 fr5), stored back to 0xFFFF728C. This is a multiplicative decay of a running value
  - 0x02F17A-0x02F1E4: for each of 0xFFFF7328/732C/7330/7334, the output 0xFFFF7290/7294/7298/729C = max(decayed value, limit), and when the limit wins the flags GBR+3..6 (0xFFFF726F..7272) are cleared to 0

### 0xAC890 -- Coolant-indexed per-step decay multiplier (one of 8 variants: fn_2F03C_gate=0, afl_diagnostic_flag=0, flag 0xFFFF72D1 != 1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD266, range 0.875 .. 0.89999. Triage hint: transient fuel.

Each time this routine runs, it multiplies a stored float at 0xFFFF728C by this coolant-dependent factor (about 0.9), clamps the result at or above 0 and writes it back, so the value decays geometrically. The decayed value is then compared with four reference values (0xFFFF7328..7334). For each, the larger of the two is written to 0xFFFF7290..729C, and the matching state flag is cleared once the reference exceeds the decayed value.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, degC (axis shared with a named definition). The lookup input fr4 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c) at 02F03E.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier applied once per execution of this routine (per-step retention factor, 0.875..0.9)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The multiplicative-decay role is fixed by the code. What physical quantity decays (seeded from 0xFFFF72DC, floored by 0xFFFF7328..7334, output at 0xFFFF7290..729C) is not shown here. The 'transient fuel' and 'coolant_decay' labels are unverified hints. The time base also depends on the task rate of this routine, which is not shown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7290, 0xFFFF7294, 0xFFFF7298 and 0xFFFF729C to find what consumes the decayed values. Search for writers of 0xFFFF72DC and 0xFFFF7328..7334 to identify the seed and floor quantities. Find the task-table entry or caller of 0x02EFD4 to get the step period.
- **Cited:**
  - 02F068: mov.l @(0x02F10C),r4 loads descriptor 0xAC890; 02F06C jsr table_lookup_1D at 0x0BE830; result returned in fr0.
  - 02F03E: fmov.s @r2,fr4 with r2=0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c) loads the coolant input before the lookup.
  - 02F162-02F164: fr0 is stored to @(-92,r14). r14=0xFFFF72D0, so the address is 0xFFFF7274.
  - 02F166-02F16E: fr4=@(0xFFFF728C), fr8=table value, fmul fr8,fr4, so the stored state is multiplied by the table value.
  - 02F170-02F178: jsr float_max with fr5=0 (fldi0 at 02F174), then store to 0xFFFF728C. This gives state = max(state*factor, 0), i.e. geometric decay.
  - 02F17A-02F1E4: for each of 0xFFFF7328/732C/7330/7334, fcmp/gt picks max(ref, decayed) and stores it to 0xFFFF7290/7294/7298/729C. When the ref is larger it clears the GBR flags at offsets 3..6 (0xFFFF726F..7272).
  - 02F1E6-02F21E: when gbr+0 (0xFFFF726C) is nonzero, the decay state 0xFFFF728C is seeded from 0xFFFF72DC (coolant_decay_charge_value) and flags 3..6 are set to 1, which starts a new decay.
  - 02F044-02F058: selection among 8 descriptors (AC818..AC8A4) depends on 0xFFFF90C1, 0xFFFF3158 and gbr+101 (0xFFFF72D1, set from 0xFFFF7306==1 at 02EFE0-02EFE8). This table is the gate=0, diag=0, flag!=1 case.

### 0xAD420 -- Minimum RPM enable threshold vs coolant temperature (gate for the 0xAD7C4 2D-table feature, output FFFF7D24)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCEEF0, range 1300 .. 2300. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Gives an engine-speed threshold that falls with coolant temperature, from 2300 rpm when cold to 1300 rpm when warm. Engine speed must be at or above it before the routine runs the 2D lookup at 0xAD7C4, clamps the result and stores it to FFFF7D24 with an active flag. Below the threshold, or when the other preconditions fail, it falls through to 0x03CB46 instead.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (ect_current FFFF6350 passed in fr4)
- **Output units:** rpm (engine speed threshold)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The quantity gated is unknown. The listing does not reveal what FFFF7D24 (and its 0..8 step index at FFFF7D2C) does, so the feature this RPM threshold enables cannot be named. The 'ignition timing' subsystem hint is unverified. Also, the branch at 03C878 (sched_status byte == 1) skips the gate entirely, so the table only matters when that byte is not 1.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF7D24 (the clamped float), FFFF7D2C (the step index byte) and FFFF7D2D (the active flag), e.g. displacement accesses off a 0xFFFF7D2C literal, to learn what the gated intervention drives. Also disassemble 0x03CB46 (the else path) and identify the inputs FFFF5E64 and FFFF5D5C used by the 2D table 0xAD7C4.
- **Cited:**
  - 03C846-03C848: fr4 = [FFFF6350] ect_current, the argument to table_lookup_1D (jsr at 03C868, descriptor 0xAD420 loaded at 03C864). This matches the coolant axis named in the definition.
  - 03C86C-03C870: the result fr0 is stored to FFFF7D2C+8 = FFFF7D34.
  - 03C842-03C844: fr12 = [FFFF6624] rpm_current.
  - 03C8B2-03C8BA: fr8 = [FFFF7D34] (the table value); fcmp/gt fr12,fr8 then bt 0x03C8E4. If threshold > rpm, the feature path is skipped. The table value is compared directly against engine speed, so its unit is rpm.
  - 03C8BC-03C8CA: only when rpm >= threshold (and [FFFF5E64] >= const@0xCC538, and byte [FFFF5DD8] != 0) is table_lookup_2D 0xAD7C4 run with fr13=[FFFF5D5C] and fr15=[FFFF5E64]. The result goes to FFFF7D38.
  - 03C8CC-03C8E2: the 2D result is clamped to 0..const@0x03CA48 by float_clamp_range, stored to FFFF7D24, and the flag byte FFFF7D2D is set to 1.
  - 03C8FE-03C96E: a following routine turns FFFF7D24 into a 0..8 step index stored to the byte at FFFF7D2C. This suggests a staged intervention, but what it controls is not shown.

### 0xAF1B4 -- RPM-indexed per-call decay step for the EEPROM-backed value at FFFF85E4 (mode 0 only; quantity unidentified)

1D x8 float32, data 0xF8D14, range 0.1 .. 7. Triage hint: engine control.

In mode 0 (r4 arg == 0), this table gives the amount subtracted from the previous FFFF85E4 value on each call, looked up by RPM. The result is new = max(floor@FFFF8564, min(prev - thisTable(rpm), ceilingTable_AF1A8(rpm))), written back to FFFF85E4 (with a copy at FFFF856C and bitwise-complement backups at gbr+316/320). So the stored value decays toward the floor much faster at high RPM (0.1 per call at 1000-1500 rpm, 7 per call at 6000 rpm).

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: engine speed in rpm. fr14 is loaded from rpm_current (FFFF6624) at 0BC8B8 and passed as fr4 to table_lookup_1D at 0BC952.
- **Output units:** Same units as the stored value at FFFF85E4, subtracted once per execution of 0xBC886. The physical quantity is unknown.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The mechanism (rpm-dependent decrement step, clamped between a floor and an rpm ceiling) is shown directly. What FFFF85E4 physically represents is not shown here, and its 'fuel_knock_state_var' label is a name only. The per-call rate also depends on the unknown scheduling rate of 0xBC886.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF85E4 and FFFF856C, and of the complement copies at gbr+316/320 (FFFF86A4/FFFF86A8), to identify the quantity. Also decode the companion ceiling table 0xAF1A8 and whatever writes the floor at FFFF8564. Then find the callers of 0xBC886 to get the call rate and which r4 mode is used.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BC8B6-0BC8B8: fr14 = @FFFF6624 (rpm_current), which becomes the lookup input via fmov fr14,fr4 at 0BC926 and 0BC952.
  - 0BC8BA-0BC8C0: in mode 0, gbr+480 = 0xAF1A8 (companion table) and gbr+484 = 0xAF1B4 (this table). Modes 1-3 overwrite these with 0xAB70C/0xAB718 at 0BC914-0BC91A, so this table is mode-0 only.
  - 0BC89A-0BC8B0: fr15 = @FFFF8564 (floor). desc_read_int_safe(FFFF85E4) decides whether prev = @FFFF85E4 or 0 (fldi0 in the delay slot at 0BC8AC). prev is saved at @(4,r15).
  - 0BC924-0BC932: companion table AF1A8(rpm) is stored to FFFF86A4+60 = FFFF86E0.
  - 0BC936-0BC950: this table is only looked up if AF1A8(rpm) > floor and the flags from FFFF65C0, FFFF65D0, FFFF5FB6 and FFFF5FB7 are all != 1.
  - 0BC958-0BC962: this table's result (fr0) is stored to FFFF86A4+64 = FFFF86E4.
  - 0BC964-0BC974: fr4 = prev; fsub fr8,fr4 gives prev - thisTable. float_min is then called with fr5 = AF1A8 value, so the table is a subtraction step.
  - 0BCA26-0BCA34: float_max(result, floor fr15) is stored to @(4,r15).
  - 0BCA36-0BCA60: after eeprom_maturation_check(13), the result goes to FFFF85E4-120 = FFFF856C, its complement to gbr+316, desc_read_float_safe writes it to FFFF85E4, and a complement goes to gbr+320. This makes it a persistent, redundantly stored learned value.

### 0xAA89C -- IAT multiplier for the secondary (RPM-scheduled) limit path, companion to IAT table AA888

1D x16 uint8, data 0xC0CA4, range 0.29688 .. 1.1172. Triage hint: unassigned.

This is a dimensionless IAT-indexed factor (0.30 at cold intake air, 1.12 at warm). It is stored to 0xFFFF5CD4, then multiplied with an RPM-indexed 1D base value, the atmospheric-pressure factor and the ECT factor. The product is capped by the main computed value (0xFFFF5CEC) and can become the function's final output at 0xFFFF5CB4. It does the same job on this alternate path that table AA888 does on the main path; the physical quantity being scaled is not identified.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: intake air temperature, °C (axis shared with a defined table)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (×1.0 nominal, raw/128)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical identity of the scaled quantity is not shown in this function: it could be purge duty, a torque/airflow limit or a similar target clamped 0..80. The path using this table is gated by cal byte 0xC0BAC and RAM byte 0xFFFF79C8, whose values and meaning are not shown.
- **Next step:** Read the calibration byte at 0xC0BAC: if it is 0, this table never reaches the output. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF5CB4 (-96, the final output) and 0xFFFF5CE0 (-52), and for writers of 0xFFFF79C8, to identify what quantity this IAT factor scales.
- **Cited:**
  - 013E24-013E2E: descriptor AA89C is looked up via table_lookup_1D with fr4=fr15 (iat_current, loaded 013DBA). The result is stored to ssm_output_workspace-64 = 0xFFFF5CD4.
  - 013E1A-013E22: the sibling IAT table AA888, on the same fr15 input, is stored to -68. On the main path it multiplies -76 (2D rpm×requested_torque table AA9B8) ×-72 (atm-pressure 2D AA980) ×-60 (ECT 1D AA8B0) at 013E84-013E9A. That product is clamped 0..80.0 (literal 0x42A0 at 014004) to give -80.
  - 014046-014074: -56 = clamp(-104 + -92·-72·-68·-60 + -100, 0, -80).
  - 01408C-0140AE (taken when stack flag @24 is set): fr4 = -48 × -72 × -64 × -60. Here -64 is this table and -48 is an RPM-indexed 1D lookup chosen from AA950/AA968/AA95C at 013E4C-013E6E. The result is passed to float_min with -56 and stored to -52, so the table multiplies one factor of a product.
  - 0140E2-0140F0: -52 is copied to -96 (0xFFFF5CB4), which is then rate-limited/deadband-processed at 0140F2-0141B0.
  - 013F08-013F24: flag @24 is set only if calibration byte 0xC0BAC != 0, the byte at 0xFFFF79C8 == 1, and none of the earlier inhibits branched to 01400C. Those inhibits include fuel_system_state==1 and the check_cl_active result.

### 0xAAA0C -- RPM upper threshold vs vehicle speed for a low-speed/low-RPM/low-load debounced condition flag

1D x6 uint8, data 0xC15BC, range 3500 .. 5000. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Looks up a maximum engine-speed threshold from vehicle speed: 5000 rpm when stopped, falling to 3500 rpm at 60 km/h and above. The condition holds only while speed is below a constant, RPM is below this threshold and load is below a second RPM-indexed threshold. While it holds, a countdown at 0xFFFF5D24 runs, and when the countdown expires a flag is set.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: vehicle speed in km/h (fr14 loaded from vehicle_speed_kmh 0xFFFF65FC and passed in fr4)
- **Output units:** rpm (compared directly against rpm_current)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The purpose of the resulting flags (0xFFFF5D8A and 0xFFFF5D84) is not visible here, so what the 'low speed, low RPM, low load held for N ticks' condition enables is unknown. The speed constant at 0xC1590 and the counter reload at 0xC1496 were also not decoded.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF5D84 (gbr+96) and 0xFFFF5D8A (gbr+102), with 0xFFFF5E14 as a secondary check, to identify which routine uses the debounced condition flag. Also decode the float at 0xC1590 (speed limit) and the byte at 0xC1496 (debounce count).
- **Cited:**
  - 015AEC-015AEE: fr14 = @0xFFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh [TRACED]); 015B5A: fmov fr14,fr4 in the delay slot of the jsr to table_lookup_1D with descriptor 0xAAA0C, so the axis is vehicle speed.
  - 015B42/015B4C: r10 = 0xFFFF5E18 on both paths; 015B5C-015B5E: result fr0 stored to @(-4,r10) = 0xFFFF5E14.
  - 015B72-015B76: fr8 = @0xFFFF5E14 (this table's output); fcmp/gt fr15,fr8, where fr15 = rpm_current (015AF0-015AF2). The output is an RPM ceiling.
  - 015B6A-015B70: speed must be below the constant at 0xC1590. 015B7A-015B7C: load (fr12, engine_load_current) must be below a second 1D table 0xAAA20 indexed by RPM (stored at 0xFFFF5E18). If any test fails, r14 = 1 (015B80).
  - 015B98-015BA2: r14 == 1 reloads the counter at gbr+0 (0xFFFF5D24) from the constant at 0xC1496. 015BA4-015BB4: otherwise the counter is decremented while positive. 015BB6: when it reaches 0, r4 = 1, which is stored to gbr+102 (0xFFFF5D8A) at 015BBE.
  - 015BC0-015BE0: flag gbr+96 (0xFFFF5D84) = engine running AND engine_state_byte nonzero AND byte at 0xFFFF5FCA != 1 AND flag 0xFFFF5D8A == 1.

### 0xAAF84 -- Minimum throttle-plate angle vs RPM to enable a MAP/intake-pressure plausibility counter (diagnostic enable threshold)

1D x15 uint16, data 0xC3A50, range 13.64 .. 57.3. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Gives an RPM-dependent throttle-angle threshold. The counter at gbr+30 (0xFFFF68C2) only advances while throttle_plate_angle is above this value and the other enable conditions hold. Otherwise the counter and the flag at 0xFFFF68B5 are cleared.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm_current (0xFFFF6624), in rpm. The axis is not named in the definition files, but the code passes fr14 = rpm_current as the lookup input.
- **Output units:** Same units as throttle_plate_angle (0xFFFF62DC), degrees or percent depending on that variable's scaling. Range 13.6..57.3.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the gated routine actually diagnoses is not shown. Its interpretation as a MAP or intake-pressure plausibility check rests on the label of 0xFFFF620C, on the 2D table named desc_map_expected_pressure (a label, so possibly wrong), and on 0xFFFF2240 being unidentified. The table's role as a throttle-angle enable threshold is fixed by the code. The output unit depends on how throttle_plate_angle is scaled.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of the flag 0xFFFF68B5 (gbr_adc_68A4+17) and the counter 0xFFFF68C2 to see which DTC or behaviour they drive. Also identify 0xFFFF2240 (it may be barometric or boost-target pressure) by finding its writer, and read the constants at 0xC34F0, 0xC34F4, 0xC34F8, 0xC34FC and the word at 0xC30C6.
- **Cited:**
  - 024EF8: fr14 = @0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current). 024F1C: fmov fr14,fr4 is the input to table_lookup_1D (jsr at 024F1A) with descriptor 0xAAF84.
  - 024EFC: fr15 = @0xFFFF62DC (throttle_plate_angle).
  - 024F20-024F22: the lookup result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF68C0-20 = 0xFFFF68AC.
  - 024F50-024F56: the stored value is reloaded into fr8. fcmp/gt fr8,fr15 then bf 0x024F88 means that unless throttle_plate_angle > table(rpm), the code jumps to the reset path.
  - 024F88-024F8C: the reset path clears the flag byte at gbr+17 (0xFFFF68B5) and the word counter at gbr+30 (0xFFFF68C2).
  - 024F3C-024F4E: the other enable conditions are ect_current > const@0xC34F0 and const@0xC34F4 < rpm < const@0xC34F8.
  - 024F58-024F5C: the byte returned by 0x29AA0 must be 0.
  - 024F30-024F64: fr9 = @0xFFFF2240 - (MAP + 2D-table result) must not exceed const@0xC34FC.
  - 024F66-024F86: when all conditions pass, uint16_add_sat increments the counter. The flag at 0xFFFF68B5 is set when the counter >= the word at 0xC30C6.
  - The threshold rises monotonically with rpm (13.64 at 800 to 57.3 at 6400). That shape fits a 'sufficiently open throttle for this rpm' gate.

### 0xAAFC4 -- RPM-scheduled rise step for a two-channel sensor peak (max-envelope) tracker

1D x7 float32, data 0xC3B20, range 0 .. 0.312. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Gives, from engine RPM, the per-pass amount by which the learned maximum of two sensor channels may climb toward the live reading. The learned min/max envelope then normalises each channel to 0..100 %. The 0.312 step applies up to 3000 rpm, fades to 0 by 4000 rpm, and freezes upward tracking above that.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input fr4 = fr14, loaded from rpm_current 0xFFFF6624 at 020A3E)
- **Output units:** Same units as the raw sensor values at 0xFFFF44A8/44AC (probably volts, unconfirmed), per execution of this routine
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the physical sensor at 0xFFFF44A8/44AC is, and so the step's units, is not shown. Only the envelope-tracking role is fixed by the code.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of 0xFFFF44A8/0xFFFF44AC to identify the two sensor channels. Also search for readers of the normalised outputs at 0xFFFF6458/0xFFFF645C and of the GBR flag bytes 0xFFFF647C..0xFFFF6481 (GBR 6458 + 36..41).
- **Cited:**
  - 020A3C-020A3E: fr14 = @0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current); fr14 is not changed before 020C02 'fmov fr14,fr4', so the X input is RPM.
  - 020C04-020C06: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF648C-4 = 0xFFFF6488.
  - 020C08-020C1A: if sensor A (@r15, copied from 0xFFFF44A8) is not below fr13, then fr13 = float_min(fr13 + step, sensorA). This raises the channel-A maximum toward the reading by at most the table value.
  - 020C1E-020C3A: the same is done for channel B (@(4,r15), from 0xFFFF44AC) against max [28] (loaded from 0xFFFF21C4).
  - 020AE8-020B20: the companion table 0xAAFB8 (also RPM-indexed) is subtracted from minima fr15/fr12 with a float_max floor at the sensor. That is the mirror-image min tracker, so this table is the max-side rate.
  - 020C68-020C78: after a counter threshold (C30AA), the max decays by the constant C33D4, floored at C33DC. The min side grows by C33D4, capped at C33D0 (020B44-020B54). This is classic envelope learning.
  - 020CFE-020D24: range = max - min. If the range passes the deadband check, output = (sensor - min)/range * 100.0 (0x42C8 at 020D6C), clamped at 0 or above. The result goes to 0xFFFF6458 (648C-52), and channel B to 6460-ish via -48.
  - 020DB8-020DD2: the min/max values are written back to 0xFFFF21AC/21B4/21BC/21C4.
  - 020DE4-020E32: the percent outputs are compared with thresholds C3160/C3164 to set GBR flag bytes 36..41.
  - 020BBA-020BC8: this block runs only when r12 = 1 (enable from flags and battery_voltage > C33CC) and byte [20] (return of 0x61496) is nonzero.

### 0xAB558 -- Minimum engine-load threshold vs RPM for diagnostic enable conditions (g/rev)

1D x10 uint16, data 0xC6074, range 0.21997 .. 0.25. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Looked up by RPM, it gives an engine-load threshold of about 0.22–0.25 g/rev. Three separate functions compare engine_load_current against it to decide whether some monitor or diagnostic condition is enabled, i.e. whether load is above a near-idle floor.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input is rpm_current 0xFFFF6624 loaded into fr4 at all three call sites)
- **Output units:** g/rev (engine load), compared directly against engine_load_current
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Which diagnostic or monitor each comparison gates is not shown. 0xFFFFA290 and the @(32,r13) output are consumed elsewhere, so the label 'diagnostic enable' is generic. The fact that it is a load threshold vs RPM is solid.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF97E4/0xFFFF97C4 and of diag_monitor_state 0xFFFFA290 byte 0 to identify which monitors use this low-load floor. Also find the caller of 0x083FB6 to see what struct @(28/32,r13) represents.
- **Cited:**
  - 071B7E/071B86: rpm_current is loaded into fr4 in the jsr delay slot before table_lookup_1D. 083FC6 and 07D716 (stack slot 36 = rpm_current, filled at 07D688) do the same, so the X axis is RPM.
  - 071B8C: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF97E4-32 = 0xFFFF97C4. 071BBC-071BC0: it is reloaded and compared with fcmp/gt fr12,fr8, where fr12 = engine_load_current (071B78). If table <= load, the code branches to 071C68, which sets the flag at 0xFFFF97E4 to 1.
  - 07D718: the result is stored to stack slot 84. 07D8F8-07D902: fr9 = engine_load_current (slot 48, from 07D6A0) is tested with fcmp/gt fr8,fr9 against the table value. If load is not greater than the table, the code bails to 07D98C, which clears the enable byte written to diag_monitor_state 0xFFFFA290.
  - 083FD0: the result goes to fr15. 08400A: fcmp/gt fr14,fr15 with fr14 = engine_load_current (083FC2). The output flag at @(32,r13) is set only if table > load, when the r12 qualifier is set.
  - At every site the value is compared against the same-unit engine load and never multiplied or added to anything. It is a threshold, not a trim. The values (0.22 at idle rpm, rising to 0.25 at 4000+) sit at roughly the closed-throttle load floor.

### 0xAD400 -- RPM-dependent count threshold for the FFFF65D0 event counter (gates the state-4 branch of the 0xFFFF7C9A state machine)

1D x8 float32, data 0xCEEC0, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

The table is looked up against rpm_current. The result is the minimum value the saturating counter at gbr+32 (0xFFFF7CB2) must reach before this function can take the 'state 4' branch. That counter is incremented while byte FFFF65D0 == 1 and otherwise reset to 0. Below the threshold the code falls through to the graded states 3/2/1, chosen by comparing gbr+24 against thresholds at gbr+4, +2 and +0.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** engine speed, rpm (fr14 loaded from rpm_current 0xFFFF6624 at 0x03AE7E and passed as fr4 at 0x03AEB2)
- **Output units:** counts (dimensionless count, compared against a uint16 counter). The listed float values are a misdecode and the real cell values are not known yet.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The table's role as a counter threshold is observed. Two things are not. First, what the counter physically means depends on the unknown flag FFFF65D0 and on the state machine at 0xFFFF7C9A, whose consumer is reached through the tail jump to 0x0003D2FC and is not shown. Second, the actual cell values are unknown because the descriptor's float32 decode conflicts with the uint16 read path.
- **Next step:** Re-decode the 16 bytes at 0xCEEC0 as uint16, and check the descriptor 0xAD400 type and scale fields against table_desc_1d_uint16. The raw words look like 0x003F,0x0000,0x003F,0x0000,0x0026,... Then search for writers of 0xFFFF65D0 and for readers of 0xFFFF7C9A (state, gbr+8) and 0xFFFF7CB7 (gbr+37), starting at 0x0003D2FC.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x03AE7C-0x03AE7E: fr14 = @0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current); 0x03AEB2: fmov fr14,fr4 in the delay slot of the call to table_desc_1d_uint16 (0x000BE8AC) with r4 = descriptor 0xAD400.
  - 0x03AEB4: extu.w r0,r5. The result is treated as a uint16, so the float32 decoding (denormals of 5.8e-39 etc.) does not match how the routine reads the data.
  - 0x03AE88-0x03AEA4: r14 = extu.b @0xFFFF65D0. If it equals 1, @(32,gbr) is incremented through uint16_add_sat(+1); otherwise r0 = 0 is written back. So gbr+32 counts consecutive passes with FFFF65D0 == 1.
  - 0x03AEDC-0x03AEE2: r6 = extu.w @(32,gbr); cmp/ge r5,r6. The table value is the threshold for that counter. bf goes to 0x03AEF2 (states 3/2/1/0); otherwise the code continues to the fuel_mode_flags (r13) check and the flag check at 0xCBC92.
  - 0x03AEE6-0x03AEF0 and 0x03AF4A: if r13 is zero or @0xCBC92 is zero, the branch goes to 0x03AF4A, which sets @(8,gbr)=4 and flags 17/18/19/20=1. Separately, if r13 and @0xCBC92 are both nonzero, it falls through to 0x03AEF2 (staged states).
  - The surrounding preconditions are all required: @(12,gbr) != 0 (0x03AEA6), gbr+24 < gbr+6 (0x03AEBC), check_engine_running == 1 (0x03AEC0) and gbr+24 > @0xCBC90 (0x03AED2).

### 0xADFE8 -- Condition-selector to array-index remap (used when flag 0xFFFF5FB8 == 0), feeding the 0xFFFF80DC correction

1D x5 float32, data 0xD3964, range 0 .. 3. Triage hint: knock.

Maps a small integer selector, the byte at 0xFFFF6810 (values 1..5), to a slot index 0..3. The index is stored as a byte to 0xFFFF80E4 and used to pick one element from each of the float arrays at 0xFFFF6654 and 0xFFFF668C. Their difference is scaled by the constant at 0xD2D1C, added to a base 1D-table value, clamped and stored to 0xFFFF80DC. This table is used only when knock_active_flag == 1 and the byte at 0xFFFF5FB8 is zero; otherwise sibling descriptor 0xADFF4 is used.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: selector byte read from 0xFFFF6810 (labelled ol_condition_selector, name unverified), converted to float via 0xBE588 with gain 1.0 and offset 0.0; breakpoints 1..5
- **Output units:** unitless integer array index (0..3), truncated or converted to a byte by the 0xBE874 processor
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Its role as an index remap is fixed by the code. What the selector at 0xFFFF6810 and the arrays at 0xFFFF6654/0xFFFF668C physically represent is not shown; the labels and the knock-subsystem hint are unverified. The exact float-to-int rounding inside 0xBE874 is also assumed.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF6810, 0xFFFF6654[0..3] and 0xFFFF668C[0..3], and for readers of 0xFFFF80DC and 0xFFFF80E4, to learn what the selector and the two arrays mean. Also dump sibling table 0xADFF4 and compare its mapping against this one.
- **Cited:**
  - 043374-043382: byte @0xFFFF6810 is extu.b'd and passed to 0xBE588 with fr4=1.0 (fldi1) and fr5=0 (fldi0). The result goes to fr15, so the X input is that byte as a float.
  - 043384-04338C: the whole block runs only if byte @0xFFFF6790 == 1. Otherwise 0433E4 stores 0.0 to 0xFFFF80DC.
  - 04338E/04339C-0433A4: r14 = byte @0xFFFF5FB8. bf/s skips the descriptor load at 0433A4 when r14 != 0, leaving r4 = 0xADFF4. When r14 == 0, r4 = 0xADFE8, this table.
  - 0433A6-0433AE: 0xBE874 is called with fr4 = fr15. r0 is stored as a byte via mov.b r0,@r1 to 0xFFFF80E4, so the output is an integer.
  - 0433B0-0433C2: that byte is extu.b'd and shll2'd (x4), then used as an offset into float arrays at 0xFFFF6654 (to fr0) and 0xFFFF668C (to fr8). This fixes the output as an array index.
  - 0433C4-0433E2: fr4 = float @0xFFFF80E0, the earlier 1D lookup of 0xAE260 on float @0xFFFF6650. Then fr4 += (A[idx] - B[idx]) * const@0xD2D1C, clamped by 0xBE56C between consts @0xD2D14 and @0xD2D18, and stored to 0xFFFF80DC.

### 0xAE14C -- RPM hysteresis band for a low-RPM threshold flag (indexed by byte 0xFFFF72A0)

1D x16 float32, data 0xD3C80, range 100 .. 200. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Gives an RPM hysteresis width, indexed by the byte at 0xFFFF72A0. The function subtracts it from the threshold returned by sibling table 0xAE140 (same index) to get a lower trip point. The flag at gbr+2 (0xFFFF7E76) is cleared when rpm_current >= the upper threshold and set to 1 when rpm_current < upper minus this value; in between it holds its previous state.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** Index from the uint8 at 0xFFFF72A0 (values 0..15), converted to float by fmac_interp_uint8 with gain 1 and offset 0. Physical meaning unknown: possibly a mode, gear or state number.
- **Output units:** rpm (a difference, because it is subtracted from an rpm threshold and compared against rpm_current)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Only the role (an rpm hysteresis width) is fixed by the code. What the flag at 0xFFFF7E76 and the state at 0xFFFF7E88 control, and what the 0xFFFF72A0 index means, are not shown, so the subsystem (ignition, idle or something else) is unknown. Note also that 0xFFFF7E88 is used both as a float base and as a byte state; that oddity is unexplained.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7E76 (the hysteresis flag) and 0xFFFF7E88 (the state byte), and for writers of 0xFFFF72A0 (the index). Also decode table 0xAE140 to see the upper rpm thresholds; that will show the band (for example idle-region rpm) and the purpose.
- **Cited:**
  - 03E92E-03E936: extu.b r13 (byte read from 0xFFFF72A0 at 03E8F8) is passed to fmac_interp_uint8 with fr4=1 and fr5=0; the float result is kept in fr14.
  - 03E938-03E944: table 0xAE140 is looked up with the same index, and its result is stored to 0xFFFF7E80 (r6=0xFFFF7E88, offset -8).
  - 03E946-03E952: this table (0xAE14C) is looked up with fr14, and its result is stored to 0xFFFF7E84 (offset -4).
  - 03E954-03E960: fr8 = [7E80] - [7E84], stored to 0xFFFF7E78 (offset -16). This makes it the lower threshold, i.e. upper minus this table.
  - 03E962-03E968: the upper value [7E80] is copied to 0xFFFF7E7C (offset -12).
  - 03E96A-03E974: fcmp/gt fr15 (rpm_current, loaded at 03E904),fr8. If upper <= rpm, 0 is written to @(2,gbr), which is 0xFFFF7E76.
  - 03E976-03E980: if the lower threshold (upper minus this table) > rpm, 1 is written to @(2,gbr); otherwise the flag is left unchanged. This is classic hysteresis.
  - 03E982-03E9B2: the flag then drives a state byte at @(20,gbr)=0xFFFF7E88 (values 0/1/2). That logic uses the counter at 0xFFFF3200, a byte threshold at gbr+0 selected by ECT, afl_diagnostic_flag and an ECT limit at 0xD29F4.
  - 03E928-03E92A: the whole block runs only when transient_enable_gate (0xFFFF65F1) == 0.

### 0xAF580 -- ECT-dependent debounce count (ticks) for RPM-above-threshold before setting flag FFFF905B (mode where FFFF905C != 1)

1D x16 float32, data 0xD990C, range -40 .. 30. Triage hint: unassigned.

Coolant temperature indexes a uint16 count threshold. A saturating counter at FFFF9066 (FFFF905C+10) counts consecutive calls in which RPM is at or above an ECT-dependent RPM threshold (table 0xAF56C). When that counter reaches this table's value, the byte flag FFFF905B is set to 1.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (input fr14 loaded from FFFF6350 [TRACED ect_current]; breakpoints -40..110)
- **Output units:** Execution-tick count (uint16, unscaled). The float32 decode is wrong: 5.5102e-40 is the bit pattern 0x00060000, i.e. raw uint16 values around 6 and 0. The trailing -40..30 values are probably the next table's axis read past the end.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What FFFF905B means downstream is not shown, so the purpose of the RPM-held-high flag is unknown. The tick rate of 0x05FE7E is also unknown, so the count cannot be converted to seconds. The raw cell values are uncertain until 0xD990C is re-read as uint16.
- **Next step:** Re-decode the 32 bytes at 0xD990C as 16 big-endian uint16 values. Then search for readers of FFFF905B, and of FFFF905C as the mode selector, to learn what the flag gates. Find the scheduler slot that calls 0x05FE7E to get the tick period.
- **Cited:**
  - 05FE8A/05FE8C: fr14 = float at FFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED]); 05FEC0 passes fr14 as fr4 to the lookup at 05FEBE.
  - 05FEBC/05FEBE: the lookup goes through 0xBE8AC table_desc_1d_uint16 [TRACED], not the float routine 0xBE830. So the cells are uint16, and the float32 decode of 0xD990C is a mis-decode.
  - 05FEC2: the result is stored as a word with mov.w r0,@(8,r15).
  - 05FEA2-05FEA6: fr13 = table_lookup_1D(0xAF56C, ECT), an RPM threshold. 05FE90: fr12 = rpm_current (FFFF6624 [TRACED]).
  - 05FF9C-05FFB4: if fr13 > rpm, the counter @(10,r14) at FFFF9066 is reset to 0. Otherwise it is incremented by uint16_add_sat (0xBE554 [TRACED]) with r5 = 1.
  - 05FF7A-05FF86 (reached only when the call result != 1 at 05FED0 and byte FFFF905C != 1 at 05FF54): if rpm >= fr13, r5 = counter @(10,r14) is compared with this table's value from @(8,r15). cmp/hs r0,r5 then bt 0x05FF96 goes to 05FF96-05FF9A, which writes 1 to FFFF905B.
  - 05FF88-05FF94: an alternative path sets the same flag from a battery-voltage-based RPM threshold (0xAF5B8) plus counter @(14,r14) checked against the constant at 0xD9864.
  - 05FED4-05FEDA: when the 0x22F92 check returns 1, FFFF905B is cleared instead and the comparison is skipped.
  - 05FF58-05FF66: the sibling table 0xAF5A0 plays the same role for the FFFF905C == 1 mode, using counter @(12,r14).

### 0xAF5A0 -- ECT-dependent dwell-count threshold for the RPM-above-limit counter (FFFF9068) that sets flag FFFF905B, used when mode byte FFFF905C==1

1D x16 float32, data 0xD99CC, range -40 .. 120. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looked up against coolant temperature, it gives a count limit. When FFFF905C==1, once the counter at FFFF9068 (ticks with RPM at or above the AF58C ECT-based RPM threshold) reaches this limit, flag FFFF905B is set to 1.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature (ect_current, FFFF6350), degC, -40..110
- **Output units:** uint16 count of calls to this routine (scheduler ticks); tick period unknown
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What flag FFFF905B and mode byte FFFF905C mean is not visible here, so the higher-level purpose is unknown. The true uint16 cell values are also unknown because the descriptor decoded the data as float32.
- **Next step:** Re-decode the 16 cells at 0xD99CC as big-endian uint16 (with any scale or bias from descriptor 0xAF5A0) to get the real count thresholds. Then search for readers of byte FFFF905B and writers of byte FFFF905C to find what the flag gates.
- **Cited:**
  - 05FE8A/05FE8C: fr14 = ect_current (FFFF6350); 05FECA fmov fr14,fr4 makes ECT the lookup input for descriptor 0xAF5A0
  - 05FEC6/05FEC8: the lookup goes through table_desc_1d_uint16 (0xBE8AC), so the cells are uint16, not float32. The float decode (5.51e-40 = 0x00060000, followed by values that look like an axis) is a mis-decode.
  - 05FECC: extu.w r0,r5 keeps the result in r5. At 05FF60-05FF66 it is used as cmp/ge r5,r6, with r6 = the uint16 at @(12,FFFF905C), i.e. FFFF9068. If counter >= table value, the code goes to 05FF96, which writes 1 to FFFF905B.
  - That comparison is reached only when check_cl_active() != 1 (05FED0/05FED2) and byte FFFF905C == 1 (05FF52-05FF56). It is skipped when the AF58C(ECT) value, saved at @(4,r15), is greater than rpm (05FF5A-05FF5E).
  - 05FFB6-05FFCA: counter FFFF9068 is incremented with uint16_add_sat when !(AF58C(ECT) > rpm) and is zeroed otherwise. So it counts consecutive ticks with rpm >= the AF58C threshold, which makes this table a duration threshold.
  - Parallel path for FFFF905C != 1: counter @10 (FFFF9066) is compared with table AF580 (05FF7E-05FF86), using the AF56C RPM threshold
  - 05FED4-05FEDA: if check_cl_active()==1, FFFF905B is cleared to 0

### 0xACE20 -- exp(-x) math helper table (exponential decay factor, e.g. filter coefficient exp(-dt/tau))

1D x13 uint16, data 0xCDEC0, range 0 .. 0.711. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

The function at 0x03286C computes e^-x for a float argument x. Below x=0.35 it uses the Taylor polynomial 1 - x + x²/2. Above 0.35 it stores x to FFFF76E4 and tail-calls table_lookup_1D on this table, which holds e^-x sampled at x = 0.35..6.

- **Evidence class:** observed
- **Axes:** x: dimensionless argument of exp(-x), plausibly a time-step/time-constant ratio; the caller is not in the listing
- **Output units:** dimensionless factor (0..1)
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** none
- **Weakest link:** The math identity is solid. What x represents in practice (e.g. dt/tau for a filter coefficient) and which subsystem calls this helper are not shown, because no caller of 0x03286C is in the listing.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for literal 0x0003286C (jsr targets) to list the callers of this exp(-x) helper and see what argument they pass. Readers of 0xFFFF76E4 can also be checked, but it only holds the last argument.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x03286C mova @(0x03293C) and 0x03286E fmov.s @r0,fr8: the literal words are 0x3EB3, 0x3333, i.e. float 0.35. At 0x032870 fcmp/gt fr8,fr4 and 0x032872 bf 0x032880 branch on x > 0.35.
  - 0x032874-0x03287E: when x > 0.35, x is stored to 0xFFFF76E4, r4 is loaded with descriptor 0xACE20, and jmp @r2 tail-calls table_lookup_1D (0xBE830) with fr4 = x. The table value is therefore returned straight to the caller.
  - 0x032880-0x032890: otherwise the code computes fr0 = x*x / (1+1) + (1 - x), which is 1 - x + x²/2, the second-order Taylor series of e^-x. At x=0.35 this gives 0.71125, matching table cell 0 (0.711), so the two branches join continuously.
  - The table data equals e^-x at the breakpoints: 0.4→0.670, 0.5→0.607, 1→0.368, 2→0.135, 3→0.050, 4→0.018, 5→0.007, 6→~0 (e^-6 = 0.0025 stored as 0).
  - The neighbouring function at 0x032892 does discretisation-style arithmetic (fmac, fdiv by fr14, float_deadband_check guards against divide-by-zero). This fits a filter/estimator helper library, but it is context only.

### 0xAB0CC -- Vehicle-speed limiter multiplier vs speed (variant: FFFF5FB8==0, cl_ol_gate_flag!=3)

1D x2 uint8, data 0xF8950, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Looks up a 0..1 factor against the value at FFFF65FC (tagged vehicle speed). The factor is 1.0 up to 228 and falls to 0.203 at 230, so it reads like a speed-limiter reduction. It is used only when rpm is at or above a threshold, engine_mode_bits bit 7 is clear and neither diag result equals 2. The result goes to FFFF64DC, and its bitwise complement goes to FFFF6528 as a redundancy copy.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: FFFF65FC, tagged vehicle_speed_kmh (km/h), breakpoints 228..230
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no reduction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** high
- **Weakest link:** What reads FFFF64DC is not in the listing, so it is unproven that the factor scales torque, throttle or fuel. The speed name on FFFF65FC is a label that may be wrong, and 228..230 is unusual if the units are not km/h.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of FFFF64DC (and the complement check at FFFF6528) to see what the factor multiplies. Also confirm that FFFF65FC is written from the vehicle-speed sensor computation.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA38-0BBA3A: fr15 is loaded from FFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh [TRACED]) on the r10==0 path.
  - 0BBA3C-0BBA46: r12 is set to 0xAB0CC when byte FFFF5FB8 (r13) is 0 and cl_ol_gate_flag is not 3. Siblings AB0E0, AB0F4 and AB108 cover the other combinations.
  - 0BBA98-0BBAA6: table AB0A4 is looked up on rpm (fr14), and fr6 is loaded with a threshold constant from F890C.
  - 0BBAC8-0BBAD6: if rpm >= fr6, the code calls table_lookup_1D with r4=r12 (this table) and fr4=fr15 (speed), then stores fr0 to the stack slot @r15.
  - 0BBAB6-0BBABA and 0BBAF4: when r11 (derived from engine_mode_bits bit 7 at 0BBA14-0BBA1C) equals 1, the result is forced to 1.0 (fldi1). This is a bypass to a neutral multiplier.
  - 0BBAC0/0BBAC6 to 0BBAF0: when either diag result (P0137 path) equals 2, fr0 is stored without this lookup. That fr0 is the rpm lookup (AB0A4/AB0B8) left in the register, not this table.
  - 0BBB02-0BBB10: the result is stored to FFFF650C-48 = FFFF64DC, and NOT of its bit pattern is stored to FFFF650C+28 = FFFF6528. A complement copy suggests a safety-monitored limit value.
  - Data: 1.0 at 228 and 0.203 at 230. This is a sharp cut near a top-speed limit, but a magnitude match is not proof.

### 0xAB0E0 -- Vehicle-speed limiter multiplier (one variant, selected when FFFF5FB8==0 and cl_ol_gate_flag==3)

1D x2 uint8, data 0xF895C, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Gives a 0..1 factor against vehicle speed: 1.0 at 228 km/h, falling to 0.203 at 230 km/h. The factor goes to FFFF64DC, and its bitwise complement goes to FFFF6528 as a redundant check copy. It is most likely a top-speed limiter scale on torque, throttle or fuel; that reading assumes the downstream consumer, which is not in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: vehicle speed, km/h (fr15 loaded from vehicle_speed_kmh FFFF65FC)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no reduction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** high
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of FFFF64DC is not in this listing. 'Speed limiter' is inferred from the axis (vehicle speed 228-230 km/h), the 1.0-to-0.2 shape and the complement-protected storage, not from seeing what the factor multiplies.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF64DC (and the check copy FFFF6528) to see what the factor multiplies, e.g. a target torque, throttle or fuel-cut path. Also read the floats at F890C and F8910, the rpm gates, to confirm the path is reachable in normal driving.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA38/0BBA3A: fr15 is loaded from FFFF65FC vehicle_speed_kmh [TRACED]. At 0BBACC fmov fr15,fr4 passes it as the lookup input, so the X axis is vehicle speed, consistent with breakpoints 228/230.
  - 0BBA3C-0BBA48: r12 is set to 0xAB0E0 only when the byte at FFFF5FB8 (r13) is 0 and the cl_ol_gate_flag byte (r14) equals 3. Otherwise sibling tables AB0CC, AB0F4 or AB108 are used, so there are four variants of one quantity.
  - 0BBAC8-0BBAD6: the r12 table is looked up only when the constant at F890C (fr6) is not greater than rpm_current (fr14), i.e. above an rpm floor. The result fr0 is stored to @r15.
  - 0BBAF4: the other paths store fldi1 = 1.0, so the neutral value is 1.0, i.e. a multiplier.
  - 0BBB02-0BBB10: @r15 is stored to FFFF650C-48 = FFFF64DC, and the not of its bits is stored to FFFF650C+28 = FFFF6528. A complement redundancy copy is typical of safety-monitored torque or throttle values.

### 0xACD7C -- After-start enrichment initial multiplier (primary component, decays to 1.0) vs coolant temperature

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCDD50, range 1.25 .. 2.2002. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Gives the starting value, by coolant temperature, of a multiplicative component (A, stored at 0xFFFF73E0) that is added to a second component (B, from table 0xACD90, stored at 0xFFFF73E4). The sum goes into an enrichment factor at 0xFFFF7400, and after a delay A is stepped down toward a floor of 1.0 while B is stepped down toward 0.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, Celsius (ect_gated_c 0xFFFF6354; axis shared with a named definition)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (about 1.25..2.2; the component's floor is 1.0 = no enrichment)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** high
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF7400 is not in the listing, so it is assumed, not shown, that this factor multiplies fuel injection. The meaning of counter 0xFFFF67EC as time since start is also unverified (its label says DTC maturation). The upper clamp constant at 0x031368 is unknown and could cap the output.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7400 and confirm it multiplies the injector pulse width or fuel mass. Read the float at 0x031368 to check the upper clamp does not make the table inert. Find the writer of 0xFFFF67EC to confirm it counts time since start.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x030B6E-0x030B7E: the init function looks up 0xACD7C with ECT from 0xFFFF6354 and stores the result at 0xFFFF7400-32 = 0xFFFF73E0.
  - 0x030B80-0x030B98: table 0xACD90 is looked up with the same ECT and stored at 0xFFFF73E4; fadd fr9,fr8 then sums the two and the sum is stored to @r14 = 0xFFFF7400.
  - 0x031190-0x0311B4: while the 16-bit counter r14 (from 0xFFFF67EC) is below the ROM constant at 0xCBC28, 0xFFFF73E0 is reloaded from this table every call, so it is the initial value. The alternate table 0xACDA4 is used when 0xFFFF3158 == 1.
  - 0x0311C6-0x0311E4: output = (A+B) * @0xFFFF7434 (table 0xAC43C at 0x03117E), passed to float_clamp_range with low bound 1.0 (fldi1 fr5) and high bound from 0x031368, then stored to 0xFFFF7400. When mode byte @r15 == 2 it is float_min(A+B, const) instead.
  - 0x031260-0x031282: on each decay tick A = max(A - decay(0xACD54, ECT) * @0xFFFF73FC, 1.0) (fmac with fneg, then float_max with fldi1). A therefore decays toward 1.0, which fixes it as a multiplier component.
  - 0x031284-0x0312A6: B = max(B - decay(0xACD68) * 0xFFFF73FC, 0.0). B is an additive component that decays to 0.
  - 0x031146-0x03117C: when A is within the deadband of 1.0 and B within the deadband of 0, 0xFFFF7400 is forced to 1.0, i.e. the enrichment has ended.
  - 0x031240-0x03125E: the decay tick rate is gated by counter gbr+97 against a threshold from table 0xAC430 (ECT).

### 0xAF398 -- Throttle airflow model: relative airflow vs throttle opening and RPM (used for altitude-compensated target throttle)

2D 13x16 float32, data 0xFA20C, range 4.59 .. 1206.7. Triage hint: engine control.

Probably maps a base throttle opening (Y, 0..75) and engine speed (X, 800..5600) to an airflow-like quantity. The function scales that value by an atmospheric-pressure factor and passes it, with the same table descriptor and RPM, to 0xBDD9C, which looks like an inverse lookup returning a pressure-compensated throttle opening. That opening is then floored, clamped and stored.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (16 pts, 0..75): output of 1D table 0xAF1CC applied to FFFF87B0 (FFFF87D4-36), likely base/requested throttle opening; passed in fr4; X (13 pts, 800..5600): FFFF679C, passed in fr5, likely engine speed in rpm (inferred from breakpoints)
- **Output units:** Airflow-like quantity in unknown ECU units. It scales roughly with RPM at wide opening and is RPM-independent at small openings.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** high
- **Weakest link:** That 0xBDD9C is an inverse 2D lookup (solving for Y given output and X), and that FFFF679C is RPM and the Y input is throttle opening. None of these are verified in this listing.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0xBDD9C to confirm it searches the Y axis of descriptor r4 for a given output at fixed X (an inverse lookup). Then search for writers of FFFF679C and FFFF87B0 to confirm RPM and the pedal/throttle input, and readers of FFFF8798 and FFFF87B8 to see where the compensated opening goes.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BD7E2/0BD814-0BD81A: fr12 = table_lookup_1D(0xAF1CC, @(FFFF87D4-36)=FFFF87B0); fr12 becomes the fr4 input of this 2D lookup at 0BD830
  - 0BD7DE-0BD7E0 + 0BD81C-0BD826: second 1D lookup (0xAF194) on atm_pressure_current [TRACED]; result saved at @(4,r15)
  - 0BD7EC-0BD7F0/0BD82C: fr5 = @FFFF679C, the second 2D input; X breakpoints 800..5600 suggest RPM
  - 0BD828-0BD838: fr0 = table_lookup_2D(0xAF398, fr12, FFFF679C) is multiplied (fmul) by the pressure-factor 1D output and kept in fr15
  - 0BD83A-0BD844: calls 0xBDD9C with r4 = the same descriptor 0xAF398, fr5 = same X input, fr4 = the scaled 2D value; this pattern suggests an inverse lookup back to the Y axis; result in fr14
  - 0BD846-0BD85C: result floored with float_max against @FFFF8C14, then float_clamp_range between @F8AEC and fr13 (@F8AF0)
  - 0BD864-0BD886: stores to RAM: fr12 -> FFFF879C, pressure factor -> FFFF87B4, scaled flow -> FFFF87DC, inverse result -> FFFF87B8, clamped result -> FFFF8798, bitwise NOT of clamped result -> FFFF87D4
  - Data shape: at small Y the values are identical across all RPM (choked throttle flow); at large Y the values rise roughly in proportion to RPM (engine-pumping limited). This is characteristic of a throttle flow model.

### 0xAAA40 -- Temperature-dependent RPM offset above base RPM threshold (sets flag FFFF5DD9)

1D x12 float32, data 0xC16A0, range 17.5 .. 175. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF6638 to confirm it is coolant temperature. Search for readers of FFFF5DD9 (and FFFF5E10) to learn what the flag gates, for example fuel-cut or idle logic. ### 0xAAA4C -- Load-indexed threshold (possibly an RPM limit), input is engine load times a per-gear factor, output peak-held with decay

  - 0x015894-0x015896 and 0x0158AE-0x0158B0: the lookup result fr0 is stored at 0xFFFF5DD0+100 = 0xFFFF5E34

### 0xAAB7C -- Signed integrator step vs normalized error (odd, deadbanded), for the controller state written at gbr block FFFF6078+8

1D x15 uint16, data 0xC2584, range -0.29 .. 0.29. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Maps the signed quantity at FFFF6060 (breakpoints -1..+1) through an odd, inverted curve with a near-zero deadband (about ±0.0001 from -0.1 to +0.1, rising to ∓0.29 at ±1) and stores it at FFFF60E0. In one update branch that value is multiplied by three other factors (a gear-indexed constant, table AAB90 of FFFF5FFC, table AABA4 of MAP) and a lag-filtered gain. The product is then added to the previous controller value, so this table sets the size and sign of a per-iteration correction step.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: signed, apparently normalized error-like value read from RAM FFFF6060 (also used as the Y input of 2D table AAD78). Physical meaning not established.
- **Output units:** Per-update increment factor in the units of the accumulated controller output (unknown). Dimensionless before multiplication by the other factors.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the input at FFFF6060, and of the controlled quantity accumulated at FFFF60C0/FFFF6080, is not shown in this listing. ### 0xAACD0 -- Gain multiplier, larger when both inputs share a sign (likely error vs error rate, unconfirmed)

2D 9x11 uint16, data 0xC2A14, range 0.99947 .. 5.5009. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

### 0xAAD78 -- Lag-filtered integrator step-gain multiplier vs two signed inputs (X = RAM 0xFFFF6054, Y = RAM 0xFFFF6060). The loop it belongs to is unidentified.

2D 9x11 uint16, data 0xC2DD0, range 0.99947 .. 49.999. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

This 2D lookup gives a dimensionless gain of 1 (neutral) up to about 50. A lag filter moves a stored gain toward this value, or toward 1.0 when gating conditions fail. That gain then scales the step added to a running integrator kept at 0xFFFF6080. The gain is large when both inputs share a sign and their magnitudes are large, which is what an adaptive integral-gain booster looks like.

Its meaning is not shown; the shape suggests an error rate or a second error term.; X (rows, -5..2): float read from RAM 0xFFFF6054 (stack slot 4 at 0x01997E/0x019982, passed as fr4). Its meaning is not shown; the shape suggests a signed error.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no boost)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical identities of the inputs at 0xFFFF6054 and 0xFFFF6060, and of the integrator at 0xFFFF6080, are not shown. Neither is which control loop this is (idle, throttle, boost or other), so 'error vs error-rate' is only a reading of the table's shape. Also not checked: the argument order of float_lag_filter, and which RAM address the descriptor-safe write at 0xFFFF6078 actually lands on.
- **Next step:** Search for the writers of 0xFFFF6054 and 0xFFFF6060 to fix what the axes mean. Search for the readers of 0xFFFF6080 (the integrator output) to find which loop it drives. Separately, confirm float_lag_filter's argument roles at 0x0BEA40.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x0199B8-0x0199C2: descriptor 0xAAD78 is passed to table_lookup_2D with fr4=@(4,r15), the value loaded from 0xFFFF6054 at 0x01997C, and fr5=fr14, the value loaded from 0xFFFF6060 at 0x019984.
  - 0x0199C6: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF60F4.
  - 0x019A9E-0x019AB0: fr4 = @0xFFFF60F4 (the table value) is the target for float_lag_filter. fr5 = @(12,r15) is the previous value from 0xFFFF60F4-124 = 0xFFFF6078. fr6 and fr7 are constants (0xC22C8 and the literal at 0x019BB0).
  - 0x019ABC/0x019AD8: the alternative branches use the target 1.0 (fldi1), or force the result to 1.0. So 1.0 is the neutral value, which matches the table's 0.99947 floor.
  - 0x019AE6-0x019AEC: the filtered value is written through desc_read_float_safe with r4=r9=0xFFFF6078.

2D 3x3 uint16, data 0xC2F94, range 5 .. 13. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.
