  - 0x01F60C/0x01F610: fr5 = @(r6-108) = @FFFF62DC is the second lookup argument, the X input. Its writer is not in the listing.
  - 0x01F5FA-0x01F5FE: @FFFF6344 is copied to FFFF6348.
  - 0x01F600-0x01F606: @FFFF62FC (the previous output) is copied to FFFF6344. This is a history shift.
  - 0x01F60E: jsr table_lookup_2D with descriptor 0xAB058.
  - 0x01F616: the result fr0 is stored to FFFF62FC.
  - 0x01F618-0x01F624: FFFF62F4 = @FFFF62FC (new) - @FFFF6348 (value from two calls ago). This is a finite-difference rate of change.
  - Data: row X=0 is all 0; values rise monotonically with X and fall with rpm, saturating at 100. That is the shape of an opening-to-'fraction of full' map that needs a larger opening at higher rpm.
  - 0x01F622: the function returns with no clamp or multiplier applied, so the table is not made inert inside this function.

### 0xAB0B8 -- RPM-based fail-safe derate multiplier (variant B, selected when FFFF5FB8 != 0)

1D x2 uint8, data 0xF8944, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

A unitless 0..1 factor looked up against engine speed. It is 1.0 below about 5080 rpm and falls to 0.20 by about 5630 rpm. It becomes the routine's output only when either of two diagnostic-status calls returns 2. The output is stored to RAM with a bitwise-complement copy, which is the pattern of a safety-monitored limit.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed in rpm. In the live path (r4 argument == 0) fr4 = fr14 = rpm_current.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no reduction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unverified. First, that a return value of 2 from 0x297A0 or 0x299BC means 'fault present', which makes this a fail-safe derate. Second, what quantity the multiplier finally scales, since the consumer reads FFFF64DC or FFFF6528 in another function. The label 'diag_check_P0137' is not evidence of an O2-sensor link.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF64DC and its complement FFFF6528 (and FFFF6508/FFFF650C) to see what the factor multiplies. Also disassemble 0x297A0 and 0x299BC to confirm what a return value of 2 means, and find the writers of FFFF5FB8 to learn what selects AB0B8 over AB0A4.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA34/0BBA36: fr14 is loaded from rpm_current 0xFFFF6624 [TRACED]. At 0BBAB0, 'fmov fr14,fr4' is the lookup argument for this table, so the axis is rpm in the r10==0 path.
  - 0BBA94/0BBA96: r13 is the byte at FFFF5FB8. When r13 != 0 this table (AB0B8) is used; otherwise sibling AB0A4 at 0BBA98, also indexed by rpm.
  - 0BBABC-0BBAC6 and 0BBAF0: if the byte at @(4,r15) (result of jsr 0x297A0) or at @(8,r15) (result of jsr 0x299BC, 'diag_check_P0137') equals 2, the fr0 from this lookup is stored to @r15 as the routine result.
  - 0BBAC8-0BBAEE: otherwise the rpm threshold fr6 (from 0xF8910) is compared to fr14, and fr0 is overwritten by a vehicle-speed table (r12) or another table (r14). This table's value is not used on those paths.
  - 0BBAF4: the default output is fldi1, i.e. 1.0, which fits a multiplier where 1 = no effect.
  - 0BBB04-0BBB10: when r10==0 the result is stored to FFFF650C-48 = FFFF64DC, and 'not r2,r6' stores its complement to FFFF650C+28 = FFFF6528. A redundant complement copy suggests a safety-checked value.
  - 0BBBC4-0BBBD4: in the r10==1 path the result goes to FFFF6508, otherwise to FFFF650C.

### 0xAB108 -- Vehicle-speed limiter reduction factor vs vehicle speed (variant: FFFF5FB8 != 0, cl_ol_gate_flag == 3)

1D x2 uint8, data 0xF8974, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

This is a 0..1 multiplier looked up against vehicle speed. It holds at 1.0 up to 228 km/h and falls to about 0.20 by 230 km/h, which looks like a top-speed limiter clamping some demand. The result goes to RAM at FFFF64DC, and its bit-inverted copy goes to FFFF6528. What consumes it is not in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: vehicle speed in km/h. It is fed from vehicle_speed_kmh FFFF65FC via fr15 into fr4 at 0BBACC. Breakpoints are 228 and 230.
- **Output units:** dimensionless factor (1.0 = no reduction, 0.203 = strong reduction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The output is shown only being stored to FFFF64DC. Nothing in the listing shows what it multiplies (throttle, torque or something else), so the 'speed limiter' meaning rests on the speed axis and the data shape. FFFF5FB8 and the gate-flag==3 condition are also unexplained.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF64DC (and the complement check on FFFF6528). See whether it multiplies a target throttle or torque request. Also compare the sibling tables AB0CC, AB0E0 and AB0F4, and identify what FFFF5FB8 represents.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA3A: fmov.s @r2,fr15 loads fr15 from FFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh [TRACED]).
  - 0BBA3C-0BBA54: the descriptor is picked by FFFF5FB8 (r13) and cl_ol_gate_flag (r14). AB108 is taken when r13 != 0 and the gate flag == 3. AB0CC, AB0E0 and AB0F4 are sibling variants.
  - 0BBACC-0BBAD2: the lookup runs with fmov fr15,fr4 and mov r12,r4 through table_lookup_1D. The table's input is therefore vehicle speed, not rpm.
  - 0BBAC8: fcmp/gt fr14,fr6. The speed lookup runs only when rpm (fr14) >= the constant at F890C/F8910. Otherwise the AB128 path or the default applies.
  - 0BBAF4: fldi1 fr8 makes the default factor 1.0 when r11==1 (engine_mode_bits bit 7 set) or the other conditions fail. This matches a multiplier whose neutral value is 1.
  - 0BBB02-0BBB10: the result is stored to FFFF650C-48 = FFFF64DC, and its bitwise NOT is stored to FFFF650C+28 = FFFF6528. This looks like a redundant complement copy, as used for safety-relevant values.
  - Data: 1.0 at 228 km/h and 0.203 at 230 km/h, a sharp cut just above a plausible governed top speed.

### 0xAB21C -- Unknown concave curve: numerator of the clamped ratio stored at FFFF6A08 (possibly an orifice-flow-vs-pressure-difference style curve)

1D x14 uint16, data 0xC4428, range 0 .. 0.81772. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looks up a concave, saturating curve (0 to 0.818) against the float at FFFF6A18 and stores it to FFFF6A0C. That value is divided by FFFF69F8, clamped to [0, FFFF6A10] and stored to FFFF6A08. If the table output is in the deadband the result is 0; if the divisor is in the deadband the result is the FFFF6A10 ceiling.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: value read from RAM FFFF6A18 (r14-56, r14=FFFF6A50); its writer is not in this listing, so quantity and units are unknown. Breakpoints are 0..507.8 in 39.0625 steps.
- **Output units:** unknown; dimensionless-looking 0..0.818, used as a numerator over FFFF69F8
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The input quantity at FFFF6A18 and the divisor at FFFF69F8 are written elsewhere and not shown. Any physical identity, such as purge or bypass flow versus pressure difference, rests only on the curve shape and the ratio-to-capped-fraction structure, which amounts to a hunch.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of FFFF6A18 and FFFF69F8 to fix the input and divisor quantities, and for readers of FFFF6A08 and FFFF6A0C to find the actuator or consumer. Also decode the float constant at 0x027240 (the deadband threshold).
- **Cited:**
  - 0270C2-0270CA: descriptor 0xAB21C goes to r4, the lookup routine is called via @r15 (table_lookup_1D), and fr4 is loaded from @(-56,r14) = FFFF6A18.
  - 0270CC-0270CE: the result fr0 is stored to @(-68,r14) = FFFF6A0C.
  - 0270D0-0270E0: float_deadband_check runs on FFFF6A0C against the constant at 0x027240. If it reports in-band, the branch to 02711E stores 0.0 to FFFF6A08.
  - 0270E2-0270F4: the same deadband check runs on @(-88,r14) = FFFF69F8. If that is in-band, 027114 copies FFFF6A10 into FFFF6A08.
  - 0270F6-027112: float_safe_div computes FFFF6A0C / FFFF69F8, then float_clamp_range clamps it to [0 (fldi0 fr5), FFFF6A10 (fr6)] and stores it to FFFF6A08.
  - 02703E-0270C0: the ceiling FFFF6A10 is a table lookup (0xAB230, 0xAB244 or 0xAB258, chosen by flag_6254 and engine_condition_flags) on atm_pressure_current. When bits in FFFF6A53/6A52 allow, it is multiplied by lookups of rpm (0xAB168) and of FFFF62F8 (0xAB17C).
  - Data shape: zero at x=0, then a steep, saturating rise. It roughly resembles sqrt or orifice-flow behaviour but does not fit sqrt exactly.

### 0xAB674 -- Step-shaped adder (0→50 over input 2→5) on the gap between 0xFFFF7F0C and the mean of a 4-element per-cylinder array, summed into the filtered value at 0xFFFF6BC4

1D x8 uint16, data 0xC626C, range 0 .. 50.002. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

The input is 0xFFFF7F0C minus the average of the four floats at 0xFFFF7E90..7E9C. It is used only while the counter at 0xFFFF67EC lies strictly between two calibration constants, otherwise the input is 0. The result (0, then 50 once the gap reaches 5) is added to a filtered copy of 0xFFFF61FC, an rpm-excess term and a fuel_mode_flags offset, and that sum is first-order filtered into 0xFFFF6BC4.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: 0xFFFF7F0C minus the mean of the four floats at 0xFFFF7E90/94/98/9C, also stored to 0xFFFF6BBC. The input is forced to 0 unless the u16 at 0xFFFF67EC is strictly greater than the u16 at 0xC4A62 and strictly less than the u16 at 0xC4A64. Units are those of 0xFFFF7F0C (degrees if it really is timing, which is unverified).
- **Output units:** Same units as the other addends of the 0xFFFF6BC4 sum (the filtered 0xFFFF61FC value, (rpm minus 0xFFFF895C) scaled by the constant at 0xC5644, and the constant at 0xC5638); the physical unit is not established.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Nothing in this listing fixes the physical meaning of 0xFFFF6BC4 or of its addends. The idea that this is an ignition-retard-driven compensation (for example idle air or rpm) rests only on the [TRACED] names timing_blend_app and warmup_corr_cyl0, and names have repeatedly been wrong in this project.
- **Next step:** Read the constants at 0xC5640 (filter gain; if 0 the table is inert) and 0xC4A62/0xC4A64 (counter window), and check that 0xC563C, 0xC5644 and 0xC5638 are plausible. Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF6BC4 (and 0xFFFF6BBC/6BC0) to find what the filtered sum drives, and for the writers of 0xFFFF7E90..7E9C and 0xFFFF7F0C to fix the input's units.
- **Cited:**
  - 02B696-02B6A4: loads four consecutive floats at 0xFFFF7E90+0/4/8/12. 02B6D6-02B6E2 adds them and divides by the literal 0x40800000 (4.0), giving their mean.
  - 02B6E4/02B6EA: fr6 = @0xFFFF7F0C minus the mean, stored to @(-8, 0xFFFF6BC4) = 0xFFFF6BBC.
  - 02B6EC-02B700: the u16 counter at 0xFFFF67EC is compared with the u16 constants at 0xC4A62 and 0xC4A64. Only when C4A62 < counter < C4A64 does 02B708/02B70E copy 0xFFFF6BBC to stack+12; otherwise 02B714 writes 0.0 there.
  - 02B71E/02B71C: fr4 = @(12,r15) is passed to table_lookup_1D with descriptor 0xAB674. 02B720 keeps the result in fr9.
  - 02B6AA-02B6CA: fr14 = max(rpm_current minus @0xFFFF895C, 0) times the constant at 0xC5644.
  - 02B6CC-02B6D4: fr15 = 0, or the constant at 0xC5638 when fuel_mode_flags (0xFFFF8E46) is nonzero.
  - 02B730-02B73E: 0xFFFF6BC0 += (@0xFFFF61FC minus 0xFFFF6BC0) times min(1, @0xC563C), a first-order filter.
  - 02B74C-02B75E: 0xFFFF6BC4 += ((0xFFFF6BC0 + table output + fr14 + fr15) minus 0xFFFF6BC4) times min(1, @0xC5640). So the table output is an additive term in the filtered target at 0xFFFF6BC4.

### 0xAB880 -- RPM-based threshold for a per-cylinder exceedance counter (primary variant, chosen by flag from 0x22CF4); identity of the monitored quantity unknown

1D x10 float32, data 0xC69D8, range 9 .. 8000. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Looked up against rpm_current, it is multiplied by a second RPM table (0xAD464), and the product is the threshold that the float at FFFF99B4 must exceed to increment a per-cylinder event counter. When that counter reaches the limit at 0xCBBFB, a per-cylinder float (FFFF7A78+4*cyl) is ramped up by a step, capped and clamped. The 8000 entries from 4000 rpm upward effectively disable the detection there.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input fr14 = rpm_current FFFF6624, passed in fr4 at 0x037772)
- **Output units:** Unknown. These are the same units as FFFF99B4 divided by the 0xAD464 multiplier. 8000 acts as a 'never' sentinel.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of FFFF99B4 is not shown in this function, so the threshold's quantity (e.g. roughness/misfire-like crank variation versus something else) cannot be fixed. The per-cylinder interpretation of r4 also rests on four-wide loops rather than a traced caller.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF99B4 to identify the compared quantity. Also search for readers of the per-cylinder floats FFFF7A78..FFFF7A84 and the bytes FFFF7A98..7A9F to see what the ramped value corrects.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x03772E/0x037730 load rpm_current into fr14, and 0x037772 passes fr14 as the lookup input to table_lookup_1D (0xBE830) with descriptor 0xAB880.
  - 0x037764-0x037768: this table is used only when the byte at @(8,r15)==1. That byte is the return of 0x22CF4, stored in the jsr delay slot at 0x037740. Otherwise the alternate table 0xAB51C is used.
  - 0x037784/0x037786: the result is stored to 0xFFFF7AA4 (r12=0xFFFF7AA8, offset -4). 0x037788-0x037790: the second RPM table 0xAD464 is looked up and its result stored to 0xFFFF7AA8.
  - 0x03783A-0x037840: fr8 = [7AA4]*[7AA8], then fcmp/gt fr8,fr15, where fr15 = [FFFF99B4] loaded at 0x03772C.
  - 0x037846-0x03785E: if FFFF99B4 > product, the per-cylinder byte counter A[r13] (r13 = argument r4) is incremented via uint8_add_sat and counter B[r13] is cleared. 0x037860-0x037886: otherwise B is incremented, and A is cleared if B exceeds the byte at 0xCBBFA.
  - 0x037974-0x0379B2: when A[i] >= the byte at 0xCBBFB, the float at FFFF7A78+4i is raised by [0xCC2E4], min-limited via the pointer table at 0x63B20, and clamped to [0, FFFF7A88+4i]. 0x0379E2-0x037A0A: if no cylinder triggered, all four floats decay by [0xCC2E8].
  - 0x037792-0x037796: the comparison path runs only when gbr byte @40 (0xFFFF7AA0) is nonzero. Otherwise the counters are zeroed.

### 0xABE7C -- Low-speed RPM x barometric-pressure target, blend endpoint A (output FFFF9A54), meaning unknown

2D 14x7 uint16, data 0xC8444, range 151 .. 359. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

At low vehicle speed the result at FFFF9A54 is a weighted blend: w*ThisTable(baro,rpm) + (1-w)*Table_0xABE98(baro,rpm). The weight w is (x - T1(rpm)) / (T2(rpm) - T1(rpm)) clamped to 0..1, where x is one element of the per-cylinder float array at FFFF7E90. At or above a speed threshold, FFFF9A54 instead comes from the RPM-only 1D table 0xAB790. What the stored quantity physically is cannot be determined from this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (500..752): atmospheric pressure, probably mmHg (about 66.7 to 100 kPa); fed from fr13 = FFFF6898 atm_pressure_current; X (700..6700): engine speed in rpm; fed from fr12 = FFFFA198 rpm_snapshot
- **Output units:** unknown; raw counts, scale 1, range 151..359, rising with both rpm and pressure
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of FFFF9A54 and of the per-cylinder array at FFFF7E90 (its 'warmup_corr' label is untrusted) is unknown. The X/Y assignment also relies on matching breakpoint magnitudes rather than a verified fr4/fr5 axis convention for table_lookup_2D.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of FFFF9A54 (and writers of the FFFF7E90..FFFF7E9C per-cylinder array) to fix the quantity. Also dump 0xABE98, 0xAB790, 0xAB3F4 and 0xAB3E0 to compare their ranges with this table.
- **Cited:**
  - 0776C2/0776C6: fmov fr12,fr5 (rpm from FFFFA198 loaded at 077624) and fmov fr13,fr4 (atm pressure from FFFF6898 loaded at 077628) are the arguments to table_lookup_2D 0xBE8E4 with descriptor 0xABE7C. The breakpoints fit these inputs: 700..6700 matches rpm, 500..752 matches baro in mmHg.
  - 0776C8: result saved to fr14. 0776CA-0776D4: a second 2D table 0xABE98 with the same inputs goes to fr9.
  - 0776D8-0776E6: fr8 = w*fr14, then fmac adds (1-w)*fr9, where w = @FFFF9A58. The sum is stored to FFFF9A54 (r6-4). This is a linear blend of two tables, with this table as the w=1 endpoint.
  - 077692-0776BC: w = float_clamp_range(float_safe_div(x - T1, T2 - T1), 0, 1). T1 = 1D table 0xAB3F4(rpm) and T2 = 1D table 0xAB3E0(rpm). x = FFFF7E90[cyl] chosen by the byte at FFFF9FC6 (offsets 0/8/4/12), so it is a per-cylinder value.
  - 07765E-07767A: a speed hysteresis flag (bit0 at GBR+129 = FFFFA219) is set when vehicle_speed >= @0xC4FB0 and cleared when speed < @0xC4FAC.
  - 07767C-077690: when that flag is set, the function bypasses both 2D tables and stores 1D table 0xAB790(rpm) directly to FFFF9A54.
  - The function only produces FFFF9A54; the consumer of FFFF9A54 is not in this listing.

### 0xABE98 -- Baro×RPM blend endpoint table B (feeds FFFF9A54; quantity unknown)

2D 14x7 uint16, data 0xC855C, range 156 .. 383. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Looked up by RPM (X, 700..6700) and atmospheric pressure (Y, 500..752, probably mmHg). The result is the (1-w) end of a linear blend with the sister table 0xABE7C: FFFF9A54 = w*T_ABE7C + (1-w)*T_this. The weight w is a clamped 0..1 fraction of a per-cylinder value (the FFFF7E90 array) between two RPM-indexed 1D thresholds. When a speed-hysteresis flag is set, both tables are bypassed and a 1D RPM table is used instead.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed (rpm), from fr12 = FFFFA198; Y: atmospheric pressure, from fr13 = FFFF6898 (500..752 suggests mmHg)
- **Output units:** unknown; same units as table 0xABE7C and the 1D table 0xAB790, scale 1
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity is not fixed. Nothing in the listing consumes FFFF9A54. The meaning of the FFFF7E90 per-cylinder array (labelled warmup_corr, which is unreliable) and of the AB3F4/AB3E0 thresholds is unverified. The X/Y assignment rests on the fr4/fr5 convention of table_lookup_2D matching the breakpoint magnitudes.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of RAM FFFF9A54 to find what the blended value feeds (for example a comparison or a load/airflow limit). Also decode tables 0xABE7C and 0xAB790 to see whether they share magnitude and units with this one.
- **Cited:**
  - 0776C2/0776CC: fmov fr12,fr5 (rpm); 0776C4/0776D2: fmov fr13,fr4 (atm_pressure_current) before both table_lookup_2D calls (0xABE7C, then 0xABE98). Breakpoints 700..6700 fit rpm and 500..752 fit barometric mmHg.
  - 0776D4: result goes to fr9. 0776D8-0776E2: fr8 = w*fr14 (ABE7C result) + (1-w)*fr9, with w read from @FFFF9A58.
  - 0776E4/0776E6: blended value stored to @(-4,FFFF9A58) = FFFF9A54.
  - 0776A4-0776BC: w = clamp((x - T_AB3F4(rpm)) / (T_AB3E0(rpm) - T_AB3F4(rpm)), 0, 1), where x is FFFF7E90[+0/8/4/12], chosen by the byte at FFFF9FC6 (00762C-07765C). w is stored to FFFF9A58.
  - 07765E-077680: speed (FFFF65FC) is compared against the constants at C4FB0/C4FAC with hysteresis on bit 0 of FFFFA219. When the bit is set, FFFF9A54 instead comes from the 1D rpm table AB790 (07768A-077690) and this table is skipped.

### 0xAC388 -- Coolant-temperature-dependent delay count (ticks) before stepwise decay of float FFFF72F4 during transient state (primary variant; AC394 is the alternate selected when FFFF65C1==1)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xCC8C1, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: base fuelling.

While transient_state_flag (FFFF726C) equals 1, each call increments a saturating byte counter at FFFF72F8, looks up an ECT-indexed byte threshold from this table and stores it to FFFF72F9. Once the counter reaches the threshold, and only if FFFF7306 is zero, the float at FFFF72F4 is reduced by the constant at 0xCBDE8 and floored at the constant at 0xCBDE4. The table therefore sets how many calls the value at FFFF72F4 is held before it starts to decay: about 116 calls at -40 C, 76 at -30 and -20 C, 53 at -10 C, and 38 from 0 C upward.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, degC, from ect_current FFFF6350 (axis shared with a definition-named table)
- **Output units:** Raw uint8 count of executions of this routine since the counter was last reset. The stored scale and bias (about 1e-39) are misdecoded and the physical values should be ignored. Back-computing from them gives raw values 116, 76, 76, 53, then 38 flat.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The role of the table (delay threshold for a counter) is observed in the code. What physical quantity FFFF72F4 holds is not shown, and neither is the reset of FFFF72F8. Calling it a transient or tip-in fuel enrichment decay delay rests only on the transient_state_flag label and the base-fuelling hint. The raw counts are back-computed from a scale and bias that are themselves misdecoded.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of float FFFF72F4 to identify what is decaying and where it is consumed. Search for writers of FFFF72F8 to find the counter reset or transient trigger, and of FFFF7306 for the inhibit. Also read the floats at 0xCBDE8 (step size) and 0xCBDE4 (floor), and read the raw bytes at 0xCC8C1 directly to confirm the counts 116/76/76/53/38.
- **Cited:**
  - 02FBA0-02FBA2: fr14 is loaded from FFFF6350 (ect_current) and passed as fr4 to LowPW_TableProcessor at 02FBDA, so the table is indexed by ECT.
  - 02FBA6/02FBAE/02FBB0: the whole body runs only when the byte at FFFF726C (transient_state_flag) equals 1.
  - 02FBBC-02FBCA: uint8_add_sat(byte at FFFF72F8, 1) is stored back to FFFF72F8, so this is an incrementing counter.
  - 02FBCC-02FBD4: if the byte from FFFF65C1 equals 1, descriptor AC394 is used instead of AC388, so this table is the non-flagged variant.
  - 02FBDE: the byte result of the lookup is stored to FFFF72F9 (offset 1 from FFFF72F8).
  - 02FBE0-02FBE6: cmp/hs compares the counter at FFFF72F8 against the looked-up threshold. The branch skips the decay while counter < threshold.
  - 02FBE8-02FBEA: the decay also requires the byte from FFFF7306 (r13) to be zero.
  - 02FBEC-02FC02: the float at FFFF72F4 (offset -4) has the constant at 0xCBDE8 subtracted, is passed through float_max against the constant at 0xCBDE4, and is written back. This is a stepwise decay with a floor.

### 0xAC394 -- Coolant-temp delay (tick count) before decay of float FFFF72F4 – alternate table used when FFFF65C1==1

1D x16 uint8, data 0xCC8D1, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: base fuelling.

While FFFF726C==1, a saturating uint8 counter at FFFF72F8 is bumped once per call. This table gives a coolant-dependent threshold stored at FFFF72F9. Once the counter reaches it, and FFFF7306 is 0, the float at FFFF72F4 is stepped down each call by the constant at 0xCBDE8, floored at the constant at 0xCBDE4.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, °C (ECT from FFFF6350; axis shared with a defined table)
- **Output units:** Raw uint8 count of calls to this routine (ticks); call period unknown. The listed scale and bias are denormal floats and are almost certainly misdecoded; treating them as linear gives raw ≈116, 76, 76, 53, 38 … 38, i.e. a longer delay when cold.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of the float at FFFF72F4 is unknown. It is set, and probably consumed, outside this function, so it could be an after-start or transient enrichment, or something else. The tick period of this routine and the conditions that set FFFF65C1 are also unknown.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers and writers of FFFF72F4, the decayed float, to learn what it modifies; it is presumably initialised on some event. Also search for writers of FFFF65C1, which selects this table over 0xAC388, and of FFFF726C. Find the task-table rate of 0x02FB9A to convert ticks to seconds.
- **Cited:**
  - 02FBA0/02FBA2: fr14 = float @FFFF6350 (ECT), passed as the lookup input via fmov fr14,fr4 at 02FBDA
  - 02FBA4-02FBB0: the whole body runs only when byte @FFFF726C == 1; otherwise it returns at 02FC14

### 0xAC430 -- After-start enrichment decay step interval (execution cycles between decay steps) vs coolant temperature

1D x16 uint8, data 0xCC9AA, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Gives a coolant-dependent count of execution cycles between decay steps. When a saturating cycle counter at gbr+97 reaches this table's byte, two coolant-initialised fuelling coefficients at 0xFFFF73E0 and 0xFFFF73E4 are each reduced by one decay step and the counter resets, so the table sets how fast those coefficients decay; colder starts (below 0 C) use a different interval than warmer ones.

- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (ect_gated_c at 0xFFFF6354), -40..110 in 10 C steps

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The role as a decay-step interval is shown directly by the code. Calling the decayed quantity 'after-start enrichment' rests on two unverified points: reading 0xFFFF67EC (labelled a DTC counter) as a time-since-start counter, and assuming the multiplier stored at 0xFFFF7400 feeds injector pulse width.
- **Next step:** Dump the raw 16 bytes at 0xCC9AA to get the real cycle counts and confirm they are nonzero (a zero would make every pass a decay step). Then search for readers of 0xFFFF7400, and for writers of 0xFFFF67EC, to confirm it is an after-start enrichment multiplier and a post-start timer.

  - 031256-03125E: cmp/hs compares that counter with the table value; if the counter is lower the function exits (bf 0x0312AC) without updating.
  - 031260-031282: once the threshold is reached, coef@0xFFFF73E0 (r12-100) is set to max(coef - tbl(0xACD54)*@0xFFFF73FC, 1.0) via fmac and float_max with fr5 = 1. This is a decay step toward 1.0.
  - 031284-0312A6: likewise coef@0xFFFF73E4 (r12-96) is set to max(coef - tbl(0xACD68)*@0xFFFF73FC, 0.0). This is a decay step toward 0.
  - 0312A8-0312AA: counter gbr+97 is reset to 0 after a step, and also on the other exit paths that branch to 0312A8.
  - 03118A-0311BE: while r14 (word at 0xFFFF67EC) is below the constant at 0xCBC28, both coefficients are initialised from coolant tables 0xACD7C/0xACD90, or 0xACDA4/0xACDB8 if afl_diagnostic_flag = 1.

Then check that AD168, AD1B8 and the AD118/AD12C clamp limits are nonzero, to confirm the table is not inert.

  - 038E82-038E86: rpm_current is stored to @r15; 038F9A: fmov.s @r15,fr4 is the lookup input, so the axis is RPM
  - 038EDA-038EE0: the whole block is skipped (bra 0x039284) unless rpm < cal_ltft_rpm_threshold 0x0CC3C4. Further gates are engine_status_flag, 0xFFFF7D2E and 0xFFFF90BD
  - 038F56-038F8C: the lookup runs only when bit 6/7 of gbr+35 or gbr+36 is set. 038F8E-038F92 zeroes the paired slot @(8,r15); 038FAC-038FAE resets counter byte @(12,r15), which is written back to gbr+32 at 039050-039052. This is an event-triggered (re)start
  - 038F9C-038F9E: the result goes to @(4,r15), overwriting the previous state loaded at 038EFA-038F00 from 0xFFFF7B7C (r10-36)
  - 03915C-03917E: fr4 = tbl(rpm) * AD168(load) * AD1B8(ECT) + [AC724(rpm)*AD17C(load)*AD1CC(ECT)] * 0xFFFF7B98. 0xFFFF7B98 is a product of (x-1)*k+1 terms from 0xFFFF73E8 and base_fuel_table_output (038EB2-038ED4)
  - 039180-03918E: the term is clamped by float_clamp_range between AD118(ECT) and AD12C(ECT) and stored to 0xFFFF7B90. 0391C4-0391D0 adds it to 0xFFFF7B94 and stores the sum at 0xFFFF7B8C
  - 039200-039224: @(4,r15) goes with counter gbr+32, step table AD208(rpm) and cal byte 0xCBBFF into bsr 0x03949E. The result is written back to 0xFFFF7B7C, so the state is ramped/decayed after the event

### 0xAC724 -- RPM-indexed initial magnitude of the second (cross-coupled) term of an event-triggered, decaying fuel-correction fraction (likely transient/AFL enrichment), pair partner of table 0xAC710

1D x7 uint8, data 0xCD008, range 0.01172 .. 0.02344. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

When gbr flag bits 6/7 (FFFF7B9F or FFFF7BA0) fire, this table is looked up against rpm_current, latched into fr15 and its step counter (gbr+32) is reset. Its value is then scaled by load- and ECT-indexed tables and by a multiplier built from FFFF73E8 and base_fuel_table_output. It is added to the AC710 term, clamped and stored as a small correction fraction. It is also passed through a counter-driven decay routine (0x03949E) and stored as held state at FFFF7B80.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm. The lookup input is fmov.s @r15, which holds rpm_current FFFF6624, stored at 0x038E86.
- **Output units:** dimensionless fraction, about 0.012..0.023 (1.2-2.3%), probably a fuel-correction fraction; not proven
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the summed output (FFFF7B8C/FFFF7B90) is not shown here; its consumer is in another function. What triggers bits 6/7 of gbr+35/36 is also unknown, so 'fuel enrichment fraction' is inferred from magnitude, the multiplier structure and the subsystem hint rather than proven. The behaviour of 0x03949E (decay) is assumed, not shown.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x03949E to confirm it decays its fr4 input by counter/rate (fr5, r5). Then search for readers of FFFF7B8C and FFFF7B90 (and FFFF7B80) to see whether they multiply injector pulse width or fuel mass. Also find the writers of bits 6/7 at FFFF7B9F/FFFF7BA0 (gbr+35/36) to identify the triggering event.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x038E82-0x038E86: rpm_current is loaded and stored to @r15. 0x038FA2/0x038FA8 pass @r15 as fr4 to table_lookup_1D with r4=0xAC724, so the X axis is rpm.
  - 0x038FAA: the result goes to fr15. 0x038FAE resets the byte at sp+12, which is copied to gbr+32 at 0x039052. That is a saturating counter incremented by uint8_add_sat at 0x038F20.
  - 0x038F0A-0x038F0C: on the non-triggered path fr15 = @(-32,r10), i.e. FFFF7B80, the held previous value. The lookup only happens when gbr+35/36 bits 6/7 are set (0x038F60-0x038F8C).
  - 0x03916C-0x03917E: fr0 = fr15 * @80 * @96. Slot @80 is the load-indexed table AD17C (input fr13 = engine_load). Slot @96 is the ECT-indexed table AD1CC. fmac multiplies by @(-8,r10) = FFFF7B98 and adds to fr4. FFFF7B98 is the product ((FFFF73E8-1)*k+1)*((base_fuel-1)*k+1), computed at 0x038EB2-0x038ED4.
  - fr4 is the AC710 term times load and ECT tables (0x03915C-0x03916A). At 0x039188 float_clamp_range clamps the sum between ECT tables @104/@108. The result is stored to FFFF7B90 (0x03918E) and summed with the other pair into FFFF7B8C (0x0391CC-0x0391D0).
  - 0x039246-0x039262: fr15 is passed to bsr 0x03949E together with counter gbr+32, table AD230 (rpm-indexed) and cal byte 0xCBC01. The result is stored to FFFF7B80. This looks like a decay/ramp of the latched value, consistent with a transient enrichment.

### 0xAC788 -- Gating factor (0..1) on the ramp target, indexed by the float at 0xFFFF6574 (input quantity unidentified)

1D x7 uint8, data 0xCD0AC, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: knock.

Gives a 0..1 multiplier from the float at 0xFFFF6574. It is 0 at an input of 0, reaches 1 by 60 and stays 1. It is one of four factors multiplied into a target, and a ramped state value at 0xFFFF7BE4 steps up toward that target. In effect it holds the target at zero until the input gets past about 60.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (0..1)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity at 0xFFFF6574 is not established anywhere in this listing. Its 0..300 range could be a timer, a speed or some other quantity, so the table's meaning beyond 'enable gate above an input of about 60' is unknown. What the ramped output 0xFFFF7BE4 feeds is also not shown.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of 0xFFFF6574 to identify the X quantity, and the readers of 0xFFFF7BE4 to identify what the ramped value controls. Also read ROM byte 0xCBC06: if it is 0, this lookup path is never taken and the table is inert.
- **Cited:**
  - 039F62-039F64: fr13 is loaded from @0xFFFF6574. 039FDE moves fr13 into fr4 as the X input to table_lookup_1D (jsr at 039FDC) with descriptor 0xAC788.
  - 039FE0-039FE2: the result fr0 is stored to @(12,r11), with r11=0xFFFF7BEC, so it goes to 0xFFFF7BF8.
  - 039FF0-03A004: the values at r11+4 (2D table on fr14 = @0xFFFF8E38 and on the interpolated @0xFFFF67EC), r11+8 (1D table on ECT), r11+12 (this table) and r11+16 (1D table 0xAC79C on @0xFFFF68D0) are multiplied together with fmul.
  - 03A006-03A008: the product is stored to r11-4, which is 0xFFFF7BE8, the target.
  - 03A00A-03A01A: the state at r11-8 (0xFFFF7BE4) gets the constant @0xCC45C added, and float_min takes the lesser of that and the target. The state therefore rises in steps and is capped at the product; this table scales the cap.
  - 03A17C-03A17E: the final result is written back to 0xFFFF7BE4.
  - Path gating: 039FA4-039FAC require ROM byte 0xCBC06 to be nonzero. 039FB2-039FB6 require byte @0xFFFF7C18 != 1. 039FCC-039FD0 require r14 (byte @0xFFFF8E7E) == 1. 039FD2-039FD6 require byte @0xFFFF7BEC == 0.

### 0xAC818 -- Coolant-dependent per-cycle decay multiplier for state 0xFFFF728C (variant: gate 0xFFFF90C1 set, 0xFFFF3158 clear, flag 0xFFFF72D1 = 1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD1A6, range 0.89999 .. 0.96001. Triage hint: transient fuel.

Gives a factor of about 0.90 to 0.96, looked up against gated coolant temperature. Each pass, the float state at 0xFFFF728C is multiplied by this factor and clamped at ≥0, so the state decays geometrically from a seeded start value. Four outputs each follow the decaying value until it drops below their own floor (0xFFFF7328/2C/30/34); the matching latch flag then clears and the output holds the floor.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, °C (from definition; input fr4 = ect_gated_c 0xFFFF6354 loaded at 02F03E and not overwritten before the call)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplicative retention factor per execution of this routine (1 − decay fraction per cycle)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the decayed quantity (0xFFFF728C / 0xFFFF72DC) and of the four outputs is not shown; whether it is an after-start enrichment, a transient-fuel term or something else is unverified. The time base (call rate of 0x02EFD4) is also unknown, so the factor cannot be converted to a time constant.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7290, 0xFFFF7294, 0xFFFF7298 and 0xFFFF729C to learn what the four decayed outputs feed (fuel or ignition, per-cylinder or not). Find the writer of 0xFFFF72DC (seed value). Find the caller/task rate of 0x02EFD4 to turn 0.90..0.96 per call into a time constant.
- **Cited:**
  - 02F03C-02F03E: fr4 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c) as the lookup input, consistent with the coolant-temperature X axis.
  - 02F118-02F126: this descriptor is chosen when 0xFFFF90C1 is nonzero, 0xFFFF3158 (r6) is zero and the byte at gbr+101 (0xFFFF72D1) equals 1. Seven sibling tables (0xAC82C/868/87C/840/854/890/8A4) cover the other flag combinations.
  - 02F162-02F164: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF72D0-92 = 0xFFFF7274.
  - 02F166-02F16E: the state at 0xFFFF728C (r14-68) is loaded and multiplied by the table value.
  - 02F170-02F178: the product passes through float_max with fr5=0 (clamp ≥0) and is written back to 0xFFFF728C. This is a geometric decay, and values <1 make it a decay.
  - 02F17A-02F1E4: four outputs at 0xFFFF7290/7294/7298/729C are set to max(decayed state, floor at 0xFFFF7328/732C/7330/7334). Whenever a floor exceeds the state, gbr flags +3..+6 are cleared.
  - 02F1E6-02F21E: when gbr+0 (0xFFFF726C) is set, the state and all four outputs are seeded from 0xFFFF72DC (coolant_decay_charge_value) and flags +3..+6 are set to 1.
  - 02EFF4-02F03A: once all four flags are clear, the routine just copies the floors and skips the decay.

### 0xAC840 -- Coolant-temp decay multiplier for a 4-channel transient term (variant: 0xFFFF90C1 gate=0, afl_diagnostic_flag=0, flag 0xFFFF72D1=1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD1E6, range 0.85001 .. 0.94. Triage hint: transient fuel.

- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity being decayed is unknown, and so is the trigger: the charge value at 0xFFFF72DC, the gbr+0 event, and whatever reads 0xFFFF7290-0xFFFF729C are all outside this listing. The 'transient fuel' context and the 4 channels being cylinders or banks rest on labels and hints only.

Also find the writers of 0xFFFF72DC (initial charge), 0xFFFF726C (trigger flag) and 0xFFFF7306 (sets the 0xFFFF72D1 selector) to identify the event and the quantity being decayed.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x02F03C-0x02F03E: fr4 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c), the lookup input. This matches the coolant-temperature axis.
  - 0x02F044-0x02F058: the table is chosen from 8 descriptors by three conditions: byte 0xFFFF90C1 (tst r5 at 0x02F048), byte 0xFFFF3158 (tst r6 at 0x02F04C) and byte gbr+101 = 0xFFFF72D1 (cmp/eq #1 at 0x02F056). This one, 0xAC840, is used when 90C1=0, 3158=0 and 72D1=1.
  - 0x02F162-0x02F164: the result fr0 is stored to r14-92 = 0xFFFF7274.

  - 0x02F170-0x02F178: float_max(fr4, fr5=0, set by fldi0 in the delay slot). The product is floored at 0 and written back to 0xFFFF728C.
  - 0x02F17A-0x02F1E4: for each of 4 channels the decayed value is compared with its reference at 0xFFFF7328+4i. If the reference is greater (fcmp/gt), the reference is stored to the channel at 0xFFFF7290+4i and the flag gbr+3+i is cleared. Otherwise the decayed value is stored.
  - 0x02F1E6-0x02F21E: when the gbr+0 flag (0xFFFF726C) is set, 0xFFFF728C is charged from 0xFFFF72DC. All four channels are set to that value and all four flags are set to 1, so the table drives decay after a trigger event.
  - 0x02EFF4-0x02F03A: if no channel flag is 1, the channels copy their references directly and the table is not consulted.

### 0xAC87C -- Coolant-temp decay multiplier for a four-channel decaying value (variant: gate 0xFFFF90C1 set, flag 0xFFFF3158 set, flag 0xFFFF72D1 not 1)

### 0xACB54 -- Coolant-temperature gain for one of three summed transient fuel terms (variant used when mode byte = 2 and flag FFFF844F != 1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD706, range 0.2998 .. 3.3999. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Indexed by coolant temperature, it gives a unitless gain that falls from 3.4 when cold to 0.3 when hot. The gain is stored at 0xFFFF73B8 and multiplied with an RPM-indexed factor and a state variable to form one of three terms. The three terms are summed, scaled and floored at zero into 0xFFFF73B4. That structure looks like transient (wall-wetting or tip-in) fuel compensation, but the quantity has not been confirmed.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, degC (ect_current 0xFFFF6350 loaded into fr15 and passed as fr4 at 030D44; the axis is also shared per the definition)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (gain)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The 'transient fuel' identity rests on the structure (three gain-weighted terms, a history shift, an ECT-cold-high profile), not on a consumer. The multiplicand at 0xFFFF73E0 ([r8-36]) and the final output 0xFFFF73B4 are not traced to an injector-pulse calculation in this listing. Which operating mode selects this variant (return of 0x21016 = 2, FFFF844F != 1) is also unknown.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF73B4 (final output) to see whether it is added to the fuel pulse or fuel mass. Also find the writer of 0xFFFF73E0 ([r8-36], probably in 0x0312DE/0x030F02/0x03110C) to identify the state variable this gain scales. Disassemble 0x21016 and the writer of 0xFFFF844F to learn when the ACB54 variant is selected.
- **Cited:**
  - 030C5C: fr15 = @0xFFFF6350 (ect_current). 030D42/030D44: jsr table_lookup_1D with fr4 = fr15, so the input is coolant temperature.
  - 030D2E-030D40: the descriptor is chosen by condition. If byte @(8,r15) (the return of 0x21016) != 2, ACB68 is used. If it is 2, ACB54 (this table) is used when byte r10 (@0xFFFF844F) != 1, and ACB40 when it is 1. That makes three alternate calibrations of one quantity. The block is reached only if float [r8-40] >= 0 (030D22-030D2A); otherwise ACB90/ACB7C are used.
  - 030D46-030D4C: the result is stored to [r8-92] = 0xFFFF73B8 (r8 = 0xFFFF7414).
  - 030D60-030D66: a companion 1D lookup ACBB8 is indexed by @r8, which holds rpm (030C60/030C68 store rpm_current to @r14). Its result goes to [r8-80].
  - 030EA2-030EB0: fr0 = [r8-36]*[r8-80], then fr4 += fr0*[r8-92]. This table multiplies an RPM factor and the state value at 0xFFFF73E0 inside a sum of three such products ([r8-32]*[r8-76]*[r8-88] and [r8-28]*[r8-72]*[r8-84]).

  - 030D04-030D0E and 030EE2-030EE6: if the counter at 0xFFFF67EC is below a calibrated threshold, or byte @r15 == 1, the whole output is forced to 0.
  - 030C92-030CB0: a history buffer of [r8-100] is shifted each call, which fits a transient/derivative-style compensation.

### 0xACB68 -- ECT gain (multiplier) for one term of a three-term transient/feedback fuel correction; positive-sign branch, mode != 2

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD726, range 0.25 .. 3.3999. Triage hint: base fuelling.

This is a coolant-temperature gain, falling from 3.4 when cold to 0.25 when hot. It is selected when the value at 0xFFFF73EC is >= 0 and the mode byte returned by 0x021016 is not 2. Its result goes to 0xFFFF73B8, is multiplied by an RPM-indexed gain (0xFFFF73C4) and by a state value at 0xFFFF73E8, and that product is one of three summed terms whose total, scaled by 0xFFFF7400, is stored to 0xFFFF73D4.

Both are produced by subroutines 0x0312BC, 0x0312DE, 0x030F02 and 0x03110C, which are not shown. 'Transient/tip-in style fuel term' is therefore only an interpretation of the gain structure. The consumer of 0xFFFF73D4 is also not in the listing.

Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF73D4 (literal 0xFFFF73D4, or GBR 0xFFFF73B0 with displacement 36) to see whether it is added to fuel injection pulse width or to some other target.

  - 030D22-030D2A: fr9 = @(r8-40) = 0xFFFF73EC; if 0 > fr9 the code branches to 030D4E, which uses ACB90/ACB7C. Otherwise it continues toward this table, so the sign of 0xFFFF73EC picks the table pair.

  - 030E92-030EC6: total = @(r8-20) * (@-32*@-76*@-88 + @-36*@-80*@-92 + @-28*@-72*@-84), three gain-weighted terms.

### 0xACCA8 -- Signed-rate shaping curve for a transient (tip-in/tip-out style) correction, the variant used when flag FFFF844F != 1

  - 030F4A-030F4E: the lookup input is fr4 = @(r12-64) = FFFF73EC.

### 0xACD34 -- RPM(?)-dependent deadband threshold on the delta ([FFFF7408]-[FFFF7410]), probably a load-change deadband for transient fuelling

- **Output units:** Same units as the delta [FFFF7408]-[FFFF7410]. Possibly g/rev if these are load samples; not proven.

- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of FFFF7408, FFFF7410 and FFFF7414 is not established in this listing. Reading the X axis as rpm rests only on the breakpoint pattern 800..6400, and reading the delta as a load change rests only on the triage RAM name engine_load_current.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of FFFF7408, FFFF7410 and FFFF7414 to confirm that the delta is a load (or other) change between samples and that the X input is engine speed. Then search for readers of FFFF73EC and FFFF73D8 to see which fuelling correction this deadbanded delta drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 0312EC-0312F6: r1=0xFFFF7414; fr14 = @(FFFF7408) - @(FFFF7410), a difference of two RAM floats.
  - 0312F8-031302: if the delta is not below the constant at 0xCBE30, it is replaced by that constant and the lookup is skipped (upper clamp).

  - 031310-031316: float_abs_diff(fr14, 0) gives |delta|.
  - 031318-031320: if table > |delta| then fr14=0, so the table is a deadband width.
  - 0313B0-0313B8: otherwise the delta is lower-clamped by the constant at 0xCBE34.
  - 0313BA-0313BE: the result is stored to FFFF7414-40 = FFFF73EC.
  - 0313FA-031404 and 03140A-031414: FFFF73EC is later compared against constants 0xCBE58/0xCBE5C to choose between float_lag_filter paths (0x03143E, 0x031454, 0x03145E, 0x031474).

### 0xACD90 -- After-start enrichment, secondary additive component: initial value vs coolant temp (normal path; ACDB8 replaces it when afl_diagnostic_flag=1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCDD70, range 0 .. 2.75. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Sets the starting value, from coolant temperature, of a second additive term stored at FFFF73E4. That term is added to a primary term at FFFF73E0 (from table ACD7C), and the sum is clamped into the multiplier at FFFF7400. Afterwards the term decays toward 0 by a coolant-dependent rate (table ACD68) on a timer, which is the typical shape of a post-start enrichment that tapers off.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (from definition; read from ect_gated_c FFFF6354)
- **Output units:** Dimensionless, added to a multiplier term that is floored at 1. A cell of 2.75 would raise the summed multiplier by 2.75 before the upper clamp; the clamp constant at 0x031368 is not shown.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** That FFFF7400 is actually applied as a fuel-injection multiplier, which would make this an enrichment. The label base_fuel_table_output is only a name. Its consumer is not in either listing, and the upper clamp constant at 0x031368 is unknown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7400 (and its copies 0xFFFF7408-0x7410) to confirm it multiplies injector pulse width. Also read the float at 0x031368 (the upper clamp), decode tables ACD7C and ACD68, and identify the counter at 0xFFFF67EC and the routine at 0x21016.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x030B80/0x030B84/0x030B8A: this table is looked up with ECT (fr14 = ect_gated_c) and stored to r14-28 = 0xFFFF73E4. 0x030B7E stores ACD7C(ECT) to 0xFFFF73E0.
  - 0x030B8E-0x030B98: 73E0 + 73E4 is summed (fadd) and stored to 0xFFFF7400 (base_fuel_table_output [TRACED]).
  - 0x0311AC-0x0311BE: same pairing in the second function. r12 = 0xFFFF7444, so -100 is 73E0 and -96 is 73E4. At 0x03119E-0x0311A6, when afl_diagnostic_flag (FFFF3158) == 1, tables ACDA4 and ACDB8 are used instead.
  - 0x0311C4-0x0311E4: unless the byte returned by 0x21016 == 2, (73E0+73E4) is multiplied by 7434 = ACD43C(ECT) at 0x0311D4. It is then passed through float_clamp_range with a lower bound of 1 (fldi1 fr5) and stored to 7400 (r12-68). In the ==2 case, float_min against the constant at 0x031368 is used instead (0x0311E6).
  - 0x031188-0x031194: this branch runs only while the counter 0xFFFF67EC is below the constant at 0x0CBC28. That looks like a time-since-start gate, though the RAM name says DTC counter.
  - 0x031284-0x0312A6: decay step 73E4 = max(73E4 - ACD68(ECT)*73FC, 0). The matching step 73E0 = max(73E0 - ACD54(ECT)*73FC, 1) is at 0x03126A-0x031282. Both fire when the timer at gbr+97 reaches the period from LowPW_TableProcessor(AC430) (0x031240-0x03125E).

### 0xACDA4 -- After-start enrichment, initial multiplier term, alternate set used when flag FFFF3158 == 1

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCDD90, range 1.25 .. 2.2002. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Gives a coolant-temperature-based starting value, 1.25 to 2.2, for a multiplicative term stored at FFFF73E0. While counter FFFF67EC is below a threshold the term is reloaded every call; after that it decays by a step toward a floor of 1.0. - **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (a ratio, at least 1.0, that decays to 1.0)

- **Weakest link:** Calling it after-start fuel enrichment depends on two unverified things: that FFFF67EC is a time or revolutions-since-start counter (its label says DTC maturation), and that FFFF7400 is consumed as a fuel multiplier. Its consumer is not in this listing. When FFFF3158 equals 1 is also unknown, so it is unclear which of ACDA4 or ACD7C is normally live.
- **Next step:** Find the readers of FFFF7400 to confirm it multiplies injector pulse width. Find the writers of FFFF3158 and FFFF67EC to establish the selector condition and whether 67EC counts time since start. Read the float constants at 0x031368 (upper clamp) and 0xCBC28 (counter threshold) to judge whether the 2.2 peak survives the clamp.
- **Cited:**
  - 03111E-031120: fr14 is loaded from FFFF6354 (coolant) and passed as fr4 to every table_lookup_1D call, including the one at 0311A0.
  - 031122/031128: r14 is loaded from FFFF67EC. At 03118A-031194 it is compared against the ROM constant at 0xCBC28; this table is only loaded while r14 is below that constant.
  - 031196-03119C: this table (ACDA4) is chosen only when byte FFFF3158 == 1. Otherwise ACD7C is used at 0311AC. Two tables fill the same slot.
  - 0311A4-0311AA: the result is stored to @(-100,r12), i.e. FFFF7444-100 = FFFF73E0 (the gbr base). The companion table ACDB8 or ACD90 goes to FFFF73E4.
  - 0311C4-0311CE: FFFF73E0 and FFFF73E4 are added together.
  - 0311D0-0311E4: unless the mode byte from the call at 0x21016 equals 2, the sum is multiplied by the ACD43C lookup held in FFFF7434. It is then passed through float_clamp_range with low limit 1.0 and high limit from the constant at 0x031368, and stored to FFFF7400.
  - 0311E6-0311F2: in mode 2 the sum goes through float_min against the same 0x031368 constant and is stored to FFFF7400.
  - 031260-031282: once past the threshold, FFFF73E0 = max(FFFF73E0 - lookup(ACD54)*FFFF73FC, 1.0). This is a decay toward 1.0, with the rate taken from a coolant-indexed table and step FFFF73FC (set at 031132-031144 to 1.0 or the constant at 0xCBE60).
  - 031284-0312A6: FFFF73E4 decays the same way toward 0.0.
  - 03114E-03117C: when FFFF73E0 is near 1 and FFFF73E4 is near 0 (float_deadband_check), and gbr+101 == 0, FFFF7400 is forced to 1.0. This reads as the enrichment having finished.

### 0xACE00 -- Closed-loop enable minimum counter vs coolant temp (alternate schedule, used when 0xFFFF7829 == 1)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCDE30, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: OL enrichment / thermal.

Looks up a 16-bit threshold from the gated coolant temperature and stores it to 0xFFFF7456. When the selector byte at 0xFFFF7829 is 1, closed-loop readiness can only pass once the word counter at 0xFFFF67EC is at or above this threshold. Cold coolant gives a large threshold (about 12.7k raw) and warm coolant a small one (about 400 raw).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature in °C (ect_gated_c at 0xFFFF6354 passed in fr4; axis shared with a named coolant table)
- **Output units:** Raw counter counts, the same units as the word at 0xFFFF67EC (probably time ticks since start; tick rate unknown). The descriptor's scale and bias are garbage (denormals, 0..0 range). Back-solving them gives raw values of about 12718, 12718, 7605, 2588 ×8, 1311, 655, 393, 393, 393.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What the word counter at 0xFFFF67EC means. Its label says DTC maturation, but the comparison pattern suggests elapsed time or count since start. Its units and tick rate are not visible in this listing, and the raw table values depend on the bad descriptor scaling.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of 0xFFFF67EC to confirm it is an incrementing time-since-start counter and get its tick rate. Find the writers of 0xFFFF7829 to see when this table, rather than the 0x03160E-selected value, is active. Dump the raw uint16 bytes at 0xCDE30 to confirm the back-solved raw thresholds.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x03186C-0x031882: fr4 is loaded from stack @(4,r15), which 0x031644-0x03164A filled from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c). The call is to table_desc_1d_uint16 0x0BE8AC with r4=0x0ACE00.
  - 0x031884: mov.w r0,@(6,gbr) stores the result to 0xFFFF7456 (GBR=0xFFFF7450, set at 0x031638/0x03163A).
  - 0x031876: the sibling value from the local routine bsr 0x03160E (r4=byte at 0xFFFF72A1) goes to @(4,gbr), i.e. 0xFFFF7454.
  - 0x031890-0x0318B6: r9 is the byte at 0xFFFF7829 (loaded at 0x03167E). If r9==0, @(4,gbr) is compared with r8. If r9==1, @(6,gbr), this table, is compared. extu.w then cmp/ge r6,r2 means the path continues to 0x0318B8 only when r8 >= the table value; otherwise it branches to 0x0318F8 (r6=0).
  - 0x031674-0x031676: r8 = mov.w from 0xFFFF67EC (labelled dtc_maturation_counter_67EC; this name is unverified as to meaning).
  - 0x0318B8-0x0318F6: passing the threshold, plus deadband checks on 0xFFFF798C and 0xFFFF7A20, sched_status_R1==0 and afc_output > CL_AFC_Min, sets r6=1.
  - 0x031932-0x0319E6: r6 (copied to r4) gates the readiness flags written to @(0..3,gbr), i.e. 0xFFFF7450-0xFFFF7453.
  - 0x031886-0x03188E: the whole comparison is skipped (r6=0) when ect_current is below CL_RPM_Sanity_Min (0x0CBE64).

### 0xAD0F0 -- X-dependent (likely RPM) set-threshold for status flag byte 0xFFFF7A3C; compared quantity at 0xFFFF7A30 unidentified

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCE72C, range 11.6 .. 80.999. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

The table is looked up with the float at 0xFFFF7A34 as input. If the float at 0xFFFF7A30 is greater than or equal to the result, flag byte 0xFFFF7A3C (GBR+16) is set to 1. The flag is cleared only when 0xFFFF7A30 falls below the fixed constant at 0xCC268, which gives a hysteresis pair with a variable set point and a constant release point.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at RAM 0xFFFF7A34. Breakpoints 0..6000 in steps of 400 suggest engine speed in rpm, but nothing in the listing confirms it.
- **Output units:** Same units as RAM float 0xFFFF7A30, which is unidentified (range 11.6..81).
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity in 0xFFFF7A30 and the axis variable in 0xFFFF7A34 are not written in this listing. The rpm reading rests only on the breakpoint spacing, and the meaning of the flag depends on readers outside the listing. The function entry also appears to start late: r14 is popped at 037082 but never pushed here.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of RAM 0xFFFF7A30 and 0xFFFF7A34 to identify the compared quantity and the axis input, and for readers of flag byte 0xFFFF7A3C (and 0xFFFF7A3D/7A3E) to see what the flag gates. Also decode the float constants at 0xCC260-0xCC270 to get the release and set thresholds.
- **Cited:**
  - 037018: r14 = 0xFFFF7A3C; 03701A/03701E: fr4 = @(r14-8) = 0xFFFF7A34 is passed as the lookup input to table_lookup_1D (0xBE830) in the jsr delay slot.
  - 037020-037026: fr8 = @(r14-12) = 0xFFFF7A30; fcmp/gt fr8,fr0 with bt skip means the set path runs when table >= 0xFFFF7A30 is false, i.e. when 0xFFFF7A30 >= table.
  - 037028-03702A: mov #1 then mov.b r0,@(16,gbr); GBR = 0xFFFF7A2C (037010), so the flag byte is 0xFFFF7A3C.
  - 03702C-03703A: if the constant @0xCC268 is greater than 0xFFFF7A30, the flag at GBR+16 is cleared, so the release threshold is a fixed constant, not the table.
  - 03703C-03707A: the same set/clear hysteresis pattern runs for 0xFFFF7A2C against constants 0xCC264/0xCC260 (flag at GBR+17) and for the axis input 0xFFFF7A34 against 0xCC270/0xCC26C (flag at GBR+18).
  - Table data: the threshold falls from 81 at X <= 1200 to 11.6 at X >= 2800, so the condition is much easier to meet at high X.

### 0xAD168 -- Load-dependent weighting factor (0..1) for correction term 1, multiplied by an rpm-indexed state value and an ECT factor, feeding RAM 0xFFFF7B90

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE7CC, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

A 1D lookup on engine_load_current gives a 0..1 weight. It scales one product term, which is then clamped and stored to 0xFFFF7B90. The weight is largest (1.0) at 0.5 g/rev, 0.8 at 0.7 g/rev and 0.3 at the ends, and it is 0 at 1.1 g/rev and above, which disables that term at high load.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (0..1)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the term being weighted is unknown. Neither the multiplicand @(4,r15) (0xFFFF7B7C state / AC710 rpm table) nor the destination 0xFFFF7B90/7B8C has a confirmed identity, so 'AFL/AFC trim' is only a hint.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7B8C and 0xFFFF7B90, using literal pools that contain FFFF7B8C/FFFF7B90 or the r10=0xFFFF7BA0 base with displacements -20/-16, to see whether they enter the fuel pulse or AF-learning path. Also decode helper 0x03949E to confirm whether 0xFFFF7B7C is a learned value.
- **Cited:**
  - 038E8C-038E8E: fr13 is loaded from 0xFFFF63F8 engine_load_current [TRACED]. The delay slot at 039064 passes fmov fr13,fr4 as the lookup input for descriptor 0xAD168 at 039060.
  - 039066-039068: the result fr0 is stored to stack @(72,r15).
  - 039160-03916A: fr4 = @(4,r15) * @(72,r15) * @(88,r15). @(88) is the AD1B8 lookup of fr12 = ECT (038E8A, 03908C).
  - 03916C-03917E: a second product fr15*@(80)*@(96) is multiplied by @(-8,r10) = 0xFFFF7B98 and added with fmac. 0xFFFF7B98 was built at 038EBA-038ED4 from (0xFFFF73E8 - 1) and (base_fuel_table_output - 1).
  - 039180-03918E: float_clamp_range [TRACED] limits the sum between @(104) and @(108), which are ECT lookups of AD118 and AD12C. The result is stored to @(-16,r10) = 0xFFFF7B90.
  - 0391C4-0391D0: 0xFFFF7B90 + 0xFFFF7B94 is stored to 0xFFFF7B8C.
  - @(4,r15) comes from 0xFFFF7B7C (038EFA-038F00) or, under gbr flag bits, from table AC710 looked up by rpm (038F94-038F9E). At 039200-039224 it is written back to 0xFFFF7B7C through helper 03949E together with AD208(rpm) and the gbr+32 counter. That looks like a filtered or learned state.

### 0xAD1B8 -- Coolant-temperature weighting factor (0→1 ramp, 40–60 °C) for trim term 1, primary component (written to 0xFFFF7B90)

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE85E, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Looked up against coolant temperature, then multiplied with a ramped RPM-table value and a load-table value to form the primary part of a correction term. The term is clamped by coolant-indexed min/max tables and stored to 0xFFFF7B90. With data 0,0,0,0,0,1,1 the component is zero at or below 40 °C, ramps linearly to full strength at 60 °C, and stays at full strength above that.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine coolant temperature, °C (input fr12 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350, ect_current [TRACED])
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier, 0..1
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The use as a multiplier is observed. What the resulting term physically is (which fuel or trim correction) is not: 0xFFFF7B8C and 0xFFFF7B90 are consumed outside this listing, and the 'AFC/AFL' and LTFT labels are hints only.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7B8C and 0xFFFF7B90, i.e. literal 0xFFFF7BA0 with displacement -20/-16, or a gbr base of 0xFFFF7B7C with offsets 0x10/0x14. Also decode helper 0x03949E to learn what the ramped channel values at 0xFFFF7B7C..7B88 represent.
- **Cited:**
  - 038E88/038E8A: fr12 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED]) and is not reassigned before 03908E, where fmov fr12,fr4 supplies the lookup input. The X axis (-40..80) fits a coolant temperature in °C.
  - 03908A-039092: descriptor 0xAD1B8 goes to table_lookup_1D via jsr @r14, and the result fr0 is stored to stack @(88,r15).
  - 03915C-03916A: fr4 = @(4,r15) * @(72,r15) * @(88,r15). This multiplies the ramped value (from 0xFFFF7B7C via 038EFA, or from the RPM table 0xAC710 at 038F94) by the load-indexed table 0xAD168 (fr13 = engine_load_current, 039060) and by this table. The output is therefore used purely as a multiplicative weight.
  - 03916C-03917E: a second component, fr15 * load table 0xAD17C * ECT table 0xAD1CC * the factor stored at 0xFFFF7B98, is added to fr4 with fmac.
  - 039180-03918E: the sum is passed to float_clamp_range with limits from ECT tables 0xAD118 and 0xAD12C (@104/@108). The result is stored to @(-16,r10) = 0xFFFF7B90.
  - 0391C4-0391D0: 0xFFFF7B90 and 0xFFFF7B8C (the parallel term 2 built with the sister ECT table 0xAD1E0) are added. The sum is stored to 0xFFFF7B8C, overwriting the term-2 value.
  - 038ED6-038EE0: the whole block runs only when rpm is below cal_ltft_rpm_threshold (fcmp/gt with the threshold in fr8) and the 7C68/7D2E/90BD flags permit.

### 0xAD1CC -- Coolant-temperature weighting multiplier for the second learned A/F-learning term (channel 1)

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE86C, range 1 .. 2. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

A 1D lookup on coolant temperature gives a dimensionless gain: 2.0 at -40 to -20 °C, falling linearly to 1.0 at 80 °C. It multiplies the second of two learned terms in a compensation that is then clamped and stored to 0xFFFF7B90. That compensation looks like an A/F-learning correction, but this is inferred.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 to 2.0)

- **Weakest link:** The two terms are assumed to be A/F-learning offsets ('A/B' style learned values). The consumer of 0xFFFF7B90/0xFFFF7B8C is not in this listing, so it is not shown that the result is a fuel trim. Also unshown is which physical quantity fr15 represents (additive vs multiplicative learned value).
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7B90, 0xFFFF7B8C and 0xFFFF7B80, and see where they enter the fuel pulse or fuel-correction chain. Also compare against the sister table 0xAD1E0 and the slot-88 table 0xAD1B8 to confirm the two-term (A/B) structure.

  - 0x038E88/0x038E8A: fr12 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED]). 0x03909E-0x0390A2: descriptor 0xAD1CC is looked up with fr4=fr12. 0x0390A6: the result is stored to stack slot @(96,r15).
  - 0x03916C-0x039178: fr0 = fr15 * @(80,r15) * @(96,r15). Slot 80 is load table 0xAD17C (input fr13 = engine_load_current, 0x039074-0x03907E). So this table multiplies the fr15 learned term together with a load-dependent gain.
  - 0x039176/0x039178: the fmul consumes this table's value directly. There is no intermediate clamp or blend that would make it inert.
  - 0x038EFA-0x038F0C: fr15 comes from 0xFFFF7B80 (r10-32), or from rpm table 0xAC724 at 0x038FA0-0x038FAA when gbr flag bits are set. This makes it a stored/learned value. At 0x03925C it is written back via bsr 0x03949E into r10-32, consistent with a learning update.
  - 0x03917A-0x03917E: fr4 = fr0 * @(r10-8) + fr4. @(r10-8) is the factor (1+(@0xFFFF73E8-1)*cal 0xCC3E0)*(1+(base_fuel_table_output-1)*cal 0xCC3E4) built at 0x038EB2-0x038ED4. The other addend is @(4,r15)*@(72,r15)*@(88,r15): a parallel learned term with its own load table 0xAD168 and ECT table 0xAD1B8.
  - 0x039180-0x03918E: the sum is clamped by float_clamp_range [TRACED] between ECT tables 0xAD118 and 0xAD12C (slots 104/108). It is then stored to 0xFFFF7B90 (r10-16), and summed with the parallel channel into 0xFFFF7B8C at 0x0391C4-0x0391D0.
  - The parallel channel uses ECT table 0xAD1E0 (slot 100) in the same position for the second learned value (fr14). That is the sister of this table.

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE888, range 1 .. 1.5. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Whether the multiplier is live depends on the clamp tables 0xAD140/0xAD154 and the load table 0xAD1A4, none of which are decoded here.

### 0xAD348 -- IAT-dependent floor for a ramped-down multiplier (state at FFFF7BD8, output at FFFF7BDC)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCECC8, range 0.84998 .. 0.94995. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Gives the lowest value, 0.85 at or below 10 °C and 0.95 at or above 20 °C on the presumed IAT axis, that a multiplier may ramp down to. While flags at FFFF7BE0 and FFFF7BE1 are both 1, each pass lowers the multiplier by a fixed step (constant at 0xCC454) but not below this table. The result is capped at the constant at 0xCBF40 (labelled cal_default_fuel_mult) and stored.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Probably intake air temperature in °C: the lookup input is fr4, loaded from 0xFFFF6364 (labelled iat_current [TRACED]) at 039D2E and not overwritten on any path to the call.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (ratio, 0.85..0.95)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What the multiplier at FFFF7BDC actually scales is not in this listing. The 'fuel' meaning rests only on the cal_default_fuel_mult label and the region hint. The IAT reading of the axis depends on the iat_current label for 0xFFFF6364 and on table_lookup_1D taking its input in fr4.
- **Next step:** First search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7BDC to see what this multiplier is applied to. Then read the float constants at 0xCBF40, 0xCC44C, 0xCC450, 0xCC454 and 0xCC458; if the cap at 0xCBF40 is below 0.85, or the seed at 0xCC450 is never above the floor, the table is inert. Finally find the writers of the flag bytes 0xFFFF7BE0 and 0xFFFF7BE1 to learn which event triggers the ramp.
- **Cited:**
  - 039D22: r14 = 0xFFFF7BE0, a state block. The float at r14-8 (FFFF7BD8) is the ramp state; the float at r14-4 (FFFF7BDC) is the output.
  - 039D2C/039D2E: fr4 = @0xFFFF6364 (iat_current [TRACED]). No instruction between 039D2E and the 039D8E jsr writes fr4 on the paths through 039D58 and 039D78 that reach the lookup.
  - 039D7E-039D88: the lookup runs only when flag byte @(2,r14) is 1, @r14 is 1 and @(1,r14) is 1.
  - 039D92-039DA4: fr5 = table result, fr4 = state - @0xCC454 (039DA0 fsub), then float_max, giving fr14 = max(state - step, table). The table is the floor of a downward ramp.
  - 039DB0-039DBA: fr8 = float_min(fr14, @0xCBF40), capping the output at the default multiplier.
  - 039DBC-039DC2: fr14 is stored to FFFF7BD8 (state) and fr8 to FFFF7BDC (output).
  - 039DA6-039DAE: when @r14 is not 1 the state instead rises by @0xCC458 (fadd).
  - 039D58-039D76: once the state is at or above @0xCC44C, the state is reset to @0xCBF40 and the flag at @(2,r14) is cleared.
  - 039D42-039D56: on the rising edge of the flags the state is seeded with @0xCC450 and the flag at @(2,r14) is set.

### 0xAD464 -- RPM-dependent threshold factor for per-cylinder exceedance counter (multiplied with table 0xAB880/0xAB51C)

1D x7 float32, data 0xCF02C, range 0.8 .. 5.4. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Looked up against engine speed. The result is stored at FFFF7AA8 and multiplied by a second RPM table result at FFFF7AA4 (0xAB880 or 0xAB51C, chosen by a flag). The product is a threshold: when FFFF99B4 exceeds it, a per-index (likely per-cylinder) byte counter is incremented; otherwise a second counter advances. Those counters later gate stepping and clamping of four per-index float corrections at FFFF7A78..7A84.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier, or threshold in units of FFFF99B4 once multiplied by the companion table; the units of FFFF99B4 are unknown
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of FFFF99B4, the quantity compared against the threshold, is not established in this listing. Without it the table can only be named by its role (threshold factor), not by quantity. That the index r13 is a cylinder number is also assumed from the four-entry arrays.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of FFFF99B4 to identify the compared quantity. Check the callers of 0x03771A to confirm r4 is a cylinder index 0..3. Decode the companion tables 0xAB880/0xAB51C to see which acts as the base threshold. Search for readers of FFFF7A78..FFFF7A84 to see which correction the counters drive.
- **Cited:**
  - 03772E/037730: fr14 <- @FFFF6624 rpm_current; 03778E: fmov fr14,fr4 is the lookup input for descriptor 0xAD464 loaded at 037788, so the axis is RPM
  - 03778A/03778C: calls the same table_lookup_1D pointer saved at @(16,r15) by the earlier lookup of 0xAB880/0xAB51C (03776E/03777C)
  - 037790: fmov.s fr0,@r12 with r12=FFFF7AA8 stores the result; the companion result was stored at FFFF7AA4 at 037786
  - 03783A-03783E: fr8 = @(FFFF7AA4) * @(FFFF7AA8), the product of the two tables
  - 037840: fcmp/gt fr8,fr15, where fr15 = @FFFF99B4 (03772A/03772C); if FFFF99B4 > product, 037844-037856 increments byte counter [r13] via uint8_add_sat and zeroes the companion counter
  - 037860-037886: the else path compares the companion counter with the constant at 0xCBBFA and increments it
  - 037974-0379B2: counters compared against 0xCBBFB gate stepping of floats at FFFF7A78+4i (step 0xCC2E4, min with the table at 0x63B20[i], clamp 0..FFFF7A88+4i)
  - 037792-037796: the comparison runs only when gbr byte 40 (FFFF7AA0) is nonzero

### 0xAD524 -- Cold-start / cranking multiplier vs RPM and coolant temp (engine-not-running, FFFF90BE==0 variant)

2D 7x5 uint8, data 0xCF608, range 0.54688 .. 1. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Gives a 0.55–1.0 multiplier from engine speed (115–515 rpm) and coolant temperature (-30..0 °C), stored to FFFF72F0. It is one of four sibling tables picked by the engine-running test and the FFFF90BE flag. This one is chosen when the engine is not running and FFFF90BE is zero.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (115..515): engine speed in rpm, from rpm_current FFFF6624 passed in fr4. The range fits cranking or just-caught speeds.; X (-30..0): coolant temperature in °C, from ect_current FFFF6350 passed in fr5. Only cold temperatures are covered.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (uint8 × 1/128, 0.547..1.0)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of FFFF72F0 is not in this listing. Whether the multiplier scales fuel (cranking or after-start enrichment), airflow or timing is not shown. The X/Y argument order of table_lookup_2D (fr4→Y, fr5→X) is inferred only from the axis ranges. 'Not running' is taken from the check_engine_running label, which is TRACED but whose name is unconfirmed.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for literal-pool references to 0xFFFF72F0 and look at how its readers use it (multiplied into a fuel pulse width, an airflow figure or timing?). Also find the writers of byte FFFF90BE to learn what selects this variant over AD540.
- **Cited:**
  - 02FADA/02FADC: fr15 = float at FFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]); 02FB08 passes it as fr4 to table_lookup_2D.
  - 02FADE/02FAE0: fr14 = float at FFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED]); 02FB0C passes it as fr5 in the jsr delay slot.
  - Axis ranges match these inputs: 115–515 suits low rpm and -30..0 suits cold ECT.
  - 02FAE4/02FAEC: jsr check_engine_running 0x22CF4, then cmp/eq #1. If the result is not 1, the branch goes to 02FAFE.
  - 02FAF0/02FAFE/02FB02: the byte at FFFF90BE is tested. If it is zero, 02FB04 loads this descriptor 0xAD524. If it is non-zero, AD540 is used. The running path uses AD55C or AD578.
  - 02FB0E/02FB10: the lookup result fr0 is stored to FFFF72F0, and the function returns. No clamp or arithmetic is applied in this function.
  - The data is 1.0 at 115/215 rpm and falls with rpm at cold ECT, down to 0.547 at 515 rpm and -20 °C. That shape fits a reduction that phases in as the engine catches. This last point is interpretation, not code.

### 0xAD594 -- Cold-ECT / low-barometric-pressure fuel multiplier (altitude cold-start enrichment factor)

2D 4x5 uint8, data 0xCF74C, range 1 .. 1.8125. Triage hint: base fuelling.

A 2D multiplier looked up from barometric pressure (atm_pressure_current, saved to the stack at 0x0304F6) and the gated coolant temperature (fr13 = ect_gated_c). It is 1.0 at sea level (760) and at -30 / 0 °C, and rises to 1.81 at low pressure for coolant between -10 and -3 °C. The result is multiplied with the accumulated factors at FFFF7358 and FFFF7364, and with FFFF7360 when the gbr+21 flag is set, and the product is stored to FFFF7354.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (504..760): barometric pressure from atm_pressure_current (FFFF6898), probably mmHg (760 = sea level). Fed in fr4.; X (-30..0): gated coolant temperature in °C from ect_gated_c (FFFF6354). Fed in fr5.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no change)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The pairing of fr4/fr5 to the X/Y axes inside table_lookup_2D is assumed, not shown. The axis-to-quantity mapping rests on magnitudes (760 ≈ sea-level mmHg, -30..0 °C), and the units of atm_pressure_current are not verified. What FFFF7354 ultimately scales (injector pulse, a start-up fuel term or something else) is outside this listing.
- **Next step:** Disassemble table_lookup_2D at 0xBE8E4 and confirm whether fr4 indexes the descriptor's Y axis (0xCF728) or its X axis (0xCF73C). Check the scaling of atm_pressure_current (FFFF6898) against a named barometric table. Then search for readers of FFFF7354 to see what the final product multiplies.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x0304EE-0x0304F6: fr8 is loaded from FFFF6898 atm_pressure_current and stored to @r15 in the jsr delay slot. No later code writes offset 0 of the frame (the other stack writes use offsets 4..32).
  - 0x0304EA: fr13 is loaded from FFFF6354 ect_gated_c and is not modified before the lookup.
  - 0x0306F4-0x0306FC: r4=0xAD594, fr5=fr13 (ECT), fr4=@r15 (atm pressure), then jsr table_lookup_2D.
  - 0x0306FE-0x03070E: fr8 = @(FFFF7358) * @(FFFF7364) (FFFF7364 is either the AC3CC ECT 1D table or 1.0), then fr0 *= fr8. The table output is a multiplicative factor.
  - 0x030708-0x030714: if gbr byte 21 == 1, fr0 is also multiplied by @(FFFF7360), the result of 2D table AD5B0.
  - 0x030716-0x030718: the product is stored to FFFF7370-28 = FFFF7354 (the gbr base of this fuel block).
  - Data: neutral 1.0 at 760 and at the -30 and 0 rows; enrichment grows as the Y value falls. This fits barometric pressure in mmHg and coolant in °C, but that is a magnitude match only.

### 0xAD5CC -- Altitude (barometric) x coolant-temperature multiplier on cranking/after-start enrichment

2D 4x5 uint8, data 0xCF884, range 1 .. 1.8125. Triage hint: base fuelling.

A multiplier of 1.0 to 1.81, indexed by barometric pressure and coolant temperature. It scales the larger of two decaying, ECT-scheduled enrichment terms, and the product is stored to 0xFFFF7374 (the GBR base). Extra enrichment applies only at low barometric pressure and coolant between about -10 and -3 C; it is 1.0 at -30 C, at 0 C and at 760.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (504..760): barometric/atmospheric pressure, most likely mmHg (760 = sea level, 504 is roughly 3,400 m). Fed from 0xFFFF6898, which is stored to @r15 at 030856 and loaded into fr4 at 030AA6.; X (-30..0): coolant temperature in C. Fed from fr13 = 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c), loaded at 03084C and moved to fr5 at 030AA2.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 = no change)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unverified. First, that 0xFFFF6898 really is barometric pressure in mmHg: this rests on its label plus the 504..760 breakpoint fit. Second, that the decaying terms at 0xFFFF7374 are cranking or after-start fuel enrichment rather than some other decaying correction: the consumer of 0xFFFF7374 is not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of RAM 0xFFFF7374 (GBR-relative @(0,gbr) under gbr=0xFFFF7374, or absolute loads) and confirm it feeds the injector pulse-width or fuel-enrichment path. Also check the writer of 0xFFFF6898 to confirm it comes from the barometric sensor scaled to mmHg.
- **Cited:**
  - 030850/030856: fr8 is loaded from 0xFFFF6898 and stored to @r15. 030AA6 fmov.s @r15,fr4 makes that value the first argument of table_lookup_2D (030AA4).
  - 03084C: fr13 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354. 030AA2 fmov fr13,fr5 makes it the second argument. The breakpoint ranges (504..760 and -30..0) fit pressure and temperature.
  - 030A84-030A9A: fr4 = [r11-8] + fr0*[r11-16] and fr5 = [r11-4] + fr8*[r11-12], then jsr float_max at 030A98. fr14 = max of the two terms (030A9C).
  - 030AA8: fmul fr0,fr14 multiplies that max by this table's output. 030AAA/030AAC stores it to r11-44 = 0xFFFF73A0-0x2C = 0xFFFF7374, the GBR base set at 030840.
  - 0308F4-0309B2: [r11-40..-28], fr15 and fr14 are decremented each pass by ECT-indexed 1D tables (some scaled by [r11-24]/[r11-20]) and floored at 0 by float_max. This decaying-term shape is typical of start/after-start enrichment, which the table then scales.
  - No clamp follows the fmul, and the data reaches 1.8125, so the table is not inert.

### 0xAD7A8 -- unknown – first (base) gain factor of the multi-factor target ramped into 0xFFFF7BE4 (FFFF7BEC block)

2D 12x10 uint8, data 0xD0E68, range 0 .. 0.29688. Triage hint: knock.

The table is looked up with the float at 0xFFFF8E38 and a scaled copy of the uint16 at 0xFFFF67EC, and the result is stored to 0xFFFF7BF0. If ROM flag 0xCBC06 is set, it is multiplied with three 1D factors (ECT and two others) into a target at 0xFFFF7BE8, and 0xFFFF7BE4 ramps up toward that target. The breakpoint range looks temperature-like, but that is not proven.

What reads the final 0xFFFF7BE4 is not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7BE4 (and 0xFFFF7BF0) to see what the ramped value drives. Find the writer of 0xFFFF8E38. Disassemble fmac_interp_uint16 (0xBE598) and subroutine 0x03A196 to fix the Y-axis scaling and the alternate-path formula.

  - 039F92-039F96: result stored to @(4,r11) = 0xFFFF7BF0

### 0xAD7F4 -- Cold-coolant gain (2D, ECT x unknown input at 0xFFFF73B0) for the first term of a three-term transient/wall-wetting style fuel correction

2D 3x16 uint16, data 0xD1098, range 1 .. 12.109. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Returns a gain that is high when the engine is cold (up to about 12) and falls to 1.0 at 90 °C and above. Larger X values also lower it. The result is stored at 0xFFFF73BC and multiplied with two other quantities to form the first of three summed product terms. The sum is scaled and clamped, then stored at 0xFFFF73B4.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium

They are probably set in 0x0312BC, 0x0312DE, 0x030F02 or 0x03110C. So the claim that this is transient or wall-wetting fuel rests only on the shape of the code: three ECT×rpm×state product terms, a history shift, and gating. The consumer of 0xFFFF73B4 is also not shown.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x0312BC and 0x0312DE to see how 0xFFFF73B0 (X) and 0xFFFF73F4 (the [-32] multiplicand) are produced. Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF73B4 to see whether the final value is added to injector pulse width or to fuel mass. Also check whether 0xFFFF844F selects a hardware/variant flag.

2D 15x12 uint16, data 0xD1C38, range 390.5 .. 866.25. Triage hint: OL enrichment / thermal.

The value is multiplied by a 1D correction factor and becomes the target of a two-stage first-order lag model. The lag uses different coefficient tables for rising and falling targets, which is the structure of a steady-state exhaust/catalyst temperature estimate.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity, exhaust temperature in degrees C, rests on the value magnitude, the 1500/65536 scale and the lag-model structure. The code fixes the table only as the steady-state target of a filtered model. Which axis maps to fr4 versus fr5 is also assumed from the breakpoint ranges.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF79A8 (second-stage output) and 0xFFFF79A4 (first-stage output) and check whether they are compared with thresholds that trigger OL enrichment or component protection. Also decode tables AD998, AD9B4 and AD9D0 to confirm they are lag coefficients in the 0..1 range, and AC5F8 to check that the multiplier is not uniformly 1.0.

  - 03685A/03685C and 03685E/036860: fr12=rpm_current (FFFF6624) and fr15=engine_load_current (FFFF63F8); at 036896/03689A these are passed as fr5=rpm and fr4=load to table_lookup_2D (0BE8E4)

  - 03689C/0368A2: the result is stored to r13-36 (0xFFFF79D4)
