### 0xAD998 -- Open-loop enrichment target rise-rate lag-filter coefficient (rpm x load)

2D 13x12 uint16, data 0xD1FD8, range 0.01953 .. 0.07422. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

This table is the per-call smoothing coefficient used when the freshly computed enrichment target (map x scale) jumps above the previous target by more than a threshold. The selected coefficient is looked up by rpm and load and passed as fr6 to float_lag_filter, which moves the smoothed target toward the new one. The function at 0x036008 also installs this table as the default descriptor.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unconfirmed. First, float_lag_filter (0xBEA40) is assumed to take fr6 as the blend coefficient; its body is not shown. Second, the filtered quantity is called open-loop enrichment only because of the OL_Enrich_* labels, which are names and not proof.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0xBEA40 to confirm the result is fr5 + fr6*(fr4 - fr5), possibly gated by fr7. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF79A8 and 0xFFFF79A4 to see whether the double-filtered target feeds the fuel-injection enrichment.
- **Cited:**
  - 0368C4-0368CE: map result (@-36) is multiplied by the 1D scale result (@-32) and stored as the new target at r13-76 (0xFFFF79AC); fr14 holds the old value of that slot, loaded at 036866.
  - 0368D0-0368E2: if new target > old + OL_Enrich_Accum_Offset (0xCC224), descriptor 0xAD998 is written to @(108,gbr) = 0xFFFF798C+0x6C = 0xFFFF79F8, the selected-descriptor pointer.
  - 0368E4-036902: on the falling branch (|diff| > offset and old - offset > new) the alternative descriptor 0xAD9B4 is written instead; otherwise the previous selection is kept.
  - 036904-036912: the selected descriptor is looked up with fr4=load and fr5=rpm through table_lookup_2D; the result is stored at r13-72 (0xFFFF79B0).
  - 036914-03692C: float_lag_filter is called with fr4=target(@-76), fr5=previous filtered(@-84), fr6=this coefficient(@-72) and fr7=constant from 0x036A64; the result is written back to @-84 (0xFFFF79A4).
  - 03692E-036950: a second filter stage uses coefficient table 0xAD9D0 on @-84 into @-80 (0xFFFF79A8).
  - 036008-036018: the init routine stores 0xAD998 to 0xFFFF79F8 (@(4,r5) with r5=0xFFFF79F4), making it the default coefficient table.
  - Data are small fractions that grow with rpm and load (faster convergence at high airflow), consistent with a per-event lag coefficient.

### 0xAD9B4 -- OL enrichment target smoothing coefficient, falling direction (rpm x load)

2D 13x12 uint16, data 0xD2174, range 0.01172 .. 0.10938. Triage hint: OL enrichment / thermal.

Gives the lag-filter weight used to smooth the open-loop enrichment target when the newly computed target has dropped by more than the deadband OL_Enrich_Accum_Offset below the previous target. The filtered result goes to 0xFFFF79A4 (r13-84) and is then filtered a second time using table 0xAD9D0.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (breakpoints 800..6400; register is fr12 = rpm_current, passed in fr5); Y: engine load, g/rev (breakpoints 0.15..2.5; register is fr15 = engine_load_current, passed in fr4)
- **Output units:** dimensionless filter weight per execution (0.012..0.109)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** float_lag_filter (0xBEA40) is a name only; its body is not shown. That fr6 is the blend coefficient, rather than for example a step limit, is assumed from the name and from the argument pattern. Whether X is rpm and Y is load also rests on the argument-order convention of table_lookup_2D, although the breakpoint magnitudes agree.
- **Next step:** Disassemble float_lag_filter at 0xBEA40 to confirm that fr6 is the weight in something like out = prev + k*(in - prev). Also compare the data of descriptor 0xAD998 (rising-direction counterpart) and 0xAD9D0 (second-stage filter) to confirm they form a matched rise/fall coefficient set. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF79A4 (r13-84) and 0xFFFF79A8 (r13-80) to see where the smoothed enrichment is consumed.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x036890-0x0368BC: the new target is a 2D map (OL_Enrich_Map_Normal or _Reduced, indexed by load and rpm) times a 1D scale of fr8. It is stored at r13-76 by fmul at 0x0368CA and the store at 0x0368CE. The old value of r13-76 was saved in fr14 at 0x036866.
  - 0x0368D2-0x0368E2: if new target > old + OL_Enrich_Accum_Offset, descriptor 0xAD998 is written to @(108,gbr), which is 0xFFFF79F8.
  - 0x0368E4-0x036902: otherwise, if |new - old| > offset and (old - offset) > new (fcmp/gt fr8,fr14 at 0x0368FC), this table's descriptor 0xAD9B4 is written to the same slot at 0x036900-0x036902. So the table is selected only when the target is falling past the deadband. If neither branch fires, the previously selected descriptor is kept.
  - 0x036904-0x036912: the selected descriptor is looked up with fr4 = load and fr5 = rpm through table_lookup_2D, which is held at @r15. The result is stored to r13-72.
  - 0x036914-0x03692C: float_lag_filter is called with fr4 = new target (r13-76), fr5 = previous filtered state (r13-84), fr6 = this table's value (r13-72) and fr7 = a constant. The result is written back to r13-84. The table value therefore sits in the coefficient slot.
  - Data: small values in 0..0.11 that rise with both rpm and load, which fits a per-step filter weight (faster decay at high airflow).

### 0xAD9EC -- RPM x speed threshold, compared against RAM FFFF6400, that sets/clears flag FFFF7A28 (variant used when check_engine_running() != 1; ADA08 is the other variant)

2D 7x10 uint16, data 0xD248C, range 0.90002 .. 1.6. Triage hint: idle.

The function looks up a threshold of about 0.9 to 1.6 from RPM and vehicle speed, saves it to FFFF7A24 and compares it with the float at FFFF6400. If FFFF6400 stays at or above the threshold for CBBF3 consecutive calls, flag FFFF7A28 is set to 1. If FFFF6400 stays below it for CBBF4 calls while ECT is below the constant at CC25C, the flag is cleared.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X (400..6000): engine speed, rpm. Input fr5 comes from rpm_current. The axis assignment is inferred from breakpoint magnitudes, not from the lookup's register convention.; Y (48..192): vehicle speed, km/h. Input fr4 comes from vehicle_speed_kmh. Also assigned by magnitude.
- **Output units:** Same units as the RAM float at FFFF6400, which is not identified. The magnitude would fit engine load in g/rev, but that is unconfirmed. The value is dimensionless only if FFFF6400 is a ratio.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The identity of RAM FFFF6400, the quantity compared against the table, is unknown, so the table's units and meaning depend on it. A secondary weak link: the rpm/speed axis assignment rests on breakpoint magnitudes, and the 'engine running' meaning of the selector depends on an unverified function name.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of RAM FFFF6400 to identify the compared quantity (for example, whether it is engine load in g/rev). Then search for readers of flag FFFF7A28 (GBR+4) to see what the flag gates. Also check what 0x22CF4 actually returns, to confirm which condition selects AD9EC over ADA08.
- **Cited:**
  - 036F88-036F8A: fr5 = @FFFF6624 (rpm_current); 036F8C/036F94: fr4 = @FFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh). These are the two lookup inputs.
  - 036F8E-036F96: r4=0xAD9EC is the default. If check_engine_running returns 1, bf/s falls through and r4 is replaced by 0xADA08. So this table is used when the return value is not 1.
  - 036F9E-036FA0: the lookup result fr0 is stored to FFFF7A24, the GBR base at offset 0.
  - 036FA2-036FA8: fr9=@FFFF6400; fcmp/gt fr9,fr0. When table > RAM the code branches to 036FCA; otherwise it takes the 'at/above threshold' path.
  - 036FAA-036FC8: on the at/above path, counter @(28,gbr) is cleared and counter @(27,gbr) is incremented with saturation. When it reaches the byte at CBBF3, flag @(4,gbr) (FFFF7A28) is set to 1.
  - 036FCA-036FFE: on the below path, counter 27 is cleared. Only if the constant at CC25C > ect_current is counter 28 incremented; when it reaches the byte at CBBF4, the flag is cleared. Otherwise counter 28 is reset and the flag is left unchanged.
  - No clamp or multiplier is applied to fr0, so the table acts directly as a hysteresis/debounce threshold.

### 0xADAAC -- Coolant-temperature minimum floor for the selector/RPM-based value at FFFF7ED0, output to FFFF7ECC (units unproven, possibly ignition advance in degrees)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD30A8, range -20 .. 20.078. Triage hint: ignition timing.

The table is looked up with coolant temperature. The result becomes a lower bound: float_max(FFFF7ED0, table) is stored to FFFF7ECC. FFFF7ED0 is either an RPM-indexed 1D table chosen by ol_condition_selector or a calibration constant. The floor is about +20 at or below 30°C, ramps down through 40–50°C and drops to -20 at 60°C and above, so it only bites on a cold engine.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, °C (from definition; the code passes ect_current FFFF6350 in fr15 as the lookup input)
- **Output units:** Same units as FFFF7ED0. Not proven; the 0.3515625 scale with -20 bias is the common Subaru encoding for degrees of ignition advance.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What FFFF7ED0/FFFF7ECC physically represent. The consumer of FFFF7ECC is not in this listing. The 'ignition advance' reading rests only on the scale/bias convention and a gbr triage hint. The not-running constant of 69.65 is unusually large for timing degrees.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of RAM 0xFFFF7ECC (and 0xFFFF7ED0) to see what the clamped value feeds. In the same pass, decode tables 0xADAC0, 0xADAD4 and 0xADAE8 and the floats at 0xD2A90, 0xD2A94 and 0xD2A98 to check whether their values ever fall below ~20, which would make this cold-engine floor active.
- **Cited:**
  - 03ED66-03ED68: fr15 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current); 03EE96 fmov fr15,fr4 makes it the lookup input for descriptor 0xADAAC at 03EE90/03EE94.
  - 03ED62-03ED64: fr14 = rpm_current, used as the input for the alternative tables 0xADAC0, 0xADAD4 and 0xADAE8 (03EDBC/03EDCC/03EDDC). Their results are stored to 0xFFFF7ED0 (03EDC2/03EDD2/03EDE2).
  - 03EE74-03EE8E: for selector values 4, 5 and default, 0xFFFF7ED0 instead gets the constants at 0xD2A90, 0xD2A94 and 0xD2A98. 03ED88-03ED8E: when the engine is not running and the counter at FFFF5E74 is at or above the cal at 0xD29AE, it gets the literal 0x428B4CCC (≈69.65).
  - 03EE98-03EE9E: the table result goes to fr5, fr4 = @r14 (FFFF7ED0), and float_max (0xBE960) is called.
  - 03EEA0-03EEA2: the max result is stored to @(-4,r14) = 0xFFFF7ECC. So the table acts purely as a coolant-temperature-dependent lower clamp on the FFFF7ED0 value.
  - Data: the floor is 20.078 from -40 to 30°C, then 15.16, 9.88 and 4.96 at 40–60°C... actually 15.16/9.88/4.96 at 40/50/60°C, then -20 (the cell minimum) from 70°C up. It is inert when warm unless FFFF7ED0 can go below -20.

### 0xADAC0 -- unknown: RPM-indexed candidate value for selector state 1, floored by an ECT table (output at FFFF7ECC)

1D x5 uint8, data 0xD30CC, range -2.0703 .. 8.125. Triage hint: ignition timing.

When the engine-running check is true, or the FFFF5E74 word is below a calibration threshold, the function uses the byte at FFFF6810 to pick a value. Selector 1 interpolates this table on rpm, selectors 2 and 3 use sibling rpm tables, and 4, 5 and default use scalar constants; the pick goes to FFFF7ED0. That value is then max'd against an ECT-indexed table (0xADAAC) and the result is stored to FFFF7ECC.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (fr14 loaded from 0xFFFF6624 at 03ED62/03ED64 and passed as fr4 at 03EDBC); breakpoints 1600-3200
- **Output units:** unknown (physical range -2.07..8.13 after scale 0.35156 and bias -20; same units as the 0xADAAC ECT table and the FFFF7ED0/FFFF7ECC values)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Nothing in this listing shows what consumes FFFF7ECC or FFFF7ED0, so the physical quantity is unknown. The ignition-timing subsystem hint and the labels on 0xFFFF6810 and the 0xD2Axx scalars are names only. The ~69.65 constant used when not running implies the max output is often dominated by other sources.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7ECC (and 0xFFFF7ED0) to see what the max'd value feeds: a timing sum, a limit or a comparison. Also find the writers of 0xFFFF6810 to learn when the selector equals 1.
- **Cited:**
  - 03ED62-03ED64: fr14 = float at 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current); 03ED66-03ED68: fr15 = float at 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current)
  - 03ED6E/03ED74: r13 = byte at 0xFFFF6810; 03ED96-03EDB0: compare chain dispatches on values 1..5
  - 03EDB4-03EDBC: selector==1 loads descriptor 0xADAC0 and calls table_lookup_1D 0xBE830 with fr4=fr14 (rpm)
  - 03EDBE-03EDC2: result fr0 stored to 0xFFFF7ED0
  - 03EDC4/03EDD4: selectors 2 and 3 use sibling rpm tables 0xADAD4 and 0xADAE8 with the same output path
  - 03EE74-03EE8E: selectors 4, 5 and default store scalar cals 0xD2A90, 0xD2A94, 0xD2A98 to 0xFFFF7ED0 instead
  - 03ED88-03ED8E: if not running and the FFFF5E74 word >= cal at 0xD29AE, constant 0x428B4CCC (~69.65) is stored to 0xFFFF7ED0
  - 03EE90-03EE96: lookup 0xADAAC indexed by fr15 (ECT)
  - 03EE98-03EEA2: float_max(@FFFF7ED0, ECT-table value) stored to 0xFFFF7ECC (r14-4)

### 0xADB10 -- MAP-indexed latched initial value on a flag rising edge, decayed to zero afterwards (consumer unknown)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD3174, range 7.3828 .. 20.039. Triage hint: unassigned.

On the call where the GBR flag byte 0xFFFF7EED (gbr+45) changes from 0 to 1, the function looks this table up by manifold pressure and latches the result into RAM 0xFFFF7EC8. While the flag stays 1 the value is held. Once the flag is 0, the value is reduced each call by the constant at 0xD2ABC and floored at 0. What finally uses 0xFFFF7EC8 is not in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: manifold pressure from RAM 0xFFFF620C (manifold_pressure_map [TRACED]), breakpoints 0..1500. Units not established: possibly mmHg absolute, possibly something else.
- **Output units:** unknown; raw*0.35156 (=90/256), 7.38..20.04, rising with MAP between breakpoints 400 and 700
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF7EC8 is not in the listing, so the meaning and units of the output (and whether it is a timing quantity at all) are unestablished. The meaning of flag gbr+45 (0xFFFF7EED) is also unknown. The 'ignition_timing_workspace' label is only a hint.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7EC8 (as an absolute literal, as gbr+8 with GBR=0xFFFF7EC0, and as 0xFFFF7F08 with displacement -64), and for writers of 0xFFFF7EED (gbr+45). Together these fix what the latched-and-decayed value modifies and what event triggers it.
- **Cited:**
  - 03F06A-03F070: the float at 0xFFFF620C (manifold_pressure_map) is stored to stack slot @(24,r15).
  - 03F20A-03F20E: the lookup input fr4 is loaded from @(24,r15), so the X input is that MAP value; the descriptor 0xADB10 is passed in r4 at 03F206.
  - 03F210-03F216: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF7F08-64 = 0xFFFF7EC8, which is gbr+8.
  - 03F08A-03F090: byte gbr+45 is read into r0 and copied to r11 in the jsr delay slot; 03F09E: r10 = extu.b r11.
  - 03F2C8-03F2CA: gbr+75 is set to r11, so gbr+75 holds the previous call's value of gbr+45.
  - 03F1FA-03F204: the lookup runs only when gbr+75==0 and r10==1, i.e. on a 0-to-1 edge of gbr+45.
  - 03F2AC-03F2C6: if r10==0, 0xFFFF7EC8 = max(0xFFFF7EC8 - cal@0xD2ABC, 0), a per-call decrement to zero. If r10==1 and gbr+75!=0, the value is left unchanged (held).
  - No read of 0xFFFF7EC8 as a consumer appears inside 0x03F01A-0x03F374.

### 0xADBB0 -- Post-start / stationary timing adder vs coolant temperature (ramped out on vehicle movement or timeout)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD31F6, range 0.03906 .. 8.125. Triage hint: unassigned.

When the vehicle is essentially stopped (speed <= 1 km/h), RPM is below a calibrated maximum and a uint16 counter at FFFF67EC is below a calibrated limit, the code looks this table up on coolant temperature and stores the result at FFFF7F38. Otherwise the stored value is ramped down toward 0 by a fixed step per call, or forced to 0. The value is then added to the blended timing term at FFFF7F1C to form the output at FFFF7F18. The table gives about 0 below 0 °C, about 2.9 at 10 °C and 8.1 at 20 °C and above.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine coolant temperature, °C (fr13 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350, ect_current, at 03FCCA/03FCCC and passed as fr4 at 03FF20)
- **Output units:** Probably degrees of ignition advance. This rests only on the cell scaling of 0.3515625 with bias -20, the usual Subaru timing encoding, and on the value being added to a timing-path variable.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unverified. First, that FFFF67EC is a time-since-start counter: its label says DTC maturation counter, and the cal it is compared with is named after map-switching. Second, that the sum at FFFF7F18 is actually used as ignition advance. That consumer is not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7F18 (the sum) and 0xFFFF7F38 (this table's slot) to confirm they feed the final ignition advance. Then find the writer of 0xFFFF67EC to confirm it counts time since engine start.
- **Cited:**
  - 03FCCA-03FCCC: fr13 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current). 03FF1A-03FF20: the descriptor 0xADBB0 goes into r4, then table_lookup_1D is called with fr4=fr13.
  - 03FF22-03FF26: the result fr0 is stored to @(-44,r8). r8=0xFFFF7F64, so the slot is 0xFFFF7F38.
  - 03FEEA-03FEF0: if ect_gated_c (fr12) is below cal 0xD2AFC, control branches to 03FF3E, which stores 0.0 to the slot.
  - 03FEF2-03FEFE: if afl_diagnostic_flag==1 and byte 0xFFFF3202==1, the slot is forced to 0.
  - 03FF00-03FF04: fldi1 / fcmp/gt fr7,fr15. If vehicle speed > 1, control goes to 03FF28: the slot is decremented by cal 0xD2B04, then float_max with 0 is applied (ramp-out).
  - 03FF06-03FF10: the uint16 at FFFF67EC (r9) is compared with cal 0xD29B2. If the counter is >= the cal, the same ramp-out runs.
  - 03FF12-03FF18: if rpm >= cal 0xD2B00, the lookup is skipped and the previous value is held.
  - 03FFAA-03FFB6 and 03FF98-03FFA2: the output at -76 (0xFFFF7F18) is formed by adding @(-44) to @(-72), or to the clamped (-72 minus -80) term. So the table is a pure additive term on that path.

### 0xADDE0 -- Low-RPM fade-in multiplier on the ramp target in the FFFF801C-block routine (state 1/2 path)

1D x7 uint8, data 0xD340C, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

A 1D table indexed by engine speed. It returns 0 at 500 rpm, 0.5 at 1000 rpm and 1.0 from 1500 rpm up, and it multiplies the target ceiling that a ramped accumulator (fr12) is allowed to climb to in states 1 and 2. Below about 1500 rpm it scales that target down, and at 500 rpm it holds the target at zero.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input is the float at 0xFFFF6624, labelled rpm_current [TRACED], saved to sp+12 at 0415FC and reloaded into fr4 at 041682)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier, 0..1
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The use as an rpm-dependent multiplier on the ramp ceiling is observed. The physical meaning of the ramped quantity at 0xFFFF8004/0xFFFF8000 is not known: the 'percond' and 'AFC' names are labels, not evidence. The rpm input also rests on the rpm_current name, although the 500-3200 breakpoints fit rpm.
- **Next step:** Search for code that reads 0xFFFF8004 and 0xFFFF8000 (and 0xFFFF8008, the factor at r10-20) to find what the ramped value drives. Also find the writer of the state byte at GBR+30 (0xFFFF801E) to learn what states 1 and 2 mean.
- **Cited:**
  - 0415F6-0415FC: the float at 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]) is loaded into fr9 and stored to @(12,r15)
  - 04167A-041682: descriptor 0xADDE0 goes to r4 and table_lookup_1D 0xBE830 is called with fr4 = @(12,r15), so the input is the rpm value
  - 041684-041686: the result fr0 is stored to @(32,r15)
  - 041736-041748: in state r14==1/2, fr5 = [2D lookup result at @(-4,r10)] * @(28,r15) (2D table of throttle by fmac-interp of 0xFFFF67EE) * @(32,r15) (this table) * @(-20,r10)=0xFFFF8008
  - 041730-04174E: fr12 is increased by cal_percond_blend 0xD2BF4 and then float_min(fr12, fr5) is taken, so this table scales the ceiling of a rate-limited ramp
  - 041754-0417B8: in states 3 and 5, fr12 is decreased by a step and floored at 0 with float_max
  - 0417CA-0417DA: the ramp value is stored to 0xFFFF8004 (r10-24); a knock-reduced copy, fr12 - max(@0xFFFF8258 - @0xFFFF81EC, 0) floored at 0, is stored to 0xFFFF8000 (r10-28)

### 0xADE08 -- Coolant-temperature-scheduled transient negative correction (probably ignition retard), held after an rpm/state event, then ramped back to 0

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD3437, range -24.961 .. 0. Triage hint: unassigned.

While the second event counter (GBR+19) is running, this table is looked up by coolant temperature and the result is written to 0xFFFF8020. After the counter expires, the value is stepped back toward 0 by the constant at 0xD2C10 (optionally multiplied by 0xD2C14) and capped at 0 by float_min. The value is then min'ed with 0xFFFF8024 and the result stored to 0xFFFF8028, so it acts as a non-positive correction that grows with coolant temperature (0 at or below 0 C, about -25 at 70 C and above).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (input fr13 loaded from ect_current 0xFFFF6350 at 041C04 and moved to fr4 at 041CA4; axis from definition)
- **Output units:** Unknown ECU-internal unit. The cell scaling 90/256 with bias -45 is a signed degree-like encoding and the values are non-positive, so ignition timing degrees of retard are plausible but unconfirmed.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF8028 (and of 0xFFFF8020) is not in this listing. That it is ignition timing rests only on the degree-like scaling, the negative sign and an unverified 'max_retard' label at 0xD2C08. Nothing shown ties the output to spark rather than to some other correction.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF8028 (and 0xFFFF8020/0xFFFF8024) to see whether they are added into the final ignition advance or into some other path. Also check whether the gate cal at 0xD2C0C, labelled INERT, is so high that the path never runs, which would make the table dead.
- **Cited:**
  - 041CA4: fmov fr13,fr4 in the jsr delay slot of table_lookup_1D (041CA2), with descriptor 0xADE08 in r4 (041C9E). fr13 was loaded from ect_current 0xFFFF6350 at 041C02/041C04.
  - 041CA6-041CAA: the lookup result fr0 is stored to @(-20,r14) with r14=0xFFFF8034, i.e. to 0xFFFF8020.
  - 041C96-041C9A: the lookup runs only when GBR+19 is nonzero and GBR+18 is zero (041C80-041C84). If GBR+18 is nonzero, 0xFFFF8020 is forced to 0.0 instead (041C88-041C8E). So there is a two-stage counter: a delay with zero output, then a hold with the table value.
  - 041C28-041C3A: both counters are reloaded from 0xD2981 and 0xD2980 when the value at 0xFFFF7C78 is greater than rpm (041C22 fcmp/gt fr15,fr14) and the previous state byte GBR+20 equals 4. GBR+20 is refreshed from 0xFFFF7C9A at 041D10-041D12. Otherwise the counters count down (041C3C-041C5A).
  - 041CC4-041CE6 (counters expired): 0xFFFF8020 is loaded, 0xD2C10 is added (and the sum multiplied by 0xD2C14 when GBR+22==0 and flag 65C0==1). float_min is then called with fr5=0 and the result is stored back to 0xFFFF8020. This is a recovery ramp clamped at <=0, consistent with the all-negative table.
  - 041C5C-041C7E and 041CEE-041CFA: the value and both counters are zeroed unless all of these hold: engine running (check_engine_running) or byte 0xFFFF5E94 >= cal 0xD2982; flag 0xFFFF6254 nonzero; engine_speed_delta >= cal 0xD2C08; rpm >= cal 0xD2C0C.
  - 041D00-041D0E: float_min(@0xFFFF8020, @0xFFFF8024) is computed and stored to 0xFFFF8028, so the most negative of the two corrections wins.

### 0xADE1C -- Initial value of a negative (retard-style) offset, set on an event and decayed back to 0; indexed by FFFF5E64

1D x7 uint8, data 0xD3464, range -20.039 .. 0. Triage hint: unassigned.

When flag FFFF5DD8 is 1, input FFFF5E64 is at least 1.0 and an event counter is under threshold, this table sets a non-positive offset from FFFF5E64. The offset is held at FFFF803C, mirrored at FFFF8044. On later passes a second, rpm-indexed table (0xADE30) is added each call and the result is clamped at 0 by float_min, so the offset recovers to 0. The scaling (0.35156 per bit, -45 bias) is typical of ignition-timing degrees, but no consumer of the offset is in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at FFFF5E64 (unknown quantity, axis 0..100 with fine steps near 0; the table is only reached when it is >= 1.0)
- **Output units:** unknown; the scaling suggests degrees of ignition timing (non-positive, i.e. retard)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Nothing in this listing reads FFFF803C or FFFF8044, so 'timing retard' rests only on the scaling convention and the knock_metric_accum combination. The identity of FFFF5E64 is also unknown. The listing starts late: r14 is popped at 041EAE but never pushed.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of FFFF803C and FFFF8044 and see whether they feed the ignition-timing sum. Find the writers of FFFF5E64 and FFFF5DD8 to identify the X input and the enabling event. Read the byte at 0xD2983 to see whether the 0xAA knock-combined branch is active in this calibration.
- **Cited:**
  - 041E10: fmov.s @r2,fr14 loads FFFF5E64; 041E40: fmov fr14,fr4 passes it as the lookup input to table_lookup_1D at 041E3E
  - 041E1C-041E38: fldi1/fcmp/gt fr14,fr8 means the lookup runs only when FFFF5E64 >= 1.0; 041E30-041E34 also require byte FFFF5DD8 == 1
  - 041E26-041E2E: uint16 counter at FFFF8048 is compared with the constant at 0xD29CC; once the counter reaches it, the lookup is skipped and the recovery path is taken
  - 041E46: fmov.s fr0,@(4,r14) stores the result to FFFF8044
  - 041E48-041E66: if the calibration byte at 0xD2983 == 0xAA, the value becomes min(knock_metric_accum[FFFF8258] - [FFFF81EC] + table, 0) and is stored to FFFF803C; otherwise 041E6C stores the table value to FFFF803C directly
  - 041E72-041E8A: on the other path, table 0xADE30, indexed by fr4 (still rpm_current from 041E14), is added to FFFF803C, clamped by float_min against 0 (fldi0 fr5) and stored back; this is a recovery toward 0
  - 041E8E: byte FFFF8040 is set to 1 or 0 depending on the path; 041E98-041EA2: FFFF8048 increments with saturation while FFFF5E64 >= 1, otherwise it is reset to 0
  - Data: 0 down to -20.04 over X = 0..7.84, then 0 at 25 and 100. The output is never positive.

### 0xADE30 -- Per-call recovery step vs RPM for the FFFF803C correction (likely knock-related)

1D x13 uint8, data 0xD34A0, range 1.0547 .. 49.922. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looked up by engine speed when the 'active' path is not taken (FFFF5E64 < 1, or flag FFFF5DD8 != 1, or counter FFFF8048 at or above the threshold at 0xD29CC). The value is added to the float at FFFF803C, the sum is limited against 0 by float_min at 0xBE970 and stored back, so FFFF803C steps back toward 0. The step is large at 400 rpm (49.9) and small everywhere else (1.05).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, RPM, from rpm_current at FFFF6624 (loaded into fr4 at 041E14, and fr4 is untouched on every path to 041E72)
- **Output units:** Same units as the FFFF803C accumulator, per execution of this routine. The 0.35156 (=90/256) scale hints at ignition degrees, but that is not shown by the code.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What FFFF803C physically represents, and whether float_min at 0xBE970 really returns the minimum. If it actually returned the maximum, the step would push the value up from 0 rather than recover it toward 0. The 'knock' link rests only on the [TRACED] label knock_metric_accum at FFFF8258, whose name could be wrong. The degrees interpretation rests only on the scale factor.
- **Next step:** Disassemble float_min at 0xBE970 to confirm min-versus-max semantics. Then search the ROM for readers of RAM 0xFFFF803C, and of the flag byte 0xFFFF8040, to see what the clamped accumulator is added to (for example an ignition-timing sum).
- **Cited:**
  - 041E12/041E14: fr4 = @FFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]). No instruction on the paths 041E2E, 041E34 or 041E38 to 041E72 writes fr4, so the lookup input is RPM. This matches the 400..5200 breakpoints.
  - 041E72-041E76: r4=0xADE30, jsr table_lookup_1D, so the result is in fr0.
  - 041E7A-041E80: r14=0xFFFF8040, fr4 = @(-4,r14) = FFFF803C, then fadd fr0,fr4 adds the table value to the accumulator.
  - 041E82-041E8A: float_min(fr4, fr5=0.0) via fldi0 fr5, and the result is stored back to FFFF803C. The accumulator is limited against 0, so it moves toward 0 by the table step each call.
  - 041E8C/041E8E: byte FFFF8040 = 0 on this path. The other path sets it to 1 (041E70).
  - Other path, 041E3A-041E66: when fr14 (FFFF5E64) >= 1, flag FFFF5DD8 == 1 and counter FFFF8048 < @0xD29CC, table 0xADE1C(fr14) goes to FFFF8044. If the cal byte at 0xD2983 == 0xAA, FFFF803C = min(knock_metric_accum(FFFF8258) - FFFF81EC + FFFF8044, 0). So FFFF803C is seeded from a knock-metric difference and then recovered by this table.
  - 041E90-041EA2: counter FFFF8048 is incremented with saturation while fr14 >= 1 and reset to 0 otherwise.

### 0xADE58 -- Transient correction base magnitude vs peak-held input at 0xFFFF62F4 (selector=1, engine-running set). Quantity unknown; ignition-retard degrees is a hunch.

1D x8 uint8, data 0xD34F8, range -24.961 .. -15.117. Triage hint: FLKC.

Gives a negative base value from a running peak of the float at 0xFFFF62F4. This table is used when the selector at 0xFFFF6810 equals 1 and check_engine_running returned 1. The result goes to 0xFFFF8060 and is then multiplied by three other floats to form the output at 0xFFFF805C.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: peak-held (running max) value of the float at 0xFFFF62F4, stored at 0xFFFF8070. Breakpoints are 0..87.5 in 12.5 steps. The physical quantity is not identified in this listing.
- **Output units:** Unknown. The data range -25..-15 is negative, and the scale 0.35156 with bias -45 resembles a timing-degree encoding, but this is unconfirmed.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity at 0xFFFF62F4 and the meaning of the output at 0xFFFF805C are not established in this listing. The selector at 0xFFFF6810 being gear is only suggested by its six-way switch, and its name is not trusted. Whether the factors at 0xFFFF8068 and 0xFFFF806C are nonzero, which would make the table live, is not shown.
- **Next step:** Search for code that writes 0xFFFF62F4 to identify the input quantity, and for code that reads 0xFFFF805C to see where the product is applied (for example whether it is added to ignition timing). Also find the writers of 0xFFFF8068 and 0xFFFF806C to check that the multipliers are not constant zero.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x042526: fmov.s @r2,fr15 reads the float at 0xFFFF62F4, the raw input.
  - 0x0427E6-0x0427F4: float_max(fr4 = @0xFFFF8070, fr5 = fr15) is written back to 0xFFFF8070 (r12-16). This is a peak-hold, and the peak only runs while bit 2 of gbr+25 (0xFFFF8075) is set.
  - 0x042820-0x042828: descriptor 0xADE58 is passed to table_lookup_1D with fr4 = @(r12-16), the peak-held value.
  - 0x0427F8-0x042802: this table is chosen when @r15 (the check_engine_running result from 0x04251E) == 1 and r13 (the byte at 0xFFFF6810) == 1. Selector values 2-5 and a default pick the five sibling descriptors, 0xADE80-0xADF20. The alternate set 0xADE44-0xADF0C is used when the check_engine_running result is not 1.
  - 0x04282A-0x04282E: the result in fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF8060 (r12-32).
  - 0x0429A0-0x0429BA: 0xFFFF8060 * 0xFFFF8064 (-28) * 0xFFFF806C (-20) * 0xFFFF8068 (-24) is stored to 0xFFFF805C (-36). This path runs only when word gbr+36 (0xFFFF8080) has bit 0x4000 set and 0x1000 and 0x2000 are clear. If 0x1000 is set, the constant at 0xD2C90 is used instead. Otherwise the output is 0.
  - 0x04259E-0x0425B0 and siblings: 0xFFFF8064 comes from per-selector float arrays (0xD3DA0..0xD3FC0) indexed by the step counter gbr+24 (0xFFFF8074), which counts up to 3. The arrays are chosen by rpm vs the threshold at 0xD2C94 and by the same selector.

### 0xADE80 -- Selector-2 (running-branch) 1D factor vs peak-held 0xFFFF62F4, feeding 4-way product at 0xFFFF805C

1D x8 uint8, data 0xD3548, range -24.961 .. -15.117. Triage hint: FLKC.

Looks up a negative value (-25 at x=0, flat -15.1 from x=12.5 up) from a running maximum of the RAM value at 0xFFFF62F4. The result goes to 0xFFFF8060, which is later multiplied by three other factors into 0xFFFF805C; the physical meaning of that product is not shown here.

- **Output units:** unknown. Plausibly degrees of timing given the negative -25..-15 range and the 0.35156 step, but this is not shown by the code.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Neither the input quantity at 0xFFFF62F4 nor any consumer of 0xFFFF805C appears in this listing, so the units and meaning of the product (and hence of this factor) are unknown. Whether the 1..6 selector is gear is also unverified.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF805C (and 0xFFFF8060) to see what the product is added to or compared against, and find the writer of 0xFFFF62F4 to identify the axis input. Also read the float constant at 0xD2C90, the substitute value when bit 0x1000 is set, and compare it with the data of the sibling tables.
- **Cited:**
  - 042524/042526: fr15 = @0xFFFF62F4 (unnamed RAM).
  - 0427E6-0427F4: float_max(fr4=@0xFFFF8070, fr5=fr15) is stored back to 0xFFFF8070. This is a peak hold of 0xFFFF62F4.
  - 042964-04296A: 0xFFFF8070 is cleared to 0 when gbr+25 bit1 is clear.

Five sibling descriptors (ADE58, ADEA8, ADED0, ADEF8, ADF20) cover selector 1, 3, 4, 5 and default, and a second set of six covers the other running-flag branch.
  - 042830-04283E: table_lookup_1D(desc 0xADE80, fr4=@0xFFFF8070) -> fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF8060 (r12-32).
  - 0429A0-0429BA: 0xFFFF805C = @8060 * @8064 * @806C * @8068. 0xFFFF8064 comes from float arrays 0xD3DA0.. indexed by the counter at gbr+24 (0..3), also split by selector and rpm vs threshold 0xD2C94.
  - 042972-0429C0: the product is written only if gbr+36 bit 0x1000 is clear (otherwise the constant @0xD2C90 is written), bit 0x2000 is clear (otherwise 0), and bit 0x4000 is set.

### 0xADF34 -- Coolant-temperature weighting factor written to FFFF806C, used when 0x22CF4 does not return 1 (identity unknown)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD3690, range 0.29688 .. 1. Triage hint: FLKC.

Interpolates a unitless factor from current coolant temperature: about 0.30 at or below 40 C, rising to 1.0 at 80 C and above. The result is stored as a float to RAM 0xFFFF806C. This table is chosen when the routine at 0x22CF4 returns anything other than 1; when it returns 1, the sister table 0xADF48 is used instead.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (-40..110), input read from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED])
- **Output units:** dimensionless factor (0.297..1.0), float stored to 0xFFFF806C
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF806C is not in this listing, so what the factor scales (knock learning, ignition, or something else) is unknown. The meaning of the 0x22CF4 selector rests only on its label. The FLKC subsystem hint is RAM-name triage, not evidence.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF806C (mov.l literal 0xFFFF806C) and see what the float multiplies or compares against. Also decode the alternate table 0xADF48 and disassemble 0x22CF4 to learn what condition its return value of 1 represents.
- **Cited:**
  - 0429D4/0429D6: jsr to 0x22CF4 (labelled check_engine_running, name unverified); 0429DA extu.b r0 and 0429DE cmp/eq #1,r0 test its result
  - 0429E0: r4 = 0xADF34 (this descriptor) is loaded before the branch. 0429E2 bf/s 0x0429E8 is taken when r0 != 1, so this table is used in that case
  - 0429E6: on fall-through (r0 == 1) r4 is overwritten with 0xADF48, the alternate table
  - 0429DC/0429E4: fr4 = float at 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current), loaded in the delay slot, so it is the lookup input on both paths
  - 0429E8/0429EA: jsr table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830
  - 0429EE/0429F4: fr0 (the lookup result) is stored to 0xFFFF806C in the rts delay slot; no clamp or multiply happens in this function

### 0xAE0D8 -- ECT-dependent upper limit on afc_axis_val_A (FFFF78B0) that gates the increment branch of the FFFF8004 accumulator (likely a cold/warm-up enable threshold)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD3B0A, range 0 .. 70000. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Indexed by gated coolant temperature, it gives a ceiling that is cached at FFFF8014 and compared against the float at FFFF78B0. Only while FFFF78B0 is below this ceiling, and several other conditions hold, does the routine pick states 1, 2 or 4, which raise the accumulator kept at FFFF8004. Otherwise it picks state 3 or 5, which lower it toward 0. The ceiling falls from 70000 when cold to 0 from 70 °C up, so this path is disabled when the engine is warm.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature in °C (ect_gated_c, FFFF6354, loaded into fr14 and then fr4 at 041636), breakpoints -40 to 110
- **Output units:** Same units as the float at FFFF78B0 (afc_axis_val_A). Its quantity is not established; the magnitude of up to 70000 hints at a cumulative count such as intake air mass or time ticks since start, but that is unproven.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What 0xFFFF78B0 (afc_axis_val_A) physically represents is not shown in this listing, so the units and the purpose of the threshold are unconfirmed. The role of the comparison itself is observed directly.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of 0xFFFF78B0 to fix its quantity, for example a cumulative air mass or a time-since-start counter. Also search for readers of 0xFFFF8004 and 0xFFFF8000 to learn what the gated accumulator drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 041636: fmov fr14,fr4 with fr14 loaded at 0415DA from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c), so the lookup input is coolant temperature.
  - 041630-041634: the descriptor 0xAE0D8 is passed to table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830.
  - 041638-04163A: the result fr0 is stored to @(-8,r10). r10 is 0xFFFF801C, so the store goes to 0xFFFF8014.
  - 04168C-041692: fr8 is reloaded from @(-8,r10) and fcmp/gt fr15,fr8 is executed. fr15 holds 0xFFFF78B0, loaded at 0415D4-0415D6. bf 0x0416BE skips to the decrement-state selection unless table value > FFFF78B0.
  - 041694-04169C: the second condition is word FFFF67EE (r13) < the AE0EC result held at gbr+28. 0416A0-0416A6: vehicle speed must be >= the constant at D2BF0. 0416A8: fuel_system_state must be 0.
  - 0416AC-0416B8: when all of these pass, r14 is 1, 2 or 4. 0416BE-0416C4: otherwise r14 is 5 or 3.
  - States 1, 2 and 4 (041710-0417A8) add a constant to fr12 and cap it with float_min against 2D-table products. States 3 and 5 (041754, 0417AA-0417B8) subtract a constant and floor at 0.
  - 0417CC: fr12 is stored to @(-24,r10), which is 0xFFFF8004. 0417C2-0417DA: (FFFF8258 - FFFF81EC) is subtracted and the result, floored at 0, is stored to 0xFFFF8000.
  - The values reach 0 at 70 °C and above, so the comparison can never be true when warm because it would need FFFF78B0 < 0.

### 0xAE0EC -- ECT-dependent counter limit for the AFC/AFL enrichment ramp-up branch (raw uint16 compared to counter at 0xFFFF67EE)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD3B2A, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Indexed by gated coolant temperature. It returns a raw uint16 limit: 176 counts at -40..-20 C, falling to 78 at 40..60 C and 0 from 70 C up. The limit goes to GBR+28 (0xFFFF801C). The ramp-up states 1, 2 and 4 are chosen only while the uint16 counter at 0xFFFF67EE is below this limit. Otherwise states 3 or 5 decrement fr12 toward zero.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: ect_gated_c (0xFFFF6354), coolant temperature in C, -40..110
- **Output units:** Raw uint16 counts in the same units as the counter at 0xFFFF67EE, probably a time or event count. The descriptor's scale and bias are denormal garbage, so treat the cells as unscaled integers (176,176,176,146,127,112,107,98,78,78,78,0,0,0,0,0).
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Two things are unconfirmed. First, what the counter at 0xFFFF67EE actually counts (time since start? warm-up time?) and in what units. Second, whether reading the cells as raw integers is right, given the nonsensical scale and bias fields.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF67EE to fix the counter's meaning and tick rate, and read the float constant at 0x041854 used in fmac_interp_uint16. Also search for readers of 0xFFFF801C outside this function.
- **Cited:**
  - 0415D8-0415DA: fr14 = @0xFFFF6354 ect_gated_c; 041642 fmov fr14,fr4 passes it as the lookup input
  - 04163C-041648: table_desc_1d_uint16 on 0xAE0EC; the result r0 is stored by mov.w r0,@(28,gbr) to 0xFFFF801C
  - 0415FE/041604: r13 = mov.w @0xFFFF67EE, a uint16 counter
  - 041694-04169C: r5 = extu.w @(28,gbr), the table value; r2 = extu.w r13; cmp/ge r5,r2; bt 0x0416BE. When counter >= limit, the code skips to states 3/5
  - 0416A0-0416BC: the same branch also needs afc_axis_val_A < the 1D(ECT) result at 0xFFFF8014 (fr15 vs fr8 at 04168E-041692), vehicle speed >= cal 0xD2BF0, and fuel_system_state == 0; it then sets state r14 = 1, 2 or 4
  - 041710-041752: states 1/2 ramp fr12 up by cal_percond_blend, capped by float_min at a product of 2D/1D table values
  - 041754-0417B8: state 3 subtracts cal 0xD2BF8 from fr12 (state 5 subtracts 0xD2C00), floored at 0
  - 04165E-041668: r13 is also converted to float via fmac_interp_uint16 and used as the Y input of 2D table 0xAE4DC, so it is a real scaled quantity, likely elapsed time

### 0xAE10C -- RPM-dependent slope (gain) applied to RAM 0xFFFF810C to form a 0..1 knock-path factor; identity of the factor unknown

1D x7 uint16, data 0xD3B94, range 0.05 .. 0.08299. Triage hint: knock.

The table is looked up by rpm_current. Its output is multiplied by the variable at 0xFFFF810C and divided by the ROM constant at 0xD2D80. The result is clamped between the AE0F8 rpm lookup and 1.0, multiplied by the post_A and post_B lookups, capped with float_min against 0xD2D7C, and stored to 0xFFFF8100 under an interrupt lock. The table is used only when 0xFFFF810C is at or above the threshold at 0xD2D78; below it, the factor is just AE0F8*A*B clamped to 0..1.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm. Input fr4 is fr13, loaded from 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]) at 043D82/043D84 and passed at 043DF4.
- **Output units:** Gain per unit of the 0xFFFF810C variable, scaled by 1/const(0xD2D80). The product is a dimensionless factor clamped to [AE0F8 value, 1.0].
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of 0xFFFF810C (fr12) is unknown, and the constants at 0xD2D78 and 0xD2D80 are not decoded. Without them, the 'knock' association rests on GBR and RAM-name hints and on neighbouring [TRACED] descriptor names, which this project treats as weak. The table is known to be an rpm-scheduled slope that ramps a factor toward 1.0, but not what that factor controls.
- **Next step:** Decode the float constants at 0xD2D78 (enable threshold), 0xD2D80 (divisor) and 0xD2D7C (final cap) to see whether table*fr12/const ever falls inside the clamp. Then search for writers of 0xFFFF810C to learn what fr12 is, and for readers of 0xFFFF8100 to learn what the factor drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 043D82-043D84 / 043DF4: fr13 = @0xFFFF6624 (rpm) is passed as the lookup input to the 0xAE10C descriptor via table_lookup_1D (jsr @r14).
  - 043D8A-043D92: fr12 = @(0xFFFF8128-28) = 0xFFFF810C.
  - 043DE8-043DEE: if const@0xD2D78 > fr12, the code branches to 043E2C and skips this table. The table is only active when 0xFFFF810C >= threshold.
  - 043DF6-043DF8: the result is stored to 0xFFFF81D8-20 = 0xFFFF81C4.
  - 043DFA-043E04: the result is multiplied by fr12 (fmul fr12,fr4), then float_safe_div by const@0xD2D80.
  - 043E08-043E10: float_clamp_range with lower bound = AE0F8 lookup result (@0xFFFF81C0, fr5) and upper = 1.0 (fldi1 fr6).
  - 043E12-043E1C: the clamped value is multiplied by the desc_knock_post_A result (@0xFFFF81C8) and the desc_knock_post_B result (@0xFFFF81CC).
  - 043E20-043E26: float_min against const@0xD2D7C.
  - 043E44 / 043E56: the final value goes to fr14 and is stored to 0xFFFF8128-40 = 0xFFFF8100 inside an irq_level_set/restore section.

### 0xAE290 -- Knock detection: RPM-dependent bound (likely the lower limit) on the per-cylinder adaptive knock reference level

1D x10 float32, data 0xD4358, range 8 .. 13. Triage hint: knock.

A 1D lookup on RPM (fr15 = rpm_current). The result is passed in fr5 to float_clamp_range, which clamps the freshly updated per-cylinder adaptive reference at 0xFFFF816C+cyl*4. The other bound, in fr6, is the per-cylinder RPM baseline at 0xFFFF815C+cyl*4 plus the constant at 0xD2D84. The clamped result feeds the knock threshold and margin arithmetic further down.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** engine speed (rpm), from fr15 = rpm_current 0xFFFF6624; breakpoints 800..8000
- **Output units:** same units as the knock-signal reference level held at 0xFFFF816C[cyl], ECU-internal and not scaled to a physical unit here
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The argument order of float_clamp_range (0xBE56C) is not shown, so whether fr5 is the minimum or the maximum is assumed. The physical meaning and units of the per-cylinder reference at 0xFFFF816C are also only inferred.
- **Next step:** Disassemble float_clamp_range at 0xBE56C to see whether fr5 or fr6 is the minimum. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF816C..0xFFFF8178 and 0xFFFF818C (per-cylinder) and check whether the 8..13 values ever bind against 0xFFFF815C + const@0xD2D84 (read the float at 0xD2D84 and the tables pointed to by 0x63E48).
- **Cited:**
  - 043946: fmov fr15,fr4 in the jsr delay slot. fr15 was loaded at 04379E from rpm_current 0xFFFF6624, so the X input is RPM.
  - 04396A: fmov fr0,fr5 in the delay slot of bf/s. The table result goes to fr5 on both paths, because 0x0439E8 and 0x0439FC do not touch fr5.
  - 04393A-043940: fr14 = [0xFFFF815C + cyl*4] + const@0xD2D84. That per-cylinder value was just set at 04392E from an RPM 1D lookup of the table pointer at 0x63E48[cyl].
  - 0439F2-0439FC: fr4 = [0xFFFF816C + cyl*4] + step([r9-20]) * gain([r9-88]). On one branch (04398E-0439A0) the step is negated and the gain doubled, i.e. an adaptive up/down update.
  - 043A00/043A02: jsr float_clamp_range with fr6 = fr14, so the update is clamped between this table (fr5) and baseline+const (fr6).
  - 043A0E: the clamped result is stored back to 0xFFFF816C[cyl].
  - 043A76-043A98: 2*(0xFFFF816C[cyl] - 0xFFFF815C[cyl]) goes to [r9-12]. 043AB4-043B32: that feeds the threshold computations stored to @r9 (0xFFFF8158) and to 0xFFFF818C[cyl].

### 0xAE2D8 -- IAM-dependent threshold for counting fine-learning (FLKC) cells, used to set a flag at 0xFFFF829F

1D x8 float32, data 0xD45F8, range 0.25 .. 3.75. Triage hint: FLKC.

Maps the current ignition advance multiplier (ram_IAM) to a float threshold. The routine walks 35 cells and counts each eligible cell (flag byte == 1) whose flkc_grid float is at least the threshold. When the count reaches the calibration byte at 0xD29A8, it raises a flag at 0xFFFF829F, probably as a trigger for IAM re-evaluation. The threshold is high (3.75) at low IAM and low (0.25) near IAM 1.0.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: ram_IAM (0xFFFF3234), the ignition advance multiplier, 0..1 with breakpoints at 1/16 steps
- **Output units:** Same units as the flkc_grid floats, probably degrees of learned timing. The unit is unverified: it rests only on the grid's label.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of the flkc_grid floats (0xFFFF3248, stride 8) and their units come only from a label. Also, what the 0xFFFF829F flag drives is not in this listing, so its role as an IAM-adjust trigger is an assumption.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF829F (and of 0xFFFF8299 / 0xFFFF82A4) to see whether the flag drives IAM step or reset logic. Also find the writer of 0xFFFF3248 to confirm the grid holds fine-learning knock correction in degrees, and read the threshold byte at 0xD29A8.
- **Cited:**

### 0xAE300 -- Low-RPM (cranking/start-transition) substitute value, load x RPM, likely ignition timing in degrees

2D 3x6 uint8, data 0xD4674, range 4.9609 .. 11.992. Triage hint: unassigned.

When flag byte 0xFFFF8BA1 equals 1 and the low-RPM hysteresis latch (GBR+84, i.e. 0xFFFF7F64) is set, this table is read with engine load and RPM. Its result replaces the normally computed value in slot 0xFFFF7F1C. That slot is then added to a blend term at 0xFFFF7F3C to form 0xFFFF7F18 (or is first limited against another path). The data is flat across load: about 5 at 400 and 500 rpm, rising to 12 at 600 rpm.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (6 breakpoints, 0.19..0.79): engine load in g/rev. fr4 is loaded from @r15, which holds engine_load_current stored at 0x03FCD6. The pairing of fr4 with the Y axis comes from the axis magnitudes, not from the lookup code.; X (3 breakpoints, 400..600): engine speed in rpm. fr5 = fr14 = rpm_current.
- **Output units:** Probably degrees of ignition advance. This is inferred from the 0.35156/-20 scaling, which is the usual timing scale, and from the value standing in for the base-timing-derived slot. It is not proven.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity in slot 0xFFFF7F1C is not fixed by this listing. Calling it ignition timing rests on the scale/bias convention and on unverified labels of the competing tables. The meaning of the gate flag 0xFFFF8BA1 is unknown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7F18 and 0xFFFF7F1C to see whether they feed the final spark-advance output. Also search for writers of 0xFFFF8BA1 to learn when the gate is true; if it is never set, the table is inert.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x03FEAA-0x03FEB2: descriptor 0xAE300 is passed to table_lookup_2D with fr5=fr14 (rpm_current, loaded at 0x03FCC4) and fr4=@r15 (engine_load_current, stored at 0x03FCD6).
  - 0x03FE9E-0x03FEA8: the lookup runs only if byte 0xFFFF8BA1 (r12) == 1 and GBR byte 84 == 1.
  - 0x03FDE4-0x03FDF0 and 0x03FE80-0x03FE8A: GBR byte 84 is set to 1 when rpm < cal at 0xD2AF0. It is cleared when rpm >= cal at 0xD2AEC. This is a low-RPM hysteresis latch, which fits the 400-600 rpm axis.
  - 0x03FEB4 -> 0x03FEE6: the result fr0 is stored to @(-72,r8) = 0xFFFF7F1C. The else path (0x03FEB8-0x03FEE4) stores min(max(slot -64, slot -68) or slot -64, slot -48) to the same slot. Slot -64 and slot -68 come from the 1D tables at 0xADB38 (RPM axis) and 0xADB9C (ECT axis).
  - 0x03FFAA-0x03FFB6: slot -72 is added to slot -44 (the blend/ramp term) and stored to @(-76,r8) = 0xFFFF7F18. At 0x03FF86-0x03FF96 it instead feeds min(slot -72 minus slot -80, slot -16).

### 0xAE3A8 -- Idle speed feedback ignition correction (rpm error vs target idle rpm), table A (selector byte != 2)

2D 3x9 uint8, data 0xD4E38, range -4.5312 .. 5.9375. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Below target it adds up to about +6, above target it removes up to about -4.5, with zero error giving zero. The result goes to 0xFFFF7F74 (7FAC-56); the function writes 0 there when the idle conditions are not met.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The output units and destination are not in the listing: no reader of 0xFFFF7F74/0xFFFF7F94 is shown, so degrees of timing is assumed. A second assumption is that 0xFFFF895C is target idle rpm; this rests on the 700-1500 range and the rpm subtraction, not on a traced writer.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7F74 (and 0xFFFF7F94), e.g. GBR-relative @(0,gbr) loads with gbr=0xFFFF7F74 or literal 0xFFFF7F74/0xFFFF7FAC. Confirm it is added into the ignition advance sum. Also find the writer of 0xFFFF895C to confirm it is target idle rpm, and decode 0x21016 to see what selector value 2 means.
- **Cited:**
  - 0405C6-0405DA: rpm_current (fr12 from 0xFFFF6624) minus @r15 (A, from 0xFFFF895C) is negated and stored to @(-48,0xFFFF7FAC), so 0xFFFF7F7C holds A - rpm.
  - 040822-04082A: fr5 = fr6 = @r15 = A (loaded at 040776); fr4 = @(-48,r13) = A - rpm; then jsr table_lookup_2D with r4 = 0xAE3A8.
  - 04081A/04081C/04081E: r4 = 0xAE3A8 unless the byte returned by 0x21016 (saved at @(8,r15)) equals 2, in which case 0xAE3C4 is used instead.
  - 04082C-040836: the result is stored to 0xFFFF7F94 (-24) and to 0xFFFF7F74 (-56 from 7FAC, equal to the GBR base).
  - 040886-04088C: the same output slot is set to 0.0 when any gating condition fails. The gates are: rpm-band flag gbr+49, rpm <= const 0xD2B28, counter 0xFFFF67EC > 0xD29B4, flag 0xFFFF6254 == 1, gbr+12 flag, low vehicle-speed flag gbr+48, and unchanged state bytes. This pattern fits idle feedback.
  - Data: monotonic in rpm error, odd-symmetric about 0, and gain grows with target rpm (X=1500 row is the largest). This is the shape of a proportional idle-speed timing controller.
  - Sibling branches: 0x40864 uses the 2D tables 0xAE418/0xAE434 with fr4 = 0xFFFF6630 - rpm. 0x40838 uses 0xAE3E0/0xAE3FC on input 0xFFFF663C and multiplies by a 1D lookup (0xADC28) indexed by timing_final_advance.

### 0xAE450 -- Ceiling for a ramped correction, coolant x engine load, mode 1 (alternate-flag-clear variant); possibly a timing add-on

2D 2x16 uint8, data 0xD5060, range 0 .. 4.9219. Triage hint: knock.

In mode 1 this table is looked up by coolant temperature and engine load. The result, multiplied by three other factors, caps a value that ramps up by a fixed step each pass. The capped value, minus any knock accumulation above a threshold and floored at 0, is written to 0xFFFF8000. Because the value is near 4.9 below about 60 °C and at loads up to 1.1 g/rev, and 0 at 1.5 g/rev and above, the ceiling only applies when the engine is cold and lightly loaded.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y: coolant temperature, degC (from definition; the lookup also takes ect_current 0xFFFF6350 as fr4 at 041722); X: engine load, g/rev (engine_load_current 0xFFFF63F8, saved at sp+8 and passed as fr5 at 04172A)
- **Output units:** Unknown. The 0.3515625 per-count scale is common for ignition-degree tables, so possibly degrees of timing advance, but that rests on the scale alone. Inside this function the output is used as a ceiling that is later reduced by knock accumulation.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity (timing degrees) is inferred from the per-count scale alone. The reader of 0xFFFF8000 / 0xFFFF8004 is not in the listing, so nothing shown fixes what the ramped value actually adjusts. The label knock_metric_accum is unverified as to meaning.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF8000 and 0xFFFF8004 (the final and ramped outputs) to see whether they are added to ignition timing or to something else. Also check the value of 0xFFFF8008 and the ADDE0 rpm and AE488 tables to confirm the product is not forced to zero.
- **Cited:**
  - 0416B8: r14=1 when every gate passes: @(r10-8) > afc_axis_val_A, r13 < the uint16 ECT limit, speed >= cal_percond_scale, fuel_system_state==0, check_afl_ready==0, peripheral_status==0. 0416F2 then dispatches to 041710.

  - 041722/04172A: fr4=@sp (ect_current), fr5=@(8,sp) (engine_load_current); table_lookup_2D at 0xBE8E4.
  - 04172E: the result is stored to @(r10-4) = 0xFFFF8018.
  - 041734: fr12 (previous value read from 0xFFFF8004 at 04162E) += cal_percond_blend (0xD2BF4), i.e. a ramp step.
  - 04173C-041748: fr5 = table * throttle/0xFFFF67EE 2D result (sp+28) * rpm 1D table ADDE0 (sp+32) * @0xFFFF8008.
  - 04174C: float_min(fr12, fr5), so the table product is the ceiling on the ramp.
  - 0417AE-0417B4: modes 3 and 5 instead decrement fr12 by a step and floor it at 0.
  - 0417CC: the ramped value is stored to 0xFFFF8004.
  - 0417BA-0417DA: 0xFFFF8000 = max(fr12 - max(knock_metric_accum - @0xFFFF81EC, 0), 0). The value is cut back by knock.

### 0xAE46C -- Ceiling for a ramped coolant-temperature and load compensation, mode 1 alternate variant (likely ignition degrees)

2D 6x16 uint8, data 0xD5098, range 0 .. 20.039. Triage hint: knock.

In state 1 it gives a ceiling from coolant temperature and engine load. That ceiling is multiplied by three other factors, and a ramped accumulator climbs toward it by a fixed step each call. The ramp result, minus a knock-derived amount and floored at 0, is stored to RAM. This table is chosen instead of 0xAE450 when the byte returned by sub 0x299F0 is nonzero.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y: coolant temperature, degC (ect_current, 0xFFFF6350; axis shared with a definition); X: engine load, g/rev (engine_load_current, 0xFFFF63F8, passed in fr5; breakpoints 0.27..1.5 fit g/rev)
- **Output units:** Unknown physical unit. The 0.3515625 (90/256) uint8 scale is the usual Subaru scale for ignition timing degrees, which suggests degrees, but that is not proven.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of the final quantity is unproven. Its readers (0xFFFF8000, 0xFFFF8004) are not in this listing. The degrees reading rests only on the 90/256 scale, and the sign (advance or retard) is unknown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF8000 and 0xFFFF8004 to see whether they are added to ignition timing or to fueling. Then decode sub 0x299F0, which selects this table over 0xAE450. Also find the writer of the multiplier at 0xFFFF8008: if it is always 0, the table is inert.
- **Cited:**
  - 0415E0-0415E4: ect_current is saved to @(0,r15). 0415EE-0415F4: engine_load_current is saved to @(8,r15).
  - 041722/041724/04172A: fr4 = @r15 (ECT), fr5 = @(8,r15) (load). A jsr to table_lookup_2D at 0x0BE8E4 then runs with r4 = 0xAE46C, loaded at 041716.
  - 041710-041718: the descriptor is used only when r14 == 1 and byte @(20,r15) is nonzero. That byte is the return of jsr 0x299F0 at 041618/041620. Otherwise 0xAE450 is used.
  - 0416A0-0416B8: mode r14 = 1 requires all of the following: @(0xFFFF8014) > afc_axis_val_A; r13 (from 0xFFFF67EE) < the ECT uint16 table result; vehicle_speed >= cal 0xD2BF0; fuel_system_state == 0; check_afl_ready result == 0; peripheral_status == 0.
  - 04172C/04172E: the result is stored to 0xFFFF8018 (r10-4).
  - 041736-041748: the result is multiplied by @(28,r15), by @(32,r15) and by @(0xFFFF8008). @(28,r15) is the 2D table 0xAE488 indexed by throttle angle and an interpolated 0xFFFF67EE value. @(32,r15) is the 1D rpm table 0xADDE0.
  - 041730-041734 and 04174A-041752: fr12 (the previous value from 0xFFFF8004) + cal_percond_blend 0xD2BF4 is passed to float_min against that product. This is a step-limited ramp toward the table-derived ceiling.
  - 0417BA-0417DA: fr12 is stored to 0xFFFF8004. Then max(knock_metric_accum - @0xFFFF81EC, 0) is computed, fr12 minus that amount is floored at 0, and the result is stored to 0xFFFF8000.
  - The data is nonzero only at coolant <= 60 C and load < 1.5, peaking at 20-50 C. That pattern looks like a warm-up or cold-condition authority limit.

### 0xAE488 -- Throttle-angle weighting factor (0..1) on the ramp ceiling for states 1/2 of the 0xFFFF8004 ramp; the meaning of the ramped quantity is unknown

2D 2x8 uint8, data 0xD5120, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Gives a 0..1 factor, looked up from throttle plate angle and a scaled uint16 from 0xFFFF67EE. In states 1 and 2 it scales a 2D base value, an RPM 1D factor and a RAM factor to form the ceiling a ramped value (fr12, stored at 0xFFFF8004) may climb to. The factor is 0 at closed throttle, 1 from about 6 to 22, then tapers to about 0.2 above 30.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Which lookup input maps to which axis is inferred only from the breakpoint ranges (throttle 0..75 fits Y). The physical meaning of the ramped quantity in 0xFFFF8004/0xFFFF8000, and of the 0xFFFF67EE input, is not established by this listing.

- **Cited:**
  - 0x04166A-0x041674: descriptor 0xAE488 is passed to table_lookup_2D with fr4=@(4,r15) and fr5=fr0. @(4,r15) holds throttle_plate_angle (stored at 0x0415E6-0x0415EC). fr0 comes from fmac_interp_uint16 at 0x04165C on r13=mov.w @0xFFFF67EE, with fr4 = float at 0x041854 (about 1.024) and fr5 = 0.
  - 0x041676-0x041678: the result is stored to stack slot @(28,r15).
  - 0x041738-0x04173C: in states r14==1/2, @(28,r15) is loaded and fmul'd into fr5 = 2D table (0xAE46C/0xAE450/0xAE4A4, inputs ect_current and engine_load). 0x04173E-0x041748: fr5 is further multiplied by the RPM 1D result @(32,r15) and by RAM @(0xFFFF801C-20)=0xFFFF8008.
  - 0x041730-0x041752: fr12 (the previous value from 0xFFFF8004) is increased by cal_percond_blend (0xD2BF4), then float_min(fr12, fr5). The product is therefore a ceiling on a ramping value, and this table scales that ceiling.
  - 0x041754/0x0417AA-0x0417B8: in states 3/5, fr12 is decremented by a calibration step and floored at 0.

### 0xAE7D8 -- Step-gain (filter coefficient) for rate-limited tracking of state field [0xFFFF89E8-76] toward [0xFFFF89E8-80], vs (u16 at 0xFFFF89E8 × 8)

1D x20 uint8, data 0xD6938, range 0.09375 .. 1. Triage hint: knock.

Returns a 0.09..1 fraction that scales a clamped error before it is added to a tracked float. It is a per-pass step gain, so the state follows its target fully at low X and more and more slowly as X rises. The physical quantity being tracked and the meaning of the X input are not established by this listing.

- **Output units:** dimensionless gain/fraction (0.09375..1.0), multiplier on a per-pass correction step
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of the u16 at 0xFFFF89E8, which is scaled by 8 to make X, and of the tracked float pair at offsets −76/−80. Nothing in this listing shows who writes them, so the physical quantity, including any link to knock, is unestablished. The 'knock_sensor_state_B' label is not trusted.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of the u16 at 0xFFFF89E8 to identify the X input (possibly a counter or a time/angle quantity). Search for writers of float 0xFFFF899C (offset −76 from 0xFFFF89E8) and readers of float 0xFFFF8998 (offset −80) to learn what is being tracked. Also decode the floats at 0xD6584 (step clamp) and the u16 at 0xD6218 (mode match value).
- **Cited:**
  - 051F10-051F28: mov.w @r1,r5 with r1=0xFFFF89E8; extu.w; float; fmul by the float at 0x05204C (0x4100 0000 = 8.0). The result in fr14 is the lookup input (fmov fr14,fr4 at 051FBE).
  - 051F98-051F9E: the old value of [0xFFFF89E8-76] is loaded into fr8 and saved to @(8,r15).
  - 051FA0-051FB6: fr4 = [0xFFFF89E8-80] - [0xFFFF89E8-76]. float_clamp_range clamps it to ±(float at 0xD6584), and the result is stored to [0xFFFF89E8-12].
  - 051FB8-051FC4: table_lookup_1D on descriptor 0xAE7D8; the result fr0 is stored to [0xFFFF89E8-16].
  - 051FC6-051FD8: fmac computes fr8 = table * clamped_error + old, which is stored back to [0xFFFF89E8-76]. This is a first-order, rate-limited tracking update with the table as its gain.
  - 051F86-051F94 and 051FDA-051FE0: the update runs only when the u16 at 0xFFFF8912 equals the calibration u16 at 0xD6218 and the byte at 0xFFFF8910 == 1. When the 0xD6218 match holds but the byte is not 1, [−76] is zeroed. When the match fails, the code branches to 051FE2 and [−76] is left unchanged.

### 0xAE814 -- Negative-side bound factor (0..1) for a latched, decaying correction at FFFF8BF4, vs FFFF62F8

1D x10 uint8, data 0xD6A0C, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: ignition timing.

When the latched value at FFFF8BF8 is negative, this table's 0..1 output, looked up on the float at FFFF62F8, is multiplied by that value and stored as the lower clamp bound at FFFF8C00. The decaying correction at FFFF8BF4 is then clamped between that bound and 0. The factor falls from 1 at x=0 to 0.5 at x=10 and to 0 from x=20 upward, so the negative correction is allowed less depth as x rises and none at x>=20.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier, 0..1
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the X input FFFF62F8 and of the corrected quantity at FFFF8BF4 are not established in this listing. The ignition-timing subsystem tag comes from RAM-name hints only.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of FFFF62F8 to identify the X quantity, which may be a timer or counter since the event. Find the readers of FFFF8BF4 to see where the decaying correction is applied, for example whether it is added to an ignition timing or fuel path. Decode sibling table AE800 (the positive-side factor) for comparison.
- **Cited:**
  - 0553D0: fmov.s @r2,fr4 with r2=FFFF62F8. fr4 is not touched before jsr table_lookup_1D at 055410, so FFFF62F8 is the X input.

  - 055416-055424: the result is stored to FFFF8C04, multiplied by FFFF8BF8 (fmul) and the product stored to FFFF8C00 (r13-16).
  - 05549A-05549E: in the branch taken when FFFF8BF4 <= 0, float_clamp_range is called with fr4=FFFF8BF4+lookup(AE894, engine_speed_delta), fr5=@(r13-16)=FFFF8C00 and fr6=0. The table's product is therefore the lower clamp bound of a correction that decays toward 0.
  - 055430-055450: FFFF8BF4 is re-latched as clamp(FFFF8BF4 + FFFF8C0C) when byte FFFF8910 is 0 and the stored previous state byte at FFFF8C10 is 1, i.e. on a state transition. FFFF8BF8 is set equal to it. 0554A6 stores sensor_struct_8998 into FFFF8C0C for the next pass.

### 0xAEC4C -- Idle-control base airflow (feed-forward) vs. an rpm-like input, possibly target idle speed (candidate)

1D x6 uint16, data 0xD7138, range 2.5604 .. 9.0515. Triage hint: knock.

Looks up a value from an unknown input held at 0xFFFF89B0 and stores it to 0xFFFF89BC. ### 0xAEE80 -- Decay step (per call) for a positive latched offset at FFFF8BF4, vs the value at FFFF6634 (labelled engine_speed_delta)

1D x12 uint16, data 0xD75E8, range 0 .. 1.0002. Triage hint: ignition timing.

While the accumulator at FFFF8BF4 is positive, this table gives an amount subtracted from it on every call, indexed by FFFF6634. The result is clamped to [0, FFFF8BFC], so the offset decays toward zero, barely at all when X is zero or negative and fast (about 1.0 per call) at X = +100. - **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** What FFFF8BF4 physically represents and whether FFFF6634 really is an engine-speed rate. The mechanism (a decay step on a latched offset) is observed. The quantity, and any link to ignition timing, rests only on labels and triage hints. Also, the listing starts after the r13/r14 pushes, so the function entry is slightly earlier than shown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF8BF4 (and its copy FFFF8BF8) to see which output this decaying offset feeds, e.g. whether it is added to a timing, fuel or throttle value. Also find the writers of FFFF6634 and FFFF8998 to confirm the X-axis quantity and the units of the captured offset.

### 0xAEEE4 -- RPM-dependent floor for a decaying correction held at FFFF8C88 (identity of the correction unknown; possibly a post-start or idle ignition-related offset)

1D x7 uint16, data 0xD76C4, range 0 .. 9.3994. Triage hint: ignition timing.

The value is preset to the constant at D67A0 when flag FFFF8CA2 rises, then stepped down on counter-timed intervals (step D67A4 or D67A8, chosen by a MAF-voltage hysteresis flag), and is never allowed below the table value (or below D67AC in one branch).

- **Weakest link:** What FFFF8C88 feeds is not in this listing, so the physical quantity (degrees of timing, fuel factor, etc.) is unproven. The 'ignition timing' subsystem label is only a triage hint.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of RAM FFFF8C88 (and of flag FFFF8CA2's writer) to see what the decayed value is added to or multiplied into. Also read the constants at D67A0, D67A4, D67A8, D67AC and D6264/D6266 to get the initial value, step sizes and periods.

  - 055D8A-055DA2: result = float_max(table(rpm), @D67AC), stored at FFFF8C8C-4 = FFFF8C88

### 0xAEFA8 -- Low-RPM upper cap on an ignition-system correction (FFFF8998 output), engine-running variant, vs RPM

1D x10 float32, data 0xD79C0, range 0 .. 2. Triage hint: ignition timing.

With the engine-running check true, this table is looked up against FFFF6624 (labelled rpm). Under certain conditions it acts as a ceiling on a correction value that the function finally stores to 0xFFFF8998.   - 0x0520A6: fr12 = @0xFFFF6624 (labelled rpm). 0x0522EA: fr4 = fr12 is the lookup input for descriptor 0xAEFA8.

GBR flag @(123) must equal 1; this flag is set or cleared by speed hysteresis against 0xD6598/0xD6594 at 0x05220C. r12 (diag_precondition_flag_65C0) must be 0. @0xFFFF6634 (engine_speed_delta) must be at least the sp+68 constant. If all hold, fr14 = min(fr14, table value), and flag sp+72 is set and saved to gbr+85.

### 0xAF11C -- Idle-speed feedback proportional term: (RPM minus 895C reference) x coolant-dependent gain (likely idle timing or ISC correction)

2D 8x11 float32, data 0xD859C, range -0.54615 .. 3.2769. Triage hint: ignition timing.

When the flag at 0xFFFF8C41 equals 1, the code computes rpm_current minus the value at 0xFFFF895C and looks this table up with coolant temperature and that RPM difference. The table is exactly linear in the RPM difference (zero at 0, sign-symmetric), with a slope that falls as coolant warms, so the result is a proportional correction on an RPM error. The result is stored to 0xFFFF8C2C; nothing in this function reads it back.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (11 pts, -40..60): coolant temperature in C (fr4 = ect_current, 0x0558E8); X (8 pts, -50..300): rpm_current minus the value at 0xFFFF895C, in rpm. 895C is probably a target idle RPM, so X is probably idle RPM error (fr5 = @0xFFFF8C34, written at 0x0558CE)
- **Output units:** unknown correction units, probably degrees of ignition or ISC flow; slope is 0.0109/rpm at -40C and 0.0033/rpm at 60C
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium

### 0xAF194 -- Barometric (altitude) compensation multiplier vs atmospheric pressure, applied to 2D table 0xAF398 output before inverse lookup

1D x5 uint16, data 0xF8CA8, range 1 .. 1.44. Triage hint: engine control.

This lookup is indexed by the value at FFFF6898, which is probably atmospheric pressure in mmHg-like units, since sea level is 760. It returns a multiplier that is 1.00 at 760 and rises to 1.44 at 504. The multiplier scales the output of 2D table 0xAF398. The scaled result is passed to routine 0xBDD9C, which likely does an inverse lookup of the same table, then floored by FFFF8C14, clamped and stored at FFFF8798 with a complement copy at FFFF87D4.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (1.0 at sea level)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** Both the physical quantity that table 0xAF398 produces (and so what is being compensated) and the guess that 0xBDD9C is an inverse lookup of 0xAF398 are unverified. The pressure identity of FFFF6898 rests on the label plus the 536/760 constants, not on a decoded sensor path.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0xBDD9C to confirm it inverts the 2D table (given z and y, it returns x). Then identify the axes of 0xAF398 and 0xAF1CC and the source of FFFF87B0. Finally search for readers of FFFF8798 and FFFF87B4 to see what the compensated value drives.

  - 0BD7E4: r14 = descriptor 0xAF194; 0BD81C-0BD822: table_lookup_1D(r4=r14, fr4=fr15), so this table is indexed by FFFF6898
  - 0BD7F6-0BD804: in the alternate modes (r12!=0) fr15 is instead loaded with constants 0x44060000=536.0 or 0x443E0000=760.0. These are pressure-like values that put 760 at the top of this table's axis, which corroborates the pressure input
  - 0BD826: the result is saved to @(4,r15). 0BD828-0BD836: table_lookup_2D(0xAF398, x=fr12 from 1D table 0xAF1CC fed by FFFF87B0, y=FFFF679C), and that result is multiplied by this table's value (fmul fr8,fr0)
  - 0BD83A-0BD842: the product goes to 0xBDD9C together with the same 2D descriptor r13 and the same y fr14, which suggests an inverse lookup of 0xAF398
  - 0BD84A-0BD85C: float_max with @FFFF8C14, then float_clamp_range between constants at 0xF8AEC and 0xF8AF0
  - 0BD86E-0BD870: this table's value is stored to FFFF87B4 (FFFF87D4-32). 0BD884-0BD88E: the final value is stored to FFFF8798, and its bitwise NOT is stored to FFFF87D4 as a redundancy copy

### 0xAF3C0 -- Low-load enable threshold vs RPM (max engine load, g/rev, with hysteresis) for a gated correction/learning calculation

1D x5 uint16, data 0xD8794, range 0 .. 0.79999. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Looked up by RPM to give a load ceiling. A hysteresis flag at GBR+34 (0xFFFF8D9E) is set to 1 while engine load is at or below this value and cleared once load exceeds it plus the constant at 0xD8714. That flag is one of several required conditions before the function computes a non-zero output; otherwise the output is forced to 0.

- **Next step:** Read the hysteresis float at 0xD8714 and disassemble 0x0000C70C to identify what the 0..1 fraction feeds. Also search for readers of 0xFFFF8D9E (the flag) and 0xFFFF8D84 (the stored threshold), and confirm 0xFFFF63F8 against a definition-named load table axis.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x057FF8-0x057FFA: fr13 loaded from 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current); 0x058038 fmov fr13,fr4 in the delay slot of jsr table_lookup_1D, so X is RPM
  - 0x058004-0x058006: fr14 loaded from 0xFFFF63F8 (engine_load_current)
  - 0x05803E: result fr0 stored to 0xFFFF8D84 (r10=0xFFFF8D9C, disp -24)

  - 0x05804C-0x05805C: threshold plus float at 0xD8714 is compared to load; if load is greater, the flag is cleared to 0. This is a hysteresis comparator
  - 0x0580E4-0x0580EC: flag @(34,gbr) must equal 1, otherwise branch to 0x058194 fldi0 fr4 (output 0)
  - Other gates on the same path: cl_state_struct byte zero (0x0580B0), transient flag zero (0x0580B8), battery voltage window D8704/D8708, rpm below D870C, ECT at or above D8710
  - Table falls 0.8 to 0 g/rev between 1200 and 4400 rpm, so at 4400 rpm and above the condition is effectively never met
  - Downstream: product of selected table values, clamped 0..100 (0x058196), accumulated with min 25600 (0x0581B0), divided by 100 and clamped 0..1 (0x0581BC-0x0581CC), then passed to 0xC70C under an IRQ lock

1D x13 uint16, data 0xDA450, range 0 .. 0.00159. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Gives the per-execution step by which a dimensionless correction accumulator (FFFF92E4, reset to 1.0) moves up or down, as a function of a signed error input at FFFF92FC (±0.3). Gives the per-pass increment for a learned multiplicative correction factor, looked up from a signed error input. Nor is the meaning of the gate inputs 0xFFFF91CC/0xFFFF91D0 and the counter byte 0xFFFF91F8. The structure is an integrating learn step; what is being learned is unknown.

### 0xAF84C -- VVT feedback correction vs cam-angle error and RPM (likely a proportional-term map; Y/X identities not shown)

2D 9x9 uint16, data 0xDA638, range -10.55 .. 4.6905. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Looks up a 9x9 map whose Y axis runs symmetrically from -11.25 to +11.25 and whose X axis runs 800..4000. It stores the raw result to 0xFFFF9200, then returns that result multiplied by the float at 0xFFFF920C. The output is monotonic and odd-shaped in Y, exactly 0 at Y=0, and saturates at the ends, which is the shape of an error-to-correction (feedback gain) curve.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The identity of both axis inputs. The function does not load them, so 'cam-angle error' and 'RPM' rest only on the axis shape and magnitudes. The 'vvt_error_feedback' RAM labels are only hints.
- **Next step:** Find the callers of 0x0627F2 and see which RAM values they load into fr4/fr5 before the call, to fix the axis identities. Then check the writer of 0xFFFF920C, to see whether the multiplier can be zero, and the readers of 0xFFFF9200 and of the returned fr0, to see where the correction is applied (for example summed into a VVT solenoid duty).
- **Cited:**
  - 0627F4: mov.l @(0x0628E4),r4 loads descriptor 0xAF84C; 0627F6/0627F8: jsr to table_lookup_2D 0xBE8E4, so the result is in fr0
  - 0627FC-062800: r2=0xFFFF920C, r0=-12, fmov.s fr0,@(r0,r2) stores the raw lookup to 0xFFFF9200
  - 062804: fmov.s @r2,fr8 reads the float at 0xFFFF920C; 06280A (delay slot of rts): fmul fr8,fr0 returns lookup × that value

  - The function sets no input registers itself. The axis inputs (fr4/fr5) are supplied by the caller, which is not in the listing.

### 0xAA774 -- Accelerator-pedal threshold vs vehicle speed (selects a diagnostic word constant)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xC0334, range 30 .. 75. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

The table is indexed by the float at 0xFFFF65FC (labelled vehicle speed). It returns a pedal-angle threshold, about 30 at or below ~32 km/h and 75 from 40 km/h up. If the pedal value at 0xFFFF64D8 has reached that threshold, the code writes the word constant at 0xC0216 to gbr+0 (0xFFFF5BD8); otherwise it writes 0xC0218. That write is skipped when the hysteresis flag gbr+45 is set, in which case 0xC0214 is used.

- **Output units:** same units as accel_pedal_angle at 0xFFFF64D8 (probably % or degrees), raw uint16 × 1/256
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unverified. First, the speed and pedal identities of 0xFFFF65FC and 0xFFFF64D8 rest on [TRACED] labels whose names may be wrong. Second, the meaning of the word written to 0xFFFF5BD8 (a timer, limit or mode value) is not visible here, so the table's ultimate purpose is unknown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of word 0xFFFF5BD8 and float 0xFFFF5BF0. Also decode the words at 0xC0214, 0xC0216 and 0xC0218 and the floats at 0xC0258 and 0xC025C, and confirm that 0xFFFF65FC is written by the speed-sensor routine.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x0122BA/0x0122BC: fr15 = float at 0xFFFF65FC [TRACED vehicle_speed_kmh]
  - 0x0122BE/0x0122C0: fr14 = float at 0xFFFF64D8 [TRACED accel_pedal_angle]

  - 0x01240C-0x012412: result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF5C08-24 = 0xFFFF5BF0, then reloaded into fr8
  - 0x012414: fcmp/gt fr14,fr8 compares the table value against pedal angle (fr14), so the output is a pedal-angle threshold
  - 0x012416-0x012422: if threshold > pedal, word @0xC0218 is written to gbr+0 (0xFFFF5BD8); otherwise word @0xC0216 is written

### 0xAA788 -- Battery-voltage compensation multiplier (likely heater/PWM duty power correction, (14/V)^2 above 14 V)

1D x5 uint8, data 0xC03D4, range 0.76562 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Looks up a unitless factor against battery voltage. It is 1.0 from 8 to 14 V and falls to 0.765625 at 16 V, and the result is stored to RAM at 0xFFFF5C54. The 16 V value equals (14/16)^2 exactly, which suggests a constant-power correction for a resistive load driven by PWM, such as a sensor heater. That load identity is not shown in this listing.
