- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low

- **Next step:** Search the ROM for loads of 0xFFFF5C54 (literal 0xFFFF5C54, or base 0xFFFF5C68 with displacement -20, or GBR 0xFFFF5C3C with displacement +0x18). Check whether the reader multiplies a duty or PWM value that is then written to a timer/ATU output register, and which pin that is (heater, purge, wastegate, etc.).

### 0xAADCC -- Lag-filter coefficient for the smoothed 0xFFFF5FA0-derived signal, by its previous filtered value (X axis flat)

2D 5x4 float32, data 0xC2FCC, range 0.25 .. 0.8. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

The table result (fr0, copied to fr6 at 0x018EBC) is passed unchanged as the fr6 argument to float_lag_filter in both filtering branches. That filter smooths the signal at 0xFFFF5FA0 (optionally scaled by a constant), using the previous history sample as its prior state. The cells fall from 0.8 to 0.25 as the Y-axis value rises from 100 to 200, so if fr6 is the gain, larger values are filtered more heavily; X has no effect.

After the 20-deep shift loop this equals the last output, which is capped at 256. It plausibly maps to the Y axis (50..200).; The other input is clamp(old buf[0] - [0xFFFF61B0], -128, 128), stored at 0xFFFF6060 (fr5, loaded at 0x018EB4). It is zero unless adc_channel_status [0xFFFF6155]==1. It plausibly maps to the X axis (-1..1), which is inert because every row is identical. The fr4/fr5-to-axis assignment of table_lookup_2D is not shown in this listing.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That fr6 is the gain argument of float_lag_filter (0xBEA40) rather than another parameter; fr7 is clearly a separate small constant (1/256 here, 0.195 in the rpm call). The physical quantity at 0xFFFF5FA0 is also unidentified; vehicle speed is only a hunch based on the 0..256 range.
- **Next step:** Disassemble float_lag_filter at 0xBEA40 to confirm fr6 is the coefficient (out = prev + k*(in-prev)). Then find the writer of 0xFFFF5FA0 to identify the signal, and search for readers of 0xFFFF5FFC (buf[0]) and 0xFFFF6054 to see what consumes the smoothed value and its rate of change.

  - 0x018ED6-0x018EEE: fr15=[0xFFFF5FA0]*const (0xC2240 or 0xC2244, chosen by flag 0xFFFF5F03); fr5=buf[1]; fr7=1/256 (literal 0x019074); jsr float_lag_filter. fr6 is the table value.

### 0xAAF1C -- Vehicle-speed-dependent decrement step for the up/down counter at FFFF691C (feeds status flag FFFF691E)

1D x4 float32, data 0xC38C8, range 0 .. 20. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Once vehicle speed (fr14) reaches the threshold at 0xC3290, and neither the transient check nor flag_6254 is active, this table gives the amount subtracted from the uint16 counter at FFFF691C on each call, floored at 0. Below that speed, or with flag_6254 set, the counter instead counts up by 1 to a cap at 0xC3074. The function sets byte FFFF691E to 1 when the counter reaches 0 and the word from 0xFFFF67EC exceeds the limit at 0xC3076.

- **Weakest link:** The cell type is in doubt. The descriptor decodes as float32, but the second cell is 0x0000FFFF (a denormal float, or 65535 if the cells are really uint16), so the effective step at 10 km/h is ambiguous. The identity of the counter, and therefore what the flag means, is also unknown; the only basis for naming the input speed is the [TRACED] vehicle-speed label.

When the flag byte at FFFF691E equals 1, the table is looked up against mass airflow. The result is added to the previous value of FFFF2270, so the estimate ramps up faster at higher airflow. What it physically represents is not shown (possibly an intake, intercooler or air-charge temperature estimate). The per-step units also depend on the unknown call rate. The write semantics of 0xBDBCC ('desc_read_float_safe') are assumed from the tail-call pattern.

Also find what writes the byte at 0xFFFF691E and what 0x297C0 returns, to see how often the increment path is active. Read the constant at 0x25C04 and find the task rate that calls 0x025B28.

  - 025B5C-025B60: fr4 = [0xFFFF63C4] mass_airflow_gps feeds table_lookup_1D 0xBE830 with descriptor 0xAAF28.

  - 025B7A-025B7E: float_min against ect_current 0xFFFF6350.
  - 025B84-025B88: float_max against [0xFFFF682C].
  - 025B9C-025BA4: tail jump to 0xBDBCC with r4=0xFFFF2270 and fr4=fr14, apparently a write-back of the new state.
  - 025B46-025B50: if the byte flag returned by 0x297C0 is nonzero, the state is instead set to the constant at 0x25C04 (a reset/init path).
  - 025B52-025B56: the table is used only when byte [0xFFFF691E]==1. Otherwise there is no increment, only the clamps.

### 0xAAF98 -- Lag-filter coefficient for the MAP-derived pressure estimate (FFFF2240), indexed by the offset float at FFFF68A4

The output is then clamped and written back to FFFF2240, so the table steps from fast smoothing (0.5) at input <=50 to very slow smoothing (0.005) at input >=51.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That fr6 of float_lag_filter is the smoothing gain. This rests on the routine's [TRACED] name and the 0..1 magnitude, not on a decoded body. The physical meaning of the input float at 0xFFFF68A4 is also unknown.
- **Next step:** Disassemble float_lag_filter at 0xBEA40 to confirm that fr6 is the gain (e.g. out = prev + (target-prev)*fr6, with fr7 as a limit or scale).   - 024E34 (delay slot of the jsr to table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830): fr4 = @(r0,r6) with r0 still -16, so the table input is the float at 0xFFFF68A4.
  - 024E38/024E3A: the lookup result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF68B4-12 = 0xFFFF68A8.

FFFF2240 is therefore a pressure-like quantity selected among several sources.

### 0xAB03C -- MAP sensor rationality diagnostic: expected manifold vacuum (pressure offset) vs RPM and throttle angle

2D 31x15 uint8, data 0xC3E90, range 0 .. 100. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Looked up from RPM and throttle plate angle, stored to FFFF68A4, then added to measured manifold pressure and subtracted from the value at FFFF2240. A MAP-monitor routine compares the residual against a threshold and, when its enabling conditions hold, counts toward a fault flag. The cells are highest (saturated at 100) at small throttle and high RPM and fall toward 0 near wide-open throttle at low RPM, which is the shape of an expected intake vacuum.

- **Axes:** Y (15 pts, 800..6400): engine speed rpm_current (FFFF6624), passed in fr4; X (31 pts, 10.58..83.9): throttle_plate_angle (FFFF62DC), passed in fr5
- **Output units:** Same pressure units as manifold_pressure_map, since the output is added directly to it. Probably kPa or similar. 100 is the uint8 saturation value (raw 255 x 0.39216), not a percentage.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The identity of FFFF2240. If it is barometric/atmospheric pressure, the table is expected vacuum (baro - MAP). If it is something else, such as a modeled MAP or boost value, the meaning shifts. The MAP units and the sign or value of threshold C34FC are also unread, so which side of the comparison counts as a fault is not confirmed.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of FFFF2240 to confirm whether it is barometric pressure. Read the float constants at C34F0, C34F4, C34F8 and C34FC and the uint16 at C30C6. Search for readers of the flag FFFF68B5 (gbr_adc_68A4 + 17) to confirm that it feeds a MAP-sensor DTC (e.g. P0106/P0068).
- **Cited:**
  - 024EF6-024EFC: fr14 = [FFFF6624] rpm_current and fr15 = [FFFF62DC] throttle_plate_angle.
  - 024F0A/024F0E: fr4 = rpm and fr5 = throttle passed to table_lookup_2D at 0xBE8E4 with descriptor 0xAB03C.
  - 024F10-024F14: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF68C0-28 = FFFF68A4.
  - 024F24-024F32: fr9 = [FFFF2240] - ([FFFF620C] manifold_pressure_map + table). The table value is therefore a pressure-like offset in MAP units, added to the measured MAP.
  - 024F34-024F5C: enabling conditions are ECT > [C34F0], C34F4 < rpm < C34F8, throttle > the 1D rpm-indexed gate (descriptor AAF84, result at FFFF68AC), and the 0x29AA0 result (stack byte) == 0.
  - 024F5E-024F64: if fr9 > [C34FC] the routine branches to 024F88, which clears the counter and the flag.
  - 024F66-024F86: otherwise the uint16 counter at gbr+30 (FFFF68C2) is incremented with saturation and compared with [C30C6]. The flag at gbr+17 (FFFF68B5) is set when the counter >= that limit. This is a diagnostic debounce structure.
  - Data shape: values fall with rising throttle and rise with RPM, matching expected manifold vacuum.

### 0xAB088 -- Air density correction factor (IAT x ambient pressure) for an integrated rate model

2D 5x4 uint16, data 0xC41A8, range 0.72501 .. 1.3953. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Returns a dimensionless factor that rises in proportion to the Y input and falls as 1/absolute temperature of the IAT input. It multiplies a filtered rate (6908-52); the product divided by 60 is added to an accumulator at FFFF2254, clamped to 0..76800.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X (-20..60): intake air temperature, degC. iat_current FFFF6364 is passed in fr5.; Y (520..760): FFFF2200, passed in fr4. Probably ambient/atmospheric pressure in mmHg (about 69..101 kPa), not confirmed.
- **Output units:** dimensionless ratio (about 0.73..1.40), behaves as P/T relative to a reference
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That FFFF2200 is atmospheric pressure in mmHg. This rests only on the axis range and the exact P/T fit of the data, not on any traced writer. What the accumulated quantity at FFFF2254 physically represents is also unknown.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF2200 and check whether it is the baro/atmospheric sensor converted to mmHg. Then search for readers of the accumulator FFFF2254 and of FFFF6908-12/-48 to identify what the density-corrected integrated quantity feeds.
- **Cited:**
  - 0256E2/0256E8: fr5 = fr15, which 02567A loaded from iat_current FFFF6364 [TRACED]. fr4 = @FFFF2200. Then jsr table_lookup_2D at 0256E6.
  - Data fit: each X row is exactly linear in Y through zero (0.82434/520 = 1.2048/760 = 0.001585).
  - Data fit: the ratio between the X=-20 and X=60 rows is 1.3167, matching (60+273.15)/(-20+273.15) = 1.316. So value ∝ Y/(X+273.15), which is ideal-gas density with IAT as X and Y as pressure.
  - 0256EA-0256F6: the result is stored at 6908-12, then multiplied by 6908-52. That value is the output of float_lag_filter at 0256D6, applied to a table_lookup_1D (0x AAF08) result.
  - 0256FA-0256FE: the product is passed to float_safe_div with the constant 0x42700000 (60.0) and stored at 6908-48.
  - 025716-025720: 6908-48 is added to fr14, which 025676 loaded from FFFF2254 (225C-8). The sum goes through float_clamp_range with bounds 0 and 0x47960000 (76800), then to desc_read_float_safe with r4 = FFFF2254. This looks like an integrator.
  - 025694-02569E: this whole path runs only when afc_enable_flag_A (FFFF8E7E) equals 1 and the result of the call to 0x299F0 is zero. Otherwise the intermediate values are zeroed at 025728-025736.

### 0xAB0A4 -- RPM-based output factor applied while a diagnostic fault status is active (fault-mode limit multiplier, 1.0 tapering to 0.2 above ~5100 rpm)

1D x2 uint8, data 0xF8938, range 0.20312 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

On the normal path (r4==0, with RAM byte FFFF5FB8 == 0), the function looks this table up against rpm_current. The result becomes the function's output factor only when either of two diagnostic routines returns status 2. Otherwise the output comes from a vehicle-speed table, a second table indexed by the float at FFFF64A8, or a fixed 1.0.

- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier/factor (1.0 = no reduction, 0.203 at >=5631 rpm)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the factor at 0xFFFF64DC actually scales is not in this listing; it could be torque, throttle, boost or fuel. The meaning of diag status 2 (taken here as 'fault confirmed') is also not shown.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF64DC (and its complement copy 0xFFFF6528) to see what the factor multiplies. Also check diag_check_P0137 (0x299BC) and 0x297A0 to confirm that a return value of 2 means a confirmed fault.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA34-0BBA36: fr14 loaded from rpm_current 0xFFFF6624; 0BBA98-0BBAA0: descriptor 0xAB0A4 is passed to table_lookup_1D 0xBE830 with fr4 = fr14 (rpm). This happens only when r13 (byte at FFFF5FB8) is zero (0BBA94 tst r13 / bf); otherwise the alternate table 0xAB0B8 is used.
  - 0BBA18-0BBA24: results of diag_flag_reader_cluster_start (0x297A0) and diag_check_P0137 (0x299BC) are saved at @(4,r15) and @(8,r15). At 0BBABC-0BBAC6, if either equals 2 the code branches to 0BBAF0 'fmov.s fr0,@r15', storing this table's lookup result (fr0 from 0BBA9E) as the output.
  - 0BBAC8-0BBAD6: with no fault status, rpm is compared with the ROM threshold at 0xF890C (fr6). If rpm >= threshold, the output is a lookup of the r12 table (0xAB0CC/0xAB0E0/0xAB0F4/0xAB108, chosen by FFFF5FB8 and cl_ol_gate_flag==3) against vehicle_speed_kmh (fr15).
  - 0BBAD8-0BBAEE: if rpm < threshold, the output is a lookup of table 0xAB128 against the float in fr12 (loaded at 0BBA2A from FFFF650C-100 = FFFF64A8), but only when byte FFFF5E93 == 7 and engine_running_flag (FFFF65C5) == 0. Otherwise 0BBAF4 fldi1 gives 1.0. The r11==1 case (engine_mode_bits bit7 clear, via tst #128 / subc / add #1) also forces 1.0.
  - 0BBB02-0BBB10: on the r4==0 path the output is stored to FFFF650C-48 = 0xFFFF64DC, and its bitwise complement to FFFF650C+28 = 0xFFFF6528, which looks like a redundant integrity copy. On r4!=0 paths (0BBBC4-0BBBD4) it goes to 0xFFFF6508 or 0xFFFF650C.
  - Data: 1.0 at 5082 rpm falling to 0.203 at 5631 rpm. This is consistent with a strong high-rpm reduction applied only in a fault state.

### 0xAB26C -- unknown: 2D gain map from a 0..1 command (0xFFFF69F0) and a 0..469 quantity (0xFFFF6A18) to 0xFFFF69F4

Maps a 0..1 ratio held at 0xFFFF69F0 and a second input at 0xFFFF6A18 (0..468.75) to a 0..0.79 value stored at 0xFFFF69F4. It is a 0..1 command or ratio: function 0x02740A writes 0.0 or 1.0 to it, and functions 2-4 load it from ROM 0xC41F0, RAM 0xFFFFA53C or RAM 0xFFFFACB8.
- **Output units:** dimensionless 0..0.786 (same order as the 0..1 input ratio); true units unknown
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Neither the physical quantity in 0xFFFF6A18 nor the consumer of 0xFFFF69F4 appears in the listing. The axis-to-register pairing (fr4 to Y, fr5 to X) rests on value ranges and the 0/1 writes, not on decoded table_lookup_2D internals.

  - 0x027454-0x02745A: fldi1 fr8 is stored to 0xFFFF69F0. At 0x02744C-0x027452, fldi0 is stored there when float_deadband_check (0x027442), comparing it to 0 with a small threshold (constant at 0x027518, apparently 0x39800000 ≈ 2.4e-4), returns true. This shows 69F0 is a 0/1-valued ratio, which matches the 0..1 X axis.

Each then runs the same lookup and stores fr0 to 0xFFFF69F4 (r6-36). These look like alternate or override modes feeding the same transfer map.
  - Data: the X=0 row is all zero and rows scale roughly linearly with X. The table therefore acts as an input-dependent multiplier on the ratio, not an independent setpoint.

### 0xAB288 -- Normalised valve opening (likely purge duty) vs manifold vacuum and requested flow product

2D 13x13 uint16, data 0xC45D8, range 0.05338 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Looks up a 0..1 fraction from manifold vacuum (max(atm, const) − MAP) and from a product of two RAM values clamped to [0, limit]. The result goes to 0xFFFF69F0. Along the vacuum axis it falls roughly as a + b/vacuum, and it rises about linearly with the product, which is the shape of a duty fraction needed to pass a requested flow through a vacuum-driven valve, likely canister purge.

Passed in fr4.; X (0..0.804): product @0xFFFF69F8 × @0xFFFF69FC, clamped to [0, @0xFFFF6A0C] and stored at 0xFFFF6A04. Passed in fr5. Identity unknown; possibly a requested flow or ratio.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The purge identity rests only on the data shape and the use of vacuum as an input. The two multiplied RAM values (0xFFFF69F8, 0xFFFF69FC) and the consumer of 0xFFFF69F0 are not identified. Which register feeds which axis is also inferred from value ranges, not from the code of table_lookup_2D.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF69F0 and check whether one drives a solenoid duty output (purge or other). Also find the writers of 0xFFFF69F8, 0xFFFF69FC and 0xFFFF6A0C to identify the X-axis quantity.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x02753E-0x02754C: fr4=@0xFFFF6898 (atm), fr5=@0xC41FC, jsr float_max; result stored to 0xFFFF6A3C
  - 0x027552-0x027556: fr9=@0xFFFF6A3C minus fr14 (@0xFFFF620C MAP), so fr14 = atm − MAP = vacuum
  - 0x02755A-0x027568: fr4=@(r14-68)=0xFFFF69F8 times @(r14-64)=0xFFFF69FC; float_clamp_range with low=0 (fldi0 fr5) and high=@0xFFFF6A0C
  - 0x02756E: clamped product stored to 0xFFFF6A04 (r14-72)
  - 0x027574-0x027578: table_lookup_2D called with fr4=vacuum, fr5=@0xFFFF6A04. The X axis starts at 0, matching the 0 clamp floor, and the Y range 39..508 matches vacuum.
  - 0x02757C: result stored to 0xFFFF69F0 (r14-76); its consumer is not in this listing
  - Data: each row falls as ~1/Y, rises roughly linearly with X and saturates at 1.0, consistent with flow demand / valve capacity at a given vacuum

- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x3DAC to confirm it rounds or truncates to an integer. Then decode table 0xAB430's axis (expected 0..15) and data to see what per-temperature-bin enable it holds. Also search for other readers of FFFF6BB8.

  - 02B3D8-02B3DC: descriptor 0xAB41C is passed to table_lookup_1D (r14=0xBE830) with fr4=fr14, i.e. the same temperature input.
  - 02B3DE-02B3E2: the result fr0 is passed straight to the routine at 0x3DAC as fr4.

  - 02B61A: fcmp/eq fr5,fr0 with fr5=0. No call or write to fr0 occurs between 02B3EE and 02B61A, so the AB430 result feeds the flag stored at GBR+46.
  - Data: values 0..15 against breakpoints -40..110 step 10 form an exact identity of breakpoint index, the signature of a quantizer and not a physical calibration.

### 0xAB458 -- Diagnostic monitor debounce-count threshold, alternate set for the r4!=0 call mode (sibling of the table at 0xAC26C)

1D x2 float32, data 0xC5EC8, range -30 .. 0. Triage hint: unassigned.

When the routine is entered with a nonzero byte argument in r4, this descriptor replaces 0xAC26C as the active table. It is looked up at a fixed input of 10.0, and the integer result is stored to FFFF9770. That value is the count a saturating counter must reach before the routine's fault flag (r9) is set, packed to FFFF2508 and written to FFFF6AA4.

- **Output units:** counts (uint16 loop or sample count). The float32 decode is probably wrong: the data bytes read as 0x0001,0x0000 for uint16 cells, i.e. values 1 and 0.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The cell type. The descriptor says float32, but the routine returns an integer stored with mov.w and the bytes look like uint16 values 1 and 0. The table's meaning as a debounce threshold rests on how 0xBE8AC interprets the descriptor's type field. Which mode the caller passes in r4 is also not shown.
- **Next step:** Dump the raw descriptor bytes at 0xAB458 and compare its type field with 0xAC26C. Disassemble 0xBE8AC to confirm the cell-type handling and integer return. Then find all callers of 0x0BBD06 to see which r4 values select this mode, and search readers of FFFF9770 and FFFF2508.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBD2E/0BBD32: r11 = extu.b r4; a nonzero r11 skips the normal setup and falls to 0BBD64.
  - 0BBD6A-0BBD6C: descriptor 0xAB458 is written to @(32,gbr) = FFFF9778 with no condition; the other path writes 0xAC26C to the same slot at 0BBD3A-0BBD3C.
  - 0BBD6E-0BBD70: fr15 = literal at 0x0BBEA4 = 0x41200000 = 10.0. The normal path loads fr15 from FFFF9758 at 0BBD40-0BBD42.
  - 0BBDC4-0BBDCE: r4 = @(32,gbr) (the selected descriptor), fr4 = fr15, jsr 0xBE8AC (table_desc_1d_uint16). The integer result r0 is stored with mov.w to @(24,gbr) = FFFF9770.
  - 0BBE00-0BBE16: uint16_add_sat(counter,1) is compared by cmp/ge against extu.w @(24,gbr). Reaching it branches to 0BBEE8, which sets r9 = 1 (flag).
  - 0BBF38-0BBF4E: eeprom_maturation_check(23); r9 is passed to uint8_pack, the result is stored to FFFF2508, and not r9 is stored to FFFF6AA4+13. This is a diagnostic/maturation pattern.
  - The decoded first cell of 9.1837e-41 is a denormal with bit pattern 0x00010000. It is not a sensible float, which supports uint16 cells with values 1 and 0.

### 0xAB464 -- Diagnostic monitor enable: minimum counter/time threshold vs FFFF682C (likely a temperature), uint16

1D x6 float32, data 0xC5EE4, range -30 .. 0. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Looks up a uint16 threshold from the value at FFFF682C and stores it to FFFF9898. A DTC monitor's enable flag is set only if the word counter at FFFF67EA has reached that threshold and every other condition in the chain also passes.

- **Axes:** X: float at FFFF682C (adc_processed_misc, unverified). Breakpoints -30..20 in steps of 10 look like Celsius (intake or ambient temperature?), but the quantity is not fixed by this code.
- **Output units:** uint16 raw counts, compared directly against the word counter at FFFF67EA, whose units and tick rate are unknown. The descriptor's float32 cell type is wrong: the code calls the uint16 1D routine and stores r0 with mov.w.

- **Weakest link:** What FFFF682C actually is (the 'adc_processed_misc' label is unverified, and the -30..20 axis only suggests a temperature) and what FFFF67EA counts (time since start, or something else). Without these, the threshold's physical meaning is not fixed.

Read the raw 12 bytes at 0xC5EE4 as big-endian uint16 to get the true thresholds. Search for readers of FFFF9898 and of the flag written through @(8,r11), to identify which DTC this enable gates.

So the real table is 6 x uint16 = 12 bytes at 0xC5EE4, roughly 0x01CB, ?, 0x017D, ?, 0x012F, ? (decreasing).

The other conditions are: ECT fr15 >= the constant at C4E88 (073B86-073B8C), FFFF63D0 >= the 0xAB470 result (073B9A-073BA0), FFFF6B62 == 1, and further counter compares against C486C and C4882. This shape is a diagnostic monitor enable-condition evaluator.
  - 073B36-073B7C: DTC_DefinitionTable is indexed by r14/r13 (index x 20) against dtc_maturation_timer bits, which ties the function to DTC monitoring.

- **Next step:** Disassemble the local subroutines called just before the lookup (0x073A74, 0x073BF0, 0x073EE8, 0x073F26, 0x073FC4, 0x074054, 0x07411E, 0x0742C4) and find the store to 0xFFFF9834 to identify the axis quantity. Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF2574 (and 0xFFFF98AC) to see whether the filtered product reaches fueling or only a diagnostic/monitor decision.
- **Cited:**
  - 073604-073608: mov #-88,r0 / fmov.s @(r0,r9),fr4. The input is the float at 0xFFFF988C-88 = 0xFFFF9834.
  - 073606: jsr @r1 with r1 = 0x000BE830 table_lookup_1D, r4 = 0x000AB484 (this descriptor).
  - 07360A-07360C: fmov.s fr0,@(32,r9). The result is stored to 0xFFFF98AC.
  - 07360E-073614: the float at 0xFFFF93E0 is copied to r9+28 (0xFFFF98A8).
  - 073694-0736A6 and 07375A-07376C: fr8 = @(r9-108) [0xFFFF9820] * @(r9+32) [this table] * @(r9+28) [0xFFFF93E0 copy], stored to r9-104. This is a pure multiplier, with no clamp seen.
  - 07362A-07362C: 0xFFFF9820 is passed as r4 to the local routine bsr 0x074208, which probably produces the base quantity.
  - 0737AA-0737B8: the result at r9-100 is passed as fr4 to 0x000BEA40 float_lag_filter. fr5 is the old value from @(0xFFFF2598-36) = 0xFFFF2574; fr6 is 1.0 or the constant at 0xC4EE8.
  - 0737BE-0737C4: r4 = 0xFFFF2574 is passed to 0x000BDBCC desc_read_float_safe. The filtered value is stored on the stack at r15+4; whether it is written to 0xFFFF2574 is not shown.
  - 073680-073692 and 073750-073758: the multiply is reached only after flag gates on 0xFFFF9842 (gbr 143 clear) and 0xFFFF984B (gbr 63 = 1), plus 0xFFFF94B0 = 1 and either 0xFFFF98F2 (gbr 230) = 1 or 0xFFFF984E (gbr 66) = 1. Otherwise the factor is computed but unused here.
  - 073500-073548: the prologue snapshots afc_output, o2_sensor_conditioned, throttle_delta_pos, fuel_enrichment and others into the 0xFFFF988C block. This suggests a monitoring or diagnostic routine, but it is a hint only.

### 0xAB4AC -- unknown: multiplier (0.43..1.0) on the FFFF9820 quantity, looked up from FFFF9834, applied before a lag filter

1D x5 uint16, data 0xC5F60, range 0.42969 .. 1.001. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Looks up a factor from the float at 0xFFFF9834 (r10-88) and stores it to 0xFFFF98AC (r10+32). When flag gbr+143 is 0 and gbr+63 is 1, that factor multiplies the float at 0xFFFF9820, and the product is lag-filtered and written onward toward the 0xFFFF2574/0xFFFF2598 state. It is a 1.0 plateau for inputs of 20..34, falling to about 0.43 at 15 and about 0.70 at 45.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at 0xFFFF9834 (r10-88, where r10=0xFFFF988C; equals gbr+0x28 with gbr=0xFFFF980C). Its producer is not in the listing. Units are unknown, breakpoints 15..45.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (about 0.43..1.0)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of both the input 0xFFFF9834 and the multiplied quantity 0xFFFF9820 is unknown. Neither producer appears in this listing, so the identity of the factor cannot be fixed.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of 0xFFFF9834 (gbr+0x28 under gbr=0xFFFF980C; look for 'mov.w r0,@(40,gbr)' or float stores to r10-88), likely in the bsr targets 0x073EE8..0x07411E or 0x074208. Also disassemble 0x074208 to see what it writes to 0xFFFF9820. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF2598 and 0xFFFF2574 to learn what the filtered, weighted value drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 0738A4/0738AA: r4=0x000AB4AC goes to table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830, with fr4 loaded by 'fmov.s @(r0,r10),fr4' (r0=-88), i.e. from 0xFFFF9834
  - 0738AE-0738B0: result fr0 stored to @(32,r10) = 0xFFFF98AC
  - 0738D2-0738FE: when gbr+142==1 the module zeroes several states, including the table input at r10-88
  - 073908-073912: the consumer runs only if gbr+143==0 and gbr+63==1
  - 073914-07391C: fr8=@(r10-108)=0xFFFF9820, fr9=@(r10+32) (the table value); 'fmul fr9,fr8' makes the table a multiplicative factor
  - 07391E-073934: the product is stored to 0xFFFF9824 and 0xFFFF9828, then passed as fr4 to float_lag_filter 0xBEA40 with fr5=fr15 (from 0xFFFF2574, i.e. 0xFFFF2598-36) and fr6=fr14 (1.0 or the constant at 0xC4EE8)
  - 073936-07394E: the filter output goes to desc_read_float_safe (r4=0xFFFF2574), then uint8_pack, and the result is stored to 0xFFFF2598
  - 0738C6: 0xFFFF9820 is passed to subroutine 0x074208 just after the lookup; it is probably that quantity's producer (not shown)

### 0xAB4C0 -- Time-ramp divisor for a monitor accumulator (indexed by elapsed counter at GBR+58), meaning unknown

1D x6 uint16, data 0xC5F84, range 3.6016 .. 8. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Gives a divisor, falling from 8 to 3.6 as a scaled counter rises from 0 to 1000. The routine computes base*(1 + K*t/table(t)), with base = [r9-104]. The result goes to [r9-100], is lag-filtered, and then feeds a descriptor write to FFFF2574. Because the divisor shrinks as t grows, the added term grows faster than linearly with t.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: the uint16 word at GBR+58 (sched GBR 0xFFFF980C + 58 = 0xFFFF9846), scaled by fmac_interp_uint16 using the constant at 0x073994 with bias 0. This looks like an elapsed-time or event counter; its source is not in the listing.
- **Output units:** Dimensionless divisor. Its physical meaning depends on constant 0x000C4EE4 and the counter scale at 0x073994, neither of which is decoded.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unchecked. The operand order of float_safe_div (numerator fr4, divisor fr5) is assumed, not verified. The identity of the GBR+58 counter (time vs. events) is unknown. The function also caches AFC, O2 and throttle values into a struct, which suggests a diagnostic or monitor, but that is only a hint.
- **Next step:** Confirm the operand order of float_safe_div (0x0BE628) and decode the floats at 0x073994 and 0x000C4EE4. Then search for writers of the word at 0xFFFF9846 (GBR+58) to identify the counter, and for readers of 0xFFFF2574 and of [0xFFFF988C-100] to find the consumer.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x073772-0x07377C: fr4 = const @0x073994, fr5 = 0, r4 = extu.w of the GBR word @58. fmac_interp_uint16 converts the counter to float.
  - 0x073780: the scaled counter t is saved to @(0,r15).
  - 0x073782-0x073788: table_lookup_1D(0xAB4C0) is called with fr4 = t.
  - 0x07378A: the table output moves to fr5, the second argument of float_safe_div at 0x07379C.
  - 0x07378C-0x07379E: fr4 = const@0xC4EE4 * t * [r9-104], so the numerator scales with t.
  - 0x07375A-0x07376C: [r9-104] = [r9-108] * [r9+32] * [r9+28]. Here [r9+32] is the lookup of table 0xAB484 at 0x073600 on [r9-88], and [r9+28] is copied from FFFF93E0 at 0x07360E.
  - 0x0737A0-0x0737A8: [r9-100] = [r9-104] + quotient, i.e. base*(1 + K*t/table(t)).
  - 0x0737AA-0x0737B8: [r9-100] is passed through float_lag_filter, with fr15 = @(FFFF2598-36) and fr14 = 1 or const 0xC4EE8.
  - 0x0737BE-0x0737C4: the filtered value goes to desc_read_float_safe with r4 = FFFF2574.
  - 0x073750-0x073758: this path runs only when GBR byte @66 == 1. The parallel branch at 0x073694 (taken when GBR byte @230 == 1) skips the table entirely and uses base alone.

### 0xAB4E0 -- unknown – self-referenced offset added to the rpm-gated, lag-filtered signal at FFFF61FC (the sum saturates at 800)

1D x12 uint16, data 0xC5FCC, range -800 .. 200. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

The table's input is the first-order-filtered copy (FFFF6B4C) of the RAM signal at FFFF61FC, which is forced to 0 below an rpm threshold. Under some conditions the output is added back onto that same filtered signal, re-filtered and stored. The net effect, x + f(x), lifts low values (200→400, 400→480) and pins everything at or above 800 to 800, so it works as a shaping or ceiling curve on an unidentified quantity.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: filtered value of FFFF61FC (stored at FFFF6B4C), same units as that signal; physical meaning not established
- **Output units:** Same units as the FFFF61FC signal; the output is an additive offset to it.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the source signal at FFFF61FC physically is. Its label, adc_processed_extra, is UNVERIFIED and its writer is not in this listing. Without it the curve's purpose cannot be named. Whether the r13 path actually gets selected also depends on constants C4F1C, C4F44 and C4F18, which were not decoded.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF61FC to identify the source quantity. Search for readers of FFFF6B30, FFFF6B50 and FFFF6B40 to see where the shaped result goes. Decode floats C4F48 (rpm gate), C4F1C (the r11 threshold that enables this path), C4F44 and C4F18 to see whether the table path is reachable in practice.
- **Cited:**
  - 02ACF8-02AD28: rpm_current at FFFF6624 is compared with constant C4F48. If the threshold is greater than rpm, 0 is stored to FFFF6B34 (r8-108); otherwise the value at FFFF61FC is copied there.
  - 02AD2A-02AD46: coefficient k = min(1, C4F10). Then FFFF6B4C (r8-84) += k*(FFFF6B34 - FFFF6B4C), a first-order lag filter.
  - 02AEAA-02AEB2: r4 = descriptor 0xAB4E0 and fr4 = @(r8-84), i.e. FFFF6B4C, before jsr table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830. The X input is therefore the filtered signal.
  - 02AEB4-02AEB6: the result fr0 is stored to FFFF6B38 (r8-104). On the bypass path at 02AEA0-02AEA8 (r7==0 and r1==0), the constant at C4F18 is stored there instead.
  - 02AF52-02AFD8: fr8 is chosen by priority. If r10 is set, fr8 = constant C4F44. Otherwise, if r13 (set from r11 or gbr+144) is set, fr8 = FFFF6B38, this table's output. Otherwise fr8 = FFFF6B48, a rate/counter-based term.
  - 02AFDA-02AFE2: FFFF6B40 (r8-96) = FFFF6B4C + fr8. The table output is an additive offset to the same filtered signal it was indexed by.
  - 02AFE4-02B02A: FFFF6B50 (r8-80) lag-filters FFFF6B40 with k = min(1, C4F14). FFFF6B30 (r8-112) gets either FFFF6B50 or FFFF6B50 times a second table (0xAB4F4, indexed by a counter).
  - Data: x + f(x) gives 0, 400, 450, 480, 520, 530, 620, 710, 800, 800, 800, 800, flat at 800 from x = 800 upward.

### 0xAB4F4 -- unknown: low-end multiplier (0.85..1) on a filtered signal, indexed by scaled RAM word 0xFFFF67EE

1D x9 uint16, data 0xC6008, range 0.85001 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

When flag r12 is set, the 16-bit RAM word at 0xFFFF67EE is scaled by a float constant and used to look up a factor of 0.85 to 1.0. That factor multiplies a doubly low-pass-filtered signal at 0xFFFF6B50, and the result is stored to 0xFFFF6B30. When r12 is clear, the filtered signal is copied to 0xFFFF6B30 unmultiplied. The physical quantity of both the axis and the signal is not established.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float(uint16 @0xFFFF67EE) * float constant at 0x02B1EC. Breakpoints run 100..1000; the unit and meaning are unknown.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (0.85..1.0)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The meaning of 0xFFFF67EE (axis source) and 0xFFFF61FC (the signal being scaled) are both unidentified. The axis scale constant at 0x02B1EC lies outside the shown literal pool, so the physical axis range is unknown.
- **Next step:** Read the float at 0x02B1EC to get the axis scaling. Then search for writers of 0xFFFF67EE and 0xFFFF61FC and for readers of 0xFFFF6B30, to establish what is being scaled and who consumes it.
- **Cited:**
  - 02AD02/02AD04: the word read from 0xFFFF67EE (02ACF4-02ACF6) is zero-extended (extu.w r6,r5) and saved to @r15
  - 02B008-02B018: mov.l @r15,r5; float fpul,fr4; fmul fr8,fr4 with the constant from 0x02B1EC. This forms the lookup input, and the lookup is jsr table_lookup_1D with r4=0xAB4F4
  - 02B01A-02B024: fmov.s @(-80,r8),fr9; fmul fr0,fr9; store to @(-112,r8). The table output multiplies the value at 0xFFFF6B50 and is written to 0xFFFF6B30
  - 02B026-02B02A: the alternate path (r12==0, branch at 02B004) stores 0xFFFF6B50 to 0xFFFF6B30 unmultiplied, so the table is a gated scaling factor
  - 02AFF4-02B006: 0xFFFF6B50 is a first-order filter, (-80) += k*((-96) - (-80)), with k clamped to <=1 from 0xC4F14. Its input (-96) = filtered value at 0xFFFF6B4C (-84) plus an offset (02AFDA-02AFE2)
  - 02AD16-02AD46: 0xFFFF6B4C is itself filtered from 0xFFFF61FC. That source is forced to 0 when rpm_current (0xFFFF6624) is below the constant at 0xC4F48
  - 02AD06-02AD14: r6 = (word@0xFFFF67EE <= constant@0xC4884). The same RAM word therefore also feeds the r12 enable logic at 02AE2A-02AE5A

### 0xAB51C -- RPM-dependent comparison threshold (base factor) for the per-index event counter on FFFF99B4, the variant used when check_engine_running != 1

1D x2 uint16, data 0xC6030, range 48.462 .. 65.43. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

The table is indexed by rpm_current and gives a factor that is stored at 0xFFFF7AA4 and multiplied by a second rpm table (0xAD464, stored at 0xFFFF7AA8) to form a threshold. When the float at 0xFFFF99B4 exceeds that product, a saturating uint8 counter for the index passed in r4 is incremented and its companion counter is cleared. Those counters later drive raising or decaying four float accumulators in the 0xFFFF7A78 block.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input is fr14 = rpm_current, 0xFFFF6624), breakpoints 700 and 1000, idle region only
- **Output units:** Same units as the quantity at 0xFFFF99B4 divided by the 0xAD464 factor. The physical quantity is unknown; the stored scale 0.12207 (about 1000/8192) does not identify it.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What 0xFFFF99B4 physically is, and what the four per-index accumulators at 0xFFFF7A78..7A84 represent: per-cylinder, per-bank, or something else. Neither the producer of 99B4 nor the readers of the accumulators are in this listing. The name check_engine_running for the branch selector at 0x22CF4 is also not checked here.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF99B4 to fix the compared quantity and its units. Also search for readers of 0xFFFF7A78..0xFFFF7A84 (the four accumulators) and 0xFFFF7A98..0xFFFF7A9F (the counter copies) to see what the learned values feed. Check the callers of 0x03771A to see what index r4 takes.
- **Cited:**
  - 037730: fmov.s @r2,fr14 loads rpm_current. 037780: fmov fr14,fr4 in the delay slot makes rpm the lookup input for descriptor 0xAB51C (037778).
  - 037764-037768: the byte at @(8,r15) (result of check_engine_running at 037738) is compared with 1. If it is 1, table 0xAB880 is used (03776A); otherwise this table is used. The two tables are mutually exclusive variants.
  - 037782-037786: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF7AA8-4 = 0xFFFF7AA4. 037788-037790: table 0xAD464 at the same rpm is stored to 0xFFFF7AA8.
  - 037838-03783E: fr8 = @(7AA4) * @(7AA8), so the two tables multiply into a single value.
  - 037840: fcmp/gt fr8,fr15 compares that product with fr15, the float loaded from 0xFFFF99B4 at 03772C. The product is therefore a threshold on that value.
  - 037844-03785E: if 99B4 > threshold, the counter at @(r13,r15) is incremented through uint8_add_sat and the counter at @(r13+4,r15) is zeroed. Here r13 = extu.b r4, the function argument.
  - 037860-037886: otherwise the other counter is incremented, and the first counter is reset once the second exceeds the byte constant at 0xCBBFA.
  - 037792-037796: the whole compare is skipped (counters zeroed) when gbr byte 40 (0xFFFF7AA0) is 0.
  - 03796C-0379B2: a counter >= the byte constant at 0xCBBFB makes one of the four floats at 0xFFFF7A78 (r12-48) increase by the constant at 0xCC2E4, bounded by float_min/float_clamp_range. 0379E2-037A0A: otherwise they decay by the constant at 0xCC2E8.

### 0xAB56C -- unknown — MAF-indexed factor (1.40→1.265) written to RAM 0xFFFF97A4

1D x7 uint16, data 0xC60A4, range 1.265 .. 1.4. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looks up a dimensionless value (1.40 at low airflow, falling to 1.265 above about 11.7) from the float at 0xFFFF63CC. It stores the result to 0xFFFF97A4. A sibling table, 0xAB580, is looked up from the same input and stored next to it at 0xFFFF97A8. What consumes either value is not in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float read from 0xFFFF63CC (labelled maf_smoothed_gps [TRACED]). If the label is right this is mass airflow in g/s, with breakpoints 0..14.06, i.e. the idle and low-flow region only.
- **Output units:** dimensionless (scale 6.1035e-05 = 1/16384, so a ratio or multiplier); physical meaning unknown
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF97A4 is not in the listing, so nothing fixes the quantity's meaning. The axis identity rests on the maf_smoothed_gps label, and labels in this project have been wrong before.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for code that loads 0xFFFF97A4 and 0xFFFF97A8, either as literal-pool constants or as GBR/displacement accesses around 0xFFFF97A8. See what each value is multiplied by, compared against or stored to. Also decode table 0xAB580, since the pair's relationship may reveal the meaning. Separately, confirm that 0xFFFF63CC really is MAF by checking its writer.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x072588/0x07258A: mov.l @(0x072644),r2 (=0xFFFF63CC) then fmov.s @r2,fr4. The lookup input is the float at 0xFFFF63CC.
  - 0x07258C/0x07258E/0x072590: r4=0x000AB56C (this descriptor), jsr table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830. The input is also saved in fr14 in the delay slot at 0x072592.
  - 0x072594-0x072598: r6=0xFFFF97A8, r0=-4, fmov.s fr0,@(r0,r6). The result is stored to 0xFFFF97A4 and nothing else is done with it here: no clamp, no multiply.
  - 0x07259A-0x0725A4: the second table 0xAB580 is looked up with the same input (fmov fr14,fr4) and stored to 0xFFFF97A8. This function only builds a pair of MAF-indexed values for some other routine.
  - 0x0725A6-0x0725AA: the function returns with no further use of either value, so the consumer is elsewhere.

### 0xAB688 -- Coolant-temperature-dependent diagnostic threshold, baro-compensated (compared against FFFF6BC4)

1D x10 uint16, data 0xC62A4, range 70.999 .. 200. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Gives a threshold from gated coolant temperature. It is offset by a constant in some fuel modes and multiplied by a barometric-pressure factor, then stored to FFFF6BCC. A diagnostic routine compares it against the value at FFFF6BC4 to set a latched judgement flag at gbr+45 (FFFF6BE9).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (input ect_gated_c, FFFF6354), -40..45
- **Output units:** unknown (raw scale 300/65536; same units as the quantity at FFFF6BC4)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The quantity at FFFF6BC4 is not computed in this listing, so the physical meaning and units of the threshold cannot be fixed. The 200 cells below 0 C are presumably a saturating value that forces or disables the comparison when cold, but which of the two is not shown.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF6BC4 to learn what quantity is being thresholded. Then search for readers of the flags at FFFF6BE9 (gbr+45) and FFFF6BE8 (gbr+44) to see which DTC or monitor they drive.
- **Cited:**
  - 02B7E2-02B7E4: fr4 = @FFFF6354 (ect_gated_c) is the lookup input for descriptor 0xAB688 at 02B7E6/02B7EA.
  - 02B7DA-02B7EC: a first lookup of table 0xAB444 on atm_pressure_current (FFFF6898) has its result moved to fr14 in the delay slot of the second jsr.
  - 02B812-02B81A: fr8 = 0, or the constant at 0xC5638 when fuel_mode_flags (FFFF8E46) is nonzero; 02B81C adds fr8 to this table's output.
  - 02B81E-02B822: the sum is multiplied by fr14 (the baro factor) and stored to @(-92,r13) = FFFF6BCC.
  - 02B800: fr5 = @(-100,r13) = FFFF6BC4.
  - 02B8A6-02B8AC: FFFF6BCC is reloaded and tested fcmp/gt fr5,fr8 (threshold > FFFF6BC4). If true, r1 = 1 and is stored to gbr+45 (02B8B8), then copied to gbr+107 as the latched previous state (02B8E0-02B8E2).
  - 02B7EE-02B7F4: the whole judgement is gated by r5, derived from dtc_master_enable (FFFFB71C), and by r4, a condition built from a maturation counter, timing_final_advance and accumulated ratios. This places it in a diagnostic/monitor routine.

### 0xAB6C4 -- Self-test (modes 1/2) copy of a 0-100% normalised-ratio shaping curve; the normal-mode curve is descriptor 0xAB134

1D x3 float32, data 0xC631C, range 0 .. 100. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

When the function is called with a non-zero mode byte (r4 = 1 or 2), it loads this descriptor in place of 0xAB134. It then feeds the 1D lookup a value computed entirely from ROM constants: (a - clamp(b)) * 100 / c, clamped to 0..100. The output is stored to 0xFFFF64F8 in mode 1 or 0xFFFF64FC otherwise, which looks like a known-answer self-check of the normal-mode computation rather than live control.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: normalised ratio in percent (0..100), formed as (difference * 100.0) / denominator and clamped 0..100 by float_clamp_range. In modes 1/2 the inputs are ROM constants 0xC4B98/0xC4B9C, 0xC4BA0/0xC4BA4, 0xC4BA8, 0xC4BAC, 0xC4BB0/0xC4BB4, 0xC4BB8, 0xC4BBC/0xC4BC0, 0xC4BC4/0xC4BC8 and 0xC4BCC.
- **Output units:** percent-like 0..100 (same scale as input; physical quantity unknown)
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity of the normalised percentage is not fixed by this listing. It depends on what the normal-mode RAM inputs (0xFFFF64A0, 0xFFFF6520-based offsets, 0xFFFF21DC) are, and on whether 0xAB134 holds the same curve. The 'self-test' reading rests on the mode-1/2 inputs being constants and on the outputs going to separate RAM slots whose readers are not shown.
- **Next step:** Diff the data and axes of descriptor 0xAB134 against 0xAB6C4 (0,40,100 -> 0,50,100). Then search for readers of 0xFFFF64F8 and 0xFFFF64FC, to see whether they are compared against an expected constant or a fault-set routine. Also search for callers of 0x0BB5D0, and the value they pass in r4, to confirm that modes 1/2 are a periodic self-check.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BB604/0BB606: extu.b r4,r13; tst r13; bf selects path. With r13=0, 0BB642 loads r12=0xAB134. With r13=1 (0BB692) or other non-zero (0BB6C6), r12=0xAB6C4, this table.
  - 0BB66A-0BB69C and 0BB69E-0BB6CE: in non-zero modes fr13, fr12, fr14, fr15, fr9/fr8, stack28, stack32, stack44 and stack48 are all loaded from ROM constants at 0xC4B98..0xC4BCC, not from RAM.
  - 0BB6E6-0BB71E: stack0 = max/min chain over (fr13-fr12), fr14, fr15 and stack48. In non-zero modes all of these are constants.
  - 0BB77A-0BB7AA: fr4 = (stack0 - float_clamp_range(stack44, stack28, stack32)) * 100.0 (literal 0x42C8 at 0BB8F8), then float_safe_div by stack52, then clamped to [0,100].
  - 0BB7B0-0BB7BA: table_lookup_1D(r12) on that value; result goes to stack8.
  - 0BB84E-0BB860: if r13==1, stack8 is stored to r11-40 = 0xFFFF64F8; otherwise to r11-36 = 0xFFFF64FC. The second lookup result in fr15 (0BB7F8) is only stored on the r13==0 path (0BB846), so it is discarded in modes 1/2.
  - 0BB816-0BB83E: in normal mode, results are also stored bit-inverted (not r,r0 then mov.l to gbr+120/124/128). Redundant complement storage suggests a safety-monitored path, consistent with a known-answer self-check in the other modes.

### 0xAB6D0 -- Vehicle-speed derating factor (1.0 to 0.5 over 150..200 km/h), fixed-input variant used by mode 1/2 of a diagnostic-weight routine

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6330, range 0.5 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

When the routine is called with a nonzero argument, this two-point table is looked up with a ROM constant as its input instead of live vehicle speed. It yields a factor that falls from 1.0 at 150 to 0.5 at 200, or falls back to 1.0. The result is stored to FFFF6508 (argument 1) or FFFF650C (argument 2); what consumes it is not in this listing.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: by analogy with the live branch, a vehicle-speed-like quantity (km/h, 150..200). In this call it is fed by the ROM float at 0xC4BF8 (argument 1) or 0xC4BFC (argument 2), not by a sensor.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier/weight (1.0 = no reduction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The axis meaning is borrowed from the live branch's vehicle_speed input, but here the input is a ROM constant whose value is unread. What the stored factor multiplies is not shown. If 0xC4BF8/0xC4BFC is below 150, the output is a constant 1.0 and the table is effectively inert.
- **Next step:** Read the float32 constants at 0xC4BF8, 0xC4BFC, 0xC4BF0, 0xC4BF4, 0xF890C and 0xF8910 to see whether this lookup is reached and where on the 150..200 ramp it lands. Then search for readers of FFFF6508 and FFFF650C to find what the factor scales.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA08 extu.b r4,r10 / 0BBA0C bf/s 0x0BBA5C: a nonzero argument takes the path that loads this descriptor at 0BBA90 (mov.l ->r12 = 0xAB6D0).
  - 0BBA78/0BBA7C and 0BBA8C/0BBA8E: fr15 is loaded from ROM constant 0xC4BF8 or 0xC4BFC. 0BBACC fmov fr15,fr4 / 0BBAD2 mov r12,r4 / 0BBAD0 jsr @r5 (table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830): this table's input is that ROM constant.
  - Live branch 0BBA38: fr15 = vehicle_speed_kmh (0xFFFF65FC) feeds the same lookup slot with the tables at 0xAB0CC..0xAB108. So the slot is speed-indexed, which fits breakpoints of 150/200 km/h.
  - 0BBAC8 fcmp/gt fr14,fr6 / bt 0x0BBAD8: this table is used only when fr14 (rpm in the live path, ROM constant 0xC4BF0/0xC4BF4 here) is not below the threshold at 0xF890C/0xF8910. Otherwise it falls through to 0BBAF4 fldi1 fr8, a default of 1.0.
  - 0BBAD6 fmov.s fr0,@r15, then 0BBBC4..0BBBD4: the result is stored to @(-4,FFFF650C) = FFFF6508 if the argument is 1, else to FFFF650C. The live path instead stores to FFFF64DC (0BBB08) plus a complement at FFFF6528 (0BBB10).
  - diag_check_P0137 (0BBA20) and diag_flag_reader_cluster_start (0BBA1A) are called in the live path only; that diagnostic context is a label hint.

### 0xAB6DC -- unknown — 0..1 ramp factor, the stopped-engine branch used only in the r4≠0 (fixed-input) call of 0x0BB9F8; sibling of the table at descriptor 0xAB128

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6340, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

A straight 0→1 ramp over an input of 0..100. It is used only when the function is called with a non-zero argument. In that call its input is a fixed ROM constant, not a live sensor, and its result (1.0 by default) is written to FFFF650C or FFFF6508.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the input (FFFF64A8 in the live call, constants 0xC4BE8/0xC4BEC here) and of the 0..1 output is not established by any consumer in this listing. Also, the input being fixed implies this lookup always returns the same value, but the constants themselves are unread.
- **Next step:** Read the floats at 0xC4BE8 and 0xC4BEC: if they are fixed, this table is effectively a constant. Find callers of 0x0BB9F8 with r4=1 or 2. Search for readers of FFFF6508 and FFFF650C (and FFFF64DC) to see what the factor scales, and identify the writer of FFFF64A8.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBA08/0BBA0C: r10 = extu.b r4. When non-zero, the branch to 0BBA5C replaces the live inputs with ROM constants: fr12 from 0xC4BE8 or 0xC4BEC, fr14 from 0xC4BF0 or 0xC4BF4, fr15 from 0xC4BF8 or 0xC4BFC, r9 from byte 0xC4734 or 0xC4735, and r8 = 1 or 0.
  - 0BBA90/0BBA92: in that same branch r12=0xAB6D0 and r14=0xAB6DC (this table). In the r4==0 branch the same registers hold r14=0xAB128 (0BBA56) and r12=0xAB0CC/AB0E0/AB0F4/AB108 (0BBA44-0BBA54). So this table stands in for 0xAB128.
  - 0BBAA2-0BBAB4 and 0BBAC8: fr6 is a constant (0xF890C or 0xF8910). fcmp/gt fr14,fr6 sets T when fr6 > fr14, i.e. when fr14 (rpm_current in the live call) is below that constant; this table is reached only on that branch.
  - 0BBAD8-0BBAE2: it is reached only if r9==7 and r8==0. In the live call r8 is engine_running_flag and r9 comes from FFFF5E93, so this is the engine-not-running, state-7 branch.
  - 0BBAE4-0BBAEE: fr4=fr12, r4=r14, jsr table_lookup_1D, and the result is stored to @r15.
  - 0BBAF4: on all other paths the result is 1.0 (fldi1).
  - 0BBBC4-0BBBD4: with r10!=0 the stored result goes to FFFF6508 (r10==1) or FFFF650C (otherwise). With r10==0 it goes to FFFF64DC, and its bitwise NOT (computed on the raw word, 0BBB0A-0BBB10) goes to FFFF6528.

### 0xAB6E8 -- RPM-indexed divisor that normalises the 2D-table X input (mode 1/2 variant of table AF144)

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6350, range 0.5 .. 1. Triage hint: engine control.

In modes 1 and 2 (r4 = 1 or 2) the function looks this table up with fr15 (probably RPM) and divides fr14 by the result. That ratio is the X input to three 2D lookups. Two of those are blended by the weight at FFFF90A8 and added to fr13, and the third is the upper clamp bound; the result goes to FFFF8624 (mode 1) or FFFF8628 (mode 2). Mode 0 does the same with table AF144 and live RAM inputs, so this table is the mode-1/2 copy of AF144.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That the axis is RPM. The only support is the unverified LoadxRPM names on the 2D descriptors and the 800..4000 breakpoints. Also, in modes 1/2 the axis input is a fixed ROM constant, so what the mode represents and whether the outputs are ever used is not shown.
- **Next step:** Read the ROM floats at 0xC4C90-0xC4CA4 to see the fixed fr14/fr15/fr13 used in modes 1/2. Find the callers of 0xBC46C and the r4 values they pass. Search for readers of FFFF8624, FFFF8628 and FFFF86C4 to see whether mode-1/2 results feed anything.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BC4A2-0BC4C0: when r13 (from r4) is 1 or 2, fr14, fr15 and fr13 are loaded from ROM constants 0xC4C90/0xC4C94, 0xC4C98/0xC4C9C and 0xC4CA0/0xC4CA4. Mode 0 (0BC48C-0BC49C) loads them from RAM FFFF8558, FFFF679C and FFFF8578 and uses descriptor AF144 instead of AB6E8.
  - 0BC4C2-0BC4D2: this descriptor goes to gbr+496, then table_lookup_1D is called with fr4 = fr15.
  - 0BC4D4-0BC4D8: the result fr0 is stored to FFFF867C+72 = FFFF86C4.
  - 0BC4DA-0BC4E2: float_safe_div is called with fr4 = fr14 and fr5 = the table value; the quotient is saved at @r15.
  - 0BC4E4-0BC504: three table_lookup_2D calls (AF22C, AF248, then ABCBC from gbr+568) each take fr4 = quotient and fr5 = fr15.
  - 0BC506-0BC514: fr13 += (1 - w)*T(AF22C) + w*T(AF248), where w is read from FFFF90A8 at 0BC480.
  - 0BC51A-0BC520: float_clamp_range(fr4 = sum, fr5 = 0, fr6 = T(ABCBC)).
  - 0BC552-0BC566: the clamped result is stored to FFFF8624 in mode 1 and to FFFF8628 in mode 2. In mode 0 it goes to FFFF8550, with the quotient at FFFF8554 and complements at gbr+300 and gbr+352.

### 0xAB6F4 -- Self-test (known-answer) stand-in for table 0xAF1CC in a redundant monitoring calculation: fixed input 50 gives 30

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6360, range 0 .. 30. Triage hint: engine control.

The table is read only when the mode byte r4 is nonzero. In that case the function feeds it the constant 50.0 instead of live RAM, so it always returns 30. That result then runs through the same chain as normal mode: a 2D lookup, a multiply by an atmospheric-pressure correction, a call to 0xBDD9C, a floor and a clamp. The finished answer is stored to FFFF87BC (mode 1) or FFFF87C0 (mode 2), which looks like a known-answer check of the monitoring arithmetic.

- **Evidence class:** inferred

- **Output units:** Same units as the output of 0xAF1CC, which is the X input of the 2D table 0xAF398 (test-mode twin 0xABFC4). Physical unit unknown.
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two points are assumed rather than shown. First, that the nonzero-mode path is a known-answer self-test and not a real operating mode, since the caller and the consumers of FFFF87BC and FFFF87C0 are not in the listing. Second, that this table mirrors 0xAF1CC in meaning; that rests only on sharing the same argument slot.
- **Next step:** First, find the readers of FFFF87BC and FFFF87C0 and check whether they compare against stored constants, which would confirm a known-answer test. Second, find the callers of 0xBD7C6 to see how the mode bytes 1 and 2 are passed. Third, identify the axis source FFFF87B0 used by 0xAF1CC to give the input quantity a unit.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BD7D2-0BD7D6: extu.b r4,r12 / tst r12,r12 / bf 0x0BD7F2. Mode 0 loads live input fr4=@(FFFF87D4-36) and descriptor r4=0xAF1CC. A nonzero mode jumps to the path that loads this table.
  - 0BD7F2-0BD7F4: fmov.s @(0x0BD8D4) puts 0x42480000 = 50.0 into fr4 as the X input for this table. With breakpoints 0/50 and values 0/30, the result is always exactly 30.
  - 0BD806: r4=0xAB6F4 is loaded in the same slot where mode 0 loads 0xAF1CC (0BD7E2). The other parallel pairs are 0xAB700 vs 0xAF194 and 0xABFC4 vs 0xAF398. fr13 is the constant 20.0 vs @F8AF0, and fr14 is the constant 1200.0 vs @FFFF679C.
  - 0BD7FC/0BD802: in the parallel lookup, fr15 = 536.0 (mode 1) or 760.0 (mode 2). This replaces atm_pressure_current [TRACED], so the test path feeds constant vectors into every input.
  - 0BD81A/0BD830: the result goes to fr12 and then becomes fr4, the X argument of table_lookup_2D on r13 (0xABFC4), with Y = fr14.
  - 0BD836: the 2D result is multiplied (fmul) by the 1D lookup of 0xAB700. 0BD840 calls 0xBDD9C. 0BD84C applies float_max against @FFFF8C14, and 0BD85A applies float_clamp_range between @F8AEC and fr13.
  - 0BD884-0BD88E: in mode 0 the result goes to FFFF8798, and its bitwise complement is stored to @FFFF87D4. That complemented copy is a redundancy pattern typical of safety monitoring.
  - 0BD89A/0BD8A0: in modes 1 and 2 the result goes only to FFFF87BC or FFFF87C0, and is presumably compared elsewhere against an expected value.

### 0xAB70C -- Upper clamp (ceiling) for a limited/ramped value, modes 1-3 (quantity unidentified)

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6380, range 100 .. 200. Triage hint: engine control.

For calls with r4 = 1, 2 or 3, this table supplies the ceiling in out = max(floor, min(table(x), base − table2(x))). The result goes to 0xFFFF863C, 0xFFFF8640 or 0xFFFF8644 depending on the mode. Its X input is a ROM constant, not live RPM, so it acts as one fixed value per mode.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: a float read from ROM constant 0xC4D68, 0xC4D6C or 0xC4D70 (selected by mode). Units unknown; 0..4000 is RPM-like, but the mode-0 path feeds rpm_current to a different table (0xAF1A8).
- **Output units:** same units as the clamped quantity and its floor constants (0xC4D50/54/58); unknown
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity being clamped is unknown. The X input is a ROM constant whose value and units have not been read, and the consumers of 0xFFFF863C/40/44 are not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xC4D50..0xC4D70 to get the floors, bases and X inputs for each mode. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF863C, 0xFFFF8640, 0xFFFF8644 and 0xFFFF86E0, and for callers of 0x0BC886 with r4 = 1..3, to find what quantity is clamped.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BC894 extu.b r4,r10 selects a mode. 0BC8D6, 0BC8EE and the fall-through set fr15, the [sp+4] base and fr14 from ROM 0xC4D50..0xC4D70. They then branch to 0BC914, which installs descriptor 0xAB70C at gbr+480 and 0xAB718 at gbr+484.
  - Mode 0 (0BC89A-0BC8D2) instead uses fr14 = rpm_current, fr15 = @0xFFFF8564 and descriptors 0xAF1A8/0xAF1B4, so this table is used only in modes 1-3.
  - 0BC924-0BC932: table_lookup_1D(gbr+480, fr4=fr14) is stored to 0xFFFF86A4+60 = 0xFFFF86E0.
  - 0BC936 fcmp/gt fr15,fr8: if the lookup is not above the floor fr15, the result becomes fr15 (0BCA24).
  - 0BC952-0BC974: otherwise the second table (0xAB718) is subtracted from the base at [sp+4]. float_min(base − table2, thisTable) makes this table a ceiling.
  - 0BCA26 float_max(result, fr15) applies the floor.
  - 0BCA62-0BCA88 store the clamped result per mode to 0xFFFF863C, 0xFFFF8640 or 0xFFFF8644.

### 0xAB718 -- Margin subtracted from a reference value to cap a learned/limited float (alternate-mode copy of AF1B4); physical identity unknown

1D x2 float32, data 0xC6390, range 0 .. 20. Triage hint: engine control.

In non-zero modes of function 0xBC886 this table is looked up at a ROM-constant X. The result is subtracted from a reference value (fr8), and that difference caps a companion lookup: out = max(floor, min(table_AB70C(X), ref − this_table(X))). It is therefore a margin or offset that lowers an upper bound, growing linearly from 0 at X=0 to 20 at X=4000.

- **Output units:** same units as the reference value fr8 (ROM constants 0xC4D5C/60/64 in modes 1-3; RAM 0xFFFF85E4 in mode 0); physical unit unknown
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unconfirmed. First, the quantities behind ref, floor and output are unknown. Second, it is unknown whether this function is ever called with r4≠0; in that path X is a fixed ROM constant, so only one point of this table is ever used.
- **Next step:** First, find the callers of 0x0BC886 and the r4 values they pass, to learn whether modes 1-3 run. Second, read the float constants at 0xC4D50-0xC4D70 to see which X is actually sampled and whether ref − this_value can ever be below table_AB70C(X). Third, search for readers of 0xFFFF863C/8640/8644 and 0xFFFF86E4 to identify the consumed quantity.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BC894/0BC8D6/0BC8EE: the byte argument r10 selects mode 0, 1, 2 or other; only the non-zero modes reach 0BC914-0BC91A.
  - 0BC914-0BC91A: in non-zero modes gbr+480=0xAB70C and gbr+484=0xAB718 (this table). Mode 0 instead uses 0xAF1A8/0xAF1B4 (0BC8BA-0BC8C0).
  - 0BC8DA-0BC912: in non-zero modes fr15 (floor), fr8 (reference, saved to @(4,r15)) and fr14 (X) are all loaded from ROM constants at 0xC4D50..0xC4D70. Flags r11-r14 are cleared at 0BC91C-0BC922.
  - 0BC924-0BC932: the first lookup (gbr+480) at X=fr14 is stored to 0xFFFF86E0 (86A4+60).
  - 0BC936-0BC950: the second lookup runs only if lookup1 > fr15 and all four flags are 0.
  - 0BC952-0BC962: this table (gbr+484) is looked up at X=fr14 and stored to 0xFFFF86E4 (86A4+64).
  - 0BC964-0BC978: fr4 = ref − this_value; float_min(fr4, lookup1) is stored to @r15.
  - 0BCA26-0BCA2C: float_max(@r15, fr15) is applied, so the result is floored at fr15.
  - 0BCA62-0BCA88: the mode 1/2/other result is stored to 0xFFFF863C, 0xFFFF8640 or 0xFFFF8644 respectively (86A4−104/−100/−96).

### 0xAB730 -- Monitor/self-test reference curve, evaluated at fixed ROM test inputs (not a live calibration)

1D x2 float32, data 0xC63B0, range 300 .. 400. Triage hint: unassigned.

Lookup on this function's mode 1 and mode 2 paths only. The X input and the min() cap both come from fixed ROM constants. The result therefore never depends on engine state, which suggests it serves as a test vector for checking the lookup and min path, while mode 0 does the real work with RAM inputs and a different table.

- **Axes:** X input is a ROM constant: 0xC4DA0 in mode 1, 0xC4DA4 in mode 2. The breakpoints 0..4000 might mean rpm in the real-path analogue, but that is not shown.
- **Output units:** unknown (same units as whatever table 0xAF158 produces on the mode-0 path; values 300..400)

The comparator is not in this listing, and the four ROM constants were not decoded. If the constants vary with calibration, the paths could instead be fixed-operating-point defaults.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xC4D98, 0xC4D9C, 0xC4DA0 and 0xC4DA4. Search for readers of 0xFFFF8658 and 0xFFFF865C to see whether they are compared to expected constants and raise a fault.   - Mode 0 (0BC590-0BC59C): fr4 = RAM @0xFFFF679C, fr14 = RAM @0xFFFF855C, table = 0xAF158, a different table.
  - Mode 1 (0BC5A4-0BC5B0): fr14 = ROM @0xC4D98, descriptor 0xAB730, fr4 = ROM @0xC4DA0. Both the input and the cap are flash constants.
  - Mode 2 (0BC5B2-0BC5BC): fr14 = ROM @0xC4D9C, descriptor 0xAB730, fr4 = ROM @0xC4DA4. Again both are constants.
  - 0BC5C2: table_lookup_1D(descriptor, fr4). 0BC5CA: float_min(fr14 cap, result) clamps the output to the cap.

### 0xAB970 -- Cell-grid column breakpoint, probably RPM (13 columns of a 6x13 index grid, column index → breakpoint value)

1D x13 float32, data 0xC6F1C, range 700 .. 6500. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Converts the column part of a 0..77 cell counter (gbr+85 = FFFFA01B, mod 13) into a breakpoint value of 700..6500, most likely RPM. The function compares that value against a live quantity (float at FFFFA164+52) to decide whether the selected cell should step one column left (index-1) or right (index+1). It behaves like a cell-tracking or cell-walking routine over a 78-cell (6x13) map.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: column index 0..12 = (byte at FFFFA01B) mod 13, converted to float by fmac_interp_uint8 with gain 1, offset 0
- **Output units:** probably rpm (inferred from the 700..6500 range and from the comparison with FFFFA164+52; not proven)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That the float at FFFFA164+52 (fr15) is engine speed in rpm. The listing never shows where it is written, so the rpm unit rests only on the value range and the left/right stepping logic.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFFA164+0x34 (FFFFA198). If it copies the engine RPM variable, the unit is confirmed. Also search readers of FFFF9A8C and FFFF9A90, and decode 0x7C4D4 to learn what the 78-cell grid indexed by FFFFA01B is, e.g. a learning or monitor map.
- **Cited:**
  - 07A3DA-07A3FC: the byte at gbr+85 (FFFFA01B) is divided by 13 using the magic multiplier 0x4EC4EC4F with two shar, multiplied back by 13 and subtracted. That gives idx mod 13, the column 0..12.
  - 07A400-07A40A: fmac_interp_uint8 is called with fr4=1 and fr5=0, so the column is converted to float unchanged, and the result is stored to FFFF9A90.
  - 07A40C-07A418: this table is looked up with that column, and the result is stored to FFFF9A8C (FFFF9A90-4).
  - 07A428: gbr+85 wraps at 77, so the counter covers 78 cells = 6 rows x 13.
  - 07A50A-07A512: the stored table value at FFFF9A8C is reloaded into fr8 and compared with fcmp/gt fr15,fr8. fr15 is the float at FFFFA164+52, loaded at 07A34A.
  - 07A516-07A5D0: when the table value is greater than fr15, the code rejects left-edge columns (counter equal to 0, 13, 26, 39, 52, 65, plus ROM-offset variants) and calls 0x7C4D4 with idx-1 (r12).
  - 07A5EC-07A6B6: otherwise it rejects right-edge columns (12, 25, 38, 51, 64, 77, plus offset variants) and calls 0x7C4D4 with idx+1 (r14). This is a move toward the column whose breakpoint brackets fr15.
  - 07A442-07A44A: a sibling table 0xAB97C is indexed by the same fr15, and its result is compared with engine_load_current at FFFF63F8, used as a gating threshold.

### 0xAB9E8 -- Diagnostic monitor test-value pass-through clamp (identity 0..4), normal-state variant

1D x2 float32, data 0xC7358, range 0 .. 4. Triage hint: unassigned.

A two-point identity map (0→0, 4→4) that passes a float monitor input through unchanged but clamps it to 0..4. The result is compared against a threshold in the monitor's parameter block to choose between two diagnostic outcome calls, and is later quantised and sent to a diagnostic reporting routine.

- **Axes:** X: the float read through the pointer at offset 76 of the parameter-block struct passed in r4 (@r15). Its physical quantity is not identified in this listing.
- **Output units:** Same units as the input, because the map is identity. The quantity is unknown.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the input at struct+76 and of the threshold at struct+68 is not visible here. The roles of 0x9ED90 and 0x9EDEC rest on labels only, and the parameter-block struct is filled in by a caller outside the listing.
- **Next step:** Find the callers of 0x06A632 and decode the r4 struct they pass, especially the pointers at +76 and +68, to identify the input quantity. Also decode descriptor 0xAB594 to confirm the other variant is likewise an identity or clamp map.
- **Cited:**
  - Decoded data: X breakpoints 0,4 and values 0,4 make this an identity map with clamping at 0 and 4. It does not reshape its input.
  - 06A64E/06A650: when check_diag_state returns 2, the code branches to 06A728, which loads the alternate descriptor 0xAB594 at 06A73A instead of 0xAB9E8 at 06A664. This table is therefore one of two variants selected by diagnostic state.
  - 06A7B2-06A7BA: fr4 = @(@(r15+0)+76) is the lookup input, and the call goes to table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830.
  - 06A7BE-06A7C0: the result fr0 is stored to stack slot @(48,r15).

### 0xABBBC -- Diagnostic deviation threshold vs vehicle speed (|FFFF96B4 - FFFF7E68| limit for fault counter at FFFF96B8)

1D x8 float32, data 0xC7AB0, range 5.8635 .. 11.647. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Gives a speed-dependent limit for the absolute difference between two float signals, the one at 0xFFFF96B4 and the one at 0xFFFF7E68. While the difference exceeds the limit, a saturating counter at 0xFFFF96B8 counts up. Once it reaches the calibration byte at 0xC47C9, a monitor with code 0x9A is flagged and a second counter is advanced. Otherwise the counter is cleared and dtc_set_code(0x9A) is called.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: the float read from 0xFFFF65FC, labelled vehicle_speed_kmh [TRACED], so probably km/h (breakpoints 0..300)
- **Output units:** Same units as the float at 0xFFFF7E68 (labelled fuel_timing_corr, but the name is unverified); this is a maximum allowed absolute deviation
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the two compared signals are. The value at 0xFFFF96B4 may be a latched or previous copy of 0xFFFF7E68 (a rate or step check) or an independent second source (a plausibility check), and the label 'fuel_timing_corr' on 0xFFFF7E68 is not trustworthy. The table is clearly a fault threshold, but the physical quantity it bounds is unknown.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF96B4 and 0xFFFF7E68 to find out what is being differenced. Also find what sets the enable byte 0xFFFF96B9, and map monitor index 0x9A in DTC_DefinitionTable (0x9A834) to a P-code.
- **Cited:**
  - 06F6F0-06F6F2: fr14 is loaded from 0xFFFF65FC (vehicle_speed_kmh). 06F712 then passes fr14 as the lookup input in the delay slot of the jsr to table_lookup_1D at 06F710.
  - 06F6EC-06F6EE: fr5 is loaded from 0xFFFF7E68. 06F708 loads fr4 from @(-4,r1), which is 0xFFFF96B4. The jsr at 06F706 goes to float_abs_diff, and 06F70A saves the result as fr15 = |a-b|.
  - 06F714: fcmp/gt fr0,fr15 compares the absolute difference against the table output fr0.
  - 06F718-06F728: if the difference is greater, uint8_add_sat(+1) is applied to the counter at 0xFFFF96B8.
  - 06F72A-06F736: otherwise dtc_set_code(0x9A) is called and the counter is cleared.
  - 06F738-06F746: when the counter is >= the byte at 0xC47C9, cl_variant_init_dispatch(0x9A) is called. 06F74A-06F756 then increments the stack byte (copied from 0xFFFF24F4). At 06F7E8-06F7F0 that byte is compared against the byte at 0xC47CA, and 06F81C-06F824 packs it back to 0xFFFF24F4.
  - 06F6FA-06F700: the whole check runs only when the byte at 0xFFFF96B9 == 1, and the listing does not show what sets that byte.

### 0xABBC8 -- Fallback-mode 0..1 blend weight for the output channel saved to 0xFFFF85EC (counterpart of normal-mode table 0xAF1D8)

2D 2x2 uint8, data 0xC7AE0, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: engine control.

Gives a weight w, clamped to 0..1, that blends two candidate values: out = w*min(lookup@-124, lookup@-108) + (1-w)*min(lookup@-112, lookup@-100). The result is handed to desc_read_float_safe for 0xFFFF85EC. In the modes that use this table it is overwritten with a ROM constant from 0xC4C58, 0xC4C5C or 0xC4C60.; Lookup input fr5 = stack+40. In the normal path this slot holds accel_pedal_angle (0xFFFF64D8). In the modes that use this table it is overwritten with a ROM constant from 0xC4C40, 0xC4C44 or 0xC4C48.; Which of fr4/fr5 is the X axis (0,100) and which is the Y axis (0,50) depends on how table_lookup_2D orders its arguments. That ordering is not shown here.
- **Output units:** dimensionless weight 0..1 (blend fraction)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the two blended quantities is unknown. They come from the ABC30/ABC4C/ABC68/ABCA0 lookups, whose names are unverified. The meaning of the 0xFFFF85EC output, and of modes r4=1..3 (likely a fallback or test state), is also unknown. Axis order depends on the unconfirmed table_lookup_2D argument convention.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF85EC (and of its complement at 0xFFFF8688, i.e. gbr+8) and of 0xFFFF85A0 to learn what the blended value drives. Read the floats at 0xC4C40-0xC4C60 to see which table cell each mode hits. Find the callers of 0x0BCEB2 to learn when r4 = 1, 2 or 3.

  - 0BCFFC/0BD05C/0BD0B6: mov.l 0xABBC8 -> @(252,gbr). This happens only on the r14!=0 branches (0BCEF4 bf/s 0x0BCFAC). The normal path at 0BCF40 stores 0xAF1D8 to the same slot.
  - 0BCFC6-0BCFDC (and 0BD026.., 0BD080..): stack+40, +36 and +44 are overwritten with floats from 0xC4C40.., so the speed and pedal inputs are constants in these modes.
  - 0BD2D8-0BD2E2: fr4=@(44,r15), fr5=@(40,r15), r4=@(252,gbr), jsr table_lookup_2D (0xBE8E4).
  - 0BD2E8-0BD2F4: the result is stored to 0xFFFF8784-92, then float_clamp_range (0xBE56C) is called with bounds fr5=0 and fr6=1.
  - 0BD2FC-0BD306: fr14 = w*fr14 + (1-w)*stack68, i.e. a linear blend. fr14 comes from float_min at 0BD254 (min of lookups stored at -124 and -108). stack68 comes from float_min at 0BD28C (min of lookups at -112 and -100).
  - 0BD308: the blend goes to stack+8. Then 0BD45C-0BD464: desc_read_float_safe(r4=0xFFFF85EC, fr4=stack+8). The bitwise complement is stored to @(8,gbr) at 0BD46E.
  - 0BD3FE-0BD404: the weight itself (stack76) is stored to 0xFFFF8614-76 = 0xFFFF85A0.
  - 0BD346-0BD34C and 0BD36A-0BD370: stack+8 also replaces the stack+52 value, depending on flags r9/r10.

2D 2x2 uint8, data 0xC7AF4, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: engine control.

### 0xABCBC -- Output ceiling (upper clamp) for the blended two-map value, alternate-mode path (r4 = 1 or 2), companion of AF264

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7C80, range 0 .. 5.0011. Triage hint: engine control.

When the routine is called with mode byte r4 != 0, this table is looked up at fixed ROM-constant inputs. Its result is used as the upper bound in float_clamp_range([0, table]). The thing being clamped is a base value plus a (1-k)/k blend of tables AF22C and AF248, where k is the value read from FFFF90A8. The Y/X-to-fr4/fr5 mapping of table_lookup_2D is not verified.; X (0..10000): probably fr15, the 1D-lookup input, passed in fr5. In modes 1/2 this is ROM const 0xC4C98 (mode 1) or 0xC4C9C (mode 2). In mode 0 it is RAM FFFF679C. Magnitude suggests rpm, but that is not established.
- **Output units:** Same units as the blended AF22C/AF248 result plus base (unknown; dimensionless 0..5 in raw scaling)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The argument order of float_clamp_range (fr5 = lower, fr6 = upper) and the axis-to-register mapping of table_lookup_2D are assumed from routine names and convention, not shown. The physical quantity being limited is not identified anywhere in the listing.

Second, find the callers of 0x0BC46C that pass r4=1 or 2. Third, search for readers of FFFF8624 and FFFF8628 to learn what the clamped value feeds.
