  - 0BC488 bf/s on r13 (= extu.b r4) selects the path. Mode 0 takes fr13/fr14/fr15 from RAM (FFFF8578, FFFF8558, FFFF679C) and descriptors AF144/AF264. Modes 1/2 take them from ROM constants 0xC4C90..0xC4CA4 and descriptors AB6E8/ABCBC (0BC4C2-0BC4C8).
  - 0BC4C8 mov.l r0,@(568,gbr) parks the descriptor. 0BC4FC-0BC500 reload it and call table_lookup_2D with fr4=@r15 (ratio) and fr5=fr15. 0BC504 moves the result to fr6.
  - 0BC4D0-0BC4E0: the 1D lookup of fr15 is stored to FFFF86C4. fr14 is divided by that result via float_safe_div, and the quotient is stored at @r15 and used as the 2D input.
  - 0BC506-0BC514: fr13 += (1-k)*AF22C + k*AF248, where k = @(8,r15) = FFFF90A8, loaded at 0BC480.
  - 0BC51A-0BC520: float_clamp_range is called with fr4=blend, fr5=0 (fldi0) and fr6=this table's result. The table is therefore the upper limit.
  - 0BC55E/0BC564: the clamped result is stored to FFFF867C-88 = FFFF8624 (mode 1) or FFFF867C-84 = FFFF8628 (mode 2). In mode 0 it goes to FFFF8550 (0BC544), with a complement copy at gbr+352.

### 0xABCF4 -- Fixed-input alternate map for caller mode 2 (bilinear X·Y/1000 product map), result to FFFF8664

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7CB0, range 0 .. 400. Triage hint: engine control.

The function 0x0BD68E takes a mode byte in r4. Only in mode 2 does it pass this descriptor (via gbr+540) to the lookup wrapper 0x0BD D9C, with both inputs loaded from ROM constants at 0xC4DB0 and 0xC4DB8. The result is stored to FFFF8664 (FFFF86B4-80). The data is exactly value = X·Y/1000 at all four corners, so the table works out to a clamped product of its two inputs, here evaluated at a fixed operating point.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (0,100): unknown input; in this call site it is fed a ROM constant (0xC4DB0 or 0xC4DB8 via fr15/fr14), not a live sensor; X axis (2000,4000): unknown input; also fed a ROM constant at this call site; range suggests RPM but that is magnitude-matching only
- **Output units:** unknown; numerically X·Y/1000 (0..400)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Neither the physical meaning of the inputs nor the meaning of the consumer at FFFF8664 is shown. The lookup inputs are ROM constants not decoded here, and it is unknown whether any caller ever invokes 0x0BD68E with r4=2.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xC4DB0 and 0xC4DB8 to get the fixed lookup point and the resulting constant. Search callers of 0x0BD68E for r4=2, and search readers of FFFF8664 (and for comparison FFFF8584, FFFF8660, FFFF8668) to learn what the value feeds.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BD6E0 cmp/eq #2 path: 0BD6E4/0BD6E8 load fr15=@0xC4DB0, fr14=@0xC4DB8 (ROM constants); 0BD6EC/0BD6EE puts 0xABCD8 in gbr+536; 0BD6F0/0BD6F2 puts 0xABCF4 in gbr+540; 0BD6F6 r2=0, 0BD70A r4=0, 0BD70C r5=4
  - 0BD70E tst r2 / 0BD716 cmp/ge r2,r6: with r2=0 and 0<4 the code takes 0BD720 mov.l @(540,gbr),r0, so it selects 0xABCF4, and 0BD72E jsr 0x0BDD9C uses r4=descriptor, fr4=fr15, fr5=fr14
  - Mode 1 (0BD6D8 r2=0, 0BD6DC r4=4, r5=4 so cmp/ge is true) and the default mode (0BD708 r2=1) both take gbr+536 (0xABCD8). Although ABCF4 is loaded on those paths, it is not used there
  - 0BD736 fmov.s fr0,@r15 saves the result; mode-2 branch 0BD768 mov #-80 / 0BD76C fmov.s fr8,@(r0,r2) with r2=0xFFFF86B4 stores it to FFFF8664
  - Mode 0 uses the same 536/540 primary/alternate scheme with live RAM inputs (FFFF8574, FFFF85A0) and tables AF2D4/AF328. The choice there is gated by check_engine_running and byte FFFF5E94 compared against ROM byte 0xF8A87
  - Data: (2000,100)=200, (4000,100)=400, Y=0 row zero. This equals X·Y/1000 exactly, and bilinear interpolation reproduces a product exactly

### 0xABED0 -- unknown – per-sample weight or score table for a diagnostic monitor (integer cells, summed and averaged)

2D 5x4 float32, data 0xC8664, range 0 .. 3000. Triage hint: unassigned.

When a set of float gating conditions holds, an integer value is looked up from two inputs and written to RAM. It is added with saturation into one running total, then another. A uint8 sample counter is incremented, and a quotient (apparently total divided by count) is computed through routine 0x3944. This is an accumulate-and-average pattern typical of an OBD monitor, run once for each of two struct instances (e.g. two banks or sensors).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (10,20,30,40): unknown; float read from the struct pointer at r14+132 (first branch) or r14+180 (second branch), loaded into fr5; X (0,1000,2000,3000,3001): unknown; float from r14+128 / r14+176, loaded into fr4. It is the same quantity that is gated by fcmp/gt against the constant at 0xC5240 just before the lookup
- **Output units:** raw integer counts (16-bit); physical meaning unknown. The float decode shown is wrong.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The descriptor's cell type and data length are evidently mis-decoded, so the true cell values and the axis identities are unknown. That 0x3944 is an integer divide is assumed, not shown.
- **Next step:** Re-decode descriptor 0xABED0's type byte and read 0xC8664 as uint16 (or uint8) cells. Disassemble 0x3944 to confirm it divides. Find the caller(s) of 0x06A2E4 and resolve the struct pointer r14 at offsets 128/132 and 176/180 to name the inputs. Then search readers of the RAM pointed to by r14+208 / r14+(208+40) to see what the averaged result feeds.
- **Cited:**
  - 06A386/06A4C8: the call goes to table_lookup_2D_int (0xBE944), yet the decoded table shows float32. The 'values' are denormals (e.g. 1.121e-44 = 0x00000008, 7.347e-40 = 0x00080000), so the cells are really small integers (uint16 words 0,8,8,0,…,7,0,0x12,0,0x0B,0,0x14…). The last 10 'values' (0,10..50,0..3000) are another table's axes that run past a 40-byte data block.
  - 06A396: mov.w r2,@r6 stores the 16-bit lookup result to the RAM pointer held at r14+(r0+64)
  - 06A3A4/06A3C0: the stored value is fed twice through uint16_add_sat (0xBE554) into consecutive RAM fields, i.e. running sums
  - 06A3DA-06A3E6: a uint8 counter is incremented by 1 with uint8_add_sat and stored
  - 06A3EE-06A41E: if the counter wrapped to 0 a field is zeroed. Otherwise a uint16 at r14+200 and the uint8 at r14+204 are passed to 0x3944 and the result is stored to r14+208. This looks like sum/count.
  - 06A35C-06A366 vs 06A37C-06A382: the float at r14+128 must exceed the constant at 0xC5240, and the same r14+128 is loaded into fr4 as the lookup X input (mirrored at 06A49E/06A4C2 with r14+176)
  - 06A368/06A370: further gating requires fr9=(b-c)*k(0xC5244) > fr7=(a-b) and fr6=(d-e) < const 0xC5248. This is a stability or enable window before sampling.

### 0xABF54 -- Idle-speed diagnostic low-RPM threshold offset vs coolant temperature (ISC 'RPM lower than expected' check, primary variant)

2D 2x11 uint16, data 0xC8A28, range -300 .. -150. Triage hint: ADC / I-O.

Returns a negative RPM offset (-150 when cold, -300 from 20 C up) that is added to the value at FFFF895C. The result is compared against engine speed, and a timer runs while engine speed sits below that threshold. The timer feeds a flag that is gated by dtc_master_enable, which looks like an idle-control 'RPM lower than expected' monitor.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y: coolant temperature in C (fr4 loaded from FFFF6350 ect_current at 02B974-02B976); X: byte at FFFF8970 converted to float (02B964-02B966, 02B98A, 02B992), breakpoints 1 and 8, meaning unknown; both rows are identical, so the axis has no effect
- **Output units:** rpm (offset added to what appears to be a target idle speed)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** That FFFF895C is the target idle speed and FFFF6624 is actual engine speed. Only labels and the direct float compare support this; the writer of 895C is not in the listing. The DTC role of the gbr+26/27 flags is also not shown.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF895C to confirm it is the idle target RPM. Then search readers of FFFF6BEA (gbr+26), FFFF6BEB (gbr+27) and timer FFFF6BD8 to confirm they feed an idle-control DTC (P0506/P0507). Also check what byte FFFF8A9D represents (it selects 0xABF54 vs 0xABF70).
- **Cited:**
  - 02B974/02B976: fr4 = @FFFF6350 (ect_current) is the first lookup argument, consistent with the -40..60 Y breakpoints
  - 02B964/02B966/02B98A/02B992: fr5 = float(byte @FFFF8970) is the second argument (X axis 1..8)
  - 02B978/02B97A/02B98C/02B990/02B994: byte @FFFF8A9D selects this descriptor (0xABF54) when zero, otherwise the sibling 0xABF70
  - 02B970/02B972 then 02B99C: fadd fr0,fr14 adds the result to the float @FFFF895C (clamped_diff_895C)
  - 02B96C/02B96E and 02B99E: fcmp/gt fr13,fr14 compares (895C + table) against @FFFF6624 (rpm_current). This places the table in rpm units, as a threshold below a reference speed
  - 02B9A8-02B9DA: while the threshold exceeds rpm and gbr byte 22 (FFFF6BE6) is set, a counter at FFFF6C18 is accumulated; the counter times 32.0 (literal 0x42000000 at 02BB00) is stored to FFFF6BD8
  - 02BA42-02BA56: FFFF6BD8 is compared with the calibration word at 0xC4A6A. 02BA58-02BA84: the result, gated by the dtc_master_enable-derived flag on the stack, sets flag byte gbr+26 (FFFF6BEA)
  - 02B97C-02B986: a sibling check uses 895C + two constants (0xD2B60, 0xC5650) against rpm at 02BA10, i.e. an 'RPM higher than expected' branch, supporting a target-idle-speed reference

### 0xABFC4 -- Fixed-input reference/self-check copy of a 2D (0–84 axis × 800–4400 axis) model map, inverted after pressure correction (identity unknown)

2D 2x2 float32, data 0xC8B2C, range 5.63 .. 1824.8. Triage hint: engine control.

Read only when the mode byte r4 is non-zero (1 or 2). It is looked up with constant-derived inputs, multiplied by a pressure-dependent factor, then passed to routine 0x0BDD9C with the same table, which appears to solve back for the other axis. The clamped result is stored to FFFF87BC (mode 1) or FFFF87C0 (mode 2). In mode 0 the same code uses a different 2D table (0xAF398) with live RAM inputs, so this looks like a reference or test computation that shadows a live path.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (0, 84): probably fed by fr12, the output of 1D table 0xAB6F4 evaluated at the constant 50.0. The quantity is unknown.; X axis (800, 4400): probably fed by fr14, the constant 1200.0. In the mode-0 path the same register comes from RAM FFFF679C. RPM is plausible but not proven.
- **Output units:** unknown
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The behaviour of 0x0BDD9C (assumed inverse lookup) and the argument order of table_lookup_2D (which of fr4/fr5 is X) are unverified. The physical quantity on the 0–84 axis and in the output is not fixed by anything shown.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x0BDD9C to confirm it inverts a 2D table along one axis. Check table_lookup_2D's fr4/fr5-to-X/Y mapping. Compare the axes and data of the mode-0 table 0xAF398 with this one. Search for readers of FFFF87BC and FFFF87C0 to see what the fixed-condition results are compared against, and find the callers of 0x0BD7C6 to see when r4 = 1 or 2.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BD7D2-0BD7D6: extu.b r4,r12; tst r12,r12; bf 0x0BD7F2. This table's branch runs only when the mode byte is non-zero.
  - 0BD7F2/0BD7F4: fr4 = literal 0x42480000 (50.0), the input to 1D table 0xAB6F4 (r4 at 0BD806). Its result goes to fr12 at 0BD81A.
  - 0BD7F8-0BD804: fr15 = 0x44260000 (664.0) if mode==1, else 0x443E0000 (760.0). 760 looks like sea-level pressure in mmHg and 664 like an altitude case. fr15 is the input to 1D table 0xAB700 (r14), and the result is saved at @(4,r15) by 0BD826.
  - 0BD80E/0BD810-0BD812: r13 = 0xABFC4 (this table); fr14 = 0x44960000 (1200.0). In the mode-0 branch fr14 comes from RAM FFFF679C (0BD7EC-0BD7F0).
  - 0BD828-0BD830: table_lookup_2D(r4=0xABFC4, fr4=fr12, fr5=fr14).
  - 0BD834-0BD838: the 2D result is multiplied by the 0xAB700 output (fr8) and kept in fr15.
  - 0BD83A-0BD844: 0x0BDD9C is called with the same table r13, fr5=fr14 and fr4=the product. The result goes to fr14, consistent with an inverse lookup back onto the other axis.
  - 0BD846-0BD85C: the result passes float_max against RAM FFFF8C14, then float_clamp_range between the constant at 0xF8AEC and fr13. In this branch fr13 = 0x41A00000 (20.0).
  - 0BD890-0BD8A2: the final value is stored to FFFF87D4-24 = FFFF87BC (mode 1) or FFFF87D4-20 = FFFF87C0 (mode 2).
  - Mode 0 (0BD7D8-0BD7F0) uses table 0xAF398 with atm_pressure_current [TRACED] and RAM inputs, and stores a complemented copy at 0BD888-0BD88E (integrity check). That suggests modes 1 and 2 are reference computations at fixed conditions.

### 0xAC26C -- Diagnostic fail-debounce count threshold (case: knock_timing_retard_base <= FFFF44B4), vs float at FFFF9758

1D x4 float32, data 0xF8A44, range 0 .. 9.5. Triage hint: unassigned.

A 1D lookup that returns an integer count threshold. A fault-maturation counter is compared against it, and when the counter reaches the threshold the function sets a fail flag that is packed to FFFF2508 and passed to the DTC maturation logic (eeprom_maturation_check #23). It appears to be a rationality or deviation monitor that compares knock_timing_retard_base against FFFF44B4.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at FFFF9758 (gbr+0, read as @(-32, FFFF9778)); quantity not identified. The breakpoints 8.05/9.5/14.5/23.5 look like battery voltage, but that is a magnitude match only.
- **Output units:** debounce count (uint16, routine executions); the float32 cell decode is almost certainly wrong
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The identity of the input float at FFFF9758 is not shown in this listing. The diagnostic interpretation also rests on the [TRACED] names eeprom_maturation_check and knock_timing_retard_base, which may themselves be wrong. The real cell values depend on re-decoding the data as uint16.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of FFFF9758 to identify the X-axis quantity, and look at the writers of FFFF44B4 to identify what the retard value is compared against. Re-decode 0xF8A44 as 4x uint16 and check the descriptor's type byte against 0xAC278 and 0xAB458 to confirm the count values.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBD3A/0BBD3C: descriptor 0xAC26C is stored to @(32,gbr)=FFFF9778, only on the r11==0 path. Path r11!=0 stores 0xAB458 instead.
  - 0BBD40/0BBD42: fr15 is loaded from FFFF9778-32 = FFFF9758; it is the lookup input.
  - 0BBDC2-0BBDCE: table_desc_1d_uint16 (0xBE8AC) is called with r4=@(32,gbr) (this descriptor) and fr4=fr15. The uint16 result is stored with mov.w to @(24,gbr)=FFFF9770. The table is consumed as a uint16 table, so the float32 decode (1.148e-38, 3.49e-39) is a mis-typed read: bytes 007D 0000 0026 0000.
  - 0BBDE4-0BBDEE: the path is taken when the lookup of 0xAC284 (stored at FFFF976C) is not greater than fr12, where fr12 = float_abs_diff(knock_timing_retard_base, FFFF44B4) from 0BBDA0-0BBDB2. In other words, the deviation is at or above its allowance.
  - 0BBDF8-0BBDFE: when the hysteresis flag r13==1, the code checks knock_timing_retard_base > FFFF44B4. If false it falls to 0BBE00.
  - 0BBE00-0BBE16: counter gbr+8 is incremented with uint16_add_sat(...,1) and compared (cmp/ge) against @(24,gbr), this table's output. At or above the threshold it branches to 0BBEE8, which sets r9=1 and r8=1 (fail flag).
  - 0BBE20-0BBE38: the opposite sign case uses counter gbr+10 against @(26,gbr), the output of table 0xAC278 looked up with the same input.
  - 0BBF38-0BBF46: eeprom_maturation_check(23) is called and r9 is packed via uint8_pack into FFFF2508, which is diagnostic/DTC output.

### 0xAC278 -- Diagnostic debounce count threshold, 'A above B' branch of a two-signal rationality check (indexed by float at 0xFFFF9758)

1D x4 float32, data 0xF8A5C, range 0 .. 11.565. Triage hint: unassigned.

The lookup returns a uint16 count limit, stored to GBR+26 (0xFFFF9772). That limit is the trip point for a counter that increments while the deviation between two signals (0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4) exceeds a looked-up tolerance and 0xFFFF8CE0 is the larger one. When the counter reaches the limit, the fault flags r9 and r8 are set, and r9 is later packed to 0xFFFF2508.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float read from 0xFFFF9758 (GBR+0) in normal mode (fmov.s @(-32,r1) with r1=0xFFFF9778); replaced by the constant 10.0 in the r11!=0 (test) mode. Physical quantity unknown; the 8..23.5 span and the 10.0 test value are only consistent with a voltage, which is unproven.
- **Output units:** counts (function-call iterations) after conversion to uint16 by 0xBE8AC
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the X input (float at 0xFFFF9758) and of the two compared signals 0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4 is unknown. The 'knock_timing_retard_base' label is not trusted. The two lowest cells decode as ~1e-38, which would truncate to a 0 limit and trip at once; the cells may instead be a non-float encoding or the converter may scale. Either way the count interpretation at low X is unconfirmed.
- **Next step:** Disassemble table_desc_1d_uint16 at 0xBE8AC to confirm how the float result is converted or scaled to uint16, and re-check the raw bytes at 0xF8A5C. Then search for the writers of 0xFFFF9758, 0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4 to identify the axis quantity and the two compared signals, and the readers of 0xFFFF2508 and 0xFFFF6AB1 to map the DTC.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBDD0-0BBDD6: r4=0xAC278, jsr table_desc_1d_uint16 (0xBE8AC), fr4=fr15
  - 0BBDD8: mov.w r0,@(26,gbr): the result is stored as uint16 to 0xFFFF9772
  - 0BBD3E-0BBD42: fr15 = float @0xFFFF9758 in mode r11==0; 0BBD6E-0BBD70: fr15 = literal 0x41200000 (10.0) otherwise
  - 0BBDA0-0BBDB2: fr12 = float_abs_diff(@0xFFFF8CE0, @0xFFFF44B4)
  - 0BBDE4-0BBDEC: |diff| is compared with the 0xAC284 lookup result stored at 0xFFFF976C; this table is used only when the diff exceeds it
  - 0BBDF2-0BBDFE: the path also needs the rpm hysteresis flag r13==1 (rpm vs 0xF8A30/0xF8A2C) and fr9(@0xFFFF8CE0) > fr13(@0xFFFF44B4)
  - 0BBE20-0BBE38: counter @r15 (GBR+10) is incremented by uint16_add_sat and compared cmp/ge against GBR+26 (this table); on reaching it, 0BBEE8 sets r9=1 and r8=1
  - 0BBF1E-0BBF46: r8 is saved to GBR+29, and r9 is packed via uint8_pack to 0xFFFF2508 and inverted to 0xFFFF6AB1, i.e. a diagnostic result flag
  - The sibling table 0xAC26C (result in GBR+24) is the count limit for the opposite sign (B above A)

### 0xAC284 -- Diagnostic plausibility threshold: maximum allowed |A − B| deviation between RAM 0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4, indexed by 0xFFFF975C (monitor for DTC slot 23)

1D x5 uint16, data 0xF8A78, range 4.2191 .. 25. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looks up a deviation limit from the float at 0xFFFF975C and stores it to 0xFFFF976C. It then compares that limit with |[0xFFFF8CE0] − [0xFFFF44B4]|. Below the limit, a pass counter runs and sets a completion flag. At or above it, rpm-gated failure counters run and set the malfunction flag r9 that is packed into 0xFFFF2508 and 0xFFFF6AA4.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float read from RAM 0xFFFF975C (gbr base 0xFFFF9758 + 4). Its physical quantity is unknown; breakpoints run 6.9..30.
- **Output units:** Same units as the two compared quantities at 0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4, which are unidentified. The value is an absolute-difference limit.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical identities of the compared quantities (0xFFFF8CE0, labelled knock_timing_retard_base, a label known to be unreliable, and 0xFFFF44B4) and of the axis input 0xFFFF975C are not established in this listing. Only the table's role as a pass/fail deviation limit is fixed by the code.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of 0xFFFF975C, 0xFFFF8CE0 and 0xFFFF44B4 to identify the axis and the two compared signals. Map DTC slot 23 used by eeprom_maturation_check to its code. Search for readers of 0xFFFF976C and 0xFFFF2508.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BBD8E-0BBD98: r4=0xAC284, then jsr table_lookup_1D with fr4 = @(0xFFFF9778-28) = 0xFFFF975C as input.
  - 0BBD9A-0BBD9E: the result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF976C (0xFFFF9778-12).
  - 0BBD52-0BBD5C / 0BBDA0-0BBDB2: float_abs_diff(fr4=[0xFFFF8CE0] saved at sp+16, fr5=fr13=[0xFFFF44B4]) gives fr12=|A−B|.
  - 0BBDE4-0BBDEE: fr8=[0xFFFF976C]; fcmp/gt fr12,fr8. If table > |A−B| it goes to 0BBEEE, where counter sp+4 (gbr+14) is incremented against limit [0xF8A2A]. When the count is reached, 0BBF0A-0BBF0E sets byte sp+8 → gbr+17 (pass/complete flag).
  - 0BBDF2-0BBEE8: otherwise (|A−B| >= table), counters run gated by r13 and by the sign of A>B. The r13 flag is an rpm hysteresis flag: rpm_current is compared against [0xF8A30]/[0xF8A2C] at 0BBDAA-0BBDC0. The counter limits come from [0xF8A28] and 1D tables 0xAC26C/0xAC278 indexed by [0xFFFF9758]. Exceeding a limit reaches 0BBEE8 'mov #1,r9' (fault).
  - 0BBF38-0BBF4E: eeprom_maturation_check(#23), then uint8_pack(r9) is stored to 0xFFFF2508 and the inverse of r9 to 0xFFFF6AA4+13. This is the shape of a DTC monitor.
  - 0BBD64-0BBD8C: in the test/alternate mode (r4!=0) the constants are A=15.0 and B=45.0 (diff 30, above the table maximum of 25) and rpm=1000. This forces the fail path, which fits a fault-threshold role.

### 0xAC2B0 -- Coolant-temperature-to-index map (ECT to 0..15 row selector for seven byte tables)

1D x16 float32, data 0xCC790, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Converts gated coolant temperature (ECT) into a small integer or fractional index, 0 at -40 C up to 15 at 110 C. That index is stored to 0xFFFF72A0 and then used as the X input of seven further lookups (descriptors 0xAC2DC, 0xAC2E8, 0xAC2F4, 0xAC2D0, 0xAC300, 0xAC30C and 0xAC318), whose byte results go to gbr+51..57 (0xFFFF72D3..0xFFFF72D9).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (input is ect_gated_c at 0xFFFF6354, loaded into fr4 at 0x02F4A6)
- **Output units:** dimensionless index/bin number (0..15), stored as a byte
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The descriptor says float32, but I am reinterpreting the data as 16 uint8 cells. That rests on the denormal bit patterns and on the byte store at 0x02F4B0, not on a decode of the descriptor's type field or of routine 0xBE4FA's return width.
- **Next step:** Decode the type/format byte of descriptor 0xAC2B0 and the return path of 0xBE4FA to confirm uint8 cells, then dump 16 bytes at 0xCC790. Then identify the seven downstream descriptors and search for readers of 0xFFFF72D3-0xFFFF72D9; those are the real tuning content.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x02F4A4/0x02F4A6: fr4 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c [TRACED]) as the lookup input, which makes X a coolant temperature in C.
  - 0x02F4A8-0x02F4AC: descriptor 0xAC2B0 is passed to 0xBE4FA. This is a different routine from LowPW_TableProcessor at 0xBE874, which the other seven lookups use.
  - 0x02F4B0: the result r0 is stored as a byte to @(0,gbr), which is 0xFFFF72A0. So the output is byte-sized, not float.
  - 0x02F4B2-0x02F4BE, and repeated at 0x02F4C2 through 0x02F512: the byte is read back from gbr+0, zero-extended, converted with float and passed in fr4 as the X input to seven LowPW_TableProcessor lookups. The table's output is therefore an axis coordinate for other tables.

### 0xAC620 -- Coolant-based start value for a decaying ramp armed on the rising edge of the flag at 0xFFFF7A0C (consumer unknown)

1D x16 uint8, data 0xCCE06, range 0 .. 0.30078. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

When the byte at 0xFFFF7A0C becomes 1 while the previous-state byte at 0xFFFF7A10 was 0, the code looks this table up against coolant temperature and writes the result to the float at 0xFFFF7A08. On later calls, while the flag stays 1, that value drops by the fixed step at 0xCC230 each call and is floored at 0. If the flag clears, or the value is already inside the deadband, it is zeroed. The table only gives a non-zero start value (about 0.10 to 0.30) at 0 °C and below.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, deg C (ect_current 0xFFFF6350 loaded into fr4 before the 1D lookup; axis shared with a defined table)
- **Output units:** Unitless float stored at 0xFFFF7A08 (0..0.30, raw/256). It is the start magnitude of the decaying term; its physical meaning depends on the consumer, which is not shown.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF7A08 is not in this listing. What the decaying value is added to or multiplied into (fuel enrichment, AFR target offset, timing, and so on) is unknown. The 'OL hysteresis' meaning of the flag at 0xFFFF7A0C rests only on labels.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of the float at 0xFFFF7A08 (literal 0xFFFF7A08, or 0xFFFF7A0C with displacement -4) to see what it is added to or multiplied into. Also search for writers of the byte at 0xFFFF7A0C to learn what event arms the ramp.
- **Cited:**
  - 036B4C/036B4E: fmov.s @0xFFFF6350 (ect_current),fr4, which is the lookup input, so the axis is coolant temperature
  - 036B50-036B5C: r14=0xFFFF7A0C; the lookup runs only if byte @(4,r14) (0xFFFF7A10) == 0 and byte @r14 == 1, i.e. a rising edge of the flag
  - 036B5E-036B6A: jsr table_lookup_1D with descriptor 0xAC620, then fmov.s fr0,@(-4,r14) stores the result to 0xFFFF7A08
  - 036B6C-036B78: on other calls, float_deadband_check(fr4=@0xFFFF7A08, fr5=0, fr6=const @0x036BE8)
  - 036B86-036B9A: if the check passes and the flag is still 1, value = float_max(value - @0xCC230 (OL_Hyst_RateLimit), 0), stored back to 0xFFFF7A08, a per-call linear decay to zero
  - 036B9C-036BA0: otherwise fldi0 and store 0.0 to 0xFFFF7A08
  - 036BA2-036BA4: the current flag is copied to 0xFFFF7A10 as the previous state, confirming edge detection
  - 036BA8/036BAA: rts with mov.l @r15+,r14 in the delay slot shows the true entry (push r14) sits just before 0x036B4A; the listing starts slightly late

Neither the trigger source nor the reader of 0xFFFF7B8C is in the listing.

  - 0x0397E8-0x0397EE: on the other path fldi1 stores 1.0 to FFFF7BC4. That 1.0 default is what makes this a multiplicative factor.

### 0xAC79C -- Unity gate factor vs FFFF68D0 (0 at 0, 1 at 10 and above) in the ramp target for FFFF7BE4

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low

- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF68D0 to identify the axis quantity. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF7BE4 (the ramped output) and 0xFFFF7BE8 (the target product) to learn what this gated value drives. Also check the calibration byte at 0xCBC06 to confirm the path is enabled on this ROM.

  - 03A006-03A008: the product is stored to FFFF7BE8.
  - 03A00A-03A01A: FFFF7BE4 plus the step constant at 0xCC45C is passed to float_min together with the product. The result is written back to FFFF7BE4 at 03A17C-03A17E, so this is a rate-limited ramp capped by the product.

### 0xAC8B8 -- Coolant-temperature-dependent elapsed-count threshold that sets the readiness flag at 0xFFFF72C4+14 (likely an after-start delay)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCD2A6, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: transient fuel.

The function keeps a saturating uint16 counter at 0xFFFF72C4. It adds 1 on each call while engine_state_byte is 0 and resets to 0 otherwise. The table, looked up against coolant temperature, gives the count at which the code sets byte 0xFFFF72C4+14 to 1, clears five words at 0xFFFF3158.. and calls 0x24CA0. A fixed count (0xCBC1C) combined with battery voltage below the threshold at 0xCC5F0 is an alternative path to the same action.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (-40..110), from definition
- **Output units:** Raw counter ticks: uint16 compared directly with the counter at 0xFFFF72C4, which goes up 1 per call of this function. The decoded scale and bias are denormal-float garbage, so the physical values shown are meaningless.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two things are unsettled. First, it is not known what engine_state_byte==0 means or how often this function runs, so the counter cannot be turned into seconds or tied to a specific event (after-start or engine-stopped). Second, the descriptor's scale and bias decode as garbage, so the raw threshold values are not confirmed and could even be 0 (the reported range 0..0). If they are 0, the comparison is always true and the table is inert.
- **Next step:** Read the raw uint16 words at 0xCD2A6..0xCD2C4 to get the real thresholds and check they are not zero. Then find the task rate of 0x02F772 and the writers of engine_state_byte 0xFFFF65BD. Finally, search for readers of byte 0xFFFF72D2 (0xFFFF72C4+14) and of 0xFFFF3158..0xFFFF3160 to learn what the flag enables.
- **Cited:**
  - 02F7A6/02F7A8: jsr table_desc_1d_uint16 with fr4=fr14=ect_current (loaded at 02F77E/02F780).
  - 02F7AA-02F7B2: extu.w r0,r2; r5 = counter @r9 (0xFFFF72C4); cmp/ge r2,r5; bt 0x02F7C6. If counter >= table value, the code goes to the action block.
  - 02F78A-02F7A0: when engine_state_byte (r12) == 0 the counter becomes uint16_add_sat(counter,1); otherwise it is set to 0. So it counts calls since some engine-state condition began.
  - 02F7B4-02F7C4: alternative path. Counter >= the constant word at 0xCBC1C AND the float at 0xCC5F0 > battery_voltage (fcmp/gt fr15,fr8).
  - 02F7C6-02F7EE: the action zeroes the words at 0xFFFF3158 offsets 0,2,4,8,6 via uint8_pack(0), calls 0x24CA0 and writes 1 to byte @(14,r9).
  - Decoded values form two plateaus: a higher threshold at <=40 deg C and a lower one at >=50 deg C. That fits a longer delay when cold.

### 0xACDB8 -- After-start enrichment, decaying additive term B (alternate set, used when byte 0xFFFF3158 == 1), vs coolant temperature

1D x16 uint16, data 0xCDDB0, range 0 .. 2.75. Triage hint: base fuelling.

While the word counter at 0xFFFF67EC is below the threshold at 0xCBC28, this table seeds the additive term at 0xFFFF73E4 from coolant temperature. Afterwards that term decays by -k*step toward a floor of 0 and is summed with a floored-at-1 term A (0xFFFF73E0) to give a combined fuel multiplier at 0xFFFF7400. The pattern fits after-start or post-crank enrichment; this table is the alternate B set chosen when the byte at 0xFFFF3158 equals 1 (labelled afl_diagnostic_flag, name not relied on).

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Coolant temperature, degC (from ect_gated_c 0xFFFF6354; axis shared with a named table)
- **Output units:** Dimensionless additive term to a fuel multiplier (summed with term A, which is at least 1). Here 2.75 means +275 % at 0 degC, falling to 0 by -10 and +10 degC.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The final consumer of 0xFFFF7400 is not in this listing, so its use as a fuel-injection multiplier is assumed from the 'at least 1, sum of decaying terms' structure. The meanings of the 0xFFFF67EC counter and the 0xFFFF3158 selector byte are also unconfirmed.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF7400 (combined enrichment) to confirm it multiplies the injector pulse width. Search for the writer of the byte at 0xFFFF3158 to learn when this alternate table set is active. Also check the writer of the word at 0xFFFF67EC and the threshold at 0xCBC28, to confirm it is a time-since-start counter.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x031196-0x03119C: the byte at 0xFFFF3158 is compared with 1. If it equals 1, 0xACDA4 is looked up into 0xFFFF73E0 (r12-100) and this table 0xACDB8 into 0xFFFF73E4. Otherwise 0xACD7C and 0xACD90 are used.
  - 0x0311B8-0x0311BE: jsr table_lookup_1D with fr4 = fr14 (coolant temp from 0xFFFF6354). fr0 is stored to @(-96,r12) = 0xFFFF73E4.
  - 0x03118A-0x031194: the seeding path runs only when the word from 0xFFFF67EC (in r14) is below the calibration word at 0xCBC28. Otherwise the code goes to the decay path at 0x0311F4.
  - 0x0311C4-0x0311CE: fadd sums 73E0 and 73E4. 0x0311D4: if the mode byte returned by 0x21016 is not 2, the sum is multiplied by the 0xAC43C lookup (stored at 0xFFFF7434).
  - 0x0311D4-0x0311E4: the product is clamped by float_clamp_range between 1 and the constant at 0x031368, then stored to 0xFFFF7400 (r12-68).
  - 0x0311E6-0x0311F2: if the mode is 2, float_min against the constant is applied instead.
  - 0x03128E-0x0312A6: decay of term B is B = max(B - k*step, 0). k comes from the 0xACD68 lookup and step from 0xFFFF73FC, which is set to 1.0 or to the constant at 0xCBE60 at 0x031132/0x03113C.
  - 0x03126A-0x031282: term A decays the same way with floor 1.0 (fldi1 at 0x03127E).
  - 0x031240-0x03125E: decay steps are paced by an LowPW_TableProcessor (0xAC430) period against the counter at gbr+97.
  - 0x031146-0x03117C: when A is about 1, B is about 0 and gbr+101 is 0, the output 0xFFFF7400 is forced to 1.0, meaning the enrichment is finished.

### 0xACE34 -- Load-dependent hold count loaded when FFFF7BCC leaves zero (gates a floor on the fuel factor)

1D x16 float32, data 0xCDF1C, range -0 .. 0. Triage hint: base fuelling.

On the pass where the float at FFFF7BCC first moves off zero, engine_load_current indexes this table. The 16-bit result is stored to a word at struct+20. On later passes, while FFFF7BCC stays non-zero and that word is positive, a factor derived from table 0xAC4D4 is floored at the constant 0xCBF38. It looks like a load-scheduled hold count or timer, but the float32 decode gives near-zero data and may mean the cell type is wrong.

- **Evidence class:** inferred

- **Output units:** Unsigned 16-bit count, probably a hold timer in loop ticks. Unconfirmed: under the float32 decode the data is 0 or -4.2e-08, which truncates to 0.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The cell type and meaning of the output. The descriptor decodes as float32 (all values 0 or -4.184e-08), but the routine name says uint16 and the result is stored as a word. If the cells really are those floats, they truncate to 0, the >0 gate at 032FE8 never fires, and the table is inert. Whether the stored word is a down-counting timer cannot be seen here.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0xBE8AC to see how it reads cells and what it returns (truncates a float or returns raw uint16). Re-decode the 64 raw bytes at 0xCDF1C as big-endian uint16. Find this function's callers to resolve the struct pointer r4, so the word at struct+20 gets an absolute RAM address, then search that address for decrementers and readers.
- **Cited:**
  - 032E4A/032E4E: fr13 is loaded from 0xFFFF63F8 (engine_load_current). 032FB4 moves fr13 into fr4 in the delay slot of the jsr to 0xBE8AC at 032FB2, so load is the lookup input.
  - 032FAE-032FB8: descriptor 0xACE34 goes to routine 0xBE8AC, labelled table_desc_1d_uint16. The result r0 is written as a 16-bit word (mov.w r0,@r6) to the pointer held at @(20,r14).
  - 032F8A-032F96: the deadband check at 0xBE608 is applied to fr12 (the value at 0xFFFF7BCC, band 0 to fr15). If it returns non-zero (bf 032FBA), the lookup is skipped.
  - 032F9E-032FAC: the same check is applied to @(16,r14). Per 033074-033076 that slot holds the previous pass's fr12. The lookup runs only when this check returns non-zero. So the table loads on a transition: last pass FFFF7BCC was inside the band around zero, this pass it is outside.
  - 032FE2-032FF8: later, if fr12 is still outside the band and the stored word is positive (extu.w, cmp/pl), fr14 = float_max(fr14, const@0xCBF38). That fr14 already came from the 0xAC4D4 lookup on FFFF7720, floored at 0xCBF28.
  - 03301C-03302E: fr14 is then clamped with float_clamp_range and stored to @r14. This makes the table an indirect gate on a fuelling-path output, not an additive term.
  - No decrement of the word at @(20,r14) appears in this listing. The countdown, if there is one, happens elsewhere.

Then find the callers of 0x033AD6 to see what struct r4/r14 points to, and so what the counter at @(4,r14) is. Search for the readers of that counter address and for the writer of FFFF7854. Also check the flag FFFFA4DD, which selects between this table and 0xACE60.

  - 033C94-033C98: mov.w r4,@r2. The sum is stored back to the counter.
  - 033BC6-033BEE: state 0 runs the same pattern with table 0xACE60 and ceiling 0xCBC3C.
  - 033C9A-033CA0: state 2 writes 0 to the counter.
  - 033CA2-033CB4: state 4 decrements the counter while it is positive.
  - 033B4A-033B50: state 0 vs 1 is chosen as NOT(FFFFA4DD==1), using r1 from 033AFC-033B00.

### 0xACE60 -- Counter increment step vs. FFFF7854 (×64), mode 0 of an up/down counter (AFC/AFL gating, unconfirmed)

In mode 0 of a five-way state machine, the lookup result is added to a 16-bit counter reached through a caller-supplied struct (*(r14+4)) and clamped to the word at 0xCBC3C. In the other modes the counter takes a sibling table's step (ACE54, mode 1), is zeroed (mode 2), is decremented by 1 (mode 4) or is held (mode 3). ### 0xAD17C -- Load-dependent weight on the fuel-scaled learned term B of channel-1 estimate (0xFFFF7B90)

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE7F8, range 0.30005 .. 1. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Engine load (g/rev) is looked up in this 7-point curve. The result multiplies the learned or tabled state B (fr15) and an ECT-table factor, and that product is scaled by a fuel-ratio product K. The sum is added to a parallel term A and clamped into RAM 0xFFFF7B90, which is then summed with a second channel into 0xFFFF7B8C. The weight peaks at 1.0 near 0.7 g/rev and falls to 0.3 at light load and 0.5 at high load. What the estimate physically represents is not shown.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine load, g/rev (input is fr13, loaded from 0xFFFF63F8 engine_load_current [TRACED] at 0x038E8C; the breakpoints 0.2..1.3 are consistent with g/rev)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (0.3..1.0)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The arithmetic role is observed: a load weight on term B inside a clamped sum. The physical meaning of 0xFFFF7B90/0xFFFF7B8C is not established. The gbr label 'afl' is UNVERIFIED, and nothing in the listing shows what reads these estimates or whether they reach fueling.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7B8C, 0xFFFF7B90 and 0xFFFF7B94, for example literal-pool references within the gbr 0xFFFF7B7C block, to see whether the clamped sum feeds a fuel or ignition calculation. Also disassemble 0x03949E to see how the state at 0xFFFF7B80 is integrated.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x038E8C: fmov.s @r2,fr13 loads fr13 from 0xFFFF63F8 (engine_load_current).
  - 0x039074-0x039078: the descriptor 0xAD17C is passed to table_lookup_1D (r14) with fr4=fr13.
  - 0x03907E: the result is stored to stack slot sp+80.
  - 0x03916C-0x039178: fr0 = fr15 * [sp+80] * [sp+96]; [sp+96] is an ECT-indexed table result (0xAD1CC, looked up with fr12 = ECT at 0x03909E).
  - 0x03917C-0x03917E: fr8 = [r10-8] = 0xFFFF7B98, the factor K. K = (1+(x[0xFFFF73E8]-1)*c[0xCC3E0]) * (1+(base_fuel_table_output-1)*c[0xCC3E4]), built at 0x038EB2-0x038ED4. fmac then adds fr0*K to fr4.
  - 0x03915C-0x03916A: fr4 = [sp+4] * [sp+72] * [sp+88]. This is the parallel term A: the load table 0xAD168 times the ECT table 0xAD1B8.
  - 0x039180-0x03918E: float_clamp_range is applied with ECT-table limits [sp+104]/[sp+108], and the result is stored to 0xFFFF7B90 (r10-16).
  - 0x0391C4-0x0391D0: 0xFFFF7B90 + 0xFFFF7B94 is stored to 0xFFFF7B8C.
  - 0x038F0C and 0x038FA6-0x038FAA: fr15 comes from RAM 0xFFFF7B80, or from the rpm table 0xAC724 when gbr flag bits are set. It is updated later via bsr 0x03949E and stored back to 0xFFFF7B80 at 0x039262.

### 0xAD230 -- RPM-indexed step/rate parameter for the adaptive term at FFFF7B80 (third of four parallel learn updates)

1D x7 uint16, data 0xCE90C, range 0.00024 .. 0.00073. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Looked up against engine speed and passed as the fr5 argument to helper 0x03949E. That helper updates the adaptive value stored at FFFF7B80 (r10-32), working from that term's previous value, the gbr+32 counter byte and the calibration byte at 0xCBC01. - **Next step:** Find the writer of FFFF62F8 to identify the gated quantity (e.g. elapsed time, voltage, pressure), and search for readers of byte FFFF7BE0 offset 0 to learn which routine (learning or diagnostic) this enable flag permits. Also read the constant at 0xCC420 to see the window's upper bound.

  - 039A0E-039A10: fr12 <- @FFFF62F8 (unnamed)
  - 039A72-039A74: fcmp/gt fr12,fr0; bt 0x039B06. If table(IAT) > FFFF62F8 the path goes to 039B06, which stores 0 to the FFFF7BE0 flag, so the table is a minimum required value of FFFF62F8
  - 039A76-039A7C: constant @0xCC420 compared with fr12; if fr12 > constant the flag is also cleared, so FFFF62F8 must lie in the window [table(IAT), CC420]
  - 039A7E-039A84: requires the hysteresis flag at FFFF7BE0+3 == 1, which is set or cleared at 039A40-039A68 from float_abs_diff(FFFF64E0, 0) against CC424/CC428, a near-zero / steady-state condition
  - 039A86-039AB6: rpm_current and engine_load_current window checks; 039AB8-039AC2: timing_state_var limit; 039AEA-039AF4: ECT minimum (CC448); 039AF6-039AFC: sched_status_R1 must be 0
  - 039B04-039B0A: if all checks pass, r2=1 is stored to the byte at FFFF7BE0; otherwise 0 is stored

### 0xAD440 -- State-indexed uint16 output bit-pattern table (misdecoded as float); exact function unknown

1D x9 float32, data 0xCEF6C, range -700 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

A byte state at gbr+8 (0xFFFF7D10) indexes this table, and the 16-bit result is stored to 0xFFFF7D08. It is then combined with a second lookup by bsr 0x03C784, and individual bits of the combined word are unpacked into four inverted on/off flags at 0xFFFF7D14..7D17. The flags are refreshed in two phases of a 0..7 counter. It behaves like a pattern or mask table, not a physical calibration curve.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: small integer state or mode index, 0..8, read as a byte from gbr+8 (0xFFFF7D10). The float axis values 0, 1.4e-45 … 1.12e-44 are just the integers 0..8 read as denormal floats.
- **Output units:** unitless bit pattern (uint16 mask). The float values -700..0 are a decoding artefact.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical function of the four output flags (gbr+12..15) and of the combining routine 0x03C784 is not shown. What the pattern ultimately drives (indicator, output port, injector or coil enable, diagnostic) is therefore unknown. The 'ignition timing' subsystem hint is unsupported.
- **Next step:** Re-decode 0xAD440's data as uint16 to get the real 9 masks. Then disassemble 0x03C784 to see how the mask combines with gbr+23. Finally, search for readers of 0xFFFF7D14..0xFFFF7D17 (the flag bytes) and for writers of 0xFFFF7D10 (the state index) to see what the bits drive.
- **Cited:**
  - 03C534/03C536/03C538: r4=0x000AD440 is passed to 0x000BE8C4 (table_desc_2d_uint16 [TRACED]). So the cell type is uint16, not float32, and the float decode is invalid.
  - 03C532/03C53A: the input is mov.b @(8,gbr) zero-extended into r5, a byte index. This matches axis breakpoints that are the integers 0..8 as raw bits.
  - 03C53C: the result is stored with mov.w r0,@(0,gbr), i.e. word 0xFFFF7D08.
  - 03C576-03C580: that word plus the byte at gbr+23 go into bsr 0x03C784, and the result is stored to @(2,gbr) (0xFFFF7D0A).
  - 03C564-03C56C: gbr+23 comes from a second table, 0xAC298 (uint8), indexed by the byte at 0xFFFF65F0 (r14).
  - 03C58C-03C5B4 and 03C62E-03C652: when the phase counter gbr+22 is 0, bits 0x80/0x20/0x08/0x02 of the gbr+2 word are tested. When it is 4, bits 0x40/0x10/0x04/0x01 are tested. Each result is inverted (xor #1) and stored to flag bytes gbr+12, +14, +13, +15. This is bitmask consumption, not arithmetic.
  - 03C53E-03C54E: gbr+22 is a counter that wraps from 7 back to 0, giving the two-phase bit extraction.
  - Float 'values' reinterpreted as raw bits give uint16 halves such as 0x0001, 0x0003/0x0012, 0x0033/0x0040, and ~0x00FF. These look like bit patterns, though this decode is approximate.

### 0xAD540 -- Cranking-state RPM x coolant multiplier, flag-FFFF90BE-set variant (possibly cranking fuel taper)

2D 7x5 uint8, data 0xCF65C, range 0.54688 .. 1. Triage hint: base fuelling.

When check_engine_running does not return 1, and the byte at FFFF90BE is non-zero, the code looks this table up with engine speed and coolant temperature. It stores the resulting dimensionless 0.55..1.0 factor as a float to FFFF72F0. The factor falls below 1 only at 315-515 rpm and -25..0 C, which looks like a taper applied as a cold engine begins to catch during cranking.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (115..515): engine speed in rpm, fed from rpm_current FFFF6624 via fr4 (axis-to-register assignment inferred from the ranges, not verified in table_lookup_2D); X axis (-30..0): coolant temperature in C, fed from ect_current FFFF6350 via fr5
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (0.0078125/count, 1.0 = no change)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of FFFF72F0 is not in this listing, so it is unknown whether the factor scales fuel, timing or something else. The meaning of check_engine_running's return value and of flag FFFF90BE rests on a label or is unknown. The 'fuel' reading is a hunch; the sibling AD578 is labelled TimingAdv, which is not evidence either way.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for literal-pool references to 0xFFFF72F0 and inspect what each reader multiplies it into (e.g. an injector pulse width or a cranking fuel base). Also find writers of byte 0xFFFF90BE to learn what selects AD540 over AD524.
- **Cited:**
  - 02FADA/02FADC: fr15 is loaded from 0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]); 02FB08: fmov fr15,fr4 passes it as the first lookup argument
  - 02FADE/02FAE0: fr14 is loaded from 0xFFFF6350 (ect_current [TRACED]); 02FB0C: fmov fr14,fr5 passes it as the second argument
  - 02FAE2-02FAEE: jsr check_engine_running, then cmp/eq #1,r0 / bf/s 0x02FAFE, so this descriptor is reached only when the routine does not return 1 (a cranking or not-running state, if the label is right)
  - 02FAF0: mov.b @r2,r5 reads byte FFFF90BE; 02FAFE/02FB02: tst r5,r5 / bf selects AD540 when it is non-zero, otherwise AD524 at 02FB04
  - 02FAF4/02FAF8: when the running result is 1, the same flag chooses between AD578 and AD55C instead, giving a four-way table family
  - 02FB0E/02FB10: the lookup result fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF72F0 and the function returns; no clamp or arithmetic is applied here
  - The data are 1.0 at 115/215 rpm and throughout most of the 0 C row, with a minimum of 0.547 at 515 rpm and -20 C: a multiplier shape, and rpm 115-515 fits cranking speeds

### 0xAD63C -- ECT x elapsed-counter gain factor (first factor of a three-way product stored to 0xFFFF77FC)

2D 10x16 uint8, data 0xCFAFC, range 0 .. 0.03125. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Gives a small gain (0 to 0.03125) that is non-zero only for ECT 40-70 C and a scaled counter below about 70000. The code multiplies it by a load/rpm table and a 1D table of RAM 0xFFFF62F8, stores the product at 0xFFFF77FC and uses it as a negative threshold that decides whether to copy it to 0xFFFF7800 and set a flag at 0xFFFF782C.

- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Which axis argument (fr4 or fr5) maps to X or Y is inferred from breakpoint magnitudes, not from the lookup routine. The physical meaning of the 0xFFFF67EC counter and of the downstream threshold/correction is unknown, because the readers of 0xFFFF77FC and 0xFFFF7800 are not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Read the calibration byte at 0xCBBE3; if it is not 0xFF, 0xFFFF7800 is always zeroed and this table is inert there. Read the float at 0x341DC to scale the counter at 0xFFFF67EC. Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF77FC, 0xFFFF7800 and the flag byte 0xFFFF782C, and for writers of 0xFFFF67EC, to fix what the product means.
- **Cited:**
  - 033FE0/033FE2: fr14 <- float at 0xFFFF6354 (ECT); 034036: fmov fr14,fr5 is passed as an axis input to table_lookup_2D. The -40..100 X breakpoints fit a temperature.
  - 034000/034006: r14 <- mov.w @0xFFFF67EC; 034024-03402C: extu.w r14 goes through fmac_interp_uint16 with the constant at 0x341DC; 03402E: the result becomes fr4, the other axis input.
  - 034030/034034: this descriptor 0xAD63C is looked up; 034038: result to fr14.
  - 03403A-034044: a second 2D lookup (0xAD658) on load (fr12) and rpm (fr15) gives fr15; 034046-03404C: a 1D lookup (0xAC4E8) on 0xFFFF62F8 gives fr0.
  - 03404E/034050: fr14 = this_table * table2 * table3; 034056: stored to 0xFFFF782C-48 = 0xFFFF77FC.
  - 034058-03406C: (@r13 - @0xFFFF7784 - @0xFFFF77B8) is compared against -product. If greater and byte 0xCBBE3 == 0xFF (034070-034078), the product is copied to 0xFFFF7800 (03407E) and flag 1 is written to 0xFFFF782C (03408C); otherwise 0xFFFF7800 = 0 and the flag = 0.
  - 03400A-034020: unless check_afl_ready returns 1 or byte 0xCBBE3 == 0xFF, the lookup is skipped and 0xFFFF77FC is forced to 0.

### 0xAD658 -- Load-based 0..1 weighting factor (fades to zero above ~1.2 g/rev, flat in rpm) for the threshold stored at 0xFFFF77FC

2D 10x16 uint8, data 0xCFC04, range 0 .. 1. Triage hint: base fuelling.

Looked up with engine_load_current and rpm_current. The result is a 0..1 multiplier: 1 up to 1.0 g/rev, 0.5 at 1.2 and 0 from 1.4 g/rev, the same at every rpm. It is multiplied with two other table outputs into a magnitude stored at 0xFFFF77FC. That magnitude is then used as a negative-bound threshold that gates a value copied to 0xFFFF7800 and a flag byte at 0xFFFF782C.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (16 pts, 0..3): engine load, g/rev (engine_load_current 0xFFFF63F8, passed in fr4). Inferred from the breakpoints fitting this pairing, not from the lookup routine.; X (10 pts, 0..7200): engine speed, rpm (rpm_current 0xFFFF6624, passed in fr5)
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier 0..1
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the product at 0xFFFF77FC physically represents (a deviation threshold or an allowed correction magnitude) is not fixed by this listing. The load/rpm axis pairing also relies on the breakpoints matching, because table_lookup_2D's argument order was not checked.
- **Next step:** Read the calibration byte at 0xCBBE3: if it is not 0xFF, the gated copy to 0xFFFF7800 never happens and 0xFFFF77FC is only computed when check_afl_ready returns 1. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF77FC, 0xFFFF7800 and flag byte 0xFFFF782C, and find the caller's r4 argument (the pointer dereferenced at 034058) to identify the quantity being compared.
- **Cited:**
  - 033FE4/033FE6: fr12 <- @0xFFFF63F8 (engine_load_current); 033FE8/033FEA: fr15 <- @0xFFFF6624 (rpm_current)
  - 03403A-034042: r4=0xAD658, fr4=fr12 (load), fr5=fr15 (rpm), jsr table_lookup_2D; 034044: fr15 <- result
  - 03402E-034038: fr14 <- 2D table 0xAD63C, indexed by the interpolated uint16 at 0xFFFF67EC and by ect_gated_c
  - 034046-03404C: fr0 <- 1D table 0xAC4E8 of @0xFFFF62F8
  - 03404E/034050: fr14 = fr14 * fr15 * fr0, so this table is a pure multiplier in a three-factor product
  - 034052-034056: product stored to 0xFFFF782C-48 = 0xFFFF77FC
  - 034058-03406E: (@@r13 - @0xFFFF77D4 - @0xFFFF77F8) compared against -@0xFFFF77FC (fcmp/gt)
  - 034070-03408C: if that holds and byte @0xCBBE3 == 0xFF, 0xFFFF77FC is copied to 0xFFFF7800 and flag byte 0xFFFF782C is set to 1; otherwise both are 0
  - 034000-034020: if check_afl_ready != 1 and @0xCBBE3 != 0xFF, the lookup is skipped and 0xFFFF77FC is forced to 0

### 0xAD810 -- ECT- and FFFF73B0-dependent weighting factor for the middle term of a three-term sum (FFFF844F==1 variant, mode 2)

2D 3x16 uint16, data 0xD1104, range 1 .. 12.109. Triage hint: base fuelling.

This is a 2D lookup on coolant temperature (fr15) and the float at FFFF73B0. The result is stored to FFFF73BC (r8-88) and multiplied into the second of three product terms. The three terms are summed, scaled and stored to FFFF73B4. The value is a dimensionless factor from 1 to 12. It falls as coolant warms and as the X input rises, and is pinned at 1 from 90 °C up.

- **Evidence class:** inferred

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the X input (FFFF73B0) and of the sum written to FFFF73B4 is not in this listing. The writer of FFFF73B0 is probably one of the subroutines at 0x0312DE, 0x030F02 or 0x03110C, and the reader of FFFF73B4 is in another function. Without these the purpose cannot be pinned down (it might be a warm-up enrichment, a thermal or heat model, or a diagnostic expectation).
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x0312DE, 0x030F02 and 0x03110C to find what writes FFFF73B0 (gbr+0). Then search the ROM for readers of FFFF73B4 to see what the summed result controls. Also check what FFFF844F selects, comparing table 0xAD810 with 0xAD7F4.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x030C62/0x030C68: r8 = r14 = 0xFFFF7414, and rpm (FFFF6624) is stored to @r8; 0x030C5C loads ECT (FFFF6350) into fr15
  - 0x030E28-0x030E30: r10 (byte at FFFF844F) == 1 selects descriptor 0xAD810, otherwise 0xAD7F4
  - 0x030E34-0x030E3A: table_lookup_2D is called with fr4 = fr15 (ECT) and fr5 = @(r8-100) = FFFF73B0
  - 0x030E3C-0x030E3E: the result is stored to @(r8-88) = FFFF73BC
  - This path is reached only when @(r8-60) = FFFF73D8 >= 0 (0x030D68-0x030D70) and the mode byte from 0x021016 == 2 (0x030D74-0x030D76)
  - 0x030E92-0x030EA0: fr4 = @(-32) * @(-76) * @(-88); @(-76) is a 1D lookup on rpm (0x030E4A-0x030E56)
  - 0x030EA2-0x030EC0: fmac adds @(-36)*@(-80)*@(-92) and @(-28)*@(-72)*@(-84); 0x030EC2-0x030EC6 multiplies the sum by @(-20)
  - 0x030EC8-0x030EE0: if the counter word from FFFF67EC is below the threshold at 0xCBC26, float_max(x, 0) clamps the sum at zero; the sum is stored to @(r8-96) = FFFF73B4
  - 0x030D04-0x030D0E: if the counter is below the threshold in gbr+84 (from 0xCBC2C or 0xCBC2A), the path at 0x030EE2 stores 0 to FFFF73B4 and skips the whole calculation

2D 9x8 uint16, data 0xD1314, range 2 .. 8.75. Triage hint: transient fuel.

When flag_6254 != 1 and the byte flag at @(8,r13) is set, the code looks this table up with MAF and RPM and stores the result at 0xFFFF76F0. ### 0xAD944 -- Coolant-temperature-dependent minimum threshold on FFFF63D4 (likely time or accumulated count since start) for a diagnostic or monitor enable counter

2D 2x16 uint16, data 0xD1B8C, range 400 .. 4250. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

The table is looked up once per call, likely on coolant temperature, and the result is stored to FFFF793C (FFFF7928+20). The function that follows aborts and resets its enable counter whenever this value is greater than the value copied from FFFF63D4. So the counter at struct+104 can only advance once FFFF63D4 has reached the table value: 4250 when cold, falling to 400 when hot.

The breakpoints match coolant °C, but which argument register maps to which descriptor axis is not shown.; X (2 pts, -10..0): probably FFFF6368, passed in fr5. Its identity is unknown, and the axis is inert because both rows are identical.
- **Output units:** Same units as FFFF63D4, which is unknown. The data is raw uint16 with scale 1. The 400..4250 magnitude would fit a timer or an accumulated count, but that is not established.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What FFFF63D4 is. The table's meaning is fixed only as 'minimum required value of FFFF63D4', and that quantity is not identified in this listing. A second weak link: the fr4/fr5 to Y/X axis mapping is assumed, not shown.
- **Next step:** Search the writers of FFFF63D4 to identify the quantity, e.g. whether it is a time-since-start counter and how fast it increments. Also find the readers of the struct pointers at 0x63A88+104 and +108 (the counter and flag) to see what monitor they enable. Finally, confirm in table_lookup_2D (0xBE8E4) which of fr4/fr5 indexes which descriptor axis.
- **Cited:**
  - 034FB8-034FBE: fr4 = @FFFF6354 (ect_gated_c), fr5 = @FFFF6368, then at 034FC6-034FCA r4 = 0xAD944 and jsr table_lookup_2D (0xBE8E4).
  - 034FCE-034FD2: the result fr0 is stored to @(20, FFFF7928), i.e. FFFF793C.
  - 034FB0-034FB6: @FFFF63D4 is copied to @(16, FFFF7928), i.e. FFFF7938.
  - 034FD4-034FD8: r4 = 0x63A88, r5 = 0, then lds.l pr and fall-through into the function that starts at 034FDA (a tail call into a monitor routine).
  - 0350E6-0350F0: fr9 = @(20, r1) (table value), fr8 = @(16, r1) (FFFF63D4 copy); fcmp/gt fr8,fr9; bt 0x0351D8. If table > FFFF63D4 the routine branches to the reset path.
  - 0351D8-0351E4: the reset path clears the byte flag at @(108, r13) and the word counter at @(104, r13).
  - 035100-03511C: if all conditions pass, it sets the flag at @(108, r13) = 1 and increments the counter at @(104, r13) via uint16_add_sat.
  - The other enable conditions are rpm < CC094 (035088-035094), the fr12 band check (03509A-0350AC), load < CC0A0 (0350B2-0350BC), ECT < CC0A4 (0350C2-0350CC), clol_mode_flag == 1 (035072-035076), and cl_enable_final == 0 (0350F8-0350FE). Together these look like a monitor-enable condition set.
  - Data: both X rows are identical, so the second input has no effect.

### 0xADAFC -- Ramp-in limit vs. tick count after a status-triggered event (writes 0xFFFF7EC4)

1D x8 uint8, data 0xD312C, range 0.03906 .. 68.945. Triage hint: unassigned.

While latch flag GBR+43 (0xFFFF7EEB) is set, the saturating counter at GBR+40 (0xFFFF7EE8) is looked up in this table and the result is stored to 0xFFFF7EC4. The output stays near 0 for counts 1..9, then ramps to about 68.9 by count 25. When the latch is clear, the same RAM gets a constant of about 69.65, so the table reads as a time-based ramp back up to that full or ceiling value.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: value of the uint8 counter at 0xFFFF7EE8 (GBR+40). It is incremented by 1, saturating, on each execution of this routine. It is reset to 0 whenever the byte returned by check_engine_status (0x3AB20) equals 1. Units are routine ticks; the task rate is unknown.
- **Output units:** Same units as the constant 69.65 (0x428B4CCC at 0x3F288). That constant is the default written to 0xFFFF7EC4 and also the float_min ceiling applied to 0xFFFF7EC0. Physical unit unknown; a 0..~70 range does not prove a percentage.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of 0xFFFF7EC4 is not in this listing, so what the ramped value limits or blends is unknown. The meaning of the status byte from check_engine_status (0x3AB20), which triggers the event, is also unverified.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF7EC4 (GBR 0xFFFF7EC0 +4, or 0xFFFF7F08 -68) to see what it is multiplied, min-ed or compared against. Also check the cal byte at 0xD2979: if it is below 10, the ramp never leaves its near-zero region and the table is effectively inert.
- **Cited:**
  - 03F1D4-03F1E0: mov.b @(40,gbr),r0, then lds/float fpul,fr4. The counter byte is the X input to table_lookup_1D with descriptor 0xADAFC.
  - 03F1E2-03F1E8: fmov.s fr0,@(-68,r7) with r7=0xFFFF7F08. The result is stored to 0xFFFF7EC4.
  - 03F1EA-03F1F2: when GBR+43 != 1, the constant at 0x3F288 (0x428B4CCC, about 69.65) is stored to the same 0xFFFF7EC4 instead. This makes the table's 68.9 upper plateau the near-default value.
  - 03F17A-03F188: bt/s with mov #0,r0 in the delay slot resets GBR+40 to 0 when the check_engine_status result (sp+32) ==1; otherwise uint8_add_sat(GBR+40,1) increments it. The axis is therefore an elapsed-tick counter.
  - 03F18E-03F1B4: GBR+43 is set to 1 only when all of these hold: rpm >= cal 0xD2A9C, load >= cal 0xD2AA0, the status byte ==1, and the previous status byte GBR+70 ==0. This is a rising-edge latch.
  - 03F1B6-03F1CC: GBR+43 is cleared when the counter >= cal byte 0xD2979 or GBR+41 >= cal byte 0xD297A. This ends the ramp window.
  - 03F146-03F154: float_min with the same 69.65 constant caps 0xFFFF7EC0 (offset -72), so -68 and -72 appear to share units and ceiling.

1D x16 uint8, data 0xD338A, range 75 .. 100. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Looks up a coolant-temperature-indexed value: 100 at or below 30 °C, falling to 75 from 70 °C up. The result is stored to RAM 0xFFFF7FB8 (gbr+8) and nothing in this function reads it back. A sister table, 0xADD90, takes its place when the byte at 0xFFFF8970 is anything other than 4, 7 or 8.

### 0xADED0 -- unknown – lookup on a peak-held value (0xFFFF8070), selector case 4, engine-running branch; one factor of the product stored to 0xFFFF805C

1D x8 uint8, data 0xD35E8, range -24.961 .. -15.117. Triage hint: FLKC.

Each pass, 0xFFFF8070 is set to the larger of its previous value and the live variable at 0xFFFF62F4, so it holds the peak. - **Output units:** unknown (stored as uint8 ×0.35156 −45; the physical meaning is not fixed by the code shown)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The identity of the input at 0xFFFF62F4 and the meaning of the product at 0xFFFF805C are both outside this listing. The FLKC subsystem hint and the labels on 0xFFFF6810 and 0xFFFF8080 are names only. Nothing shown fixes the physical quantity or the units of this table's output.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF805C (the product) and 0xFFFF8060 to see what the result feeds, for example whether it is added to timing or compared as a threshold. Search for writers of 0xFFFF62F4 to identify the axis quantity. Also find who sets bits 0x1000, 0x2000 and 0x4000 of word 0xFFFF8080 and bit 2 of byte 0xFFFF8075, which gate whether this table matters.
- **Cited:**
  - 0427E6-0427F4: float_max(fr4=@(-16,r12)=[0xFFFF8070], fr5=fr15) and the result is written back to 0xFFFF8070, making it a peak-hold. fr15 was loaded at 042524-042526 from 0xFFFF62F4.
  - 0427F8-0427FC: the branch is chosen by the check_engine_running result saved at @r15 (042522). Equal to 1 takes the 042800 chain that contains this table.
  - 042812-042814: selector r13 (byte from 0xFFFF6810 via 042528-04252A) == 4 branches to 042850.
  - 042850-04285E: r4=0xADED0, jsr table_lookup_1D with fr4=[0xFFFF8070]; fr0 is stored to @(-32,r12)=0xFFFF8060.
  - 0427DC-0427E2: if bit 2 of byte 0xFFFF8075 is clear, the code jumps to 042964, which zeroes 0xFFFF8070, and the lookup is skipped.
  - 0429A0-0429BA: when word 0xFFFF8080 has bit 0x1000 clear, bit 0x2000 clear and bit 0x4000 set, the code computes [8060]*[8064]*[806C]*[8068] and stores it to 0xFFFF805C (the GBR base).
  - 042972-042986: if bit 0x1000 is set, 0xFFFF805C instead gets the constant at 0xD2C90. 04298A-042990 and 0429BC-0429C0: if bit 0x2000 is set, it gets 0. In those cases this table's output is unused that cycle.

### 0xADEF8 -- Unknown: factor 1 of a 4-term product, vs peak-held 0xFFFF62F4 (selector 5, engine running)

1D x8 uint8, data 0xD3638, range -24.961 .. -15.117. Triage hint: FLKC.

Interpolates over a running maximum of the float at 0xFFFF62F4 and stores the result at 0xFFFF8060. That value is then multiplied by three other terms (0xFFFF8064, 0xFFFF806C, 0xFFFF8068) to form the result stored at 0xFFFF805C. This descriptor is the one chosen when ol_condition_selector equals 5 and check_engine_running returned 1.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: running max (peak hold) of the float at RAM 0xFFFF62F4, held in 0xFFFF8070. The physical quantity is unidentified; breakpoints run 0..87.5 in steps of 12.5.
- **Output units:** unknown; the value is a dimensionless multiplicand in the product at 0xFFFF805C. The -45 bias / 90/256 scaling is not proof of degrees.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The identity of the input at 0xFFFF62F4 and what reads the product at 0xFFFF805C are both unknown. The FLKC label on 0xFFFF8080 is [UNVERIFIED], and with negative cell values the meaning of a multiplicative term is unclear.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF805C (the product) and for the writer of 0xFFFF62F4 (the input). Also check which code sets bit 0x4000 in the word at 0xFFFF8080, to see whether the product path is ever taken.
- **Cited:**
  - 0x042524-0x042526: fr15 is loaded from 0xFFFF62F4.
  - 0x0427E6-0x0427F4: float_max(fr4=@(0xFFFF8070), fr5=fr15) is stored back to 0xFFFF8070, so this is a peak hold of 0xFFFF62F4.
  - 0x04251C-0x042522: the check_engine_running result is stored at @r15. 0x0427F8-0x0427FC: the ==1 path takes 0x042800.
  - 0x042528-0x04252A: r13 = ol_condition_selector. 0x042818: cmp/eq #5 branches to 0x042860.
  - 0x042860-0x04286E: this descriptor (0xADEF8) is loaded and table_lookup_1D is called with fr4=@(0xFFFF8070). fr0 is stored to r12-32 = 0xFFFF8060.
  - Sibling descriptors 0xADE58/80/A8/D0 and 0xADF20 (running) and 0xADE44..0xADF0C (not running) are a 6x2 family indexed by selector and running state.
  - 0x0429A0-0x0429BA: 0xFFFF8060 * 0xFFFF8064 * 0xFFFF806C * 0xFFFF8068 is stored to 0xFFFF805C. This happens only when GBR flag word 0xFFFF8080 has bit 0x4000 set and bits 0x1000/0x2000 are clear.
  - 0x0429BC/0x04297E: otherwise 0xFFFF805C is zeroed or set from the constant at 0xD2C94-adjacent 0xD2C90. Factor 0xFFFF8064 comes from RPM/selector/counter-indexed float arrays at 0xD3DA0-0xD3FC0 (0x0425A8 etc.).

### 0xAE140 -- Low-RPM detect threshold (upper, with hysteresis), indexed by state byte 0xFFFF72A0

1D x16 float32, data 0xD3C40, range 300 .. 500. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Gives an RPM level (500 for index 0-3, 300 for index 4-15). When rpm_current is at or above it, a flag at 0xFFFF7E76 is cleared. The flag is set again only when rpm falls below this value minus the companion table 0xAE14C, so the two tables form a hysteresis pair. The flag then feeds a state byte at 0xFFFF7E88 together with a counter and a coolant-temperature check.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Unidentified integer index 0..15: the byte at 0xFFFF72A0, converted to float by fmac_interp_uint8 with scale 1 and offset 0 (fr4=1, fr5=0). Possibly a counter or state; not determined from this listing.
- **Output units:** rpm (compared directly against rpm_current)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The output is observed to be an rpm hysteresis threshold, provided the rpm_current label is right; values of 300-500 fit. It is not known what the low-RPM flag means (stall, crank, or near-stop detection) or what the axis byte at 0xFFFF72A0 represents. The downstream readers of 0xFFFF7E76 and 0xFFFF7E88 are not in this listing.
- **Next step:** Search for code that writes byte 0xFFFF72A0 to identify the axis. Search for readers of 0xFFFF7E76 (flag) and 0xFFFF7E88 (state byte) to learn what the low-RPM condition gates. Also decode table 0xAE14C to confirm it holds a small hysteresis band (e.g. tens of rpm).
- **Cited:**
  - 03E8F6/03E8F8: r13 = byte @0xFFFF72A0; 03E92C-03E936: fmac_interp_uint8(r13, fr4=1.0, fr5=0.0) -> fr14, the axis input
  - 03E938-03E93E: table_lookup_1D(0xAE140, fr4=fr14); 03E940-03E944: result stored to 0xFFFF7E80 (0x7E88-8)
  - 03E946-03E952: companion table 0xAE14C looked up with the same input and stored to 0xFFFF7E84
  - 03E954-03E960: 0xFFFF7E78 = 7E80 - 7E84 (threshold minus hysteresis band)
  - 03E904: fr15 = rpm_current @0xFFFF6624 [TRACED]; 03E96A-03E974: if NOT (table > rpm) then mov.b #0 -> @(2,gbr) = 0xFFFF7E76
  - 03E976-03E980: else, if (table - band) > rpm then @(2,gbr) = 1. This is set-low / clear-high hysteresis on rpm
  - 03E982-03E9B2: flag==1 leads to a compare of byte @(0,gbr) with the counter from 0xFFFF3200, then afl_diagnostic_flag==1 and ect_current < const @0xD29F4, ending in a write of 0, 1 or 2 to @(20,gbr) = 0xFFFF7E88
  - 03E928/03E92A: the whole block runs only when transient_enable_gate @0xFFFF65F1 == 0

### 0xAE284 -- Knock signal amplitude to log2 scale conversion (32 counts per doubling), per-channel knock intensity front end

1D x65 float32, data 0xD422C, range 0 .. 304. Triage hint: knock.

Turns a raw non-negative signal magnitude read from RAM 0xFFFF4304 into a logarithmic value: 32 counts per doubling of the input, 0 at an input of 0.00488. The code then subtracts a per-channel byte×32 (a gain-step correction), adds a per-channel byte offset, clamps the result at 0 and stores it to 0xFFFF8138. It then feeds comparisons against a per-channel background level and lag filters in what looks like knock detection.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: raw signal magnitude from RAM 0xFFFF4304, clamped >= 0 (source not in listing; range 0..3.5, possibly sensor volts or a DSP amplitude)
- **Output units:** Log2 counts, 32 per factor-of-2 of the input (about 0.19 dB per count). Output = 32*log2(x/0.00488), floored at 0.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the input at 0xFFFF4304, a knock sensor or DSP amplitude, is not shown; its writer is outside this listing. The knock interpretation rests on the downstream background/threshold structure and the gain-step ×32 subtraction, not on any traced source.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of RAM 0xFFFF4304 to see whether it comes from the knock sensor ADC/DSP path. Find the readers of 0xFFFF8138 and of the per-channel array at 0xFFFF817C to confirm the knock-intensity versus background comparison.
- **Cited:**
  - Table data: x=0.00977→32, 0.0195→64, 0.039→96, 0.078→128, 0.156→160, 0.3125→192, 0.625→224, 1.25→256, 2.5→288, 3.5→304. That is exactly 32*log2(x/0.00488), so the table is a log2 converter.
  - 0437A8-0437B2: fr8 = float @0xFFFF4304, clamped to >=0 (fcmp/gt against fldi0 fr12). 0437B8 stores it to 0xFFFF8134 (r9-36). 0437C0 loads it into fr4 as the lookup input for jsr table_lookup_1D at 0437BE.
  - 0437CA-0437DC: a byte at knock_det_GBR_base+212, indexed by channel byte @(176,gbr), is shifted left 5 (×32), floated and subtracted from fr0. Subtracting whole multiples of 32 equals dividing the linear input by 2^n, i.e. removing amplifier gain steps in the log domain.
  - 0437E4-0437F0: a byte at base+186[ch] is added. 0437F2-0437FA clamp the result to >=0 and store it to 0xFFFF8138 (r9-32).
  - 043876-0438B0: 0xFFFF8138 minus a per-channel float at 0xFFFF817C[ch] is taken as an absolute value, scaled, and clamped by two values (one of them the 1.0 from fldi1).
  - 043A10-043A24 and 043A60-043A6A: 0xFFFF8138 is passed into float_lag_filter.
  - 043B34-043B5E: 0xFFFF8138 is compared against derived thresholds (r9-4 and @r9) to set flags at gbr+190/191. This is consistent with background-noise tracking and knock decision.

### 0xAE774 -- Hysteresis threshold A for a lag-filtered rpm-product estimate (X = FFFF6210 signal, Y = battery voltage, voltage-independent)

2D 2x2 float32, data 0xD6058, range -21 .. 40. Triage hint: unassigned.

Supplies the ON threshold for a hysteresis comparator. The comparator sets byte flag FFFF8374 when a lag-filtered estimate at FFFF8360 reaches the table value and clears it when the estimate falls below the table value minus a hysteresis constant (D6020). In the init routine the same lookup seeds the filter state FFFF8360. The output falls from 40 at X=-760 to -21 at X=1000 and is identical at 8 V and 12 V.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y: battery voltage (FFFF4130, passed in fr4), breakpoints 8 and 12 V; both columns are equal, so the axis has no effect; X: value read from FFFF6210 (passed in fr5), breakpoints -760 and 1000; the physical quantity is unknown
- **Output units:** Same units as the filtered estimate at FFFF8360. That estimate is a lag-filtered product of FFFF7D94, rpm and two constants, plus an offset, so its physical unit is unknown.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the compared quantity is unestablished. It depends on what FFFF7D94 and FFFF6210 represent, and neither has a known writer in the listing. The threshold role itself is observed.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of FFFF8368 and FFFF8369 (the final decision bytes) and of FFFF8374 and FFFF8375. Also find the writers of FFFF7D94 and FFFF6210 to fix the physical quantity.
- **Cited:**
  - 04BDCC/04BDD0 and 04BEC8/04BECC: fr4 = battery_voltage (FFFF4130) and fr5 = FFFF6210 are the lookup inputs, so Y = voltage and X = FFFF6210.
  - 04BE92-04BEA2: fr15 (from FFFF7D94) is multiplied by D6028, by rpm (FFFF6624) and by a pool constant, then stored to FFFF8374-24 = FFFF835C.
  - 04BEA4-04BEC2: float_lag_filter is applied, the offset at FFFF835C-? (FFFF8374-28, either 0 or D6024) is added, and the result is stored to FFFF8360 and loaded into fr14.
  - 04BECE-04BED6: if table <= fr14, the code writes 1 to @(28,gbr), i.e. FFFF8358+28 = FFFF8374.
  - 04BED8-04BEE4: if (table - D6020) > fr14, the code writes 0 to FFFF8374. This is a hysteresis comparator with the table as threshold.
  - 04BEE6-04BF06: the same pattern repeats with sibling table 0xAE788, giving flag FFFF8375.
  - 04BF08-04BF42: the two flags, or a calibration override byte at D6010 (values 1/2/3), decide the bytes written to FFFF8368 and FFFF8369.
  - 04B97A-04B986: in init, the table value is stored to FFFF8364-4 = FFFF8360, seeding the filter state at the threshold.

1D x10 uint8, data 0xD6870, range 15 .. 100. Triage hint: engine control.

Looks up a value of 15..100 against vehicle speed. The result is multiplied by a scalar chosen by ol_condition_selector and by a scalar gated on cl_ol_gate_flag==2 (otherwise 1.0), and the product is stored to RAM 0xFFFF8588 for some other routine.
