### 0xAE85C -- unknown – signed (0x8000-offset) lookup, lower/negative half of a paired band, min-peak-held into FFFF8D24 and packed into FFFF8D44

1D x4 float32, data 0xD6AA8, range -0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

The table is read by table_desc_2d_uint16 with the same input as its sibling 0xAE850. The result has 0x8000 subtracted to give a signed word, which is stored to FFFF8D20. A running minimum of it is kept at FFFF8D24, then scaled by the ROM constant at 0xD619E/256, floored at -32768 and min'ed with the live value. That result is packed together with the sibling's max-held value and written to FFFF8D44. What physical quantity it is cannot be told from this function.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: the 16-bit value at FFFF8D5A (gbr+70), returned by the routine at 0xBDD88 (label 'peripheral_io_write' unverified) called with r4 = word at FFFF8D14 and r5 = 0. Physical meaning unknown.
- **Output units:** unknown; the uint16 cell minus 0x8000 gives a signed raw count. The float32 decode shown (values ~0, axis like denormals 33/66/132/198 as integers) looks like the wrong cell type for this routine.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Nothing in the listing ties the input at FFFF8D5A or the outputs FFFF8D20/8D24/8D44 to a physical quantity. The 'ignition timing' subsystem and the 0xBDD88 name are unverified hints. The decoded cell values also come from a float32 interpretation that disagrees with the uint16 lookup routine actually called.
- **Next step:** Re-decode descriptor 0xAE85C using the uint16 layout expected by 0xBE8C4 to get the real axis and cell values (cell minus 0x8000). Then search for readers of FFFF8D44, FFFF8D20 and FFFF8D24, for whatever resets FFFF8D24/FFFF8D22, and for the producer of FFFF8D14, to learn what the min/max band feeds.
- **Cited:**
  - 05724E/057252: descriptor 0xAE85C is passed in r4 to 0xBE8C4 (table_desc_2d_uint16 [TRACED]). r5 = extu.w of the word at gbr+70 (FFFF8D5A), the same input used for table 0xAE850 at 05723C.
  - 05723A/057238: gbr+70 is written from the r0 returned by jsr 0xBDD88 at 057234, whose r4 is the word at gbr+0 (FFFF8D14).
  - 057256-05725A: exts.w, then sub r14 (r14 = 0x8000), then mov.w r0,@(12,gbr). The output is a signed offset value stored at FFFF8D20.
  - 05726C-05727A: if the new value is below the word at gbr+16 (cmp/ge then bt skip), it is stored to gbr+16 (FFFF8D24). This is a running minimum, mirroring the running maximum of the sibling table at gbr+14 (05725C-05726A).
  - 0572AE-0572D4: the minimum is multiplied by the word at 0xD619E, divided by 256 with rounding toward zero for negatives, and floored at 0x8000 (-32768).
  - 0572D6-0572E2: the smaller of that scaled minimum and the live gbr+12 value is chosen.
  - 0572DC-0572EC: the chosen value goes through uint16_pack [TRACED] at 0xBE9B0 and the 32-bit result is stored at gbr+48 (FFFF8D44). FFFF8D44 is the consumer handoff; there is no use in this listing beyond that.
  - 0572AC: gbr+18 (FFFF8D26) receives the sibling's max-held result, built the same way with the constant at 0xD619C.

### 0xAE874 -- Battery-voltage compensation gain for a signed actuator duty (approx. 14 V / Vbatt, 1/256 units)

1D x6 float32, data 0xD6AE0, range 0 .. 16. Triage hint: unassigned.

Looks up a voltage-compensation gain from a float at FFFF6598 over 6..16 breakpoints, multiplies it by the signed 16-bit command at FFFF8D38 and divides by 256. The result is clamped to plus or minus the word at 0xD6204 and stored to FFFF8D3A, so the actuator gets the same effective drive whatever the supply voltage.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at FFFF6598 (labelled adc_processed_temp [UNVERIFIED]); 6..16 breakpoints fit battery/supply voltage in volts much better than temperature
- **Output units:** Dimensionless gain in 1/256 steps (0x100 = 1.0). Cells are really uint16, not float32: about 597, ?, 358, ?, 256, 224 at 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 16 V, i.e. 2.33, 1.40, 1.00, 0.875, which matches 14/V.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Two links are unverified. First, that FFFF6598 is battery voltage: its label says temperature and only the 6..16 breakpoints and the 14/V shape argue otherwise. Second, which actuator FFFF8D38/8D3A drives is not shown.
- **Next step:** Find the writer of float FFFF6598 and check that it scales the battery-voltage ADC channel. Then search the readers of FFFF8D3A and FFFF8D53, and the writer of FFFF8D38, to identify the actuator. Also re-decode 0xD6AE0 as 6 big-endian uint16 values to get the 8 V and 12 V cells.
- **Cited:**
  - 0577EC/0577F0: the lookup goes through table_desc_1d_uint16 [TRACED], not a float routine. So the float32 cell type in the descriptor is wrong and the 'denormal' floats are uint16 pairs.
  - Reinterpreting the data as uint16 gives high halves 0x0255, 0x0166, 0x0100 (and 0x00E0 as the low half of the third float). These equal 14/6, 14/10, 14/14 and 14/16 times 256, the classic 1/V supply compensation.
  - 0577EE/0577F2: the input is fmov.s @FFFF6598 into fr4, a float read before the call.
  - 0577F4-0577FC: extu.w r0 then mul.l with the signed word at FFFF8D38. The table output multiplies a signed command value.
  - 057808-05781A: add 255 if negative, then shift right by 8. This is a signed divide by 256, so the table is in 1/256 units.
  - 057800-05780C: the word at 0xD6204 is loaded into r5 (as r1) and its negation into r6. The routine at 0xBDD5A, labelled peripheral_io_read [TRACED], is then called with value, +limit and -limit, which looks like a symmetric clamp.
  - 05782A: the result is stored with mov.w r0,@(2,r7), r7=FFFF8D38, i.e. to FFFF8D3A.
  - 05782C-05783A: the following function sets byte FFFF8D53 = (FFFF8D3A < 0), a direction flag consistent with a bidirectional (H-bridge) actuator.

### 0xAE880 -- Input-indexed upper cap (uint16) on the RAM word at 0xFFFF8D3A, written to 0xFFFF8D40; purpose unknown

1D x2 float32, data 0xD6AF4, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

A 1D lookup indexed by the float at 0xFFFF6598 returns a 16-bit limit. When the flag byte at 0xFFFF8D65 equals 1 and the value derived from 0xFFFF8D3A exceeds that limit, the limit is written to 0xFFFF8D40. Otherwise the derived value is clamped between fixed ROM bounds before being written there.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float read from 0xFFFF6598 (labelled adc_processed_temp [UNVERIFIED]). The breakpoints 6 and 16 fit a temperature in °C, but nothing in the listing fixes the quantity.
- **Output units:** Same raw uint16 units as the value at 0xFFFF8D3A and 0xFFFF8D40. The physical unit is not established. The float decode 3.85e-34 is almost certainly the bytes 0x08000000 read as float. Read as uint16 that is 2048 at x=6 and 0 at x=16.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Several links are unknown. The physical quantity of 0xFFFF8D3A/0xFFFF8D40 is not established. 0xBDD88 is not shown: it is labelled peripheral_io_write [UNVERIFIED] yet returns a value, so it may be a conversion or read accessor. The axis is a temperature only by label. The uint16 reading of the data (2048, 0) rests on the routine name and the bit pattern, not on a checked descriptor byte.
- **Next step:** First, dump the descriptor at 0xAE880 and the data bytes at 0xD6AF4 to confirm uint16 cells 0x0800, 0x0000 and any scale. Second, read the constants at 0xD6206, 0xD6208, 0xD620A and 0xD620C. Third, disassemble 0xBDD88. Finally, search for readers of 0xFFFF8D40 and 0xFFFF8D55 and for writers of 0xFFFF8D65 and 0xFFFF8D3A to learn what quantity is being capped.
- **Cited:**
  - 057846/05784C: descriptor 0xAE880 is passed to 0xBDDBAC... correction, to 0xBE8AC (table_desc_1d_uint16 [TRACED]), with fr4 loaded from @0xFFFF6598 in the delay slot at 05784E. A uint16 routine on a descriptor decoded as float32 means the cell type in the decode is probably wrong.
  - 057850: the 16-bit result is saved to @r15.
  - 057852-05785C: the word at gbr+0 (0xFFFF8D3A) goes through 0xBDD88 with r5=0, and the result is zero-extended into r5.
  - 057860-057864: cmp/gt r2,r5 tests whether the derived value is above the table value (unsigned).
  - 057866-05786A: the cap applies only if the byte at gbr+43 (0xFFFF8D65) equals 1.
  - 05786C-05786E: in that case the table value is stored to gbr+6 (0xFFFF8D40), and at 0578C2 the byte at gbr+27 (0xFFFF8D55) is set to 1.
  - 057882-0578BC: otherwise the derived value is clamped. Above the word at 0xD6206 it becomes the word at 0xD620A. Below the word at 0xD6208 it becomes the word at 0xD620C. In range it passes through unchanged.
  - 0578BE-0578C2: the result is stored to 0xFFFF8D40 and flag 0xFFFF8D55 is cleared.
  - The table therefore acts as a ceiling on a 16-bit quantity that depends on the input.

### 0xAEC60 -- RPM-stability tolerance band vs. time-stable (narrowing |rpm - reference| window)

1D x20 uint16, data 0xD7194, range 40.039 .. 400. Triage hint: knock.

Gives the allowed rpm deviation, in rpm. It starts at 400 and narrows to 40 as a stable-time counter grows. Each pass the code compares |rpm_current - value@FFFF895C| against it: within the band, a stable flag is set and the counter is incremented; outside it, the flag is cleared and the counter is reset to 0.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X = uint16 counter at FFFF89E8 (gbr+0) times 8.0. It counts consecutive passes in the stable condition, so it is probably elapsed time in ms if the task runs every 8 ms. The task period is not shown. Range 0..~10000.
- **Output units:** rpm (deviation threshold), assuming FFFF895C is an rpm-like reference
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low

Check the scheduler for this function's call period to fix the X units.

  - 051C80/051C82/051C90: fr8 is loaded from the pool at 0x051D60. The words are 0x4100 0x0000, so the float is 8.0. fmul fr8,fr4 runs in the delay slot, so the table input is counter*8

  - 051C94-051C98: ignition_system_state (FFFF8910) must equal 1, or the flag is cleared at 051CC2

### 0xAEEF8 -- ECT-dependent persistence-count threshold for a low-RPM condition (sets flag FFFF8C8E)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD76D2, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

A per-call counter at FFFF8C8C counts up, saturating, while flag FFFF8CA2 == 1 and rpm is at or below the float limit at 0xD67B0, and resets to 0 otherwise. This table gives the count the counter must reach, as a function of coolant temperature, before flag byte FFFF8C8E is set to 1.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, deg C (-40..110), from definition
- **Output units:** Counter ticks (one per execution of this function; period unknown). The descriptor scale and bias are denormal and therefore bogus. Back-solving the decoded values gives raw integers 24,24,24,24,24,24,24,30,37,41,44,48,52,55,55,55: a longer persistence is required when warm.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The units and real meaning depend on things outside this listing: the call rate of this function, what FFFF8CA2 signals, the rpm limit at 0xD67B0, and how the bogus scale relates to the raw cells. What consumes flag FFFF8C8E is also not shown. The ignition-timing subsystem hint is unverified.
- **Next step:** First, search for readers of byte FFFF8C8E to learn what the flag gates. Then search for writers of FFFF8CA2 to see what condition it represents, read the float at 0xD67B0 for the rpm limit, and find which scheduler task calls 0x055E60 so the counts can be converted to seconds.
- **Cited:**
  - 055E64-055E6A: fr8 = rpm_current (FFFF6624), fr14 = ect_current (FFFF6350).
  - 055E6C-055E72: the count-up path runs only when byte FFFF8CA2 == 1 (cmp/eq #1).
  - 055E74-055E7A: it also requires rpm (fr8) not greater than the float at 0xD67B0 (fcmp/gt fr9,fr8; bt skips).
  - 055E7C-055E8A: uint16_add_sat(FFFF8C8C, 1) is stored back to FFFF8C8C, a saturating increment.
  - 055E8C-055E90: otherwise FFFF8C8C is cleared to 0, so it counts consecutive qualifying calls.
  - 055E92-055E98: this table is looked up through table_desc_1d_uint16 with fr4 = ECT (fmov fr14,fr4 in the delay slot).
  - 055E9A-055EA4: r2 = extu.w(result) and r4 = counter; cmp/ge r2,r4; movt; mov.b r0,@(2,r14). So FFFF8C8E = (counter >= table(ECT)).
  - No clamp or multiplier follows the lookup. The value is used directly as the threshold.

### 0xAF3D4 -- Coolant-temperature-dependent start delay (minimum counter value before a staged correction is enabled)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD879E, range 0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

This table looks up a uint16 threshold from gated coolant temperature and stores it to 0xFFFF8D9C (GBR+32). A counter word at 0xFFFF67EE must reach this threshold before the staged correction (ECT-table × battery-table product, clamped 0..100) can be non-zero. A second counter at 0xFFFF67EA is compared against the threshold plus three ROM offsets to choose which stage table is applied.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature, °C (the input register fr15 is loaded from ect_gated_c 0xFFFF6354; breakpoints run -40..110)
- **Output units:** Raw integer counts, compared directly with a uint16 counter of unknown tick (likely time or cycles since some event). The listed float scale and bias are denormal garbage. Raw cells decode to roughly 61,61,39,20,10,0,...: about 61 counts at -40 to -30 °C, falling to 0 at 10 °C and above.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What the counters at 0xFFFF67EE and 0xFFFF67EA count (time since start, ignitions, or something else) and what the final 0..1 output written via 0xC70C controls are not shown. The name 'start delay' rests on the counter meaning. The 'ignition timing' subsystem comes only from the GBR label.
- **Next step:** Read the float at 0xD8710, the minimum-ECT gate. If it is 10 °C or higher, the non-zero cells (at or below 0 °C) can never take effect and the table is inert. Then find the writers of 0xFFFF67EE and 0xFFFF67EA to fix the counter units. Also decode what 0xC70C does with fr4 and r4=0, and search for readers of 0xFFFF8D9C.
- **Cited:**
  - 0580A2: fmov fr15,fr4 sets the lookup input. fr15 was loaded at 05800E from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c [TRACED]), so the X axis is coolant temperature.
  - 05809E-0580A4: the table is read via table_desc_1d_uint16 and the integer result is stored with mov.w r0,@(32,gbr), i.e. to 0xFFFF8D9C.
  - 0580EE-0580F6: the stored value is reloaded with mov.w @(32,gbr). It is compared by cmp/ge r5,r2 against r12, the word from 0xFFFF67EE (05800A). If the counter is below the table value, the code branches to 058194, where fldi0 fr4 forces the correction to 0.
  - 058110-05811A, 058136-058140, 05815C-058166: the table value plus ROM words at 0xD86FC, 0xD86FE and 0xD8700 is compared (cmp/hs) against r11, the word from 0xFFFF67EA. This selects among the ECT tables at r10-20, r10-16 and r10-12, with r10-8 (the 2D rpm table) as the fallback. Each choice is multiplied by r10-4, the battery-voltage table (fmul at 058128 and similar).
  - 058196-0581CC: the selected product is clamped 0..100 (0x42C8) and written to 0xFFFF8D7C (r10-32). That value is added to r10-28 and capped at 25600 (0x46C8), then divided by 100 and clamped 0..1. The result goes to 0xC70C with r4=0 under IRQ masking.
  - Other gates on the same path: the cl_state byte (r13) must be 0, the transient flag must be 0, battery voltage must lie within the 0xD8704/0xD8708 window, rpm must be below 0xD870C, ECT must be at or above 0xD8710, and the load hysteresis flag GBR+34 must equal 1.

### 0xAF3E0 -- ECT-based duty, first time stage, of a battery-compensated PWM output (identity of output unknown)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD87BE, range 50 .. 99.2. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Looks up a 16-point value against coolant temperature. During the first time window after an event, when all enable conditions hold, it is multiplied by a battery-voltage table, clamped to 0..100, divided by 100 and passed as a 0..1 fraction to output routine 0xC70C with channel argument r4=0. In the second and third windows two sibling ECT tables take its place, and a 2D rpm table follows after that.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature in °C, input fr15 = ect_gated_c (0xFFFF6354), breakpoints -40..110
- **Output units:** percent-like duty (0..100 after the voltage multiplier; converted to a 0..1 fraction before output)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What 0xC70C drives with channel 0 is not in the listing. Without it, the output cannot be called a heater, solenoid or other actuator. The 0..100 clamp suggests percent but does not prove it, because the voltage-table multiplier could rescale.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x0000C70C to see which peripheral or PWM register channel r4=0 writes. Also search for readers of 0xFFFF8D7C and of the accumulator 0xFFFF8D80, and for writers of the counters 0xFFFF67EA and 0xFFFF67EE, to learn what event the time stages count from.
- **Cited:**
  - 058070-058078: descriptor 0xAF3E0 is looked up via table_lookup_1D (r14=0xBE830) with fr4=fr15, which is ect_gated_c loaded at 05800C. The result is stored at r10-20 = 0xFFFF8D88.
  - 05807A-05808C: sibling ECT tables 0xAF3F4 and 0xAF408 are stored at 0xFFFF8D8C and 0xFFFF8D90. 2D table 0xAF41C (rpm fr13 x 0xFFFF63E8) is stored at 0xFFFF8D94. Battery-voltage table 0xAF3AC is stored at 0xFFFF8D98 (0580A6-0580B4).
  - 05810E-058132: if r11 (0xFFFF67EA) < word@0xD86FC + gbr@32, then fr4 = [8D88]*[8D98]. gbr@32 is the ECT uint16 table 0xAF3D4. Later windows use offsets 0xD86FE and 0xD8700 with the sibling tables, and the final case uses the 2D table.
  - 058196-0581A4: the product goes through float_clamp_range(fr4, 0, 100.0), using literal 0x42C8 at 058290, and is stored to 0xFFFF8D7C.
  - 0581A6-0581BA: 0xFFFF8D80 += clamped value, then float_min(25600) applied (literal 0x46C8 at 058298). This is an accumulator.
  - 0581BC-0581E0: clamped value / 100 goes through clamp 0..1 and is sent to 0xC70C with r4=0, inside irq_level_set/restore. This is a hardware or output write.
  - 0580B0-0580F6: enables are cl_state byte==0, transient flag==0, battery inside the D8704/D8708 window, rpm < D870C, ECT >= D8710, gbr@34==1 (load-vs-rpm hysteresis), and counter 0xFFFF67EE >= ECT table 0xAF3D4. Otherwise the output is 0 (058194).

### 0xAF3F4 -- Stage-2 coolant-temperature duty base (percent, before battery-voltage factor) for a PWM output written through 0xC70C, channel 0

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD87DE, range 29.691 .. 59.769. Triage hint: ignition timing.

Gives a base duty (about 30..60) against coolant temperature. It is the middle of three ECT tables (AF3E0, AF3F4, AF408) that are picked by an elapsed counter. The chosen value is multiplied by a battery-voltage factor, clamped to 0..100 and divided by 100, and the resulting 0..1 command goes to routine 0xC70C under an interrupt-level guard.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: coolant temperature in °C, read from ect_gated_c at 0xFFFF6354 (fr15). The -40..110 breakpoints fit a Celsius axis.
- **Output units:** Probably percent duty, pre-compensation. The product with the battery-voltage factor is clamped to 0..100 and then divided by 100.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** What 0xC70C actually drives is unknown. So is the scale of the AF3AC battery factor. If that factor is not near 1.0, this table is not directly percent duty, and the physical actuator is unidentified. Nothing shown ties it to ignition timing.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0xC70C and see which peripheral register channel r4=0 writes, for example an ATU/PWM duty register. Decode table AF3AC (battery-voltage axis) to fix the multiplier scale. Search for readers of 0xFFFF8D7C and 0xFFFF8D80 to identify the actuator.
- **Cited:**
  - 05800C/05800E: fr15 is loaded from 0xFFFF6354 (ect_gated_c). 05807A-05807E: descriptor 0xAF3F4 goes to table_lookup_1D (r14=0xBE830) with fr4=fr15.
  - 058080/058082: the result is stored to r10-16, which is 0xFFFF8D8C (r10=0xFFFF8D9C).
  - 0580A6-0580B4: table AF3AC is looked up with battery_voltage (stack+4) and the result is stored to r10-4 (0xFFFF8D98).
  - 058134-058156: if the counter in r11 (uint16 from 0xFFFF67EA) is below *0xD86FE plus gbr@32, then fr4 = [r10-16] * [r10-4]. That is this table times the battery-voltage factor. The neighbouring branches at 058120 and 05816C use the r10-20 and r10-12 tables against thresholds 0xD86FC and 0xD8700, so this is a time-staged schedule.
  - gbr@32 comes from the ECT uint16 table AF3D4 (05809C-0580A4), so the stage boundaries are offset by an ECT-dependent amount.
  - Staging is only reached when all of these hold: r13 (cl_state) is 0, the transient flag is 0, battery voltage is inside the 0xD8704..0xD8708 window, rpm is below 0xD870C, ECT is at or above 0xD8710, the low-load hysteresis flag (gbr@34) is 1, and the counter at 0xFFFF67EE is at or above gbr@32. Otherwise fr4 is 0 (058194).
  - 058196-0581A4: float_clamp_range clamps fr4 to 0..100 (0x42C8 = 100.0) and stores it to 0xFFFF8D7C. 0581A6-0581BA: it is also accumulated into 0xFFFF8D80, capped at 25600.
  - 0581BC-0581CE: the value is divided by 100 and clamped to 0..1. 0581D2-0581E2: it is passed in fr4, with r4=0, to 0xC70C between irq_level_set and irq_level_restore.

### 0xAF438 -- Intake-air temperature-ratio square-root correction, sqrt(T_ref/T_intake), for a normalized airflow ratio

1D x17 float32, data 0xD895C, range 0.87063 .. 1.1309. Triage hint: unassigned.

Maps a clamped temperature ratio, a constant divided by (IAT + offset, likely Tref/(IAT+273.15) Kelvin), to its square root. The result multiplies an rpm-based reference airflow and a barometric ratio. MAF is then divided by that product, capped at 1 and scaled to a uint8 stored at 0xFFFFAF73.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: intake temperature ratio T_ref/(IAT+273.15), dimensionless. The breakpoints 0.758..1.279 match 298.15K/(IAT+273.15) for IAT 120..-40 C.
- **Output units:** dimensionless multiplier (sqrt of temperature ratio)
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The pool constants at 0x0A87FC, 0x0A8804 and 0x0A8818 are not shown. That IAT+273.15 and 298.15 (and 101.325 kPa) are the values is inferred from the axis matching -40..120 C. The purpose of the byte at 0xFFFFAF73, a 'diag_fault_history_byte' label that may be wrong, is not established.
- **Next step:** Dump the floats at 0x0A87FC, 0x0A8804, 0x0A8818 and 0x0A8828 to confirm the 273.15 / 298.15 / atm-reference constants. Then search for readers of 0xFFFFAF73 (and of 0xFFFFAFB0/0xFFFFAFB8) to learn what consumes the normalized MAF ratio.
- **Cited:**
  - 0A861A/0A861C: fr14 = iat_current (0xFFFF6364)
  - 0A8642-0A8646: fr14 += pool constant at 0x0A87FC (likely 273.15, a Kelvin offset)
  - 0A8648-0A8650: float_safe_div(const@0x0A8804, fr14) gives a ratio of a constant over absolute temperature
  - 0A8654-0A8662: float_clamp_range of that ratio, stored at r14-12 = 0xFFFFAFAC
  - 0A8664-0A866A: this table is looked up with fr4 = @(-12,r14), the clamped temperature ratio
  - 0A866C/0A866E: result stored at 0xFFFFAFB0
  - Table data: every value equals sqrt(breakpoint), e.g. sqrt(0.758)=0.8706 and sqrt(1.279)=1.131, so the table is a square-root function
  - 0A8670-0A867E: fr15 = (atm_pressure/const) * rpm-table result @0xFFFFAFB8 (from descriptor 0xAF444 at 0A8636) * this table. This is the classic P/P0*sqrt(T0/T) choked-flow or density correction of a reference flow
  - 0A8680-0A86B6: if fr15 passes a deadband check, compute MAF(0xFFFF63B4)/fr15, take float_min(...,1), scale, convert with axis_frac_to_uint8 and store a byte to 0xFFFFAF73

### 0xAF444 -- OBD calculated-load reference: peak (WOT) airflow vs RPM at standard conditions

1D x18 float32, data 0xD89E8, range 1 .. 10000. Triage hint: unassigned.

Gives the reference full-load mass airflow for the current RPM. The code corrects it for intake temperature (second table AF438) and barometric pressure, divides measured MAF by the result, caps the ratio at 1, scales it and stores it as a byte. This matches the SAE J1979 PID 0x04 'calculated load value' definition: current airflow divided by peak WOT airflow as a function of RPM, corrected for altitude and ambient temperature.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: engine speed, rpm (input fr4 = rpm_current, loaded at 0A8618)
- **Output units:** g/s of mass airflow (reference peak airflow), inferred from magnitude and the division into the MAF sample
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The pool constants at 0A87FC/0A8804/0A8818/0A8828 are not decoded, so the 273.15 K, 101.3 kPa and 255 scaling are assumed. maf_sample_in at FFFF63B4 is only [CITED], so the numerator is not confirmed to be MAF in g/s.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0x0A87FC, 0x0A8804, 0x0A8818 and 0x0A8828; the expected values are about 273.15, 293-298, 101.3 and 255 or 100. Then search for readers of FFFFAF73 to confirm it feeds OBD Mode 01 PID 04 reporting or similar, rather than fuel or timing logic. Also confirm FFFF63B4 is MAF in g/s.
- **Cited:**
  - 0A8618: fmov.s @r2,fr4 loads rpm_current [TRACED] into fr4, the argument register for table_lookup_1D at 0A863A, so the X axis is RPM.
  - 0A863E-0A8640: the lookup result fr0 is stored to FFFFAFB8 (pointer kept in r14).
  - 0A8646-0A8650: iat_current + const, then float_safe_div(const, IAT+const). This looks like a T_ref/(IAT+273.15) temperature ratio; the pool constants are not shown.
  - 0A8654-0A8662: the ratio is clamped and stored to FFFFAFAC. 0A8664-0A866E: that ratio indexes table AF438, with the result stored to FFFFAFB0 as the temperature correction.
  - 0A8674-0A867E: fr15 = (atm_pressure/const) * table_AF444 * table_AF438, i.e. reference airflow corrected for baro and temperature.
  - 0A8680-0A8692: float_deadband_check on fr15. If it fails, the output is 0xFF (-1).
  - 0A8694: fdiv fr15,fr12 computes maf_sample / corrected reference. 0A869C: float_min(...,1.0) caps it at 100%. 0A86A2: multiplied by a constant. 0A86A6: axis_frac_to_uint8. 0A86B6: byte stored to FFFFAF73.
  - Data shape: 14.8 at 800 rpm rising to about 221 at 6000-6800 fits WOT airflow of a 2.5 L turbo in g/s. The 10000 at 0 rpm and 1 at 60 rpm force the load to about 0 near standstill.
  - 0A862A-0A8634: if the cal byte at 0xD88AF == 90 and FFFF9A54 > FFFF61FC, the output is forced to 0. This guard is unrelated to the identity.

### 0xAF510 -- Hot-condition duration/threshold vs max(ECT, IAT), latched at entry (meaning unconfirmed)

1D x16 float32, data 0xD94F0, range 0 .. 4500. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

On the pass where the byte flag at GBR+18 (0xFFFF8E46) is zero, the code takes max(ECT, IAT), stores it at 0xFFFF8E34, and looks up two 1D tables against it. This table's result goes as a 16-bit word to 0xFFFF8E3E. - **Next step:** Search for readers of 0xFFFF8E3E (a GBR+10 word access with GBR=0xFFFF8E34, or an absolute load of 0xFFFF8E3E) and check whether it is compared against the counters at 0xFFFF365C/0xFFFF3660. Separately, re-decode the 0xD94F0 data as uint16 to confirm the real cell type.

  - 0x059710-0x059716: the lookups only run when the byte at GBR+18 is zero, which suggests a one-time latch, e.g. at start or reset.
  - 0x05972A: the sibling table 0xAF504 is looked up with the same input and stored to GBR+8 (0xFFFF8E3C).
  - 0x059760-0x059790: when the flag equals 1, counters at 0xFFFF365C and 0xFFFF3660 are incremented with saturation. The second increment is gated by fr14 (0xFFFF68D0) not exceeding the constant at 0xD93D4. This is consistent with the latched value being a time limit compared against those counters elsewhere, but that comparison is not shown.

### 0xAF550 -- Cumulative-airflow threshold that ends a latched post-event flag (packed to 0xFFFF366A)

2D 11x9 uint16, data 0xD971C, range 5000.4 .. 76700. Triage hint: knock.

Gives an accumulated-airflow limit that falls from 76700 at X=0 to about 5000 at X>=10000 and is flat across Y. Once the running sum of maf_smoothed_gps reaches this limit, a latched flag is cleared. The flag is also cleared by a uint16 counter reaching a companion table, or by vehicle speed reaching a constant.

- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The X input 0xFFFF8E38 and Y input 0xFFFF2254 are untraced, so what scales the threshold, and what the latched flag gates downstream, are unknown. The fr4=X / fr5=Y order of table_lookup_2D is assumed from convention.
- **Next step:** Search for readers of byte 0xFFFF366A (cal_mirror_cache_B+2) and of 0xFFFF8E4C to learn what the latched flag gates. Search for writers of 0xFFFF8E38 and 0xFFFF2254 to identify the axes. Also find what drives gbr bytes 0xFFFF8E4E (+38) and 0xFFFF8E49 (+17), which form the set/reset event.
- **Cited:**

  - 059A1C-059A1E: fr13 = @(0xFFFF3668-28) = 0xFFFF364C, the previous accumulator value.
  - 059A54: fr13 += fr14, the accumulator adds MAF each run.
  - 059A58-059A60: the sum is clamped by float_clamp_range between 0 and the constant at 0x059BB8.
  - 059A64-059A6A: the sum is written back to 0xFFFF364C via desc_read_float_safe.
  - 059A4A-059A4E: the accumulator is reset to 0 when gbr+38==0 and gbr+17==1.
  - 059A50-059A52: accumulation is skipped when check_transient_flag returns nonzero.
  - 059A6E-059A74: the table is looked up with fr4 = fr15 = @(0xFFFF8E5C-36) = 0xFFFF8E38 and fr5 = fr12 = @0xFFFF2254.
  - 059A76-059A78: the result is stored to 0xFFFF8E4C.
  - 059AAE-059AB4: fr8 = table result; fcmp/gt fr13,fr8 then bf -> 059AC0 r14=0. When the accumulated airflow is >= the table value, the flag is cleared.
  - 059A7A-059A84 and 059AA4-059AAC: a companion int table 0xAF53C on the same inputs is compared with the uint16 at 0xFFFF67EC (r13). counter >= threshold also clears r14.
  - 059A86-059A92: vehicle_speed_kmh >= constant @0xD93E8 sets gbr+39; at 059AB6-059AC0 that flag also clears r14.
  - 059A9A-059AA2: r14 is set to 1 under the same gbr+38/+17 condition that resets the accumulator, so this is a latch set by an event.
  - 059AC2-059ACA: r14 is packed by uint8_pack and stored to 0xFFFF3668+2.
  - 059A34-059A3A: the previous r14 comes from byte 0xFFFF366A, so the flag holds until a clear condition is met.

That result is the threshold below which the float at 0xFFFF85E4 sets flag byte 0xFFFF9116. The companion table 0xAF66C, stored at 0xFFFF90F8, gives the clear threshold.   - 060F0A-060F0C: result fr0 is stored at 0xFFFF90F4 (r13-44).

### 0xAF66C -- Upper (clear) hysteresis threshold for flag 0xFFFF9116, mode 2 of 0xFFFF5DB5 (rpm-like X axis)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD9D04, range 0 .. 350. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

When byte 0xFFFF5DB5 == 2, this table is looked up with the float at 0xFFFF90E8. The result goes to 0xFFFF90F8 and acts as the clear threshold of a hysteresis test on the float at 0xFFFF85E4. A companion table, 0xAF658, supplies the set threshold, stored at 0xFFFF90F4. ### 0xAF680 -- Upper threshold (flag-set) on the value at FFFF85E4 versus the input at FFFF90E8 (probably RPM). This is the mode-3 / FFFF5DB8 variant of a hysteresis pair with AF694.

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD9D64, range 0 .. 340. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

The table gives a threshold from the input at FFFF90E8, stored to FFFF90F4. If that threshold is greater than the float at FFFF85E4, the flag at FFFF9116 (gbr+100) is set to 1. The flag is cleared only once FFFF85E4 reaches the threshold from companion table AF694 (stored to FFFF90F8), so the two tables form a set/clear hysteresis band.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: float at FFFF90E8 (0xFFFF9120-56). The breakpoints 300..6000 look like engine speed in rpm, but the source of the variable is not in the listing.
- **Output units:** Same units as the float at FFFF85E4, whatever that quantity is. It is not established; the label 'fuel_knock_state_var' is unverified.
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of both FFFF85E4 (the compared quantity) and FFFF90E8 (the axis input) is not shown. The axis-as-RPM reading rests only on the breakpoint magnitudes, and the existing label on FFFF85E4 is untrusted.
- **Next step:** Find the writers of FFFF85E4 and FFFF90E8 to fix both units. Decode AF694 to confirm it is at or above AF680 (the clear threshold). Search for readers of FFFF9116 and FFFF90C8 to learn what the resulting condition flag drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 060EE2-060EE4: fr14 is loaded from @0xFFFF85E4 and is the comparand for this table's output.
  - 060EE6-060F1C: the mode byte at FFFF5DB5 selects a table pair. Mode 1 uses AF630/AF644 and mode 2 uses AF658/AF66C. Mode 3, or a non-zero FFFF5DB8, branches to 060F36 and uses this table AF680 with AF694. Otherwise the code falls to AF630.
  - 060F36-060F40: r4=0xAF680 and fr4=@(r13-56)=FFFF90E8, then jsr table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830.
  - 060F42-060F44: the result is stored to FFFF90F4 (r13-44).
  - 060F46-060F52: AF694 is looked up with the same input and the result stored to FFFF90F8.
  - 060F54-060F60: if FFFF90F4 > fr14, gbr+100 (FFFF9116) is set to 1.
  - 060F62-060F6C: else, if FFFF90F8 <= fr14, gbr+100 is cleared. This is hysteresis.
  - 06108A-0610BE: the gbr+100 flag is one of several conditions, along with gbr+101, FFFF90E0 against the constant at D9B30, and r12 (FFFF5E96)==0. Together they gate a saturating counter at gbr+0 (FFFF90B2) via uint16_add_sat, and the outcome is written to gbr+22 (FFFF90C8).

### 0xAF694 -- Hysteresis release threshold (default mode) for enable flag 0xFFFF9116, versus the 0xFFFF85E4 quantity, indexed by 0xFFFF90E8 (probably RPM)

1D x16 uint16, data 0xD9DC4, range 0 .. 350. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

This is the upper (clear) half of a hysteresis pair. A companion table, 0xAF680 in this branch, sets byte 0xFFFF9116 to 1 when the float at 0xFFFF85E4 is below its value. This table's value clears the byte to 0 once that float reaches or exceeds it; between the two values the byte holds. The byte is one of several conditions that gate a saturating up-counter at 0xFFFF90B2.

- **Axes:** X: float read from 0xFFFF90E8 (r13=0xFFFF9120, disp -56). Breakpoints 300..6000 look like engine speed in rpm, but the producer is not in the listing.
- **Output units:** Same units as the float at 0xFFFF85E4 (unknown). The value is a threshold in that quantity, not a percentage.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical quantity at 0xFFFF85E4 being thresholded, and the axis input at 0xFFFF90E8, are not produced in this listing. The rpm reading of the axis rests only on breakpoint magnitudes.
- **Next step:** Search for writers of 0xFFFF85E4 and 0xFFFF90E8 to fix the compared quantity and the axis. Search for readers of 0xFFFF90C4 (and 0xFFFF90B2) to learn what the counter enables, e.g. a diagnostic monitor. Also check which value of 0xFFFF5DB5 is normal at runtime, to see whether this branch executes.
- **Cited:**
  - 060F3A-060F44: companion table 0xAF680 is looked up via table_lookup_1D 0xBE830 with fr4=@(0xFFFF90E8); the result is stored to 0xFFFF90F4 (r13-44).
  - 060F46-060F52: this descriptor 0xAF694 is looked up with the same input; fr0 is stored to 0xFFFF90F8 (r13-40).
  - 060EE2-060EE4: fr14 is loaded from 0xFFFF85E4 (label not trusted).
  - 060F54-060F60: if @(0xFFFF90F4) > fr14, byte @(gbr+100)=0xFFFF9116 is set to 1.
  - 060F62-060F6C: otherwise, if @(0xFFFF90F8) > fr14 is false (fr14 >= this table's value), 0xFFFF9116 is cleared to 0. This is classic hysteresis, with this table as the release threshold.
  - 060EF4-060F1C: the table pair is chosen by byte 0xFFFF5DB5 (values 1/2/3) and 0xFFFF5DB8; this pair is the fall-through/mode-3 set. Sibling pairs are (0xAF630, 0xAF644) and (0xAF658, 0xAF66C).
  - 06108A-0610BE: 0xFFFF9116==1 is required, together with other flags, for uint16_add_sat to increment the counter at gbr+0 = 0xFFFF90B2.
  - 0610E6-0610EE: a separate routine compares 0xFFFF90B2 against calibration 0xD9AD8 to set 0xFFFF90C4.

### 0xAF6BC -- Battery-voltage (and probably temperature) compensation multiplier for a bidirectional actuator drive command

2D 4x3 uint16, data 0xD9E44, range 0.80902 .. 1.99. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

Looks up a unitless gain near 1.0 (exactly 1.0 at X=14, Y=20) from battery voltage and a second input at 0xFFFF90E0. It multiplies a base command at 0xFFFF9094 by that gain, caps the product at a constant, divides by the same constant to get a fraction, sets the sign from a direction flag and tail-calls 0x133D8, which looks like an output driver.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (-20, 20, 100): the float at 0xFFFF90E0 (0xFFFF90B8+40), passed in fr4. The range suggests a temperature in °C (coolant or an actuator temperature estimate), but its writer is not in the listing.; X (10, 12, 14, 16): almost certainly battery voltage in volts, from fr5 = @0xFFFF4130 [TRACED battery_voltage]. This rests on the magnitude match; the register-to-axis convention of table_lookup_2D was not checked.
- **Output units:** unitless multiplier (scale 1/16384); 1.0 at 14 V / 20 °C
- **Live lever:** yes. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The identity of the actuator and of the 0xFFFF90E0 input are unverified, and the voltage-to-X mapping rests on the breakpoint magnitudes, not on a checked register convention for table_lookup_2D. That 0x133D8 is a signed duty/PWM driver is also inferred, not shown.
- **Next step:** Disassemble 0x133D8 to see which output port or timer it writes, which fixes the actuator. Find the writers of 0xFFFF90E0 (Y input) and 0xFFFF9094 (base command). Read the float literals at 0x060A7C (the cap) and 0xD9AE0 (the bypass value). Search for readers of 0xFFFF9098 and 0xFFFF90A4.
- **Cited:**
  - 0608A0: fmov.s @r2,fr5 loads battery_voltage from 0xFFFF4130 [TRACED] as one lookup input; the 10..16 breakpoints match a 12 V system.
  - 0608AE/0608B2: mov #40,r0; fmov.s @(r0,r5),fr4 loads the other input from 0xFFFF90E0 in the jsr delay slot.
  - 0608A4-0608A8: mov.b @(2,r5),r0; tst; bf 0x0608C6. If the flag byte at 0xFFFF90BA is non-zero, the table is skipped and the constant at 0xD9AE0 is used in its place.
  - 0608B6/0608B8: the lookup result is stored to 0xFFFF9098 (0xFFFF90B8-32).
  - 0608BA-0608C4: fr8 = @0xFFFF9094 times @0xFFFF9098, i.e. a base command scaled by the table value. That makes it a multiplier, not a direct setpoint.
  - 0608CA-0608D2: fr9 = literal at 0x060A7C; fcmp/gt fr8,fr9 / fmov fr9,fr8 caps the product at that constant. The capped value is stored to 0xFFFF90A4 at 0608D6.
  - 0608D8-0608E4: fdiv fr9,fr8 normalises the capped command to a fraction of the cap. fneg is applied when the flag byte at 0xFFFF90BB is zero, giving a signed, bidirectional output.
  - 0608E6-0608EC: the signed fraction goes in fr4 to a tail jump to 0x133D8, consistent with a PWM or H-bridge driver.
  - Data shape: the gain falls monotonically with voltage (1.99 at 10 V to 0.809 at 16 V at -20 °C). That is the expected form of a 'hold drive constant despite supply voltage' compensation.

### 0xAF7C0 -- Duration limit (in FFFF67EC counter units) for holding flag FFFF925A, vs FFFF8E38 (axis range suggests a temperature)

1D x16 float32, data 0xDA340, range -40 .. 30. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

The table maps the float at FFFF8E38 to an integer that is stored at FFFF9278 and compared against the 16-bit counter at FFFF67EC. Once the counter reaches that value, flag FFFF925A is cleared. The flag is set on a rising edge of FFFF65C2 when FFFF3654 minus FFFF8E38 is at or above the calibration constant at 0xD9F20.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** X: value of float RAM FFFF8E38 (passed in fr4 from fr14). The -40..110 breakpoints look like a temperature in °C, but the 'knock_level_accum' label is unverified and nothing in the listing fixes the quantity.
- **Output units:** Count compared directly against the uint16 at FFFF67EC (a counter or timer, tick rate unknown). It is returned in r0 by the uint16 lookup variant and stored with mov.w to FFFF9278.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the input FFFF8E38 and the counter FFFF67EC is not established. In addition, the decoded cell values (zeros, then -40..30, i.e. X-80) look odd for a uint16 count limit. The cell type or scaling may be misdecoded, so the effective thresholds are uncertain.
- **Next step:** Find the readers of FFFF925A and the writer and increment rate of FFFF67EC to learn what the flag gates and what one count means. Also confirm how 0xBE8AC converts float cells to uint16, including what it does with negative values, and identify the source of FFFF8E38.
- **Cited:**
  - 062A9A-062A9C: fr14 = float @FFFF8E38; 062AB4 fmov fr14,fr4 passes it as the lookup input for descriptor 0xAF7C0 (062AAE).
  - 062AB0-062AB2: lookup goes through 0xBE8AC (table_desc_1d_uint16 [TRACED]), and 062AB6-062AB8 stores r0 as a word to FFFF9278.
  - 062ABA-062AC6: a second table, 0xAF7CC, is looked up with the same input and its float result is stored to FFFF9274 (r1-4).
  - 062AA6-062AA8: r14 = word @FFFF67EC. At 062AE6-062AEE, r5 = extu.w @FFFF9278, r2 = extu.w r14, and cmp/ge r5,r2 branches to 062AF8, which writes 0 to FFFF925A. So the counter reaching the table value clears the flag.
  - 062AF0-062AF6: if the counter is below the limit, the flag is kept only while float @FFFF9274 > fr12 (@FFFF364C); otherwise it is cleared.
  - 062AC8-062AE4: if the previous state byte FFFF927A is 0, r13 (@FFFF65C2) == 1, and (FFFF3654 - fr14) is not below the constant @0xD9F20, then FFFF925A is set to 1. This is an edge-triggered set; 062B00 then saves r13 to FFFF927A.

### 0xABC4C -- Self-test stub for pedal-based request map (2x2 known-answer table, slots gbr+236/240)

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7C20, range 0 .. 400. Triage hint: engine control.

It is a 2x2 linear stand-in for the normal pedal-based request maps. When the function is called with a non-zero mode (1-3), it replaces those maps with ABxxxx stubs, feeds them fixed ROM constants as inputs, and runs the normal min/blend arithmetic. It then subtracts per-mode expected constants from the result, which looks like a known-answer check of the request calculation rather than a driving calibration.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (50,100): in normal mode this input is the second lookup argument fr5 = stack[36] = FFFF679C, source unknown. In test modes it is a ROM constant (C4C4C/C4C50/C4C54).; X axis (0,4000): in normal mode this input is fr4 = stack[40] = accel_pedal_angle FFFF64D8. In test modes it is a ROM constant (C4C40/C4C44/C4C48). Which lookup argument maps to X versus Y is not confirmed.
- **Output units:** Probably the same as the normal-mode maps it stands in for (AF360/AF30C). Their unit is not established. The 0..400 range alone does not fix it.
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** none
- **Weakest link:** Reading the mode != 0 path as a known-answer self-test rests on the structure: constant inputs, a trivial linear 2x2 table, and subtraction of constant expected values. The callers of 0x0BCEB2 and the values they pass in r4 are not in the listing. The constant values at C4C40..C4C88 have not been checked to reproduce the expected results.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xC4C40-0xC4C88 and hand-compute the mode-1 chain: lookup of ABC4C at (C4C40, C4C4C), min with C4C64, then the blends. Check whether the result equals C4C74 (fr13), i.e. whether the difference is zero. Also find the callers of 0x0BCEB2 and confirm which r4 values they pass.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BCEF4: bf/s 0x0BCFAC. A non-zero argument r4 (copied to r14 at 0BCEC4) skips the live inputs read at 0BCEF8-0BCF0E (FFFF679C, accel_pedal_angle, vehicle_speed_kmh) and the normal table set AF328/AF360/AF30C etc.
  - 0BCFC6-0BCFDC, 0BD026-0BD03C, 0BD080-0BD096: in modes 1/2/3, stack[40], stack[36] and stack[44] (the lookup inputs) are loaded from ROM constants at C4C40..C4C60, so the table only ever sees fixed inputs.
  - 0BCFE4/0BD044/0BD09E: descriptor 0xABC4C is written to both gbr+236 and gbr+240 in every test mode.
  - 0BD106-0BD116 and 0BD214-0BD224: table_lookup_2D on gbr+236 or gbr+240 with fr4=stack[40] and fr5=stack[36]. The result is stored to FFFF8784-104 = FFFF871C.
  - 0BD25E-0BD268: float_min(fr15, [FFFF871C]). In test modes fr15 is the ROM constant at C4C64 (0BCFF4), so the table output is capped by a constant. The result goes to stack[64], then to FFFF8614-120 = FFFF859C (0BD3F2).
  - 0BD330-0BD33A: fr15 = min_result*clamp01(lookup gbr+256) + (1-clamp)*stack[68], a blend. The result is stored to stack[12].
  - 0BD008-0BD018 etc.: per-mode constants C4C68..C4C88 are loaded into stack[52], fr13 and fr12.
  - 0BD38C-0BD39A: the code forms stack[8]-stack[52], stack[12]-fr13 and stack[16]-fr12, i.e. computed minus expected. 0BD3A0-0BD3DC selects one difference by mode.
  - 0BD444-0BD482: outputs are written with bitwise-complement copies (not r2,r0 to gbr+0/4/8/12) and via desc_read_float_safe to FFFF85EC/85F4. Storing complement copies alongside values is a safety-monitor redundancy pattern.

### 0xABC68 -- Arithmetic self-test dummy table (test-vector stand-in for the 15x17 maps at gbr+244/+248)

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7C38, range 0 .. 400. Triage hint: engine control.

When the function's argument r4 is 1, 2 or 3, this 2x2 table replaces the two normal maps 0xAF344 and 0xAF2F0 in the third lookup slot. It is then evaluated at fixed ROM-constant inputs, and the pipeline result is subtracted from ROM-constant expected values. That makes it a known-answer self-test of the lookup and blend calculation, not a working calibration.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (50, 100): fed from stack slot 36. In test mode that slot holds a ROM constant (0xC4C4C, 0xC4C50 or 0xC4C54 depending on mode). In normal mode the same slot holds RAM FFFF679C. Which descriptor axis pairs with fr4 versus fr5 is not shown in the listing.; X axis (0, 4000): fed from stack slot 40. In test mode that slot holds a ROM constant (0xC4C40, 0xC4C44 or 0xC4C48). In normal mode the same slot holds accel_pedal_angle FFFF64D8. The pairing with fr4 versus fr5 is likewise not shown.
- **Output units:** Same internal quantity as the normal maps 0xAF344/0xAF2F0, unit unknown. The values (0, 0, 200, 400) are a synthetic test pattern, not physical calibration.
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** none
- **Weakest link:** The claim that 0xC4C68-0xC4C88 are expected answers and that the differences at 0BD394-0BD3AA feed a pass/fail check rests on code after 0BD49A, which is not in the listing.
- **Next step:** Read the floats at 0xC4C40-0xC4C88. Run the same pipeline by hand for mode 1, using this table plus 0xABC30, 0xABC4C, 0xABC84, 0xABCA0, 0xABBC8, 0xABC00 and 0xABBE4. Check whether the results equal 0xC4C68, 0xC4C74 and 0xC4C80. Then disassemble from 0x0BD49A to see the differences compared against a tolerance and a fault flag being set. Also find the callers of 0x0BCEB2 to see when r4 is 1, 2 or 3.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BCEF0-0BCEF4: the argument r14 is tested. When it is zero, 0BCF28-0BCF2E put the real maps 0xAF344/0xAF2F0 into gbr+244/+248. When it is 1, 2 or 3, 0BCFEA, 0BD04A and 0BD0A4 put 0xABC68 into both gbr+244 and gbr+248 instead.
  - Test branches 0BCFC6-0BCFDC, 0BD026-0BD03C and 0BD080-0BD096: the lookup inputs (stack 40, 36, 44) are overwritten with ROM constants from 0xC4C40-0xC4C60, chosen per mode. The live sensor values are not used.
  - The test branches also load stack52, fr13 and fr12 from ROM constants 0xC4C68-0xC4C88 (0BD008-0BD018 and the equivalents). In normal mode these come from RAM FFFF8614-40/-32/-24.
  - 0BD226-0BD236: the descriptor from gbr+248 is looked up with fr4 = stack40 and fr5 = stack36. The result goes to FFFF8784-100 (FFFF86F0).
  - 0BD286-0BD292: that result is passed through float_min against the gbr+224 lookup and stored at stack68.
  - 0BD2C2-0BD2CA and 0BD2FC-0BD33A: stack68 is blended with other lookups using weights clamped to 0..1, giving results at stack16, stack8 and stack12.

### 0xABCD8 -- Lookup-routine self-test table (synthetic 2x2 test vector for the 2D interpolator; monitoring/integrity check)

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7C98, range 0 .. 300. Triage hint: engine control.

Selector r4=1 and r4>=3 interpolate this tiny synthetic table at fixed ROM constants rather than live sensor values, and store the results to RAM. This looks like a known-answer test of the 2D lookup routine 0xBDD9C in a redundant monitoring layer. Selector 0 runs the real lookup (throttle x RPM table AF2D4/AF328) on live inputs and stores the result with its bitwise complement.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y (0xC7C88, 0..100): synthetic test axis; fed a fixed ROM constant (0xC4DAC or 0xC4DB0), not a sensor; X (0xC7C90, 2000..4000): synthetic test axis; fed a fixed ROM constant (0xC4DB4 or 0xC4DB8), not a sensor
- **Output units:** none (arbitrary test values; the interpolated result is compared, presumably elsewhere, to an expected answer)
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** none
- **Weakest link:** No comparison of the stored results (0xFFFF8660/8664/8668) against expected values is visible in this listing, so 'self-test' rests on the constant inputs and the synthetic data, not on an observed check. The constants at 0xC4DAC-0xC4DB8 are not decoded here.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xC4DAC, 0xC4DB0, 0xC4DB4 and 0xC4DB8 and interpolate this table by hand. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF8660, 0xFFFF8664 and 0xFFFF8668 (and of 0xFFFF86B4/0xFFFF8584) to check whether they compare against an expected constant and set a fault/diagnostic flag.
- **Cited:**
  - 0BD6C8-0BD6CE (r14==1 path): fr15/fr14 are loaded by fmov.s from ROM addresses 0xC4DAC and 0xC4DB4, which are constants, not RAM sensor values. The same applies at 0BD6F8-0BD6FE for the default path.
  - 0BD6A2-0BD6A8 (r14==0 path): by contrast, live RAM inputs are used: @(0xFFFF85A0-44)=0xFFFF8574 and @0xFFFF85A0, with the real throttle x RPM descriptor 0xAF2D4/0xAF328.
  - 0BD6D0/0BD6EC/0BD700: descriptor 0xABCD8 goes to gbr+536 and 0xABCF4 to gbr+540. The logic at 0BD70E-0BD72C picks one of them for the interpolator 0xBDD9C. Mode 1 (r4=4, r5=4) and mode >=3 (r2=1) both use 0xABCD8. Mode 2 (r4=0 < r5=4) uses 0xABCF4.
  - 0BD71C/0BD728: fr4=fr15 and fr5=fr14 are the lookup inputs. 0BD72E jsr 0xBDD9C and 0BD736 stores fr0 to the stack.
  - 0BD758-0BD75C: the mode-1 result is stored to 0xFFFF86B4-84 = 0xFFFF8660. 0BD76E-0BD770: the mode->=3 result is stored to 0xFFFF86B4-76 = 0xFFFF8668.
  - 0BD744-0BD74C: the mode-0 result is stored to 0xFFFF8584, and its bitwise NOT is stored to gbr+320 = 0xFFFF86B4. A complement copy is a typical redundant-safety-monitor pattern.
  - The table content (axes 0/100 and 2000/4000, cells 0,100,0,300) is a round-number synthetic grid with no physical shape, consistent with a known-answer test.

### 0xABD10 -- Table-interpolation self-test reference table (shadow/test copy for the 0xAF280 IAM-based lookup; not a real calibration)

2D 2x2 uint16, data 0xC7CC8, range 200 .. 400. Triage hint: knock.

In mode 0 the function looks up the real table 0xAF280 with ram_IAM and RAM 0xFFFF679C, caps the result with float_min, and stores it to 0xFFFF8570 with a bit-inverted copy at 0xFFFF86C0. In modes 1 and 2 it instead looks up this 2x2 table at fixed literal-pool inputs, caps the result with a constant, and stores it to 0xFFFF8674 (mode 1) or 0xFFFF8678 (mode 2). That pattern looks like a known-answer check of the lookup routine and FPU path.

- **Evidence class:** inferred
- **Axes:** Y axis (0, 1): synthetic test-input axis. In this path it is fed only by a constant from the literal pool (0xBCC7C or 0xBCC80); no physical sensor feeds it.; X axis (1000, 3000): synthetic test-input axis, fed only by the other literal-pool constant. The magnitude is not evidence of RPM.
- **Output units:** None physical. The result is compared against an expected value; the cells form an exact plane (200 + 100*Y + 0.05*(X-1000)), so any interpolation error is detectable.
- **Live lever:** no. **Tuning relevance:** none
- **Weakest link:** The 'self-test / known-answer' interpretation rests on the inputs being literal constants. The comparison against an expected result is not in this listing: whoever reads 0xFFFF8674/0xFFFF8678 has not been seen, and the literal-pool float values are not decoded.
- **Next step:** Decode the floats at 0xBCC74, 0xBCC78, 0xBCC7C and 0xBCC80. Then search for readers of 0xFFFF8674 and 0xFFFF8678 (and 0xFFFF86C0/0xFFFF8570) to confirm they are compared against an expected constant in a monitoring/diagnostic routine. Also identify the real table 0xAF280 (IAM vs 0xFFFF679C).
- **Cited:**
  - 0BCAB4-0BCAC2: when r4==0, fr4=@0xFFFF3234 (ram_IAM), fr5=@0xFFFF679C, fr14=@0xFFFF8574, and the descriptor is 0x000AF280. This table is NOT used in the live mode.
  - 0BCAC4-0BCADC: when r4!=0, fr14 comes from literal 0xBCC74 (mode 1) or 0xBCC78 (otherwise). fr4 and fr5 come from literals 0xBCC7C and 0xBCC80 via mova. The descriptor is 0x000ABD10. So this table's inputs are compile-time constants, not sensors.
  - 0BCAE0-0BCAEE: the table_lookup_2D result is passed to float_min together with fr14 (a constant cap in test modes).
  - 0BCB0A-0BCB1C: in test modes the result is stored to 0xFFFF86C0-76 = 0xFFFF8674 (mode 1) or 0xFFFF86C0-72 = 0xFFFF8678 (mode 2). No other consumer appears in this listing.
  - 0BCAFE-0BCB08: in live mode the value goes to 0xFFFF8570, and its bitwise NOT goes to @(336,gbr) = 0xFFFF86C0. That complement-copy redundancy is typical of safety-monitor code.
  - Data: the four cells 200/300/300/400 over axes 0..1 and 1000..3000 form a perfectly linear plane, ideal as a known-answer test pattern.

### 0xAD8D4 -- Closed-loop fuel target offset, variant selected by flags (rpm x load), probably lambda/EQ-ratio minus 1

2D 10x11 uint16, data 0xD1600, range -0.14999 .. -0.008. Triage hint: AFC / AFL trim.

Interpolates a value from engine load (g/rev) and rpm. It is one of four sibling tables picked by the caller's argument r5, a byte at FFFF984D and the byte returned by the routine at 0x22CF4. The result goes through a caller-supplied pointer. Its small negative values (-0.008 near idle/cruise, down to -0.15 at 5000 rpm and 1.6 g/rev) look like a rich-of-stoich target offset, but the code shown does not prove that.

- **Evidence class:** hunch
- **Axes:** Y (0xD15AC, 0.2..1.6): engine load, g/rev. The lookup is passed fr14, loaded from engine_load_current FFFF63F8, which fits the breakpoint range.; X (0xD15D8, 800..5000): engine speed, rpm. The lookup is passed fr15, loaded from rpm_current FFFF6624, which fits the breakpoint range.
- **Output units:** Dimensionless, raw/65536 - 0.5. Likely a lambda or EQ-ratio deviation from 1.0 (negative = rich), but not confirmed.
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** medium
- **Weakest link:** The consumer is outside this function. The result is written through a pointer passed in r4 by the caller, so nothing here shows what the value is added to, multiplied by or compared against. The closed-loop target identity rests only on the sibling labels and on the magnitude and sign of the values.
- **Next step:** Find the callers of 0x033CC4 (search for literal 0x00033CC4). Note the pointer passed in r4 and the flag passed in r5, then trace the RAM float that pointer refers to. If that float is added to 1.0 and multiplies or divides the stoich fuel calculation, or is compared with front O2 lambda, the table is a closed-loop target offset. Also search for writers of FFFF984D, and check what the routine at 0x22CF4 returns (MT/AT or another mode flag).
- **Cited:**
  - 033CCC/033CCE: fr15 = float at FFFF6624 (rpm_current [TRACED]); 033CD0/033CD2: fr14 = float at FFFF63F8 (engine_load_current [TRACED]).
  - 033D06/033D0A: fr5 = fr15 (rpm) and fr4 = fr14 (load) are passed to table_lookup_2D at 0xBE8E4. The axis ranges 800..5000 and 0.2..1.6 fit rpm and g/rev.
  - 033CD6-033CDA: r6 = byte returned by the routine at 0x22CF4. Its label 'check_engine_running' is doubtful, since the result is compared to 1 and picks between table pairs.
  - 033CDC-033CE6: if the argument r5 (copied to r14) is zero, r2 = byte at FFFF984D; otherwise r2 = 0.
  - 033CEA-033CF4: with r2 == 0 and r6 != 1, r4 = this descriptor 0xAD8D4. With r6 == 1 it is replaced by 0xAD8B8 (033CF6). With r2 != 0 the choice is 0xAD90C/0xAD8F0 (033CFE/033D02).
  - 033D0C/033D0E: the fr0 result is stored to *(*r4_in): r13 holds the caller's r4, so the address is supplied by the caller. No clamp, multiply or compare is applied in this function.
  - Sibling descriptors carry 'cl_target' labels. Labels are not evidence, but this one is consistent with a four-way family of closed-loop target tables.

2D 9x9 uint16, data 0xDA54C, range -10.55 .. 4.6905. Triage hint: scheduler / task.

### 0xADEA8 -- unknown (candidate: a factor of the product written to 0xFFFF805C, indexed by the peak-held value of RAM 0xFFFF62F4; variant for selector value 3 with engine running)

This table is one of twelve 1D curves. The ECU picks one by the byte at 0xFFFF6810 (cases 1–5 plus a default) and by whether the engine is running. Maps a signed float read from FFFF8C6C through a curve that is flat at -18 for negative inputs, 0 at 0, and rises steeply to 381 for positive inputs. The result is stored to FFFF8C60 and is not used again in this function. The same input also drives a separate clamped accumulator at FFFF8C64, which steps by one of two ROM constants depending on input thresholds.

- **Evidence class:** unknown
- **Axes:** X: signed float read from RAM 0xFFFF8C6C (8C78-12). The quantity is unknown. Breakpoints are symmetric, ±145.6 in steps of 20.8.
- **Output units:** unknown (ECU-internal; uint16*0.0076294-62.5, range -18..381)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The consumer of FFFF8C60 is not in this listing, and neither is the producer of the input at FFFF8C6C. Nothing shown fixes the physical meaning of the input or the output. The 'ignition timing' hint comes only from the gbr base and is unverified.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF8C60 (including gbr=0xFFFF8C60 offset 0 and @(-24,0xFFFF8C78) accesses) and for the writer of 0xFFFF8C6C. Also dump the floats at 0xD6334-0xD6348 to see the thresholds, step sizes and clamp limits of the companion integrator at 0xFFFF8C64.
- **Cited:**
  - 055A9C/055A9E/055AA2: r5=0xFFFF8C78, r0=-12, fmov.s @(r0,r5),fr4 loads the X input from 0xFFFF8C6C into fr4 before jsr table_lookup_1D at 0xBE830.
  - 055AA6/055AA8: the lookup result fr0 is stored with fmov.s fr0,@(r0,r5), r0=-24, i.e. to 0xFFFF8C60 (the gbr base). Nothing later in the function reads it.
  - 055AAC-055AB4: the same input (8C6C) is compared with ROM constant @0xD6334. If the constant is greater than the input, 8C64 is loaded and constant @0xD6344 is added (055AB6-055AC0).
  - 055AC4-055AD6: otherwise the input is compared with @0xD6338. If it is below that constant, the step is skipped. Else constant @0xD6348 is added to 8C64. This is a hysteresis or dead-band integrator.
  - 055AD8-055AEC: the sum goes through float_clamp_range (0xBE56C) with limits @0xD6340 and @0xD633C, and the result is stored to 0xFFFF8C64.
  - 055A84-055A96 and 055AEE-055AF8: if gbr+24 is nonzero, or if the state byte from 0xFFFF5F94 has just become 1 (previous copy at gbr+25 is 0), both 8C60 and 8C64 are zeroed. 055AFC saves the state byte to gbr+25.

### 0xAE850 -- unknown: upper-bound (max-side) offset vs a computed input; partner of the 0xAE85C lower-bound table

1D x4 float32, data 0xD6A90, range -0 .. 0. Triage hint: ignition timing.

This 1D uint16 map, biased by 0x8000, turns a value computed from FFFF8D14 into a signed 16-bit quantity stored at FFFF8D1E. That quantity feeds a running maximum and a max(scaled peak, current) result. Its sibling table 0xAE85C does the same on the minimum side. What the quantity physically is cannot be determined from this listing.

- **Evidence class:** unknown
- **Axes:** X: return value of routine 0xBDD88(word @FFFF8D14, 0), also stored to FFFF8D5A. Its physical quantity is unknown.
- **Output units:** unknown; raw uint16 with 0x8000 subtracted, giving signed 16-bit counts
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** The physical meaning of the X input depends on routine 0xBDD88 and the RAM word FFFF8D14, and neither is shown. The output's consumers (FFFF8D26, FFFF8D44) are read elsewhere. The upper/lower envelope role is observed, but the quantity is not.
- **Next step:** First re-decode the axis at 0xD6A80 and the data at 0xD6A90 as big-endian uint16, and check the descriptor's element count, to get the real breakpoints and cells. Then disassemble 0xBDD88 and find the writers of FFFF8D14 to identify the X quantity. Then search for readers of FFFF8D26 and FFFF8D44, and for code that resets FFFF8D22/FFFF8D24, to see what the max/min envelope drives.
- **Cited:**
  - 05723E/057240: the lookup goes through table_desc_2d_uint16 (0xBE8C4), not a float routine. The float32 decode in the header is therefore wrong, and the 'values' and 'breakpoints' shown (denormals of about 33/66/132/198 counts, and words near 0x81xx) are mis-decoded uint16 words.
  - 05722E-057238: input r5 = extu.w of 0xBDD88(r4 = word @gbr+0 = FFFF8D14, r5 = 0). The result is stored to FFFF8D5A (gbr+70) and reloaded as the X input for both tables.
  - 057244-05724A: result exts.w, minus 0x8000 (r14), stored to FFFF8D1E (gbr+10), making the cells signed with a 0x8000 bias.
  - 05724E-05725A: the second descriptor 0xAE85C uses the same input and the same bias, stored to FFFF8D20 (gbr+12).
  - 05725C-05726A: if FFFF8D1E > FFFF8D22 then FFFF8D22 = FFFF8D1E. This is a peak-hold maximum of this table's output.
  - 05726C-05727A: FFFF8D24 = min(FFFF8D24, FFFF8D20). The sibling table feeds a minimum hold.
  - 05727C-0572AC: FFFF8D22 * word@0xD619C, >>8 with round-toward-zero, clamped to <= 0x7FFF. Then max(that, FFFF8D1E) is stored to FFFF8D26 (gbr+18).

1D x4 float32, data 0xD6AC0, range -0 .. 8. Triage hint: unassigned.

This is a 1D lookup on the 16-bit word at 0xFFFF8D5C (GBR+48), done by the uint16 routine at 0xBE8C4. The integer result has 0x8000 subtracted, giving a signed value that is stored as a word to 0xFFFF8D2E (GBR+2). - **Output units:** Raw signed 16-bit integer (uint16 cell minus 0x8000). Physical scaling is unknown.

Then search the ROM for readers of 0xFFFF8D2E (GBR-relative @(2,gbr) with GBR=0xFFFF8D2C, or absolute) to find what the signed value feeds. Read as big-endian words, the data starts 0x8000, 0x8666, which is plausible for signed-offset uint16 cells (0, +1638). The later words 0x40C0 0000 / 0x4100 0000 look like 6.0f and 8.0f from a neighbouring object.

### 0xAF450 -- unknown (1D float map of a lag-filtered ratio-shaped signal derived from FFFF5EC0; output to FFFF6374)

1D x16 float32, data 0xD8B84, range 0 .. 65. Triage hint: unassigned.

The function reads float FFFF5EC0 and forms a hyperbolic ratio of it with the calibration constant at 0xD8AD4, probably x/(x+c), then scales it by a pool constant. It lag-filters that result, using a coefficient interpolated from the uint16 calibration at 0xD8A64, and looks the filtered value up in this table. The output (65 falling to 0 by x=7) is stored to FFFF6374 and is not consumed in this function.

- **Evidence class:** unknown
- **Axes:** X: lag-filtered value of (scaled) FFFF5EC0/(FFFF5EC0 + const@0xD8AD4), held in FFFF6390 and FFFF638C. Its physical quantity is unknown, and the ratio orientation depends on float_safe_div argument order.
- **Output units:** unknown (0..65, monotonically decreasing; magnitude alone does not fix units)
- **Live lever:** cannot_tell. **Tuning relevance:** low
- **Weakest link:** Nothing in the listing identifies what FFFF5EC0 physically is, and the only consumer of the table output is a RAM store to FFFF6374 that is read elsewhere. The meaning of both axis and output is therefore unestablished. The pool constants at 0x1FC38, 0x1FC4C and 0x1FC58 and the calibration at 0xD8AD4 were not decoded either.
- **Next step:** Search the ROM for readers of FFFF6374 to see what the table output feeds (compare, multiply, store), and for writers of FFFF5EC0 to identify the input signal. Also decode the float constants at 0xD8AD4, 0x1FC4C and 0x1FC58, and the word at 0xD8A64, to pin down the ratio scaling and filter constant.
- **Cited:**
  - 01FAB4-01FAC2: fr4=fr5=@FFFF5EC0, then in the delay slot fadd fr8,fr5 with fr8=@0xD8AD4, then jsr float_safe_div 0xBE628. This is a ratio of the form x/(x+c) (or its inverse).
  - 01FAC4-01FACE: the quotient is multiplied by the pool constant @0x1FC4C and stored to FFFF6388 (r14=FFFF6390, disp -8).
  - 01FAA2-01FAB2: fmac_interp_uint16 on the calibration word at 0xD8A64 gives fr14. At 01FAD2 fr14 is passed as fr6 to float_lag_filter 0xBEA40, so it is presumably the filter coefficient.
  - 01FAD4-01FAE6: lag filter of FFFF6388 against the previous value FFFF638C, with fr7 = const @0x1FC58. The result is written to FFFF638C and to FFFF6390.
  - 01FAE8-01FAEE: table_lookup_1D on descriptor 0xAF450 with fr4=@FFFF6390.
  - 01FAF0-01FAF2: the lookup result fr0 is stored to FFFF6374 (FFFF6390-28). The function then returns, so the consumer is not in this listing.
